Namo tassa bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa

Homage to that Blessed One, the Worthy One, the Perfectly Enlightened One.

Khuddakanikāye

The Khuddaka Nikāya

Udānapāḷi

Udāna

1. Bodhivaggo

1. The Chapter on Enlightenment

1. Paṭhamabodhisuttaṃ

1. The First Discourse on Enlightenment

1. Evaṃ [V 0.0069][M 0.0077][P 0.0001][T 0.0073] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā uruvelāyaṃ viharati najjā nerañjarāya tīre bodhirukkhamūle paṭhamābhisambuddho. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā sattāhaṃ ekapallaṅkena nisinno hoti vimuttisukhapaṭisaṃvedī vimuttisukhaṃ paṭisaṃvedī (syā. pī. ka.). Atha kho bhagavā tassa sattāhassa accayena tamhā samādhimhā vuṭṭhahitvā rattiyā paṭhamaṃ yāmaṃ paṭiccasamuppādaṃ anulomaṃ sādhukaṃ manasākāsi –

1. Thus have I heard: At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Uruvelā, on the bank of the Nerañjarā River, at the foot of the Bodhi tree, having just attained full enlightenment. At that time, the Blessed One remained seated in one posture for seven days, experiencing the bliss of liberation. Then, at the end of those seven days, the Blessed One arose from that concentration and, in the first watch of the night, thoroughly attended to dependent origination in forward order:

“Iti imasmiṃ sati idaṃ hoti, imassuppādā idaṃ uppajjati, yadidaṃ – avijjāpaccayā saṅkhārā, saṅkhārapaccayā viññāṇaṃ, viññāṇapaccayā nāmarūpaṃ, nāmarūpapaccayā saḷāyatanaṃ, saḷāyatanapaccayā phasso, phassapaccayā vedanā, vedanāpaccayā taṇhā, taṇhāpaccayā upādānaṃ, upādānapaccayā [V 0.0070] bhavo, bhavapaccayā jāti, jātipaccayā jarāmaraṇaṃ sokaparidevadukkhadomanassupāyāsā sambhavanti. Evametassa kevalassa dukkhakkhandhassa samudayo hotī”ti.

“When this exists, that comes to be; with the arising of this, that arises. That is: With ignorance as condition, volitional formations come to be; with volitional formations as condition, consciousness comes to be; with consciousness as condition, name-and-form comes to be; with name-and-form as condition, the six sense bases come to be; with the six sense bases as condition, contact comes to be; with contact as condition, feeling comes to be; with feeling as condition, craving comes to be; with craving as condition, clinging comes to be; with clinging as condition, becoming comes to be; with becoming as condition, birth comes to be; with birth as condition, aging and death, sorrow, lamentation, pain, grief, and despair come to be. Thus is the arising of this entire mass of suffering.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, having understood this matter, on that occasion the Blessed One uttered this inspired utterance:

“Yadā [M 0.0078][T 0.0074] have pātubhavanti dhammā,

Ātāpino jhāyato brāhmaṇassa;

Athassa kaṅkhā vapayanti sabbā,

Yato pajānāti sahetudhamma”nti. paṭhamaṃ;

“When phenomena become manifest to the ardent, meditating brahmin, then all his doubts vanish, since he comprehends the phenomena with their cause.” First.

2. Dutiyabodhisuttaṃ

2. The Second Discourse on Enlightenment

2. Evaṃ [P 0.0002] me sutaṃ – eka samayaṃ bhagavā uruvelāyaṃ viharati najjā nerañjarāya tīre bodhirukkhamūle paṭhamābhisambuddho. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā sattāhaṃ ekapallaṅkena nisinno hoti vimuttisukhapaṭisaṃvedī. Atha kho bhagavā tassa sattāhassa accayena tamhā samādhimhā vuṭṭhahitvā rattiyā majjhimaṃ yāmaṃ paṭiccasamuppādaṃ paṭilomaṃ sādhukaṃ manasākāsi –

2. Thus have I heard: At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Uruvelā, on the bank of the Nerañjarā River, at the foot of the Bodhi tree, having newly attained full enlightenment. At that time, the Blessed One sat for seven days in one posture, experiencing the bliss of liberation. Then, at the end of those seven days, the Blessed One emerged from that concentration and, in the middle watch of the night, thoroughly attended to dependent origination in reverse order:

“Iti imasmiṃ asati idaṃ na hoti, imassa nirodhā idaṃ nirujjhati, yadidaṃ – avijjānirodhā saṅkhāranirodho, saṅkhāranirodhā viññāṇanirodho, viññāṇanirodhā nāmarūpanirodho, nāmarūpanirodhā saḷāyatananirodho, saḷāyatananirodhā phassanirodho, phassanirodhā vedanānirodho, vedanānirodhā taṇhānirodho, taṇhānirodhā upādānanirodho, upādānanirodhā bhavanirodho, bhavanirodhā jātinirodho, jātinirodhā jarāmaraṇaṃ sokaparidevadukkhadomanassupāyāsā nirujjhanti. Evametassa kevalassa dukkhakkhandhassa nirodho hotī”ti.

“When this is not, that is not; with the cessation of this, that ceases. That is: With the cessation of ignorance, the cessation of volitional formations comes to be; with the cessation of volitional formations, the cessation of consciousness; with the cessation of consciousness, the cessation of name-and-form; with the cessation of name-and-form, the cessation of the six sense bases; with the cessation of the six sense bases, the cessation of contact; with the cessation of contact, the cessation of feeling; with the cessation of feeling, the cessation of craving; with the cessation of craving, the cessation of clinging; with the cessation of clinging, the cessation of becoming; with the cessation of becoming, the cessation of birth; with the cessation of birth, aging and death, sorrow, lamentation, pain, grief, and despair cease. Thus is the cessation of this entire mass of suffering.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, having understood this matter, on that occasion, the Blessed One uttered this inspired utterance:

“Yadā have pātubhavanti dhammā,

Ātāpino [T 0.0075] jhāyato brāhmaṇassa;

Athassa kaṅkhā vapayanti sabbā,

Yato khayaṃ paccayānaṃ avedī”ti. dutiyaṃ;

“When phenomena become manifest to the ardent, meditating brahmin, then all his doubts vanish, since he has understood the destruction of conditions.” Second.

3. Tatiyabodhisuttaṃ

3. The Third Discourse on Enlightenment

3. Evaṃ [V 0.0071] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā uruvelāyaṃ viharati najjā nerañjarāya tīre bodhirukkhamūle paṭhamābhisambuddho. Tena kho pana samayena [M 0.0079] bhagavā sattāhaṃ ekapallaṅkena nisinno hoti vimuttisukhapaṭisaṃvedī. Atha kho bhagavā tassa sattāhassa accayena tamhā samādhimhā vuṭṭhahitvā rattiyā pacchimaṃ yāmaṃ paṭiccasamuppādaṃ anulomapaṭilomaṃ sādhukaṃ manasākāsi –

3. Thus have I heard. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Uruvelā on the bank of the Nerañjarā River, at the foot of the Bodhi tree, having just attained full enlightenment. Then, for seven days, the Blessed One sat in one posture, experiencing the bliss of liberation. Then, after the passing of those seven days, the Blessed One arose from that concentration and, in the last watch of the night, thoroughly attended to dependent origination in forward and reverse order:

“Iti imasmiṃ sati idaṃ hoti, imassuppādā idaṃ uppajjati, imasmiṃ asati idaṃ na hoti, imassa nirodhā idaṃ nirujjhati; yadidaṃ – avijjāpaccayā saṅkhārā, saṅkhārapaccayā viññāṇaṃ, viññāṇapaccayā nāmarūpaṃ, nāmarūpapaccayā saḷāyatanaṃ, saḷāyatanapaccayā phasso, phassapaccayā vedanā, vedanāpaccayā taṇhā, taṇhāpaccayā upādānaṃ, upādānapaccayā bhavo, bhavapaccayā jāti, jātipaccayā jarāmaraṇaṃ sokaparidevadukkhadomanassupāyāsā sambhavanti. Evametassa kevalassa dukkhakkhandhassa samudayo hoti.

“When this exists, that comes to be; with the arising of this, that arises. When this does not exist, that does not come to be; with the cessation of this, that ceases. That is: With ignorance as condition, volitional formations come to be; with volitional formations as condition, consciousness comes to be; with consciousness as condition, name-and-form comes to be; with name-and-form as condition, the six sense bases come to be; with the six sense bases as condition, contact comes to be; with contact as condition, feeling comes to be; with feeling as condition, craving comes to be; with craving as condition, clinging comes to be; with clinging as condition, becoming comes to be; with becoming as condition, birth comes to be; with birth as condition, aging and death, sorrow, lamentation, pain, grief, and despair come to be. Thus is the arising of this entire mass of suffering.

“Avijjāya tveva asesavirāganirodhā saṅkhāranirodho, saṅkhāranirodhā viññāṇanirodho, viññāṇanirodhā nāmarūpanirodho, nāmarūpanirodhā saḷāyatananirodho, saḷāyatananirodhā phassanirodho, phassanirodhā vedanānirodho, vedanānirodhā taṇhānirodho[T 0.0076], taṇhānirodhā upādānanirodho, upādānanirodhā bhavanirodho, bhavanirodhā jātinirodho, jātinirodhā jarāmaraṇaṃ sokaparidevadukkhadomanassupāyāsā nirujjhanti. Evametassa kevalassa dukkhakkhandhassa nirodho [P 0.0003] hotī”ti.

“With the remainderless fading away and cessation of that very ignorance, the cessation of volitional formations comes to be; with the cessation of volitional formations, the cessation of consciousness; with the cessation of consciousness, the cessation of name-and-form; with the cessation of name-and-form, the cessation of the six sense bases; with the cessation of the six sense bases, the cessation of contact; with the cessation of contact, the cessation of feeling; with the cessation of feeling, the cessation of craving; with the cessation of craving, the cessation of clinging; with the cessation of clinging, the cessation of becoming; with the cessation of becoming, the cessation of birth; with the cessation of birth, aging and death, sorrow, lamentation, pain, grief, and despair cease. Thus is the cessation of this entire mass of suffering.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Yadā have pātubhavanti dhammā,

Ātāpino jhāyato brāhmaṇassa;

Vidhūpayaṃ tiṭṭhati mārasenaṃ,

Sūriyova suriyova (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.) obhāsayamantalikkha”nti. tatiyaṃ;

“When phenomena become manifest to the ardent, meditating brahmin, he stands dispelling Māra’s army, like the sun illuminating the sky.” Third.

4. Huṃhuṅkasuttaṃ

4. The Discourse on ‘Huṃhuṅka’

4. Evaṃ [M 0.0080][V 0.0072] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā uruvelāyaṃ viharati najjā nerañjarāya tīre ajapālanigrodhe paṭhamābhisambuddho. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā sattāhaṃ ekapallaṅkena nisinno hoti vimuttisukhapaṭisaṃvedī. Atha kho bhagavā tassa sattāhassa accayena tamhā samādhimhā vuṭṭhāsi.

4. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Uruvelā on the bank of the Nerañjarā River, at the Ajapāla Banyan tree, having first attained full enlightenment. At that time, the Blessed One sat for seven days in one posture, experiencing the bliss of liberation. Then, at the end of those seven days, the Blessed One arose from that concentration.

Atha kho aññataro huṃhuṅkajātiko huhuṅkajātiko (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.) brāhmaṇo yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavatā saddhiṃ sammodi. Sammodanīyaṃ kathaṃ sāraṇīyaṃ vītisāretvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhāsi. Ekamantaṃ ṭhito kho so brāhmaṇo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “kittāvatā nu kho, bho gotama, brāhmaṇo hoti, katame ca pana brāhmaṇakaraṇā brāhmaṇakārakā (ka.) dhammā”ti?

Then a certain brahmin who had the habit of making the sound 'huṃ huṃ' approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he exchanged joyful greetings with the Blessed One. After concluding a conversation that was courteous and memorable, he stood to one side. Standing to one side, that brahmin said this to the Blessed One: 'To what extent, Master Gotama, is one a brahmin, and what are the qualities that make one a brahmin?'

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā [T 0.0077] tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, having understood the meaning of this matter, the Blessed One on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Yo brāhmaṇo bāhitapāpadhammo,

Nihuṃhuṅko nihuhuṅko (sī. syā. kaṃ pī.) nikkasāvo yatatto;

Vedantagū vūsitabrahmacariyo,

Dhammena so brahmavādaṃ vadeyya;

Yassussadā natthi kuhiñci loke”ti. catutthaṃ;

The brahmin who has evil states expelled, is without the 'huṃhuṃ' of arrogance, is stainless, and self-controlled; who has reached the end of knowledge, has lived the holy life—he may rightly speak the word 'brahmin,' for whom there is no swelling up of defilements anywhere in the world.

5. Brāhmaṇasuttaṃ

5. The Brahmin Sutta

5. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā ca sāriputto āyasmā ca mahāmoggallāno āyasmā ca mahākassapo āyasmā ca mahākaccāno mahākaccāyano (sī. pī. ka.) āyasmā ca mahākoṭṭhiko āyasmā ca mahākappino āyasmā ca mahācundo āyasmā ca anuruddho āyasmā ca revato āyasmā ca nando ānando (sī. pī.) yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu[P 0.0004].

5. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling in Sāvatthī, in the Jeta Grove, the monastery of Anāthapiṇḍika. At that time, the Venerable Sāriputta, the Venerable Mahāmoggallāna, the Venerable Mahākassapa, the Venerable Mahākaccāna, the Venerable Mahākoṭṭhika, the Venerable Mahākappina, the Venerable Mahācunda, the Venerable Anuruddha, the Venerable Revata, and the Venerable Nanda approached where the Blessed One was.

Addasā [M 0.0081] kho bhagavā te āyasmante dūratova āgacchante; disvāna bhikkhū āmantesi – “ete, bhikkhave, brāhmaṇā āgacchanti; ete, bhikkhave, brāhmaṇā āgacchantī”ti. Evaṃ vutte[V 0.0073], aññataro brāhmaṇajātiko bhikkhu bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “kittāvatā nu kho, bhante, brāhmaṇo hoti, katame ca pana brāhmaṇakaraṇā dhammā”ti?

The Blessed One saw those venerable ones coming from afar. Having seen them, he addressed the monks: 'Monks, these are brahmins coming; monks, these are brahmins coming.' When this was said, a certain monk of brahmin birth said this to the Blessed One: 'Venerable Sir, to what extent is one a brahmin, and what are the qualities that make one a brahmin?'

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, having understood the meaning of this matter, the Blessed One on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Bāhitvā pāpake dhamme, ye caranti sadā satā;

Khīṇasaṃyojanā [T 0.0078] buddhā, te ve teva (sī.) lokasmi brāhmaṇā”ti. pañcamaṃ;

Having expelled evil states, those who always fare mindfully, with fetters destroyed, enlightened—they, indeed, are brahmins in the world.

6. Mahākassapasuttaṃ

6. The Mahākassapa Sutta

6. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā rājagahe viharati veḷuvane kalandakanivāpe. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā mahākassapo pippaliguhāyaṃ pipphaliguhāyaṃ (syā.), simbaliguhāyaṃ (ka.) viharati ābādhiko ābādhiko hoti (syā. pī.) dukkhito bāḷhagilāno. Atha kho āyasmā mahākassapo aparena samayena tamhā ābādhā vuṭṭhāsi. Atha kho āyasmato mahākassapassa tamhā ābādhā vuṭṭhitassa etadahosi – “yaṃnūnāhaṃ rājagahaṃ piṇḍāya paviseyya”nti.

6. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Rājagaha in the Veḷuvana, the Kalandakanivāpa. Now at that time the Venerable Mahākassapa was dwelling in the Pipphali Cave, sick, afflicted, and gravely ill. Then, after some time, the Venerable Mahākassapa arose from that sickness. Then, having arisen from that sickness, this thought occurred to the Venerable Mahākassapa: "What if I were to enter Rājagaha for alms?"

Tena kho pana samayena pañcamattāni devatāsatāni ussukkaṃ āpannāni honti āyasmato mahākassapassa piṇḍapātapaṭilābhāya. Atha kho āyasmā mahākassapo tāni pañcamattāni devatāsatāni paṭikkhipitvā pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya rājagahaṃ piṇḍāya pāvisi – yena daliddavisikhā kapaṇavisikhā pesakāravisikhā. Addasā kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ mahākassapaṃ rājagahe piṇḍāya carantaṃ yena daliddavisikhā kapaṇavisikhā pesakāravisikhā.

Now at that time, five hundred deities had become anxious for the Venerable Mahākassapa to obtain almsfood. Then the Venerable Mahākassapa, having dismissed those five hundred deities, dressed in the morning, took his bowl and robe, and entered Rājagaha for alms, toward the quarter of the poor, the quarter of the destitute, the quarter of the weavers. The Blessed One saw the Venerable Mahākassapa walking for alms in Rājagaha, toward the quarter of the poor, the quarter of the destitute, the quarter of the weavers.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, having understood this matter, on that occasion the Blessed One uttered this inspired utterance:

“Anaññaposimaññātaṃ, dantaṃ sāre patiṭṭhitaṃ;

Khīṇāsavaṃ vantadosaṃ, tamahaṃ brūmi brāhmaṇa”nti. chaṭṭhaṃ;

Not supported by others, unrenowned, tamed, established in the essence, with taints destroyed, faults expelled—him I call a brahmin.

7. Ajakalāpakasuttaṃ

7. The Ajakalāpaka Sutta

7. Evaṃ [M 0.0082][V 0.0074] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā pāvāyaṃ pāṭaliyaṃ (pī.) viharati ajakalāpake [T 0.0079] cetiye, ajakalāpakassa yakkhassa bhavane. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā rattandhakāratimisāyaṃ [P 0.0005] abbhokāse nisinno hoti; devo ca ekamekaṃ phusāyati. Atha kho ajakalāpako yakkho bhagavato bhayaṃ chambhitattaṃ lomahaṃsaṃ uppādetukāmo yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavato avidūre tikkhattuṃ “akkulo pakkulo”ti akkulapakkulikaṃ akāsi – “eso te, samaṇa, pisāco”ti.

7. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Pāvā, at the Ajakalāpaka Shrine, in the dwelling of the yakkha Ajakalāpaka. Now at that time, the Blessed One was sitting in the open air in the thick darkness of the night, and the rain was drizzling drop by drop. Then the yakkha Ajakalāpaka, wishing to arouse fear, trembling, and horripilation in the Blessed One, approached where the Blessed One was. Having approached, not far from the Blessed One, he three times made a clamorous noise, saying, “Akkulo pakkulo,” and he said: “This is a pisāca for you, recluse!”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Yadā sakesu dhammesu, pāragū hoti brāhmaṇo;

Atha etaṃ pisācañca, pakkulañcātivattatī”ti. sattamaṃ;

When a brahmin has gone to the far shore in his own dhammas, he then overcomes this pisāca and that clamor.

8. Saṅgāmajisuttaṃ

8. The Saṅgāmaji Sutta

8. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā saṅgāmaji sāvatthiṃ anuppatto hoti bhagavantaṃ dassanāya. Assosi kho āyasmato saṅgāmajissa purāṇadutiyikā – “ayyo kira saṅgāmaji sāvatthiṃ anuppatto”ti. Sā dārakaṃ ādāya jetavanaṃ agamāsi.

8. Thus have I heard. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika's monastery. Now at that time, the Venerable Saṅgāmaji had arrived in Sāvatthī to see the Blessed One. The former wife of the Venerable Saṅgāmaji heard, "The noble Saṅgāmaji, it is said, has arrived in Sāvatthī." Taking her child, she went to Jeta's Grove.

Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā saṅgāmaji aññatarasmiṃ rukkhamūle divāvihāraṃ nisinno hoti. Atha kho āyasmato saṅgāmajissa purāṇadutiyikā yenāyasmā saṅgāmaji tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā āyasmantaṃ saṅgāmajiṃ etadavoca – “khuddaputtañhi khuddaputtāmhi (sī.), samaṇa, posa ma”nti. Evaṃ vutte, āyasmā saṅgāmaji tuṇhī ahosi.

Now at that time, the Venerable Saṅgāmaji was seated for his day-sojourn at the root of a certain tree. Then, the former wife of the Venerable Saṅgāmaji approached where the Venerable Saṅgāmaji was; having approached, she said this to the Venerable Saṅgāmaji: “Ascetic, support me, who has a young son.” When this was said, the Venerable Saṅgāmaji became silent.

Dutiyampi kho āyasmato saṅgāmajissa purāṇadutiyikā āyasmantaṃ [T 0.0080] saṅgāmajiṃ etadavoca – “khuddaputtañhi, samaṇa, posa ma”nti. Dutiyampi kho āyasmā saṅgāmaji tuṇhī ahosi.

For a second time, the former wife of the Venerable Saṅgāmaji said this to the Venerable Saṅgāmaji: “Ascetic, support me, who has a young son.” For a second time, the Venerable Saṅgāmaji became silent.

Tatiyampi [M 0.0083][V 0.0075] kho āyasmato saṅgāmajissa purāṇadutiyikā āyasmantaṃ saṅgāmajiṃ etadavoca – “khuddaputtañhi, samaṇa, posa ma”nti. Tatiyampi kho āyasmā saṅgāmaji tuṇhī ahosi.

For a third time, the former wife of the Venerable Saṅgāmaji said this to the Venerable Saṅgāmaji: “Ascetic, support me, who has a young son.” For a third time, the Venerable Saṅgāmaji became silent.

Atha kho āyasmato saṅgāmajissa purāṇadutiyikā taṃ dārakaṃ āyasmato saṅgāmajissa purato nikkhipitvā pakkāmi pakkami (ka.) evamuparipi – “eso esa (sī. ka.) te, samaṇa, putto; posa na”nti.

Then the former wife of the Venerable Saṅgāmaji, having placed the child in front of the Venerable Saṅgāmaji, departed, saying: “This, ascetic, is your son; support him.”

Atha kho āyasmā saṅgāmaji taṃ dārakaṃ neva olokesi nāpi ālapi. Atha kho āyasmato saṅgāmajissa purāṇadutiyikā [P 0.0006] avidūraṃ avidūre (syā. pī.) gantvā apalokentī addasa āyasmantaṃ saṅgāmajiṃ taṃ dārakaṃ neva olokentaṃ nāpi ālapantaṃ, disvānassā etadahosi – “na cāyaṃ samaṇo puttenapi atthiko”ti. Tato paṭinivattitvā dārakaṃ ādāya pakkāmi. Addasā kho bhagavā dibbena cakkhunā visuddhena atikkantamānusakena āyasmato saṅgāmajissa purāṇadutiyikāya evarūpaṃ vippakāraṃ.

Then the Venerable Saṅgāmaji neither looked at the child nor spoke to him. Then the former wife of the Venerable Saṅgāmaji, having gone not far away and looking back, saw that the Venerable Saṅgāmaji was neither looking at the child nor speaking to him. Seeing this, the thought occurred to her: ‘This ascetic has no need even for his son.’ Thereupon, turning back, she took the child and departed. The Blessed One saw with the divine eye, which is purified and surpasses the human, this perverse action of the Venerable Saṅgāmaji's former wife.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Āyantiṃ nābhinandati, pakkamantiṃ na socati;

Saṅgā saṅgāmajiṃ muttaṃ, tamahaṃ brūmi brāhmaṇa”nti. aṭṭhamaṃ;

“He does not delight in her coming, nor grieve for her departing; Saṅgāmaji, freed from attachment—him I call a brahmin.” The Eighth.

9. Jaṭilasuttaṃ

9. The Discourse on the Matted-Hair Ascetics

9. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā gayāyaṃ viharati gayāsīse. Tena kho pana samayena sambahulā jaṭilā sītāsu hemantikāsu [T 0.0081] rattīsu antaraṭṭhake himapātasamaye gayāyaṃ ummujjantipi nimujjantipi, ummujjanimujjampi karonti osiñcantipi, aggimpi juhanti – “iminā suddhī”ti.

9. Thus have I heard. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Gayā, at Gayā Head. At that time, many matted-hair ascetics, during the cold winter nights, in the eight-day interval of snowfall, were at Gayā plunging into the water and emerging from it, performing the act of plunging and emerging, sprinkling themselves with water, and offering to the fire, thinking, “By this there is purification.”

Addasā kho bhagavā te sambahule jaṭile sītāsu hemantikāsu rattīsu antaraṭṭhake himapātasamaye gayāyaṃ ummujjantepi nimujjantepi ummujjanimujjampi karonte ummujjanimujjaṃ karontepi (sī. pī. ka.) osiñcantepi aggimpi juhante – “iminā suddhī”ti.

The Blessed One saw those many matted-hair ascetics, during the cold winter nights, in the eight-day interval of snowfall, at Gayā plunging into the water and emerging from it, performing the act of plunging and emerging, sprinkling themselves with water, and offering to the fire, thinking, “By this there is purification.”

Atha [M 0.0084][V 0.0076] kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Na udakena sucī hotī, bahvettha nhāyatī nahāyatī (sī.) jano;

Yamhi saccañca dhammo ca, so sucī so ca brāhmaṇo”ti. navamaṃ;

“Not by water does one become pure, though many people bathe herein. In whom there are truth and Dhamma, he is pure, and he is a brahmin.” The Ninth.

10. Bāhiyasuttaṃ

10. The Discourse on Bāhiya

10. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena bāhiyo dārucīriyo suppārake paṭivasati samuddatīre sakkato garukato mānito pūjito apacito lābhī cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ. Atha kho bāhiyassa dārucīriyassa rahogatassa paṭisallīnassa evaṃ cetaso parivitakko udapādi – “ye kho keci loke arahanto vā arahattamaggaṃ vā samāpannā, ahaṃ tesaṃ aññataro”ti.

10. Thus have I heard. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling in Sāvatthī, in Jeta's Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika's Park. At that time, Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth was dwelling in Suppāraka on the seacoast. He was honored, respected, esteemed, worshipped, and revered, a recipient of robes, almsfood, lodging, and medicinal requisites for the sick. Then, as Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth was in seclusion and solitude, this reflection arose in his mind: ‘Whoever in the world are arahants or have entered upon the path to Arahantship, I am one of them.’”

Atha kho [P 0.0007] bāhiyassa dārucīriyassa purāṇasālohitā devatā anukampikā atthakāmā bāhiyassa dārucīriyassa cetasā cetoparivitakkamaññāya yena bāhiyo dārucīriyo tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bāhiyaṃ dārucīriyaṃ etadavoca – “neva kho tvaṃ[T 0.0082], bāhiya, arahā, nāpi arahattamaggaṃ vā samāpanno. Sāpi te paṭipadā natthi yāya tvaṃ arahā vā assa assasi (syā. ka.) arahattamaggaṃ vā samāpanno”ti.

Then a deity, a former blood-relative of Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth, compassionate and desiring his welfare, having known with his own mind the reflection in the mind of Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth, approached him. Having approached, the deity said this to Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth: ‘You, Bāhiya, are by no means an arahant, nor have you entered upon the path to Arahantship. You do not even have that practice by which you could be an arahant or could enter upon the path to Arahantship.’”

“Atha ke carahi sadevake loke arahanto vā arahattamaggaṃ vā samāpanno”ti? “Atthi, bāhiya, uttaresu janapadesu janapade (sī.) sāvatthi nāma nagaraṃ. Tattha so bhagavā etarahi viharati arahaṃ sammāsambuddho. So hi, bāhiya, bhagavā arahā ceva arahattāya ca dhammaṃ desetī”ti.

“‘Then who, pray, in the world with its devas are arahants or have entered upon the path to Arahantship?’ ‘There is, Bāhiya, in the northern districts a city named Sāvatthī. The Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One, is now dwelling there. Indeed, Bāhiya, that Blessed One is an arahant and teaches the Dhamma for the attainment of Arahantship.’”

Atha kho bāhiyo dārucīriyo tāya devatāya saṃvejito tāvadeva suppārakamhā pakkāmi. Sabbattha ekarattiparivāsena yena sāvatthi jetavanaṃ anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāmo tenupasaṅkami. Tena kho pana samayena [M 0.0085] sambahulā bhikkhū abbhokāse caṅkamanti. Atha kho bāhiyo dārucīriyo yena te bhikkhū tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā te bhikkhū etadavoca – “kahaṃ nu kho, bhante, etarahi bhagavā viharati arahaṃ sammāsambuddho? Dassanakāmamhā mayaṃ taṃ [V 0.0077] bhagavantaṃ arahantaṃ sammāsambuddha”nti. “Antaragharaṃ paviṭṭho kho, bāhiya, bhagavā piṇḍāyā”ti.

Then Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth, stirred with urgency by that deity, departed from Suppāraka at that very moment. Stopping for only a single night at any place along the way, he approached Sāvatthī, Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. At that time, a number of bhikkhus were practicing walking meditation in an open space. Then Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth approached those bhikkhus and, having approached, said this to them: ‘Venerable sirs, where is the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One, now dwelling? We are desirous of seeing that Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One.’ ‘The Blessed One has entered the inner village for alms, Bāhiya.’”

Atha kho bāhiyo dārucīriyo taramānarūpo jetavanā nikkhamitvā sāvatthiṃ pavisitvā addasa bhagavantaṃ sāvatthiyaṃ piṇḍāya carantaṃ pāsādikaṃ pasādanīyaṃ santindriyaṃ santamānasaṃ [T 0.0083] uttamadamathasamathamanuppattaṃ dantaṃ guttaṃ yatindriyaṃ nāgaṃ. Disvāna yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavato pāde sirasā nipatitvā bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “desetu me, bhante bhagavā, dhammaṃ; desetu, sugato, dhammaṃ, yaṃ mamassa dīgharattaṃ hitāya sukhāyā”ti. Evaṃ vutte, bhagavā bāhiyaṃ dārucīriyaṃ etadavoca – “akālo kho tāva, bāhiya, antaragharaṃ paviṭṭhamhā piṇḍāyā”ti.

Then Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth, in great haste, departed from Jetavana, entered Sāvatthī, and saw the Blessed One walking for alms in Sāvatthī—graceful, inspiring, with tranquil faculties and a tranquil mind, having attained the highest calm and self-control, tamed, guarded, with controlled faculties, a great being. Having seen him, he approached the Blessed One and, having approached, fell with his head at the Blessed One’s feet and said this: ‘Venerable sir, let the Blessed One teach me the Dhamma! Let the Well-farer teach the Dhamma that will be for my welfare and happiness for a long time!’ When this was said, the Blessed One said to Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth: ‘It is not the right time, Bāhiya. We have entered the inner village for alms.’”

Dutiyampi kho bāhiyo dārucīriyo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “dujjānaṃ kho panetaṃ, bhante, bhagavato vā jīvitantarāyānaṃ, mayhaṃ vā jīvitantarāyānaṃ[P 0.0008]. Desetu me, bhante bhagavā, dhammaṃ; desetu, sugato, dhammaṃ, yaṃ mamassa dīgharattaṃ hitāya sukhāyā”ti. Dutiyampi kho bhagavā bāhiyaṃ dārucīriyaṃ etadavoca – “akālo kho tāva, bāhiya, antaragharaṃ paviṭṭhamhā piṇḍāyā”ti.

For a second time, Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth said this to the Blessed One: ‘Venerable sir, it is hard to know the dangers to the life of the Blessed One or the dangers to my own life. Venerable sir, let the Blessed One teach me the Dhamma! Let the Well-farer teach the Dhamma that will be for my welfare and happiness for a long time!’ For a second time, the Blessed One said this to Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth: ‘It is not the right time, Bāhiya. We have entered the inner village for alms.’”

Tatiyampi kho bāhiyo dārucīriyo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “dujjānaṃ kho panetaṃ, bhante, bhagavato vā jīvitantarāyānaṃ, mayhaṃ vā jīvitantarāyānaṃ. Desetu me bhante bhagavā, dhammaṃ; desetu, sugato, dhammaṃ, yaṃ mamassa dīgharattaṃ hitāya sukhāyā”ti.

For a third time, Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth said this to the Blessed One: ‘Venerable sir, it is hard to know the dangers to the life of the Blessed One or the dangers to my own life. Venerable sir, let the Blessed One teach me the Dhamma! Let the Sublime One teach the Dhamma that will be for my welfare and happiness for a long time!’

“Tasmātiha te, bāhiya, evaṃ sikkhitabbaṃ – ‘diṭṭhe diṭṭhamattaṃ bhavissati, sute sutamattaṃ bhavissati, mute mutamattaṃ bhavissati, viññāte viññātamattaṃ bhavissatī’ti. Evañhi te, bāhiya, sikkhitabbaṃ. Yato kho te, bāhiya, diṭṭhe diṭṭhamattaṃ bhavissati, sute sutamattaṃ bhavissati, mute [T 0.0084] mutamattaṃ bhavissati, viññāte viññātamattaṃ bhavissati, tato tvaṃ, bāhiya, na tena; yato tvaṃ, bāhiya, na tena tato tvaṃ, bāhiya, na tattha[M 0.0086]; yato tvaṃ, bāhiya, na tattha, tato tvaṃ, bāhiya, nevidha na huraṃ na ubhayamantarena. Esevanto dukkhassā”ti.

“Therefore, Bāhiya, you should train yourself thus: ‘In the seen, there will be only the seen; in the heard, only the heard; in the sensed, only the sensed; in the cognized, only the cognized.’ This is how you should train yourself, Bāhiya. When, Bāhiya, for you in the seen there is only the seen, in the heard only the heard, in the sensed only the sensed, in the cognized only the cognized, then, Bāhiya, you will not be with that. When, Bāhiya, you are not with that, then, Bāhiya, you will not be in that. When, Bāhiya, you are not in that, then, Bāhiya, you will be neither here nor beyond nor in between the two. This itself is the end of suffering.”

Atha kho bāhiyassa dārucīriyassa bhagavato imāya saṃkhittāya dhammadesanāya tāvadeva anupādāya āsavehi cittaṃ vimucci.

Then, through this brief Dhamma teaching of the Blessed One, the mind of Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth was immediately liberated from the taints, without clinging.

Atha [V 0.0078] kho bhagavā bāhiyaṃ dārucīriyaṃ iminā saṃkhittena ovādena ovaditvā pakkāmi. Atha kho acirapakkantassa bhagavato bāhiyaṃ dārucīriyaṃ gāvī taruṇavacchā adhipatitvā adhipātetvā (sī. syā. pī.), adhipātitvā (ka.) jīvitā voropesi.

Then the Blessed One, having instructed Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth with this brief instruction, departed. Then, not long after the Blessed One had departed, a cow with a young calf charged Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth and deprived him of life.

Atha kho bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ piṇḍāya caritvā pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkanto sambahulehi bhikkhūhi saddhiṃ nagaramhā nikkhamitvā addasa bāhiyaṃ dārucīriyaṃ kālaṅkataṃ kālakataṃ (sī. syā. kaṃ.); disvāna bhikkhū āmantesi – “gaṇhatha, bhikkhave, bāhiyassa dārucīriyassa sarīrakaṃ; mañcakaṃ āropetvā nīharitvā jhāpetha; thūpañcassa karotha. Sabrahmacārī vo, bhikkhave, kālaṅkato”ti.

Then the Blessed One, having walked for alms in Sāvatthī and returning from the alms-round after the meal, departed from the city together with many monks and saw Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth, who had passed away. Having seen him, he addressed the monks: “Monks, take the body of Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth; place it on a litter, carry it out, and cremate it. And make a stūpa for him. Monks, your companion in the holy life has passed away.”

“Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho te bhikkhū bhagavato paṭissutvā bāhiyassa dārucīriyassa sarīrakaṃ mañcakaṃ āropetvā nīharitvā jhāpetvā thūpañcassa katvā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu. Ekamantaṃ nisinnā kho te bhikkhū bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ – “daḍḍhaṃ, bhante, bāhiyassa dārucīriyassa sarīraṃ, thūpo cassa [T 0.0085] kato. Tassa kā gati, ko abhisamparāyo”ti? “Paṇḍito, bhikkhave, bāhiyo dārucīriyo paccapādi dhammassānudhammaṃ; na ca maṃ dhammādhikaraṇaṃ [P 0.0009] vihesesi. Parinibbuto, bhikkhave, bāhiyo dārucīriyo”ti.

“Even so, venerable sir,” those monks replied to the Blessed One. Having placed the body of Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth on a litter, carried it out, cremated it, and made a stūpa for him, they approached the Blessed One. Having approached and paid homage to the Blessed One, they sat down to one side. Seated to one side, those monks said this to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, the body of Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth has been cremated, and a stūpa has been made for him. What is his destination? What is his future state?” “Monks, Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth was wise. He practiced in accordance with the Dhamma, and he did not trouble me on account of the Dhamma. Monks, Bāhiya of the Bark-Cloth has attained final Nibbāna.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, understanding this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Yattha āpo ca pathavī, tejo vāyo na gādhati;

Na tattha sukkā jotanti, ādicco nappakāsati;

Na tattha candimā bhāti, tamo tattha na vijjati.

“Where water, earth, fire, and air have no footing; there the stars do not shine, the sun does not appear; there the moon does not gleam, and darkness is not found.

“Yadā [M 0.0087] ca attanāvedi vedhī (ka.), muni monena brāhmaṇo;

Atha rūpā arūpā ca, sukhadukkhā pamuccatī”ti. dasamaṃ;

“And when the sage, the brāhmaṇa, knows for himself through sagehood, then he is liberated from form and the formless, from pleasure and pain.”

(Ayampi udāno vutto bhagavatā iti me sutanti.) ( ) syāmapotthake natthi

This inspired utterance was also spoken by the Blessed One—so I have heard.

Bodhivaggo paṭhamo niṭṭhito.

The First Chapter on Awakening is concluded.

Tassuddānaṃ [V 0.0079] –

The summary thereof:

Tayo bodhi ca huṃhuṅko tayo ca bodhi nigrodho (sabbattha), brāhmaṇo te therā (sī. syā. pī.), thero (ka.) kassapena ca;

Aja pāvāya (sī. syā.), pāṭaliyaṃ (pī.), pāvā (ka.) saṅgāma jaṭilā, bāhiyenāti te dasāti.

Three on Awakening, Huṃhuṅka, the Brāhmaṇa, and with Kassapa; Aja, Saṅgāma, Jaṭila, and with Bāhiya—these are the ten.

2. Mucalindavaggo

2. The Mucalinda Chapter

1. Mucalindasuttaṃ

1. The Discourse on Mucalinda

11. Evaṃ [P 0.0010] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā uruvelāyaṃ viharati najjā nerañjarāya tīre mucalindamūle paṭhamābhisambuddho. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā sattāhaṃ ekapallaṅkena nisinno hoti vimuttisukhapaṭisaṃvedī.

11. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Uruvelā on the bank of the Nerañjarā River at the foot of the Mucalinda tree, having just attained full enlightenment. At that time, the Blessed One was seated for seven days in one cross-legged posture, experiencing the bliss of liberation.

Tena kho pana samayena mahā akālamegho udapādi [T 0.0086] sattāhavaddalikā sītavātaduddinī. Atha kho mucalindo nāgarājā sakabhavanā nikkhamitvā bhagavato kāyaṃ sattakkhattuṃ bhogehi parikkhipitvā uparimuddhani mahantaṃ phaṇaṃ vihacca aṭṭhāsi – “mā bhagavantaṃ sītaṃ, mā bhagavantaṃ uṇhaṃ, mā bhagavantaṃ ḍaṃsamakasavātātapasarīsapa siriṃsapa (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.) samphasso”ti.

Then at that time a great, untimely cloud arose, bringing seven days of rain, a cold wind, and gloomy weather. Then Mucalinda, the nāga king, having emerged from his own abode, encircled the Blessed One's body seven times with his coils. Spreading his great hood over the Blessed One's head, he stood there, thinking: 'May the Blessed One not be afflicted by cold. May the Blessed One not be afflicted by heat. May the Blessed One not be afflicted by the touch of gadflies, mosquitoes, wind, sun, or creeping things.'

Atha kho bhagavā tassa sattāhassa accayena tamhā samādhimhā vuṭṭhāsi. Atha kho mucalindo nāgarājā viddhaṃ vigatavalāhakaṃ devaṃ viditvā bhagavato kāyā bhoge viniveṭhetvā sakavaṇṇaṃ paṭisaṃharitvā māṇavakavaṇṇaṃ abhinimminitvā bhagavato purato aṭṭhāsi pañjaliko bhagavantaṃ namassamāno.

Then, after the passing of those seven days, the Blessed One arose from that concentration. Then Mucalinda, the nāga king, knowing the sky to be clear and free of clouds, unwound his coils from the Blessed One's body. Withdrawing his own form and creating the form of a young man, he stood before the Blessed One with his hands raised in reverence, paying homage to the Blessed One.

Atha [M 0.0088] kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, understanding the significance of this, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Sukho viveko tuṭṭhassa, sutadhammassa passato;

Abyāpajjaṃ sukhaṃ loke, pāṇabhūtesu saṃyamo.

“Blissful is seclusion for one who is content, who has heard the Dhamma and who sees. Blissful in the world is non-affliction; restraint toward living beings is bliss.

“Sukhā [V 0.0080] virāgatā loke, kāmānaṃ samatikkamo;

Asmimānassa yo vinayo, etaṃ ve paramaṃ sukha”nti. paṭhamaṃ;

“Blissful is dispassion in the world, the overcoming of sensual pleasures. The subduing of the conceit 'I am'—this, truly, is the supreme bliss.”

2. Rājasuttaṃ

2. The Discourse on the King

12. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena sambahulānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkantānaṃ [P 0.0011] upaṭṭhānasālāyaṃ sannisinnānaṃ sannipatitānaṃ ayamantarākathā udapādi – “ko nu kho, āvuso, imesaṃ dvinnaṃ rājūnaṃ mahaddhanataro vā mahābhogataro vā mahākosataro vā mahāvijitataro vā mahāvāhanataro vā [T 0.0087] mahabbalataro vā mahiddhikataro vā mahānubhāvataro vā rājā vā māgadho seniyo bimbisāro, rājā vā pasenadi kosalo”ti? Ayañcarahi tesaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ antarākathā hoti vippakatā.

12. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling in Sāvatthī, at Jeta's Grove, in Anāthapiṇḍika's Park. At that time, a number of monks, after the meal, having returned from their alms-round, were sitting together assembled in the assembly hall when this conversation arose among them: 'Friends, between these two kings, who is of greater wealth, of greater possessions, of greater treasury, of greater territory, of greater vehicles, of greater forces, of greater power, or of greater majesty—King Seniya Bimbisāra of Magadha, or King Pasenadi of Kosala?' And this conversation of the monks was left unfinished.

Atha kho bhagavā sāyanhasamayaṃ paṭisallānā vuṭṭhito yenupaṭṭhānasālā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā paññatte āsane nisīdi. Nisajja kho bhagavā bhikkhū āmantesi – “kāya nuttha, bhikkhave, etarahi kathāya sannisinnā sannipatitā, kā ca pana vo antarākathā vippakatā”ti?

Then the Blessed One, in the evening, having arisen from seclusion, went to the assembly hall. Having gone there, he sat down on the seat that had been prepared. Once seated, the Blessed One addressed the monks: 'Monks, for what topic of conversation are you now sitting together, having assembled? And what was this conversation of yours that was left unfinished?'

“Idha, bhante, amhākaṃ pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkantānaṃ upaṭṭhānasālāyaṃ sannisinnānaṃ sannipatitānaṃ ayamantarākathā udapādi – ‘ko nu kho, āvuso, imesaṃ dvinnaṃ rājūnaṃ mahaddhanataro vā mahābhogataro vā mahākosataro vā mahāvijitataro vā mahāvāhanataro vā mahabbalataro vā mahiddhikataro vā mahānubhāvataro vā rājā vā māgadho seniyo bimbisāro, rājā vā pasenadi kosalo’ti? Ayaṃ kho no, bhante, antarākathā vippakatā, atha bhagavā anuppatto”ti.

Venerable sir, here, after the meal, having returned from our alms-round, as we were sitting together assembled in the assembly hall, this conversation arose among us: 'Friends, between these two kings, who is of greater wealth, of greater possessions, of greater treasury, of greater territory, of greater vehicles, of greater forces, of greater power, or of greater majesty—King Seniya Bimbisāra of Magadha, or King Pasenadi of Kosala?' This, venerable sir, was our conversation that was left unfinished when the Blessed One arrived.

“Na [M 0.0089] khvetaṃ, bhikkhave, tumhākaṃ patirūpaṃ kulaputtānaṃ saddhā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajitānaṃ yaṃ tumhe evarūpiṃ kathaṃ katheyyātha. Sannipatitānaṃ vo, bhikkhave, dvayaṃ karaṇīyaṃ – dhammī vā kathā ariyo vā tuṇhībhāvo”ti.

Monks, it is not fitting for you, sons of good family who have gone forth from the household life into homelessness out of faith, that you should engage in such a conversation. Monks, when you have assembled, there are two things to be done: either talk on the Dhamma or maintain noble silence.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, understanding the significance of this, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Yañca [V 0.0081] kāmasukhaṃ loke, yañcidaṃ diviyaṃ sukhaṃ;

Taṇhakkhayasukhassete[T 0.0088], kalaṃ nāgghanti soḷasi”nti. dutiyaṃ;

Whatever sensual pleasure there is in the world, and whatever pleasure is divine—these are not worth a sixteenth part of the bliss that is the destruction of craving.

3. Daṇḍasuttaṃ

3. The Sutta on the Stick

13. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena sambahulā kumārakā antarā ca sāvatthiṃ antarā ca jetavanaṃ ahiṃ daṇḍena hananti. Atha kho bhagavā pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya sāvatthiṃ piṇḍāya pāvisi. Addasā kho bhagavā sambahule kumārake antarā ca sāvatthiṃ antarā ca jetavanaṃ ahiṃ daṇḍena hanante[P 0.0012].

13. Thus have I heard: At one time the Blessed One was dwelling in Sāvatthī, in Jeta's Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika's Park. Now at that time, many young boys between Sāvatthī and Jeta's Grove were striking a snake with a stick. Then the Blessed One, having dressed in the morning, took his bowl and robe and entered Sāvatthī for alms. The Blessed One saw the many young boys between Sāvatthī and Jeta's Grove striking a snake with a stick.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, having understood this matter, on that occasion the Blessed One uttered this inspired utterance:

“Sukhakāmāni bhūtāni, yo daṇḍena vihiṃsati;

Attano sukhamesāno, pecca so na labhate sukhaṃ.

Whoever, while seeking happiness for oneself, harms with a stick beings who desire happiness, will not find happiness after death.

“Sukhakāmāni bhūtāni, yo daṇḍena na hiṃsati;

Attano sukhamesāno, pecca so labhate sukha”nti. tatiyaṃ;

Whoever, while seeking happiness for oneself, does not harm with a stick beings who desire happiness, will find happiness after death.

4. Sakkārasuttaṃ

4. The Sutta on Honor

14. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā sakkato hoti garukato mānito pūjito apacito, lābhī cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ. Bhikkhusaṅghopi sakkato hoti [M 0.0090] garukato mānito pūjito apacito, lābhī cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ. Aññatitthiyā pana paribbājakā asakkatā honti agarukatā amānitā na apacitā (syā. pī.) apūjitā [T 0.0089] anapacitā, na lābhino cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ. Atha kho te aññatitthiyā paribbājakā bhagavato sakkāraṃ asahamānā bhikkhusaṅghassa ca gāme ca araññe ca bhikkhū disvā asabbhāhi pharusāhi vācāhi akkosanti paribhāsanti rosenti vihesenti.

14. Thus have I heard: At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta's Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika's monastery. At that time the Blessed One was honored, respected, revered, venerated, and esteemed, and was a recipient of robes, almsfood, lodgings, and medicinal requisites for the sick. The community of monks was also honored, respected, revered, venerated, and esteemed, and was a recipient of robes, almsfood, lodgings, and medicinal requisites for the sick. But the wandering ascetics of other sects were not honored, respected, revered, venerated, or esteemed, and were not recipients of robes, almsfood, lodgings, or medicinal requisites for the sick. Then, unable to bear the honor shown to the Blessed One and the community of monks, those wandering ascetics of other sects, upon seeing monks in villages and forests, would abuse, insult, provoke, and harass them with unsuitable and harsh words.

Atha [V 0.0082] kho sambahulā bhikkhū yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu. Ekamantaṃ nisinnā kho te bhikkhū bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ – “etarahi, bhante, bhagavā sakkato garukato mānito pūjito apacito, lābhī cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ. Bhikkhusaṅghopi sakkato garukato mānito pūjito apacito, lābhī cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ. Aññatitthiyā pana paribbājakā asakkatā agarukatā amānitā apūjitā anapacitā, na lābhino cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ. Atha kho te, bhante, aññatitthiyā paribbājakā bhagavato sakkāraṃ asahamānā bhikkhusaṅghassa ca gāme ca araññe ca bhikkhū disvā asabbhāhi pharusāhi vācāhi akkosanti paribhāsanti rosenti vihesantī”ti.

Then many monks approached the Blessed One. Having approached, they paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, those monks said to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, the Blessed One is now honored, respected, revered, venerated, and esteemed, and is a recipient of robes, almsfood, lodgings, and medicinal requisites for the sick. The community of monks is also honored, respected, revered, venerated, and esteemed, and is a recipient of robes, almsfood, lodgings, and medicinal requisites for the sick. But the wandering ascetics of other sects are not honored, respected, revered, venerated, or esteemed, nor are they recipients of robes, almsfood, lodgings, or medicinal requisites for the sick. Now, venerable sir, those wandering ascetics of other sects, unable to endure the honor accorded to the Blessed One and the community of monks, upon seeing monks in the village or in the forest, abuse, insult, provoke, and harass them with unsuitable and harsh words.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion, uttered this inspired utterance:

“Gāme araññe sukhadukkhaphuṭṭho,

Nevattato no parato dahetha;

Phusanti [T 0.0090] phassā upadhiṃ paṭicca,

Nirūpadhiṃ kena phuseyyu phassā”ti. catutthaṃ;

“In village or forest, touched by pleasure and pain, one should not establish it as from oneself, nor from another. Contacts touch one dependent on the substrate; how could contacts touch one who is without a substrate?”

5. Upāsakasuttaṃ

5. The Discourse on the Lay Follower

15. Evaṃ [P 0.0013] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena aññataro icchānaṅgalako [M 0.0091] upāsako sāvatthiṃ anuppatto hoti kenacideva karaṇīyena. Atha kho so upāsako sāvatthiyaṃ taṃ karaṇīyaṃ tīretvā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho taṃ upāsakaṃ bhagavā etadavoca – “cirassaṃ kho tvaṃ, upāsaka, imaṃ pariyāyamakāsi yadidaṃ idhāgamanāyā”ti.

15. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling in Sāvatthī, at the Jeta Grove, the monastery of Anāthapiṇḍika. Now at that time, a certain lay follower from Icchānaṅgala had arrived in Sāvatthī for some business. Then that lay follower, having completed that business in Sāvatthī, approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. To that lay follower seated to one side, the Blessed One said this: 'It has been a long time, lay follower, since you have made this occasion to come here.'

“Cirapaṭikāhaṃ, bhante, bhagavantaṃ dassanāya upasaṅkamitukāmo, api cāhaṃ kehici kehici kiccakaraṇīyehi byāvaṭo. Evāhaṃ nāsakkhiṃ bhagavantaṃ dassanāya upasaṅkamitu”nti.

'Venerable sir, for a long time I have wished to approach the Blessed One to see him, but I have been occupied with various duties. Thus, I was not able to approach the Blessed One to see him.'

Atha [V 0.0083] kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Sukhaṃ vata tassa na hoti kiñci,

Saṅkhātadhammassa bahussutassa;

Sakiñcanaṃ passa vihaññamānaṃ,

Jano janasmiṃ paṭibandharūpo”ti. pañcamaṃ;

'Indeed, happy is the learned one who has penetrated the Dhamma, for whom there is no impediment. Behold the one with impediments, who is afflicted; a person is of a nature to be bound to another person.'

6. Gabbhinīsuttaṃ

6. The Discourse on the Pregnant Woman

16. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena aññatarassa paribbājakassa daharamāṇavikā pajāpati hoti gabbhinī [T 0.0091] upavijaññā. Atha kho sā paribbājikā taṃ paribbājakaṃ etadavoca – “gaccha tvaṃ, brāhmaṇa, telaṃ āhara, yaṃ me vijātāya bhavissatī”ti.

16. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling in Sāvatthī, at the Jeta Grove, the monastery of Anāthapiṇḍika. Now at that time, a certain wanderer had a young maiden as his wife, who was pregnant and near to giving birth. Then that female wanderer said to that wanderer: 'Go, Brahmin, bring oil, which will be for me when I have given birth.'

Evaṃ vutte, so paribbājako taṃ paribbājikaṃ etadavoca – “kuto panāhaṃ, bhoti bhotiyā (syā. pī. ka.), telaṃ āharāmī”ti? Dutiyampi kho sā paribbājikā taṃ paribbājakaṃ etadavoca – “gaccha tvaṃ, brāhmaṇa, telaṃ āhara, yaṃ me vijātāya bhavissatī”ti. Dutiyampi kho so paribbājiko taṃ paribbājikaṃ etadavoca – “kuto panāhaṃ, bhoti, telaṃ āharāmī”ti? Tatiyampi kho sā paribbājikā taṃ paribbājakaṃ etadavoca – “gaccha tvaṃ, brāhmaṇa, telaṃ āhara, yaṃ me vijātāya bhavissatī”ti.

When this was said, that wanderer said to that female wanderer: 'From where, madam, shall I bring oil?' A second time, that female wanderer said to that wanderer: 'Go, Brahmin, bring oil, which will be for me when I have given birth.' A second time, that wanderer said to that female wanderer: 'From where, madam, shall I bring oil?' A third time, that female wanderer said to that wanderer: 'Go, Brahmin, bring oil, which will be for me when I have given birth.'

Tena [M 0.0092] kho pana samayena rañño [P 0.0014] pasenadissa kosalassa koṭṭhāgāre samaṇassa vā brāhmaṇassa vā sappissa vā telassa vā yāvadatthaṃ pātuṃ dīyati diyyati (sī. ka.), no nīharituṃ.

At that time, in the storehouse of King Pasenadi of Kosala, as much ghee or oil as was wished was given to an ascetic or a brahmin to drink, but not to take away.

Atha kho tassa paribbājakassa etadahosi – “rañño kho pana pasenadissa kosalassa koṭṭhāgāre samaṇassa vā brāhmaṇassa vā sappissa vā telassa vā yāvadatthaṃ pātuṃ dīyati, no nīharituṃ. Yaṃnūnāhaṃ rañño pasenadissa kosalassa koṭṭhāgāraṃ gantvā telassa yāvadatthaṃ pivitvā gharaṃ āgantvā ucchadditvāna uggiritvāna (sī. syā. pī.), ucchaditvā (sī. syā. aṭṭha.), ucchaḍḍitvāna (ka.) dadeyyaṃ, yaṃ imissā vijātāya bhavissatī”ti.

Then this thought occurred to that wanderer: 'In the storehouse of King Pasenadi of Kosala, as much ghee or oil as is wished is given to an ascetic or a brahmin to drink, but not to take away. What if I were to go to the storehouse of King Pasenadi of Kosala, drink as much oil as I wish, return home, vomit it out, and give her that oil which will be for her when she has given birth?'

Atha [V 0.0084] kho so paribbājako rañño pasenadissa kosalassa koṭṭhāgāraṃ gantvā telassa yāvadatthaṃ pivitvā gharaṃ āgantvā neva [T 0.0092] sakkoti uddhaṃ kātuṃ, na pana adho. So dukkhāhi tibbāhi tippāhi (syā.) kharāhi kaṭukāhi vedanāhi phuṭṭho āvaṭṭati parivaṭṭati.

Then that wanderer went to the storehouse of King Pasenadi of Kosala, drank as much oil as he wished, and having returned home, was able neither to bring it up nor to pass it down. He, afflicted by painful, sharp, harsh, and bitter feelings, rolled and tossed about.

Atha kho bhagavā pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya sāvatthiṃ piṇḍāya pāvisi. Addasā kho bhagavā taṃ paribbājakaṃ dukkhāhi tibbāhi kharāhi kaṭukāhi vedanāhi phuṭṭhaṃ āvaṭṭamānaṃ parivaṭṭamānaṃ.

Then the Blessed One, having dressed in the morning time and taken his bowl and robe, entered Sāvatthī for alms-food. The Blessed One saw that wanderer afflicted by painful, sharp, harsh, and bitter feelings, rolling and tossing about.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Sukhino vata ye akiñcanā,

Vedaguno hi janā akiñcanā;

Sakiñcanaṃ passa vihaññamānaṃ,

Jano janasmiṃ paṭibandhacitto” paṭibaddhacitto (syā.), paṭibandharupo (?) ti. chaṭṭhaṃ;

'Happy indeed are those without impediments. For truly, those who have reached the goal are without impediments. Behold the one with impediments, who is afflicted—a person whose mind is bound to another person.'

7. Ekaputtakasuttaṃ

7. The Discourse on the Only Son

17. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena aññatarassa upāsakassa ekaputtako piyo manāpo kālaṅkato hoti.

17. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling in Sāvatthī, in Jeta's Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika's Park. At that time a certain lay follower's only son, beloved and dear, had passed away.

Atha [M 0.0093] kho sambahulā upāsakā allavatthā allakesā divā divassa yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu. Ekamantaṃ nisinne kho te upāsake bhagavā etadavoca – “kiṃ nu kho tumhe, upāsakā, allavatthā allakesā idhūpasaṅkamantā divā divassā”ti?

Then many lay followers, with wet clothes and wet hair, approached the Blessed One in broad daylight. Having approached, they paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. To those lay followers seated to one side, the Blessed One said this: 'Why, lay followers, have you come here in broad daylight with wet clothes and wet hair?'

Evaṃ vutte, so upāsako bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “mayhaṃ kho, bhante, ekaputtako piyo manāpo [T 0.0093] kālaṅkato. Tena mayaṃ allavatthā allakesā idhūpasaṅkamantā divā divassā”ti.

When this was said, that lay follower said to the Blessed One: 'Venerable sir, my only son, beloved and dear, has passed away. That is why we, with wet clothes and wet hair, have approached here in broad daylight.'

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Piyarūpassādagadhitāse [V 0.0085][P 0.0015] piyarūpassātagadhitāse (sī. pī.),

Devakāyā puthu manussā ca;

Aghāvino parijunnā,

Maccurājassa vasaṃ gacchanti.

'Those greedy for the taste of pleasing forms—the hosts of devas and many humans, too—being sorrowful and worn out, they come under the sway of the King of Death.

“Ye ve divā ca ratto ca,

Appamattā jahanti piyarūpaṃ;

Te ve khaṇanti aghamūlaṃ,

Maccuno āmisaṃ durativatta”nti. sattamaṃ;

'But those who, by day and by night, being heedful, abandon what is pleasing—they indeed dig up the root of suffering: Death’s bait, so hard to overcome.'

8. Suppavāsāsuttaṃ

8. The Suppavāsā Discourse

18. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā kuṇḍikāyaṃ kuṇḍiyāyaṃ (sī. syā. pī.) viharati kuṇḍadhānavane kuṇḍiṭṭhānavane (syā. pī.). Tena kho pana samayena suppavāsā koliyadhītā satta vassāni gabbhaṃ dhāreti. Sattāhaṃ mūḷhagabbhā sā dukkhāhi tibbāhi kharāhi kaṭukāhi vedanāhi phuṭṭhā tīhi vitakkehi adhivāseti – “sammāsambuddho vata so bhagavā yo imassa evarūpassa dukkhassa pahānāya dhammaṃ deseti; suppaṭipanno vata tassa bhagavato sāvakasaṅgho yo imassa evarūpassa dukkhassa pahānāya paṭipanno; susukhaṃ vata taṃ nibbānaṃ yatthidaṃ evarūpaṃ dukkhaṃ na saṃvijjatī”ti.

18. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Kuṇḍikā in the Kuṇḍadhāna Grove. Now at that time Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, had been carrying a fetus for seven years. For seven days she was suffering from an obstructed labor, afflicted by painful, sharp, severe, and bitter feelings. She endured them with three thoughts: 'Truly, the Blessed One is the Perfectly Enlightened One, who teaches the Dhamma for the abandoning of such suffering; truly, well-practiced is the Blessed One's Saṅgha of disciples, who practice for the abandoning of such suffering; truly, blissful is Nibbāna, where such suffering does not exist.'

Atha [M 0.0094][T 0.0094] kho suppavāsā koliyadhītā sāmikaṃ āmantesi – “ehi tvaṃ, ayyaputta, yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkama; upasaṅkamitvā mama vacanena bhagavato pāde sirasā vandāhi; appābādhaṃ appātaṅkaṃ lahuṭṭhānaṃ balaṃ phāsuvihāraṃ puccha – ‘suppavāsā, bhante, koliyadhītā bhagavato pāde sirasā vandati; appābādhaṃ appātaṅkaṃ lahuṭṭhānaṃ balaṃ phāsuvihāraṃ pucchatī’ti. Evañca vadehi – ‘suppavāsā, bhante, koliyadhītā satta vassāni gabbhaṃ dhāreti. Sattāhaṃ mūḷhagabbhā sā dukkhāhi tibbāhi kharāhi kaṭukāhi vedanāhi phuṭṭhā tīhi vitakkehi adhivāseti – sammāsambuddho vata so bhagavā yo imassa evarūpassa dukkhassa pahānāya dhammaṃ deseti; suppaṭipanno vata tassa bhagavato sāvakasaṅgho yo imassa evarūpassa dukkhassa pahānāya paṭipanno; susukhaṃ vata taṃ nibbānaṃ yatthidaṃ evarūpaṃ dukkhaṃ na saṃvijjatī’”ti.

Then Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, addressed her husband: 'Come, noble husband, go to where the Blessed One is. Having gone there, with my words, pay homage with your head at the Blessed One's feet, and ask about his freedom from illness and affliction, his lightness of body, strength, and comfortable abiding, saying: “Venerable sir, Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, pays homage with her head at the Blessed One's feet, and asks about your freedom from illness and affliction, your lightness of body, strength, and comfortable abiding.” And say this: “Venerable sir, Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, has been carrying a fetus for seven years. For seven days she has been suffering from an obstructed labor, afflicted by painful, sharp, severe, and bitter feelings. She endures them with three thoughts: ‘Truly, the Blessed One is the Perfectly Enlightened One, who teaches the Dhamma for the abandoning of such suffering; truly, well-practiced is the Blessed One's Saṅgha of disciples, who practice for the abandoning of such suffering; truly, blissful is Nibbāna, where such suffering does not exist.’”'

“Parama”nti kho so koliyaputto suppavāsāya koliyadhītāya paṭissutvā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami[V 0.0086]; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho koliyaputto bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “suppavāsā, bhante, koliyadhītā bhagavato pāde sirasā vandati, appābādhaṃ appātaṅkaṃ lahuṭṭhānaṃ balaṃ phāsuvihāraṃ pucchati; evañca vadeti – ‘suppavāsā, bhante, koliyadhītā satta vassāni gabbhaṃ dhāreti. Sattāhaṃ mūḷhagabbhā [P 0.0016] sā dukkhāhi tibbāhi kharāhi kaṭukāhi vedanāhi phuṭṭhā tīhi vitakkehi adhivāseti – sammāsambuddho vata so bhagavā yo [T 0.0095] imassa evarūpassa dukkhassa pahānāya dhammaṃ deseti; suppaṭipanno vata tassa bhagavato sāvakasaṅgho yo imassa evarūpassa dukkhassa pahānāya paṭipanno; susukhaṃ vata nibbānaṃ yatthidaṃ evarūpaṃ dukkhaṃ na saṃvijjatī’”ti.

'Excellent!' said the Koliyan son, and having assented to Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, he approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, the Koliyan son said this to the Blessed One: 'Venerable sir, Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, pays homage with her head at the Blessed One's feet and asks about your freedom from illness and affliction, your lightness of body, strength, and comfortable abiding. And she says this: “Venerable sir, Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, has been carrying a fetus for seven years. For seven days, suffering from an obstructed labor, she has been afflicted by painful, sharp, severe, and bitter feelings. She endures them with three thoughts: ‘Truly, the Blessed One is the Perfectly Enlightened One, who teaches the Dhamma for the abandoning of such suffering; truly, well-practiced is the Blessed One's Saṅgha of disciples, who practice for the abandoning of such suffering; truly, blissful is Nibbāna, where such suffering does not exist.’”'

“Sukhinī hotu suppavāsā koliyadhītā; arogā arogaṃ puttaṃ vijāyatū”ti. Saha vacanā ca pana bhagavato suppavāsā koliyadhītā sukhinī arogā arogaṃ puttaṃ vijāyi.

'May Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, be happy; may she, being free from illness, give birth to a healthy son.' And with the utterance of the Blessed One's words, Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, became happy and, being free from illness, gave birth to a healthy son.

“Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho so koliyaputto bhagavato bhāsitaṃ abhinanditvā anumoditvā uṭṭhāyāsanā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā yena sakaṃ gharaṃ tena paccāyāsi. Addasā kho so koliyaputto suppavāsaṃ koliyadhītaraṃ sukhiniṃ arogaṃ arogaṃ [M 0.0095] puttaṃ vijātaṃ. Disvānassa etadahosi – “acchariyaṃ vata, bho, abbhutaṃ vata, bho, tathāgatassa mahiddhikatā mahānubhāvatā, yatra hi nāmāyaṃ suppavāsā koliyadhītā saha vacanā ca pana saha vacanā pana (pī.), saha vacanā (?) bhagavato sukhinī arogā arogaṃ puttaṃ vijāyissatī”ti! Attamano pamudito pītisomanassajāto ahosi.

'Very well, venerable sir,' said the Koliyan son. Having rejoiced in and approved the Blessed One’s words, he rose from his seat, paid homage to the Blessed One, circumambulated him, and went to his own home. He saw Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, happy, free from illness, and delivered of a healthy son. Seeing this, it occurred to him: 'Wonderful indeed! Amazing, indeed! The great power and might of the Tathāgata, that Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, at the very word of the Blessed One, would become happy, free from illness, and give birth to a healthy son!' He was uplifted, joyful, and filled with rapture and happiness.

Atha kho suppavāsā koliyadhītā sāmikaṃ āmantesi – “ehi tvaṃ, ayyaputta, yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkama; upasaṅkamitvā mama vacanena bhagavato pāde sirasā vandāhi – ‘suppavāsā, bhante, koliyadhītā bhagavato pāde sirasā vandatī’ti; evañca vadehi – ‘suppavāsā, bhante, koliyadhītā satta vassāni gabbhaṃ dhāreti. Sattāhaṃ mūḷhagabbhā sā etarahi sukhinī arogā arogaṃ puttaṃ vijātā. Sā sattāhaṃ buddhappamukhaṃ [T 0.0096] bhikkhusaṅghaṃ bhattena nimanteti. Adhivāsetu kira, bhante, bhagavā suppavāsāya koliyadhītāya satta bhattāni saddhiṃ bhikkhusaṅghenā’”ti.

Then Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, addressed her husband: 'Come, noble husband, go to the Blessed One. Having approached him, by my word, pay homage with your head at the Blessed One’s feet, saying: “Venerable sir, Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, pays homage with her head at the Blessed One’s feet.” And also say this: “Venerable sir, Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, bore a child in her womb for seven years. For seven days she had an obstructed pregnancy. Now she is well and healthy, and has given birth to a healthy son. She invites the Saṅgha of monks, with the Buddha at its head, for a meal for seven days. Venerable sir, may the Blessed One please consent to the seven meals from Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, together with the Saṅgha of monks.”'

“Parama”nti kho so koliyaputto suppavāsāya koliyadhītāya paṭissutvā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho so koliyaputto bhagavantaṃ etadavoca –

'Excellent!' the Koliyan son assented to Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter. He then approached the Blessed One, paid homage to him, and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, that Koliyan son said this to the Blessed One:

“Suppavāsā[V 0.0087], bhante, koliyadhītā bhagavato pāde sirasā vandati; evañca vadeti – ‘suppavāsā, bhante, koliyadhītā satta vassāni gabbhaṃ dhāreti. Sattāhaṃ mūḷhagabbhā sā etarahi sukhinī arogā arogaṃ puttaṃ vijātā. Sā sattāhaṃ buddhappamukhaṃ bhikkhusaṅghaṃ bhattena nimanteti. Adhivāsetu kira, bhante, bhagavā suppavāsāya koliyadhītāya satta bhattāni saddhiṃ bhikkhusaṅghenā’”ti.

'Venerable sir, Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, pays homage with her head at the Blessed One’s feet, and she says this: “Venerable sir, Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, bore a child in her womb for seven years. For seven days she had an obstructed pregnancy. Now she is well and healthy, and has given birth to a healthy son. She invites the Saṅgha of monks, with the Buddha at its head, for a meal for seven days. Venerable sir, may the Blessed One please consent to the seven meals from Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, together with the Saṅgha of monks.”'

Tena kho pana samayena aññatarena upāsakena buddhappamukho bhikkhusaṅgho svātanāya bhattena nimantito hoti. So ca upāsako āyasmato mahāmoggallānassa mahāmoggalānassa (ka.) upaṭṭhāko hoti. Atha kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ mahāmoggallānaṃ āmantesi – “ehi tvaṃ, moggallāna, yena so upāsako tenupasaṅkama[P 0.0017]; upasaṅkamitvā taṃ upāsakaṃ evaṃ vadehi – ‘suppavāsā, āvuso, koliyadhītā satta [M 0.0096] vassāni gabbhaṃ dhāresi. Sattāhaṃ mūḷhagabbhā sā etarahi sukhinī arogā arogaṃ puttaṃ vijātā. Sā sattāhaṃ buddhappamukhaṃ bhikkhusaṅghaṃ bhattena [T 0.0097] nimanteti. Karotu suppavāsā koliyadhītā satta bhattāni, pacchā tvaṃ karissasī’ti karissasīti saññāpehi (ka.). Tuyheso upaṭṭhāko”ti.

Now at that time, a certain lay follower had invited the Saṅgha of monks headed by the Buddha for a meal on the following day. And that lay follower was an attendant of the Venerable Mahāmoggallāna. Then the Blessed One addressed the Venerable Mahāmoggallāna: “Come, Moggallāna, go to that lay follower. Having approached him, say this to that lay follower: ‘Friend, Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, carried a pregnancy for seven years. For seven days she had an obstructed labor. Now she is well and healthy, and has given birth to a healthy son. She invites the Saṅgha of monks, with the Buddha at its head, for a meal for seven days. Let Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, provide the seven meals; afterwards, you shall provide yours. This lay follower is your attendant.’”

“Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho āyasmā mahāmoggallāno bhagavato paṭissutvā yena so upāsako tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā taṃ upāsakaṃ etadavoca – “suppavāsā, āvuso, koliyadhītā satta vassāni gabbhaṃ dhāreti. Sattāhaṃ mūḷhagabbhā sā etarahi sukhinī arogā arogaṃ puttaṃ vijātā. Sā sattāhaṃ buddhappamukhaṃ bhikkhusaṅghaṃ bhattena nimanteti. Karotu suppavāsā koliyadhītā satta bhattāni, pacchā tvaṃ karissasī”ti.

“So be it, venerable sir,” the Venerable Mahāmoggallāna assented to the Blessed One. He then approached that lay follower and, having approached, said this to him: “Friend, Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, carried a pregnancy for seven years. For seven days she had an obstructed labor. Now she is well and healthy, and has given birth to a healthy son. She invites the Saṅgha of monks, with the Buddha at its head, for a meal for seven days. Let Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, provide the seven meals; afterwards, you shall provide yours.”

“Sace me, bhante, ayyo mahāmoggallāno tiṇṇaṃ dhammānaṃ pāṭibhogo – bhogānañca jīvitassa ca saddhāya ca, karotu suppavāsā koliyadhītā satta bhattāni, pacchāhaṃ karissāmī”ti. “Dvinnaṃ kho te ahaṃ dvinnaṃ kho tesaṃ (pī.), dvinnaṃ kho nesaṃ (ka.), āvuso, dhammānaṃ pāṭibhogo – bhogānañca jīvitassa ca. Saddhāya pana tvaṃyeva pāṭibhogo”ti.

“Venerable sir, if the noble Mahāmoggallāna is a guarantor for me in three matters—for my wealth, my life, and my faith—then let Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, provide the seven meals; afterwards, I will provide mine.” “Friend, I am a guarantor for you in two of those matters—for your wealth and for your life. But as for faith, you yourself are the guarantor.”

“Sace me, bhante, ayyo mahāmoggallāno dvinnaṃ dhammānaṃ pāṭibhogo – bhogānañca jīvitassa ca, karotu suppavāsā koliyadhītā satta bhattāni, pacchāhaṃ karissāmī”ti.

“Venerable sir, if the noble Mahāmoggallāna is a guarantor for me in two matters—for my wealth and for my life—then let Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, provide the seven meals; afterwards, I will provide mine.”

Atha kho āyasmā mahāmoggallāno taṃ upāsakaṃ saññāpetvā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā [V 0.0088] bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “saññatto saññāto (syā.), bhante, so upāsako mayā; karotu suppavāsā koliyadhītā satta bhattāni, pacchā so karissatī”ti.

Then the Venerable Mahāmoggallāna, having persuaded that lay follower, approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he said this to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, that lay follower has been persuaded by me. Let Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, provide the seven meals; afterwards, he will provide his.”

Atha kho suppavāsā koliyadhītā sattāhaṃ buddhappamukhaṃ bhikkhusaṅghaṃ paṇītena khādanīyena bhojanīyena sahatthā santappesi [T 0.0098] sampavāresi, tañca dārakaṃ bhagavantaṃ vandāpesi sabbañca bhikkhusaṅghaṃ.

Then, for seven days, Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, with her own hands, served and satisfied the Saṅgha of monks headed by the Buddha with excellent hard and soft foods. And she had that child pay homage to the Blessed One and to the entire Saṅgha of monks.

Atha [M 0.0097] kho āyasmā sāriputto taṃ dārakaṃ etadavoca – “kacci te, dāraka, khamanīyaṃ, kacci yāpanīyaṃ, kacci na kiñci dukkha”nti? “Kuto me, bhante sāriputta, khamanīyaṃ, kuto yāpanīyaṃ! Satta me vassāni lohitakumbhiyaṃ vuttānī”ti.

Then the Venerable Sāriputta said this to that child: “Child, are you keeping well? Are you getting by? Is it that you have no discomfort?” “Venerable Sāriputta, how could I be well? How could I be getting by? For seven years I have dwelt in a vessel of blood.”

Atha kho suppavāsā koliyadhītā – “putto me dhammasenāpatinā saddhiṃ mantetī”ti attamanā pamuditā pītisomanassajātā ahosi. Atha kho bhagavā (suppavāsaṃ kolīyadhītaraṃ attamanaṃ pamuditaṃ pītisomanassajātaṃ viditvā disvā (sī.)) ( ) natthi iṅgalisapotthake suppavāsaṃ koliyadhītaraṃ etadavoca – “iccheyyāsi tvaṃ, suppavāse, aññampi evarūpaṃ putta”nti? “Iccheyyāmahaṃ, bhagavā, aññānipi evarūpāni satta puttānī”ti[P 0.0018].

Then Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, thinking, “My son is conversing with the General of the Dhamma,” became glad, joyful, and filled with rapture and happiness. Then the Blessed One, knowing that Suppavāsā, the Koliyan daughter, was glad, joyful, and filled with rapture and happiness, said this to her: “Suppavāsā, would you wish for another son like this one?” “Blessed One, I would wish for seven more sons like this one.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, understanding the meaning of this, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Asātaṃ sātarūpena, piyarūpena appiyaṃ;

Dukkhaṃ sukhassa rūpena, pamattamativattatī”ti. aṭṭhamaṃ;

“The disagreeable in the guise of the agreeable, the unloved in the guise of the loved, suffering in the guise of happiness, overcomes the one who is heedless.”

9. Visākhāsuttaṃ

9. The Discourse concerning Visākhā

19. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati pubbārāme migāramātupāsāde. Tena kho pana samayena visākhāya migāramātuyā kocideva attho raññe pasenadimhi kosale paṭibaddho paṭibandho (pī. ka.) hoti. Taṃ rājā pasenadi kosalo na yathādhippāyaṃ tīreti[T 0.0099].

19. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in the Eastern Park, in the Mansion of Migāra’s Mother. Now at that time, Visākhā, Migāra’s mother, had a certain matter connected with King Pasenadi of Kosala, but King Pasenadi of Kosala did not settle it according to her wish.

Atha kho visākhā migāramātā divā divassa divādivasseva (syā.), divādivasseyeva (pī.), divā divassayeva (ka.) yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho visākhaṃ migāramātaraṃ bhagavā [V 0.0089] etadavoca – “handa kuto nu tvaṃ, visākhe, āgacchasi divā divassā”ti? “Idha me, bhante, kocideva attho raññe pasenadimhi kosale paṭibaddho; taṃ rājā pasenadi kosalo na yathādhippāyaṃ tīretī”ti.

Then Visākhā, Migāra’s mother, approached the Blessed One in the middle of the day. Having approached, she paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. The Blessed One then said to Visākhā, Migāra’s mother, as she sat to one side: “Well now, Visākhā, from where are you coming in the middle of the day?” “Here, venerable sir, I have a certain matter connected with King Pasenadi of Kosala, but King Pasenadi of Kosala does not settle it according to my wish.”

Atha [M 0.0098] kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, understanding this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Sabbaṃ paravasaṃ dukkhaṃ, sabbaṃ issariyaṃ sukhaṃ;

Sādhāraṇe vihaññanti, yogā hi duratikkamā”ti. navamaṃ;

“All dependence on others is suffering; all mastery is happiness. Through what is held in common, beings are afflicted, for the yokes are indeed hard to overcome.”

10. Bhaddiyasuttaṃ

10. The Discourse concerning Bhaddiya

20. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā anupiyāyaṃ viharati ambavane. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā bhaddiyo kāḷīgodhāya putto araññagatopi rukkhamūlagatopi suññāgāragatopi abhikkhaṇaṃ udānaṃ udānesi – “aho sukhaṃ, aho sukha”nti!

20. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Anupiya in the mango grove. Now at that time the Venerable Bhaddiya, the son of Kāḷīgodhā, whether gone to the forest, to the foot of a tree, or to an empty dwelling, would repeatedly utter the inspired utterance: “Oh, what happiness! Oh, what happiness!”

Assosuṃ kho sambahulā bhikkhū āyasmato bhaddiyassa kāḷīgodhāya puttassa araññagatassapi rukkhamūlagatassapi suññāgāragatassapi abhikkhaṇaṃ udānaṃ udānentassa – “aho sukhaṃ, aho sukha”nti! Sutvāna nesaṃ etadahosi – “nissaṃsayaṃ kho, āvuso, āyasmā bhaddiyo kāḷīgodhāya putto anabhirato brahmacariyaṃ carati, yaṃsa pubbe agāriyabhūtassa agārikabhūtassa (syā.) rajjasukhaṃ, so tamanussaramāno araññagatopi [P 0.0019] rukkhamūlagatopi suññāgāragatopi [T 0.0100] abhikkhaṇaṃ udānaṃ udānesi – ‘aho sukhaṃ, aho sukha’”nti!

A number of monks heard the Venerable Bhaddiya, son of Kāḷīgodhā, repeatedly uttering the inspired utterance, “Oh, what happiness! Oh, what happiness!” whether he had gone to the forest, to the foot of a tree, or to an empty dwelling. Having heard this, the thought occurred to them: “Without a doubt, friends, the Venerable Bhaddiya, son of Kāḷīgodhā, practices the holy life without delight. Remembering the royal happiness he had formerly as a householder, he, whether gone to the forest, to the foot of a tree, or to an empty dwelling, repeatedly utters the inspired utterance: ‘Oh, what happiness! Oh, what happiness!’”

Atha kho sambahulā bhikkhū yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu. Ekamantaṃ nisinnā kho te bhikkhū bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ – “āyasmā, bhante, bhaddiyo kāḷīgodhāya putto araññagatopi rukkhamūlagatopi suññāgāragatopi abhikkhaṇaṃ udānaṃ udānesi – ‘aho sukhaṃ, aho sukha’nti! Nissaṃsayaṃ kho, bhante, āyasmā bhaddiyo kāḷīgodhāya putto anabhirato brahmacariyaṃ carati. Yaṃsa pubbe agāriyabhūtassa rajjasukhaṃ, so tamanussaramāno araññagatopi rukkhamūlagatopi suññāgāragatopi abhikkhaṇaṃ udānaṃ udānesi – ‘aho sukhaṃ, aho sukha’”nti!

Then many monks approached the Blessed One. Having approached, they paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Sitting to one side, those monks said this to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, the Venerable Bhaddiya, son of Kāḷīgodhā, when he has gone to the forest, to the foot of a tree, or to an empty dwelling, repeatedly utters the inspired utterance: ‘Oh, what happiness! Oh, what happiness!’ Without a doubt, venerable sir, the Venerable Bhaddiya, son of Kāḷīgodhā, practices the holy life without delight. Remembering the royal happiness he had formerly as a householder, he, whether gone to the forest, to the foot of a tree, or to an empty dwelling, repeatedly utters the inspired utterance: ‘Oh, what happiness! Oh, what happiness!’”

Atha [M 0.0099][V 0.0090] kho bhagavā aññataraṃ bhikkhuṃ āmantesi – “ehi tvaṃ, bhikkhu, mama vacanena bhaddiyaṃ bhikkhuṃ āmantehi – ‘satthā taṃ, āvuso bhaddiya, āmantetī’”ti.

Then the Blessed One addressed a certain monk: “Come, monk, in my name summon the monk Bhaddiya, saying, ‘The Teacher calls you, friend Bhaddiya.’”

“Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho so bhikkhu bhagavato paṭissutvā yenāyasmā bhaddiyo kāḷīgodhāya putto tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhaddiyaṃ kāḷīgodhāya puttaṃ etadavoca – “satthā taṃ, āvuso bhaddiya, āmantetī”ti. “Evamāvuso”ti kho āyasmā bhaddiyo kāḷīgodhāya putto tassa bhikkhuno paṭissutvā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho āyasmantaṃ bhaddiyaṃ kāḷīgodhāya puttaṃ bhagavā etadavoca –

“Very well, venerable sir,” that monk replied to the Blessed One. He then approached the Venerable Bhaddiya, son of Kāḷīgodhā, and having approached, said to him: “Friend Bhaddiya, the Teacher calls you.” “Very well, friend,” the Venerable Bhaddiya, son of Kāḷīgodhā, replied to that monk. He then approached the Blessed One, and having approached and paid homage to the Blessed One, sat down to one side. The Blessed One then said this to the Venerable Bhaddiya, son of Kāḷīgodhā, as he was sitting to one side:

“Saccaṃ kira tvaṃ, bhaddiya, araññagatopi rukkhamūlagatopi suññāgāragatopi abhikkhaṇaṃ udānaṃ udānesi – ‘aho sukhaṃ, aho sukha’”nti! “Evaṃ, bhante”ti.

“Is it true, Bhaddiya, that when you have gone to the forest, to the foot of a tree, or to an empty dwelling, you repeatedly utter the inspired utterance: ‘Oh, what happiness! Oh, what happiness!’?” “Yes, venerable sir.”

“Kiṃ pana kaṃ pana (syā pī.) tvaṃ, bhaddiya, atthavasaṃ sampassamāno araññagatopi rukkhamūlagatopi suññāgāragatopi [T 0.0101] abhikkhaṇaṃ udānaṃ udānesi – ‘aho sukhaṃ, aho sukha’”nti! “Pubbe me, bhante, agāriyabhūtassa rajjaṃ kārentassa antopi antepure rakkhā susaṃvihitā ahosi, bahipi antepure rakkhā susaṃvihitā ahosi, antopi nagare rakkhā susaṃvihitā ahosi, bahipi nagare rakkhā susaṃvihitā ahosi, antopi janapade rakkhā susaṃvihitā ahosi, bahipi janapade rakkhā susaṃvihitā ahosi. So kho ahaṃ, bhante, evaṃ rakkhito gopito santo bhīto ubbiggo ussaṅkī utrāsī vihāsiṃ.
