Etarahi kho panāhaṃ, bhante, araññagatopi rukkhamūlagatopi suññāgāragatopi eko ekako (syā. pī.) abhīto anubbiggo anussaṅkī anutrāsī appossukko pannalomo paradattavutto paradavutto (ka. sī. syā. pī.), migabhūtena cetasā viharāmi. Imaṃ idaṃ (sī. ka.) kho ahaṃ, bhante, atthavasaṃ sampassamāno araññagatopi [P 0.0020] rukkhamūlagatopi suññāgāragatopi abhikkhaṇaṃ udānaṃ udānesi udānemi (ka.) – ‘aho sukhaṃ, aho sukha’”nti!

“But, Bhaddiya, seeing what benefit do you, when gone to the forest, to the foot of a tree, or to an empty dwelling, repeatedly utter the inspired utterance: ‘Oh, what happiness! Oh, what happiness!’?” “Formerly, venerable sir, when I was a householder ruling the kingdom, guards were well-established for me inside the palace and outside the palace; inside the city and outside the city; within the country and outside the country. Though I was thus guarded and protected, venerable sir, I lived in fear, agitated, apprehensive, and terrified. But now, venerable sir, when gone to the forest, to the foot of a tree, or to an empty dwelling, I dwell alone—fearless, unagitated, unapprehensive, unterrified, untroubled, with hair at rest, living on what is given by others, with a mind that has become like a wild deer’s. Seeing this benefit, venerable sir, when I have gone to the forest, to the foot of a tree, or to an empty dwelling, I repeatedly utter the inspired utterance: ‘Oh, what happiness! Oh, what happiness!’”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Yassantarato [M 0.0100] na santi kopā,

Itibhavābhavatañca vītivatto;

Taṃ [V 0.0091] vigatabhayaṃ sukhiṃ asokaṃ,

Devā nānubhavanti dassanāyā”ti. dasamaṃ;

“For whom there are no angers internally, and who has overcome this state of becoming and non-becoming—that one, fearless, happy, and sorrowless, the gods are not able to perceive.” Tenth.

Mucalindavaggo dutiyo niṭṭhito.

The Mucalinda Chapter, the second, is concluded.

Tassuddānaṃ –

The summary of this chapter:

Mucalindo [T 0.0102] rājā daṇḍena, sakkāro upāsakena ca;

Gabbhinī ekaputto ca, suppavāsā visākhā ca;

Kāḷīgodhāya bhaddiyoti.

Mucalinda, Rājā, Daṇḍa, Sakkāra, and Upāsaka; Gabbhinī and Ekaputta, Suppavāsā and Visākhā; and Bhaddiya.

3. Nandavaggo

3. The Nanda Chapter

1. Kammavipākajasuttaṃ

1. The Discourse on What is Born from the Ripening of Kamma

21. Evaṃ [P 0.0021] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena aññataro bhikkhu bhagavato avidūre nisinno hoti pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya purāṇakammavipākajaṃ dukkhaṃ tibbaṃ kharaṃ kaṭukaṃ vedanaṃ adhivāsento sato sampajāno avihaññamāno.

21. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta's Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika's Park. At that time a certain bhikkhu was seated not far from the Blessed One, his legs crossed, his body held erect, enduring painful, sharp, harsh, bitter feelings born of the ripening of past kamma, mindful and clearly comprehending, untroubled.

Addasā kho bhagavā taṃ bhikkhuṃ avidūre nisinnaṃ pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya purāṇakammavipākajaṃ dukkhaṃ tibbaṃ kharaṃ kaṭukaṃ vedanaṃ adhivāsentaṃ sataṃ sampajānaṃ avihaññamānaṃ.

The Blessed One saw that bhikkhu seated not far away, his legs crossed, his body held erect, enduring painful, sharp, harsh, bitter feelings born of the ripening of past kamma, mindful and clearly comprehending, untroubled.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood the meaning of this, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Sabbakammajahassa bhikkhuno,

Dhunamānassa pure kataṃ rajaṃ;

Amamassa [V 0.0092] ṭhitassa tādino,

Attho natthi janaṃ lapetave”ti. paṭhamaṃ;

“For the bhikkhu who has abandoned all kamma, shaking off the dust of what was done before; for one without selfishness, steadfast, Such, there is no need to speak to people.” First.

2. Nandasuttaṃ

2. The Discourse about Nanda

22. Evaṃ [M 0.0101][T 0.0103] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā nando bhagavato bhātā mātucchāputto sambahulānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ evamāroceti – “anabhirato ahaṃ, āvuso, brahmacariyaṃ carāmi; na sakkomi brahmacariyaṃ sandhāretuṃ, sikkhaṃ paccakkhāya hīnāyāvattissāmī”ti.

22. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta's Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika's Park. At that time the Venerable Nanda, the Blessed One's brother, the son of his maternal aunt, announced to a number of bhikkhus: “Friends, I am dissatisfied with leading the holy life. I cannot continue the holy life. Having renounced the training, I will return to the lower life.”

Atha kho aññataro bhikkhu yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho so bhikkhu bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “āyasmā, bhante, nando bhagavato bhātā mātucchāputto sambahulānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ evamāroceti – ‘anabhirato ahaṃ, āvuso, brahmacariyaṃ carāmi, na sakkomi brahmacariyaṃ sandhāretuṃ, sikkhaṃ paccakkhāya [P 0.0022] hīnāyāvattissāmī’”ti.

Then a certain bhikkhu went to the Blessed One, and after paying homage to him, sat down at one side. Seated thus, that bhikkhu said to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, the Venerable Nanda, the Blessed One's brother, the son of his maternal aunt, has announced to a number of bhikkhus: ‘Friends, I am dissatisfied with leading the holy life. I cannot continue the holy life. Having renounced the training, I will return to the lower life.’”

Atha kho bhagavā aññataraṃ bhikkhuṃ āmantesi – “ehi tvaṃ, bhikkhu, mama vacanena nandaṃ bhikkhuṃ āmantehi – ‘satthā taṃ, āvuso nanda, āmantetī’”ti. “Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho so bhikkhu bhagavato paṭissutvā yenāyasmā nando tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā āyasmantaṃ nandaṃ etadavoca – “satthā taṃ, āvuso nanda, āmantetī”ti.

Then the Blessed One addressed a certain bhikkhu: “Come, bhikkhu, address the bhikkhu Nanda in my name, saying: ‘Friend Nanda, the Teacher calls you.’” “Yes, venerable sir,” that bhikkhu replied to the Blessed One. He then went to the Venerable Nanda and said to him: “Friend Nanda, the Teacher calls you.”

“Evamāvuso”ti kho āyasmā nando tassa bhikkhuno paṭissutvā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho āyasmantaṃ nandaṃ bhagavā etadavoca –

“So be it, friend,” the Venerable Nanda replied to that bhikkhu. He then went to the Blessed One, and after paying homage to him, sat down at one side. The Blessed One then said to the Venerable Nanda as he was sitting there:

“Saccaṃ kira tvaṃ, nanda, sambahulānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ evamārocesi – ‘anabhirato ahaṃ, āvuso, brahmacariyaṃ carāmi, na sakkomi brahmacariyaṃ sandhāretuṃ, sikkhaṃ paccakkhāya hīnāyāvattissāmī’”ti[T 0.0104]? “Evaṃ, bhante”ti.

“Is it true, Nanda, that you have announced to a number of bhikkhus: ‘Friends, I am dissatisfied with leading the holy life. I cannot continue the holy life. Having renounced the training, I will return to the lower life’?” “So it is, venerable sir.”

“Kissa pana tvaṃ, nanda, anabhirato brahmacariyaṃ carasi, na sakkosi brahmacariyaṃ sandhāretuṃ[V 0.0093], sikkhaṃ paccakkhāya hīnāyāvattissasī”ti? “Sākiyānī maṃ mama (syā., aṭṭhakathā oloketabbā), bhante, janapadakalyāṇī gharā nikkhamantassa nikkhamantaṃ (aṭṭhakathāyaṃ pāṭhantaraṃ) upaḍḍhullikhitehi kesehi apaloketvā maṃ etadavoca – ‘tuvaṭaṃ kho, ayyaputta, āgaccheyyāsī’ti. So [M 0.0102] kho ahaṃ, bhante, tamanussaramāno anabhirato brahmacariyaṃ carāmi, na sakkomi brahmacariyaṃ sandhāretuṃ, sikkhaṃ paccakkhāya hīnāyāvattissāmī”ti.

“But why, Nanda, are you dissatisfied with leading the holy life? Why can you not continue the holy life? Why will you renounce the training and return to the lower life?” “Venerable sir, as I was leaving home, the Sakyan belle, the most beautiful girl in the land, glanced at me with her hair half-combed and said to me: ‘Return quickly, my lord.’ Remembering that, venerable sir, I am dissatisfied with leading the holy life. I cannot continue the holy life. Having renounced the training, I will return to the lower life.”

Atha kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ nandaṃ bāhāyaṃ gahetvā – seyyathāpi nāma balavā puriso samiñjitaṃ sammiñjitaṃ (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.) vā bāhaṃ pasāreyya, pasāritaṃ vā bāhaṃ samiñjeyya sammiñjeyya (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.), evameva – jetavane antarahito devesu tāvatiṃsesu pāturahosi.

Then the Blessed One, taking the Venerable Nanda by the arm, just as a strong man might extend his bent arm or bend back his extended arm, vanished from Jeta’s Grove and reappeared among the Tāvatiṃsa devas.

Tena kho pana samayena pañcamattāni accharāsatāni sakkassa devānamindassa upaṭṭhānaṃ āgatāni honti kakuṭapādāni. Atha kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ nandaṃ āmantesi – “passasi no tvaṃ, nanda, imāni pañca accharāsatāni kakuṭapādānī”ti? “Evaṃ, bhante”ti.

Now at that time five hundred dove-footed nymphs had come to wait upon Sakka, lord of the devas. Then the Blessed One addressed the Venerable Nanda: “Nanda, do you see these five hundred dove-footed nymphs?” “Yes, venerable sir.”

“Taṃ kiṃ maññasi, nanda, katamā nu kho abhirūpatarā vā dassanīyatarā vā pāsādikatarā vā, sākiyānī vā janapadakalyāṇī, imāni vā pañca accharāsatāni kakuṭapādānī”ti? “Seyyathāpi, bhante, paluṭṭhamakkaṭī kaṇṇanāsacchinnā, evameva kho, bhante, sākiyānī janapadakalyāṇī [T 0.0105] imesaṃ pañcannaṃ accharāsatānaṃ [P 0.0023] upanidhāya saṅkhyampi saṅkhampi (sī.) nopeti kalabhāgampi nopeti upanidhimpi nopeti. Atha kho imāni pañca accharāsatāni abhirūpatarāni ceva dassanīyatarāni ca pāsādikatarāni cā”ti.

“What do you think, Nanda? Who is more beautiful, more lovely, and more charming: the Sakyan belle, the most beautiful girl in the land, or these five hundred dove-footed nymphs?” “Venerable sir, compared to these five hundred dove-footed nymphs, the Sakyan belle, the most beautiful girl in the land, is like a singed female monkey with its ears and nose cut off. She does not even enter the count, nor amount to a fraction, nor bear comparison. Indeed, these five hundred dove-footed nymphs are far more beautiful, far more lovely, and far more charming.”

“Abhirama, nanda, abhirama, nanda! Ahaṃ te pāṭibhogo pañcannaṃ accharāsatānaṃ paṭilābhāya kakuṭapādāna”nti. “Sace me, bhante, bhagavā pāṭibhogo pañcannaṃ accharāsatānaṃ paṭilābhāya kakuṭapādānaṃ, abhiramissāmahaṃ, bhante, bhagavati brahmacariye”ti bhagavā brahmacariyeti (syā. pī.), bhagavā brahmacariyanti (ka.).

“Delight, Nanda! Delight, Nanda! I am your guarantor for the acquisition of five hundred dove-footed nymphs.” “Venerable sir, if the Blessed One is my guarantor for the acquisition of five hundred dove-footed nymphs, I will delight in the holy life under the Blessed One.”

Atha kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ nandaṃ bāhāyaṃ gahetvā – seyyathāpi nāma balavā puriso samiñjitaṃ vā bāhaṃ pasāreyya, pasāritaṃ vā bāhaṃ samiñjeyya, evameva – devesu tāvatiṃsesu antarahito jetavane pāturahosi.

Then the Blessed One, taking the Venerable Nanda by the arm, just as a strong man might extend his bent arm or bend back his extended arm, vanished from the Tāvatiṃsa deva realm and reappeared in Jeta’s Grove.

Assosuṃ [M 0.0103] kho bhikkhū – “āyasmā kira nando bhagavato bhātā mātucchāputto accharānaṃ [V 0.0094] hetu brahmacariyaṃ carati; bhagavā kirassa pāṭibhogo pañcannaṃ accharāsatānaṃ paṭilābhāya kakuṭapādāna”nti.

The bhikkhus heard: “It is said that the Venerable Nanda, the Blessed One’s brother, the son of his maternal aunt, is leading the holy life for the sake of nymphs. The Blessed One, it is said, is his guarantor for the acquisition of five hundred dove-footed nymphs.”

Atha kho āyasmato nandassa sahāyakā bhikkhū āyasmantaṃ nandaṃ bhatakavādena ca upakkitakavādena ca samudācaranti – “bhatako kirāyasmā nando upakkitako kirāyasmā nando accharānaṃ hetu brahmacariyaṃ carati; bhagavā kirassa pāṭibhogo pañcannaṃ accharāsatānaṃ paṭilābhāya kakuṭapādāna”nti.

Then the Venerable Nanda’s companion bhikkhus taunted him with the term 'hireling' and with the term 'trader', saying: “The Venerable Nanda, it is said, is a hireling! The Venerable Nanda, it is said, is a trader! He is leading the holy life for the sake of nymphs. The Blessed One, it is said, is his guarantor for the acquisition of five hundred dove-footed nymphs.”

Atha kho āyasmā nando [T 0.0106] sahāyakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ bhatakavādena ca upakkitakavādena ca aṭṭīyamāno harāyamāno jigucchamāno eko vūpakaṭṭho appamatto ātāpī pahitatto viharanto nacirasseva – yassatthāya kulaputtā sammadeva agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajanti tadanuttaraṃ – brahmacariyapariyosānaṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja vihāsi. “Khīṇā jāti, vusitaṃ brahmacariyaṃ, kataṃ karaṇīyaṃ, nāparaṃ itthattāyā”ti abbhaññāsi. Aññataro kho panāyasmā nando arahataṃ ahosi.

Then the Venerable Nanda, being distressed, humiliated, and disgusted by his companion bhikkhus addressing him with the term 'hireling' and with the term 'trader,' dwelling alone, secluded, diligent, ardent, and resolute, in no long time entered and dwelled in that unsurpassed culmination of the holy life for which clansmen rightly go forth from the household life into homelessness, having realized it for himself with direct knowledge in this very life. He knew: “Birth is destroyed, the holy life has been lived, what had to be done has been done, there is no more for this state of being.” And the Venerable Nanda became one of the arahants.

Atha kho aññatarā devatā abhikkantāya rattiyā abhikkantavaṇṇā kevalakappaṃ jetavanaṃ obhāsetvā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhāsi. Ekamantaṃ ṭhitā kho sā devatā bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “āyasmā, bhante, nando bhagavato bhātā mātucchāputto āsavānaṃ khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā [P 0.0024] sacchikatvā upasampajja viharatī”ti. Bhagavatopi kho ñāṇaṃ udapādi – “nando āsavānaṃ khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharatī”ti.

Then a certain deity, when the night was far advanced, with surpassing radiance illuminating the entire Jeta’s Grove, approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and stood to one side. Standing to one side, that deity said to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, the Venerable Nanda, the Blessed One’s brother, the son of his maternal aunt, by the destruction of the taints, has realized for himself with direct knowledge, in this very life, the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom, and dwells having entered upon it.” And knowledge arose in the Blessed One: “Nanda, by the destruction of the taints, has realized for himself with direct knowledge, in this very life, the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom, and dwells having entered upon it.”

Atha kho āyasmā nando tassā rattiyā accayena yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho āyasmā nando [T 0.0107] bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “yaṃ me, bhante, bhagavā pāṭibhogo pañcannaṃ accharāsatānaṃ paṭilābhāya kakuṭapādānaṃ, muñcāmahaṃ, bhante, bhagavantaṃ etasmā paṭissavā”ti. “Mayāpi kho [M 0.0104] tvaṃ, nanda kho te nanda (sī. syā. pī.), kho nanda (ka.), cetasā ceto paricca vidito – ‘nando āsavānaṃ khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharatī’ti. Devatāpi me etamatthaṃ ārocesi – ‘āyasmā, bhante, nando bhagavato bhātā mātucchāputto āsavānaṃ khayā [V 0.0095] anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharatī’ti. Yadeva kho te, nanda, anupādāya āsavehi cittaṃ vimuttaṃ, athāhaṃ mutto etasmā paṭissavā”ti.

Then, when the night had passed, the Venerable Nanda approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Sitting to one side, the Venerable Nanda said to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, regarding the five hundred dove-footed nymphs for the acquisition of which the Blessed One was my guarantor, I release the Blessed One from that promise.” “I too, Nanda, have known by encompassing your mind with my own: ‘Nanda, by the destruction of the taints, has realized for himself with direct knowledge, in this very life, the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom, and dwells having entered upon it.’ A deity also informed me of this matter: ‘Venerable sir, the Venerable Nanda, the Blessed One’s brother, the son of his maternal aunt, by the destruction of the taints, has realized for himself with direct knowledge, in this very life, the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom, and dwells having entered upon it.’ Indeed, Nanda, when your mind was liberated from the taints through non-clinging, at that moment I was released from that promise.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, having understood this matter, on that occasion the Blessed One uttered this inspired utterance:

“Yassa nittiṇṇo paṅko,

Maddito kāmakaṇṭako;

Mohakkhayaṃ anuppatto,

Sukhadukkhesu na vedhatī sa bhikkhū”ti. dutiyaṃ;

“He for whom the mire has been crossed, the thorn of sensual desire crushed, who has attained the destruction of delusion—that bhikkhu does not tremble amid pleasure and pain.”

3. Yasojasuttaṃ

3. The Yasoja Sutta

23. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena yasojappamukhāni pañcamattāni bhikkhusatāni sāvatthiṃ anuppattāni honti bhagavantaṃ dassanāya. Tedha kho āgantukā bhikkhū nevāsikehi bhikkhūhi saddhiṃ paṭisammodamānā senāsanāni paññāpayamānā pattacīvarāni paṭisāmayamānā uccāsaddā mahāsaddā uccāsaddamahāsaddā (ka.) ahesuṃ[T 0.0108].

23. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. Now at that time, five hundred bhikkhus headed by Yasoja had arrived at Sāvatthī to see the Blessed One. Those visiting bhikkhus, while exchanging greetings with the resident bhikkhus, preparing their lodgings, and putting away their bowls and robes, were making a loud sound, a great sound.

Atha kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi – “ke panete, ānanda, uccāsaddā mahāsaddā kevaṭṭā maññe macchavilope”ti? “Etāni, bhante, yasojappamukhāni pañcamattāni bhikkhusatāni sāvatthiṃ anuppattāni bhagavantaṃ dassanāya. Tete āgantukā bhikkhū nevāsikehi bhikkhūhi saddhiṃ paṭisammodamānā senāsanāni paññāpayamānā pattacīvarāni paṭisāmayamānā uccāsaddā mahāsaddā”ti. “Tenahānanda, mama vacanena te bhikkhū āmantehi – ‘satthā āyasmante āmantetī’”ti.

Then the Blessed One addressed Venerable Ānanda: “Ānanda, who are these people making a loud sound, a great sound? One would think it was fishermen plundering a catch of fish.” “Venerable sir, these are five hundred bhikkhus headed by Yasoja who have arrived at Sāvatthī to see the Blessed One. Those visiting bhikkhus, while exchanging greetings with the resident bhikkhus, preparing their lodgings, and putting away their bowls and robes, are making a loud sound, a great sound.” “Then, Ānanda, address those bhikkhus in my name, saying: ‘The Teacher summons you, venerables.’”

“Evaṃ[M 0.0105], bhante”ti kho āyasmā ānando bhagavato paṭissutvā yena te bhikkhū tenupasaṅkami[P 0.0025]; upasaṅkamitvā te bhikkhū etadavoca – “satthā āyasmante āmantetī”ti. “Evamāvuso”ti kho te bhikkhū āyasmato ānandassa paṭissutvā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu[V 0.0096]; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu. Ekamantaṃ nisinne kho te bhikkhū bhagavā etadavoca –

“Yes, venerable sir,” Venerable Ānanda replied to the Blessed One. He went to those bhikkhus and said to them: “The Teacher summons you, venerables.” “Yes, friend,” those bhikkhus replied to Venerable Ānanda. Then they went to the Blessed One, paid homage to him, and sat down to one side. When they were seated to one side, the Blessed One said to them:

“Kiṃ nu tumhe, bhikkhave, uccāsaddā mahāsaddā, kevaṭṭā maññe macchavilope”ti? Evaṃ vutte, āyasmā yasojo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “imāni, bhante, pañcamattāni bhikkhusatāni sāvatthiṃ anuppattāni bhagavantaṃ dassanāya. Teme āgantukā bhikkhū nevāsikehi bhikkhūhi saddhiṃ paṭisammodamānā senāsanāni paññāpayamānā pattacīvarāni paṭisāmayamānā uccāsaddā mahāsaddā”ti. “Gacchatha, bhikkhave, paṇāmemi [T 0.0109] vo vo paṇāmemi (sabbattha) ma. ni. 2.157 passitabbaṃ; na vo mama santike vatthabba”nti.

“Why, bhikkhus, are you making a loud sound, a great sound? One would think it was fishermen plundering a catch of fish.” When this was said, Venerable Yasoja said to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, these five hundred bhikkhus have arrived at Sāvatthī to see the Blessed One. These visiting bhikkhus, while exchanging greetings with the resident bhikkhus, preparing their lodgings, and putting away their bowls and robes, are making a loud sound, a great sound.” “Go, bhikkhus! I dismiss you. You are not to live in my presence.”

“Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho te bhikkhū bhagavato paṭissutvā uṭṭhāyāsanā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā senāsanaṃ saṃsāmetvā paṭisaṃsāmetvā (syā.) pattacīvaramādāya yena vajjī tena cārikaṃ pakkamiṃsu. Vajjīsu anupubbena cārikaṃ caramānā yena vaggumudā nadī tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā vaggumudāya nadiyā tīre paṇṇakuṭiyo karitvā vassaṃ upagacchiṃsu.

“Yes, venerable sir,” those monks replied to the Blessed One. They rose from their seats, paid homage to the Blessed One, and circumambulated him, keeping him to their right. Then, having set their lodgings in order and taken their bowls and robes, they set out on a wandering tour toward the Vajjian country. Wandering in stages through the Vajjian country, they came to the Vaggumudā River. Having approached the bank of the Vaggumudā River, they built leaf huts and entered upon the rains retreat.

Atha kho āyasmā yasojo vassūpagato vassūpagate (ka.) bhikkhū āmantesi – “bhagavatā mayaṃ, āvuso, paṇāmitā atthakāmena hitesinā, anukampakena anukampaṃ upādāya. Handa mayaṃ, āvuso, tathā vihāraṃ kappema yathā no viharataṃ bhagavā attamano assā”ti. “Evamāvuso”ti kho te bhikkhū āyasmato yasojassa paccassosuṃ. Atha kho te bhikkhū vūpakaṭṭhā appamattā ātāpino pahitattā viharantā tenevantaravassena sabbeva tisso vijjā sacchākaṃsu.

Then, after the monks had entered upon the rains retreat, Venerable Yasojā addressed them: “Friends, we have been dismissed by the Blessed One, who desires our good and welfare, who is compassionate and acted out of compassion. Come, friends, let us dwell in such a way that the Blessed One will be pleased with our dwelling.” “Yes, friend,” those monks replied to Venerable Yasojā. Then those monks, dwelling secluded, heedful, ardent, and resolute, all realized the three true knowledges during that same rains retreat.

Atha kho bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ yathābhirantaṃ viharitvā yena vesālī tena cārikaṃ pakkāmi. Anupubbena cārikaṃ caramāno yena vesālī tadavasari. Tatra sudaṃ bhagavā vesāliyaṃ viharati mahāvane kūṭāgārasālāyaṃ.

Then the Blessed One, having dwelt at Sāvatthī for as long as he wished, set out on a wandering tour toward Vesālī. Wandering in stages, he eventually arrived at Vesālī. There the Blessed One dwelt at Vesālī in the Great Wood, in the Hall with the Peaked Roof.

Atha [M 0.0106] kho bhagavā vaggumudātīriyānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ cetasā ceto paricca manasi karitvā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi – “ālokajātā viya me, ānanda, esā disā, obhāsajātā viya me, ānanda, esā disā; yassaṃ disāyaṃ yāyaṃ (ka.) vaggumudātīriyā bhikkhū [T 0.0110] viharanti. Gantuṃ appaṭikūlāsi [P 0.0026] me manasi kātuṃ. Pahiṇeyyāsi tvaṃ, ānanda, vaggumudātīriyānaṃ [V 0.0097] bhikkhūnaṃ santike dūtaṃ – ‘satthā āyasmante āmanteti, satthā āyasmantānaṃ dassanakāmo’”ti.

Then the Blessed One, having encompassed with his own mind the minds of the monks on the bank of the Vaggumudā River, addressed Venerable Ānanda: “Ānanda, this direction has become bright for me, this direction has become radiant for me, the direction in which the monks on the bank of the Vaggumudā are dwelling. My mind is inclined to go there. Ānanda, send a messenger to the monks on the bank of the Vaggumudā, saying: ‘The Teacher summons you, venerables; the Teacher wishes to see you.’”

“Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho āyasmā ānando bhagavato paṭissutvā yena aññataro bhikkhu tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā taṃ bhikkhuṃ etadavoca – “ehi tvaṃ, āvuso, yena vaggumudātīriyā bhikkhū tenupasaṅkama; upasaṅkamitvā vaggumudātīriye bhikkhū evaṃ vadehi – ‘satthā āyasmante āmanteti, satthā āyasmantānaṃ dassanakāmo’”ti.

“Yes, venerable sir,” Venerable Ānanda replied to the Blessed One. He went to a certain monk and said to him: “Come, friend, go to the monks on the bank of the Vaggumudā River and say to them: ‘The Teacher summons you, venerables; the Teacher wishes to see you.’”

“Evamāvuso”ti kho so bhikkhu āyasmato ānandassa paṭissutvā – seyyathāpi nāma balavā puriso samiñjitaṃ vā bāhaṃ pasāreyya, pasāritaṃ vā bāhaṃ samiñjeyya, evameva – mahāvane kūṭāgārasālāyaṃ antarahito vaggumudāya nadiyā tīre tesaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ purato pāturahosi. Atha kho so bhikkhu vaggumudātīriye bhikkhū etadavoca – “satthā āyasmante āmanteti, satthā āyasmantānaṃ dassanakāmo”ti.

“Yes, friend,” that monk replied to Venerable Ānanda. Then, just as a strong man might extend his flexed arm or flex his extended arm, so he vanished from the Hall with the Peaked Roof in the Great Wood and reappeared on the bank of the Vaggumudā River in front of those monks. Then that monk said to the monks on the bank of the Vaggumudā River: “The Teacher summons you, venerables; the Teacher wishes to see you.”

“Evamāvuso”ti kho te bhikkhū tassa bhikkhuno paṭissutvā senāsanaṃ saṃsāmetvā pattacīvaramādāya – seyyathāpi nāma balavā puriso samiñjitaṃ vā bāhaṃ pasāreyya, pasāritaṃ vā bāhaṃ samiñjeyya, evameva – vaggumudāya nadiyā tīre antarahitā mahāvane kūṭāgārasālāyaṃ bhagavato sammukhe pāturahesuṃ. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā āneñjena samādhinā nisinno [T 0.0111] hoti. Atha kho tesaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ etadahosi – “katamena nu kho bhagavā vihārena etarahi viharatī”ti? Atha kho tesaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ etadahosi – “āneñjena kho bhagavā vihārena etarahi viharatī”ti. Sabbeva āneñjasamādhinā nisīdiṃsu.

“Yes, friend,” those monks replied to that monk. Having set their lodgings in order and taken their bowls and robes, just as a strong man might stretch out his bent arm or bend his outstretched arm, so they vanished from the bank of the Vaggumudā River and reappeared in the Great Wood, in the Hall with the Peaked Roof, in the presence of the Blessed One. Now on that occasion the Blessed One was sitting in imperturbable concentration. Then the thought occurred to those monks: “In which abiding is the Blessed One now dwelling?” Then the thought occurred to those monks: “The Blessed One is now dwelling in the imperturbable abiding.” And they all sat down in imperturbable concentration.

Atha [M 0.0107] kho āyasmā ānando abhikkantāya rattiyā, nikkhante paṭhame yāme, uṭṭhāyāsanā ekaṃsaṃ uttarāsaṅgaṃ cīvaraṃ (sabbattha) karitvā yena bhagavā tenañjaliṃ paṇāmetvā bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “abhikkantā, bhante, ratti; nikkhanto paṭhamo yāmo; ciranisinnā āgantukā bhikkhū; paṭisammodatu, bhante, bhagavā āgantukehi bhikkhūhī”ti. Evaṃ vutte, bhagavā tuṇhī ahosi.

Then, when the night was far advanced, after the first watch had passed, the Venerable Ānanda rose from his seat, arranged his upper robe over one shoulder, raised his joined palms in salutation to the Blessed One, and said to him: “Venerable sir, the night is far advanced; the first watch has passed. The visiting monks have been sitting for a long time. Let the Blessed One exchange friendly greetings with the visiting monks.” When this was said, the Blessed One remained silent.

Dutiyampi kho āyasmā ānando abhikkantāya rattiyā, nikkhante majjhime yāme, uṭṭhāyāsanā ekaṃsaṃ uttarāsaṅgaṃ karitvā yena bhagavā tenañjaliṃ paṇāmetvā [P 0.0027] bhagavantaṃ etadavoca [V 0.0098] – “abhikkantā, bhante, ratti; nikkhanto majjhimo yāmo; ciranisinnā āgantukā bhikkhū; paṭisammodatu, bhante, bhagavā āgantukehi bhikkhūhī”ti. Dutiyampi kho bhagavā tuṇhī ahosi.

For a second time, when the night was far advanced, after the middle watch had passed, the Venerable Ānanda rose from his seat, arranged his upper robe over one shoulder, raised his joined palms in salutation to the Blessed One, and said to him: “Venerable sir, the night is far advanced; the middle watch has passed. The visiting monks have been sitting for a long time. Let the Blessed One exchange friendly greetings with the visiting monks.” For a second time, the Blessed One remained silent.

Tatiyampi kho āyasmā ānando abhikkantāya rattiyā, nikkhante pacchime yāme, uddhaste aruṇe, nandimukhiyā rattiyā uṭṭhāyāsanā ekaṃsaṃ uttarāsaṅgaṃ karitvā yena bhagavā tenañjaliṃ paṇāmetvā bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “abhikkantā, bhante, ratti; nikkhanto pacchimo yāmo; uddhasto aruṇo; nandimukhī ratti; ciranisinnā āgantukā bhikkhū; paṭisammodatu, bhante, bhagavā, āgantukehi [T 0.0112] bhikkhūhī”ti.

For a third time, when the night was far advanced, after the last watch had passed, when dawn had arisen and the face of the night was pleasing, the Venerable Ānanda rose from his seat, arranged his upper robe over one shoulder, raised his joined palms in salutation to the Blessed One, and said to him: “Venerable sir, the night is far advanced; the last watch has passed; dawn has arisen; the face of the night is pleasing. The visiting monks have been sitting for a long time. Let the Blessed One exchange friendly greetings with the visiting monks.”

Atha kho bhagavā tamhā samādhimhā vuṭṭhahitvā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi – “sace kho tvaṃ, ānanda, jāneyyāsi ettakampi te nappaṭibhāseyya nappaṭibheyya (?). Ahañca, ānanda, imāni ca pañca bhikkhusatāni sabbeva āneñjasamādhinā nisīdimhā”ti.

Then the Blessed One, having emerged from that concentration, addressed the Venerable Ānanda: “Ānanda, if you knew, it would not even occur to you to say so much. I, Ānanda, and these five hundred monks have all been sitting in imperturbable concentration.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, understanding the meaning of this, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Yassa jito kāmakaṇṭako,

Akkoso ca vadho ca bandhanañca;

Pabbatova pabbato viya (sī. syā. pī.) so ṭhito anejo,

Sukhadukkhesu na vedhatī sa bhikkhū”ti. tatiyaṃ;

“He for whom the thorn of sensual desire, abuse, assault, and bondage are conquered; he stands firm like a mountain, imperturbable. That bhikkhu is not shaken by pleasure and pain.” Third.

4. Sāriputtasuttaṃ

4. The Sāriputta Sutta

24. Evaṃ [M 0.0108] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā sāriputto bhagavato avidūre nisinno hoti pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya parimukhaṃ satiṃ upaṭṭhapetvā. Addasā kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ sāriputtaṃ avidūre nisinnaṃ pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya parimukhaṃ satiṃ upaṭṭhapetvā.

24. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s monastery. Now at that time, the Venerable Sāriputta was sitting not far from the Blessed One, having folded his legs crosswise, set his body erect, and established mindfulness before him. The Blessed One saw the Venerable Sāriputta sitting not far away, having folded his legs crosswise, set his body erect, and established mindfulness before him.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Yathāpi [V 0.0099] pabbato selo, acalo suppatiṭṭhito;

Evaṃ mohakkhayā bhikkhu, pabbatova na vedhatī”ti. catutthaṃ;

“Just as a rocky mountain is unwavering and well-established, so a bhikkhu, with the destruction of delusion, like a mountain does not tremble.” Fourth.

5. Mahāmoggallānasuttaṃ

5. The Mahāmoggallāna Sutta

25. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena [P 0.0028] āyasmā [T 0.0113] mahāmoggallāno bhagavato avidūre nisinno hoti pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya kāyagatāya satiyā ajjhattaṃ sūpaṭṭhitāya. Addasā kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ mahāmoggallānaṃ avidūre nisinnaṃ pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya kāyagatāya satiyā ajjhattaṃ sūpaṭṭhitāya.

25. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s monastery. Now at that time, the Venerable Mahāmoggallāna was sitting not far from the Blessed One, having folded his legs crosswise, set his body erect, with mindfulness directed to the body well-established internally. The Blessed One saw the Venerable Mahāmoggallāna sitting not far away, having folded his legs crosswise, set his body erect, with mindfulness directed to the body well-established internally.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Sati kāyagatā upaṭṭhitā,

Chasu phassāyatanesu saṃvuto;

Satataṃ bhikkhu samāhito,

Jaññā nibbānamattano”ti. pañcamaṃ;

“With mindfulness directed to the body established, and being well-restrained in the six sense bases, a bhikkhu who is ever concentrated would know his own Nibbāna.” Fifth.

6. Pilindavacchasuttaṃ

6. The Pilindavaccha Sutta

26. Evaṃ [M 0.0109] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā rājagahe viharati veḷuvane kalandakanivāpe. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā pilindavaccho pilindivaccho (sī.) bhikkhū vasalavādena samudācarati. Atha kho sambahulā bhikkhū yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu. Ekamantaṃ nisinnā kho te bhikkhū bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ – “āyasmā, bhante, pilindavaccho bhikkhū vasalavādena samudācaratī”ti.

26. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Rājagaha in the Bamboo Grove, the Squirrels’ Feeding Place. Now at that time, the Venerable Pilindavaccha was addressing the bhikkhus with the term 'outcaste.' Then many bhikkhus approached the Blessed One, and having approached, paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, those bhikkhus said this to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, the Venerable Pilindavaccha is addressing the bhikkhus with the term 'outcaste.'”

Atha kho bhagavā aññataraṃ bhikkhuṃ āmantesi – “ehi tvaṃ, bhikkhu, mama vacanena pilindavacchaṃ bhikkhuṃ āmantehi – ‘satthā taṃ, āvuso pilindavaccha vaccha (syā.), āmantetī’”ti. “Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho so bhikkhu bhagavato paṭissutvā yenāyasmā pilindavaccho tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā [V 0.0100] āyasmantaṃ [T 0.0114] pilindavacchaṃ etadavoca – “satthā taṃ, āvuso pilindavaccha, āmantetī”ti.

Then the Blessed One addressed a certain bhikkhu: “Come, bhikkhu, in my name summon the bhikkhu Pilindavaccha, saying, ‘The Teacher calls you, friend Pilindavaccha.’” “Yes, venerable sir,” that bhikkhu assented to the Blessed One, approached the Venerable Pilindavaccha, and having approached, said this to the Venerable Pilindavaccha: “The Teacher calls you, friend Pilindavaccha.”

“Evamāvuso”ti kho āyasmā pilindavaccho tassa bhikkhuno paṭissutvā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho āyasmantaṃ pilindavacchaṃ bhagavā etadavoca – “saccaṃ kira tvaṃ, vaccha, bhikkhū vasalavādena samudācarasī”ti? “Evaṃ, bhante”ti.

“Yes, friend,” the Venerable Pilindavaccha assented to that bhikkhu and approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. The Blessed One then said this to the Venerable Pilindavaccha who was seated to one side: “Is it true, Vaccha, that you address the bhikkhus with the term 'outcaste'?” “Yes, venerable sir.”

Atha kho bhagavā āyasmato pilindavacchassa pubbenivāsaṃ manasi karitvā bhikkhū āmantesi – “mā kho tumhe, bhikkhave, vacchassa bhikkhuno ujjhāyittha. Na, bhikkhave, vaccho dosantaro bhikkhū vasalavādena samudācarati. Vacchassa, bhikkhave, bhikkhuno pañca jātisatāni abbokiṇṇāni brāhmaṇakule paccājātāni. So tassa vasalavādo dīgharattaṃ [P 0.0029] samudāciṇṇo ajjhāciṇṇo (syā. pī. ka. aṭṭhakathāyaṃ pāṭhantaraṃ). Tenāyaṃ vaccho bhikkhū vasalavādena samudācaratī”ti.

Then the Blessed One, having brought to mind the former existences of the Venerable Pilindavaccha, addressed the bhikkhus: “Bhikkhus, do not find fault with the bhikkhu Vaccha. Bhikkhus, Vaccha does not address the bhikkhus with the term 'outcaste' out of anger. For the bhikkhu Vaccha, for five hundred births in unbroken succession, he was reborn in a brahmin family. That term 'outcaste' has been long practiced by him. For that reason, this Vaccha addresses the bhikkhus with the term 'outcaste.'”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Yamhī [M 0.0110] na māyā vasatī na māno,

Yo vītalobho amamo nirāso;

Panuṇṇakodho paṇunnakodho (pī.) abhinibbutatto,

So brāhmaṇo so samaṇo sa bhikkhū”ti. chaṭṭhaṃ;

“In whom deceit and conceit do not dwell, who is free from greed, without possessiveness, without longing, with anger dispelled, and with a perfectly cooled mind—he is a brahmin, he is an ascetic, he is a bhikkhu.” Sixth.

7. Sakkudānasuttaṃ

7. The Sakkudāna Sutta

27. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā rājagahe viharati veḷuvane kalandakanivāpe. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā mahākassapo pippaliguhāyaṃ viharati, sattāhaṃ ekapallaṅkena nisinno hoti aññataraṃ [T 0.0115] nisinno aññataraṃ (syā. ka.) samādhiṃ samāpajjitvā. Atha kho āyasmā mahākassapo tassa sattāhassa accayena tamhā samādhimhā vuṭṭhāsi. Atha kho āyasmato mahākassapassa tamhā samādhimhā vuṭṭhitassa etadahosi – “yaṃnūnāhaṃ rājagahaṃ piṇḍāya paviseyya”nti.

27. Thus have I heard. At one time, the Blessed One was dwelling in Rājagaha, in the Bamboo Grove, the Squirrels’ Feeding Place. Now at that time, the Venerable Mahākassapa was dwelling in the Pipphali Cave; having attained a certain concentration, he was seated in a single cross-legged posture for seven days. Then, after the passing of those seven days, the Venerable Mahākassapa arose from that concentration. Then, to the Venerable Mahākassapa who had arisen from that concentration, this thought occurred: “I should now enter Rājagaha for alms.”

Tena kho pana samayena pañcamattāni devatāsatāni ussukkaṃ āpannāni honti āyasmato [V 0.0101] mahākassapassa piṇḍapātapaṭilābhāya. Atha kho āyasmā mahākassapo tāni pañcamattāni devatāsatāni paṭikkhipitvā pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya rājagahaṃ piṇḍāya pāvisi.

Now at that time, five hundred deities had become eager for the Venerable Mahākassapa to receive alms-food. Then, the Venerable Mahākassapa, having dismissed those five hundred deities, dressed himself in the morning, and taking his bowl and robe, entered Rājagaha for alms.

Tena kho pana samayena sakko devānamindo āyasmato mahākassapassa piṇḍapātaṃ dātukāmo hoti. Pesakāravaṇṇaṃ abhinimminitvā tantaṃ vināti. Sujā sujātā (syā. pī. ka.) asurakaññā tasaraṃ pūreti. Atha kho āyasmā mahākassapo rājagahe sapadānaṃ piṇḍāya caramāno yena sakkassa devānamindassa nivesanaṃ tenupasaṅkami. Addasā kho sakko devānamindo āyasmantaṃ mahākassapaṃ dūratova āgacchantaṃ. Disvāna gharā nikkhamitvā paccugantvā hatthato pattaṃ gahetvā gharaṃ pavisitvā pavisetvā (ka.) ghaṭiyā odanaṃ uddharitvā pattaṃ pūretvā āyasmato mahākassapassa adāsi. So ahosi piṇḍapāto anekasūpo anekabyañjano anekarasabyañjano anekasūparasabyañjano (sī. pī.). Atha kho āyasmato mahākassapassa etadahosi – “ko nu kho ayaṃ satto yassāyaṃ [P 0.0030] evarūpo iddhānubhāvo”ti[M 0.0111]? Atha kho āyasmato mahākassapassa etadahosi – “sakko kho [T 0.0116] ayaṃ devānamindo”ti. Iti viditvā sakkaṃ devānamindaṃ etadavoca – “kataṃ kho te idaṃ, kosiya; mā māssu (sī. syā.) punapi evarūpamakāsī”ti. “Amhākampi, bhante kassapa, puññena attho; amhākampi puññena karaṇīya”nti.

Now at that time, Sakka, lord of the devas, wished to give alms-food to the Venerable Mahākassapa. Having created the appearance of a weaver, he was weaving thread. The Asura maiden Sujā was filling the shuttle. Then, as the Venerable Mahākassapa was wandering for alms from house to house in Rājagaha, he approached the dwelling of Sakka, lord of the devas. Sakka, lord of the devas, saw the Venerable Mahākassapa coming from afar. Having seen him, he came out of the house, went to meet him, took the bowl from his hand, entered the house, scooped rice from a pot, filled the bowl, and gave it to the Venerable Mahākassapa. That alms-food had many soups, many curries, and many savory dishes of various flavors. Then it occurred to the Venerable Mahākassapa: “Who indeed is this being who possesses such psychic power?” Then it occurred to the Venerable Mahākassapa: “This is indeed Sakka, lord of the devas.” Knowing this, he said this to Sakka, lord of the devas: “This has been done by you, Kosiya. Do not do such a thing again.” “For us too, Venerable Kassapa, there is a need for merit; for us too, there is merit to be made.”

Atha kho sakko devānamindo āyasmantaṃ mahākassapaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā vehāsaṃ abbhuggantvā ākāse antalikkhe tikkhattuṃ udānaṃ udānesi – “aho dānaṃ paramadānaṃ paramaṃ dānaṃ (pī. ka.) kassape suppatiṭṭhitaṃ! Aho dānaṃ paramadānaṃ kassape suppatiṭṭhitaṃ!! Aho dānaṃ paramadānaṃ kassape suppatiṭṭhita”nti!!! Assosi kho bhagavā dibbāya sotadhātuyā visuddhāya atikkantamānusikāya sakkassa devānamindassa vehāsaṃ abbhuggantvā ākāse antalikkhe tikkhattuṃ udānaṃ udānentassa – “aho dānaṃ paramadānaṃ kassape suppatiṭṭhitaṃ! Aho dānaṃ paramadānaṃ kassape suppatiṭṭhitaṃ!! Aho dānaṃ paramadānaṃ kassape suppatiṭṭhita”nti!!!

Then Sakka, lord of the devas, having paid homage to the Venerable Mahākassapa and circumambulated him respectfully, rose up into the air and, in the sky, in open space, three times uttered this inspired utterance: “Oh, what a gift, a supreme gift, so well-established in Kassapa! Oh, what a gift, a supreme gift, so well-established in Kassapa! Oh, what a gift, a supreme gift, so well-established in Kassapa!” The Blessed One, with the divine ear element, which is purified and surpasses the human, heard Sakka, lord of the devas, as he rose up into the air and, in the sky, in open space, uttered this inspired utterance three times: “Oh, what a gift, a supreme gift, so well-established in Kassapa! Oh, what a gift, a supreme gift, so well-established in Kassapa! Oh, what a gift, a supreme gift, so well-established in Kassapa!”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood the meaning of this, on that occasion, uttered this inspired utterance:

“Piṇḍapātikassa bhikkhuno,

Attabharassa anaññaposino;

Devā [V 0.0102] pihayanti tādino,

Upasantassa sadā satīmato”ti. sattamaṃ;

“The monk who lives on alms-food, self-supporting, not supporting others—the devas cherish such a steadfast one, peaceful and always mindful.” Seventh.

8. Piṇḍapātikasuttaṃ

8. The Sutta on the Alms-gatherer

28. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena sambahulānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkantānaṃ karerimaṇḍalamāḷe [T 0.0117] sannisinnānaṃ sannipatitānaṃ ayamantarākathā udapādi –

28. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. Now on that occasion, for a number of bhikkhus who, after the meal, had returned from their alms round, as they were sitting down and assembled in the Karerī-creeper Pavilion, this conversation arose in their midst:

“Piṇḍapātiko, āvuso, bhikkhu piṇḍāya caranto labhati kālena kālaṃ manāpike cakkhunā rūpe passituṃ, labhati kālena kālaṃ manāpike sotena sadde sotuṃ, labhati kālena kālaṃ manāpike ghānena [M 0.0112] gandhe ghāyituṃ, labhati kālena kālaṃ manāpike jivhāya rase sāyituṃ, labhati kālena kālaṃ manāpike kāyena phoṭṭhabbe phusituṃ. Piṇḍapātiko, āvuso, bhikkhu sakkato garukato mānito pūjito apacito piṇḍāya carati. Handāvuso, mayampi piṇḍapātikā homa. Mayampi lacchāma kālena kālaṃ manāpike cakkhunā rūpe passituṃ, mayampi lacchāma kālena kālaṃ manāpike sotena sadde sotuṃ, mayampi lacchāma kālena kālaṃ manāpike ghānena gandhe ghāyituṃ, mayampi lacchāma kālena kālaṃ manāpike jivhāya rase sāyituṃ, mayampi lacchāma kālena kālaṃ manāpike kāyena phoṭṭhabbe [P 0.0031] phusituṃ; mayampi sakkatā garukatā mānitā pūjitā apacitā piṇḍāya carissāmā”ti. Ayañcarahi tesaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ antarākathā hoti vippakatā.

“Friends, a bhikkhu who undertakes the alms-gathering practice, while wandering for alms, from time to time gets to see agreeable forms with the eye, from time to time gets to hear agreeable sounds with the ear, from time to time gets to smell agreeable odors with the nose, from time to time gets to taste agreeable flavors with the tongue, and from time to time gets to touch agreeable tangibles with the body. A bhikkhu who undertakes the alms-gathering practice, friends, is well-treated, respected, esteemed, worshipped, and venerated while wandering for alms. Come now, friends, let us too become undertakers of the alms-gathering practice! We too will get to see agreeable forms with the eye from time to time; we too will get to hear agreeable sounds with the ear from time to time; we too will get to smell agreeable odors with the nose from time to time; we too will get to taste agreeable flavors with the tongue from time to time; we too will get to touch agreeable tangibles with the body from time to time. We too, being well-treated, respected, esteemed, worshipped, and venerated, will wander for alms.” And this conversation in their midst was unfinished.

Atha kho bhagavā sāyanhasamayaṃ paṭisallānā vuṭṭhito yena karerimaṇḍalamāḷo tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā paññatte āsane nisīdi. Nisajja kho bhagavā bhikkhū āmantesi – “kāya nuttha, bhikkhave, etarahi kathāya sannisinnā, kā ca pana vo antarākathā vippakatā”ti?

Then, in the evening, the Blessed One, having arisen from seclusion, went to the Karerī-creeper Pavilion. Having approached, he sat down on the prepared seat. Having sat down, the Blessed One addressed the bhikkhus: “For what discussion, bhikkhus, are you now sitting together? And what was your unfinished conversation in your midst?”

“Idha, bhante, amhākaṃ pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkantānaṃ karerimaṇḍalamāḷe sannisinnānaṃ [T 0.0118] sannipatitānaṃ ayamantarākathā udapādi –

“Here, venerable sir, for us who, after the meal, had returned from our alms round, as we were sitting down and assembled in this Karerī-creeper Pavilion, this conversation arose in our midst:

‘Piṇḍapātiko, āvuso, bhikkhu piṇḍāya caranto labhati kālena kālaṃ manāpike cakkhunā [V 0.0103] rūpe passituṃ, labhati kālena kālaṃ manāpike sotena sadde sotuṃ, labhati kālena kālaṃ manāpike ghānena gandhe ghāyituṃ, labhati kālena kālaṃ manāpike jivhāya rase sāyituṃ, labhati kālena kālaṃ manāpike kāyena phoṭṭhabbe phusituṃ. Piṇḍapātiko, āvuso, bhikkhu sakkato garukato mānito pūjito apacito piṇḍāya carati. Handāvuso, mayampi piṇḍapātikā homa. Mayampi lacchāma kālena kālaṃ manāpike cakkhunā rūpe passituṃ…pe… kāyena phoṭṭhabbe phusituṃ. Mayampi sakkatā garukatā mānitā pūjitā apacitā piṇḍāya carissāmā’ti. Ayaṃ kho no, bhante, antarākathā vippakatā, atha bhagavā anuppatto”ti.

“‘Friends, a bhikkhu who undertakes the alms-gathering practice, while wandering for alms, from time to time gets to see agreeable forms with the eye... gets to touch agreeable tangibles with the body. A bhikkhu who undertakes the alms-gathering practice, friends, is well-treated, respected, esteemed, worshipped, and venerated while wandering for alms. Come now, friends, let us too become undertakers of the alms-gathering practice! We too will get to see agreeable forms with the eye from time to time … and to touch agreeable tangibles with the body. We too, being well-treated, respected, esteemed, worshipped, and venerated, will wander for alms.’ This, venerable sir, was our unfinished conversation in our midst, and then the Blessed One arrived.”

“Na [M 0.0113] khvetaṃ, bhikkhave, tumhākaṃ patirūpaṃ kulaputtānaṃ saddhā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajitānaṃ yaṃ tumhe evarūpiṃ kathaṃ katheyyātha. Sannipatitānaṃ vo, bhikkhave, dvayaṃ karaṇīyaṃ – dhammī vā kathā ariyo vā tuṇhībhāvo”ti.

“It is not fitting, bhikkhus, for you clansmen who have gone forth from the household life into homelessness out of faith that you should speak such talk. When you are assembled, bhikkhus, two things are to be done: either talk on the Dhamma or noble silence.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ [T 0.0119] udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Piṇḍapātikassa bhikkhuno,

Attabharassa anaññaposino;

Devā pihayanti tādino,

No ce saddasilokanissito”ti. aṭṭhamaṃ;

“Of the bhikkhu who lives on almsfood, who supports himself and not another, the devas are fond of such a steadfast one, if he is not dependent on fame and renown.” Eighth.

9. Sippasuttaṃ

9. The Discourse on Skills

29. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena sambahulānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkantānaṃ maṇḍalamāḷe sannisinnānaṃ sannipatitānaṃ ayamantarākathā udapādi – “ko nu kho, āvuso, sippaṃ jānāti? Ko kiṃ sippaṃ sikkhi? Kataraṃ sippaṃ sippānaṃ agga”nti?

29. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. Now on that occasion, a number of bhikkhus, having returned from their alms-round after the meal, were sitting together assembled in the assembly hall when this discussion arose among them: “Friends, who indeed knows a skill? Who has learned what skill? Which skill is the chief among skills?”

Tatthekacce evamāhaṃsu – “hatthisippaṃ sippānaṃ agga”nti. Ekacce evamāhaṃsu – “assasippaṃ sippānaṃ agga”nti. Ekacce evamāhaṃsu – “rathasippaṃ sippānaṃ agga”nti. Ekacce evamāhaṃsu – “dhanusippaṃ sippānaṃ agga”nti. Ekacce evamāhaṃsu – “tharusippaṃ sippānaṃ agga”nti. Ekacce evamāhaṃsu – “muddāsippaṃ sippānaṃ agga”nti. Ekacce evamāhaṃsu [V 0.0104] – “gaṇanāsippaṃ sippānaṃ agga”nti. Ekacce evamāhaṃsu [P 0.0032] – “saṅkhānasippaṃ sippānaṃ agga”nti. Ekacce evamāhaṃsu – “lekhāsippaṃ sippānaṃ agga”nti. Ekacce evamāhaṃsu – “kāveyyasippaṃ kābyasippaṃ (syā.) sippānaṃ agga”nti. Ekacce evamāhaṃsu – “lokāyatasippaṃ sippānaṃ agga”nti. Ekacce evamāhaṃsu – “khattavijjāsippaṃ sippānaṃ agga”nti. Ayañcarahi tesaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ antarākathā hoti vippakatā.

There some said thus: ‘The elephant-skill is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The horse-skill is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The chariot-skill is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The skill of archery is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘Swordsmanship is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The art of finger-reckoning is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The art of calculation is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The art of enumeration is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The art of writing is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The art of poetry is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The art of worldly philosophy is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The science of fields is the chief among skills.’ And this discussion among those bhikkhus was unfinished.

Atha kho bhagavā [T 0.0120] sāyanhasamayaṃ paṭisallānā vuṭṭhito yena maṇḍalamāḷo tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā paññatte āsane nisīdi. Nisajja [M 0.0114] kho bhagavā bhikkhū āmantesi – “kāya nuttha, bhikkhave, etarahi kathāya sannisinnā, kā ca pana vo antarākathā vippakatā”ti?

Then, in the evening, the Blessed One, having arisen from seclusion, went to the assembly hall, and sat down on the seat that had been prepared. Having sat down, the Blessed One addressed the bhikkhus: “For what discussion are you sitting together here now, bhikkhus? And what was your unfinished discussion?”

“Idha, bhante, amhākaṃ pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkantānaṃ maṇḍalamāḷe sannisinnānaṃ ayamantarākathā udapādi – ‘ko nu kho, āvuso, sippaṃ jānāti? Ko kiṃ sippaṃ sikkhi? Kataraṃ sippaṃ sippānaṃ agga’nti?

“Here, venerable sir, for us, after the meal, having returned from our alms-round, as we were sitting together in the assembly hall, this discussion arose among us: ‘Friends, who indeed knows a skill? Who has learned what skill? Which skill is the chief among skills?’”

“Tatthekacce evamāhaṃsu – ‘hatthisippaṃ sippānaṃ agga’nti. Ekacce evamāhaṃsu – ‘assasippaṃ sippānaṃ agga’nti; ekacce evamāhaṃsu – ‘rathasippaṃ sippānaṃ agga’nti; ekacce evamāhaṃsu – ‘dhanusippaṃ sippānaṃ agga’nti; ekacce evamāhaṃsu – ‘tharusippaṃ sippānaṃ agga’nti, ekacce evamāhaṃsu – ‘muddāsippaṃ sippānaṃ agga’nti ekacce evamāhaṃsu – ‘gaṇanāsippaṃ sippānaṃ agga’nti; ekacce evamāhaṃsu – ‘saṅkhānasippaṃ sippānaṃ agga’nti; ekacce evamāhaṃsu – ‘lekhāsippaṃ sippānaṃ agga’nti; ekacce evamāhaṃsu – ‘kāveyyasippaṃ sippānaṃ agga’nti; ekacce evamāhaṃsu – ‘lokāyatasippaṃ sippānaṃ agga’nti; ekacce evamāhaṃsu – ‘khattavijjāsippaṃ sippānaṃ agga’nti. Ayaṃ kho no, bhante, antarākathā hoti vippakatā, atha bhagavā anuppatto”ti.

“There some said thus: ‘The elephant-skill is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The horse-skill is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The chariot-skill is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The skill of archery is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘Swordsmanship is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The art of finger-reckoning is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The art of calculation is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The art of enumeration is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The art of writing is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The art of poetry is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The art of worldly philosophy is the chief among skills.’ Some said thus: ‘The science of fields is the chief among skills.’ This, venerable sir, was our unfinished discussion, and then the Blessed One arrived.”

“Na khvetaṃ, bhikkhave, tumhākaṃ patirūpaṃ kulaputtānaṃ saddhā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajitānaṃ yaṃ tumhe evarūpiṃ kathaṃ katheyyātha. Sannipatitānaṃ [T 0.0121] vo, bhikkhave, dvayaṃ karaṇīyaṃ – dhammī vā kathā ariyo vā tuṇhībhāvo”ti.

“It is not fitting, bhikkhus, for you clansmen who have gone forth from the household life into homelessness out of faith that you should engage in such a discussion. When you are assembled, bhikkhus, two things should be done: either talk on the Dhamma or noble silence.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Asippajīvī [V 0.0105] lahu atthakāmo,

Yatindriyo sabbadhi vippamutto;

Anokasārī amamo nirāso,

Hitvā mānaṃ ekacaro sa bhikkhū”ti. navamaṃ;

“Not living by a skill, unburdened, aspiring for the goal, with faculties restrained, in every way released; homeless, without possessiveness, free from craving, having abandoned conceit, faring alone—he is a bhikkhu.” Ninth.

10. Lokasuttaṃ

10. The Discourse on the World

30. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā uruvelāyaṃ viharati najjā nerañjarāya tīre bodhirukkhamūle paṭhamābhisambuddho. Tena kho pana samayena [M 0.0115] bhagavā sattāhaṃ ekapallaṅkena nisinno hoti vimuttisukhapaṭisaṃvedī.

30. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Uruvelā, on the bank of the river Nerañjarā, at the foot of the Bodhi tree, having just attained full enlightenment. At that time, the Blessed One was seated in one cross-legged posture for seven days, experiencing the bliss of liberation.

Atha kho bhagavā tassa sattāhassa accayena tamhā samādhimhā vuṭṭhahitvā buddhacakkhunā lokaṃ volokesi. Addasā kho bhagavā buddhacakkhunā volokento satte anekehi santāpehi santappamāne, anekehi ca pariḷāhehi pariḍayhamāne – rāgajehipi, dosajehipi, mohajehipi mohajehipīti (sabbattha).

Then, after the passing of those seven days, the Blessed One arose from that concentration and surveyed the world with his Buddha-eye. As the Blessed One surveyed the world with his Buddha-eye, he saw beings being tormented by many afflictions and being burned all around by many fevers—those born of lust, those born of hatred, and those born of delusion.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, having understood this matter, the Blessed One at that time uttered this inspired utterance:

“Ayaṃ loko santāpajāto,

Phassapareto rogaṃ vadati attato;

Yena yena hi maññati yena hi maññati (syā. pī.),

Tato taṃ hoti aññathā.

“This world is born of affliction; overcome by contact, it speaks of disease as ‘self.’ In whatever way it conceives, that becomes otherwise.

“Aññathābhāvī bhavasatto loko,

Bhavapareto bhavamevābhinandati;

Yadabhinandati [P 0.0033][T 0.0122] taṃ bhayaṃ,

Yassa bhāyati taṃ dukkhaṃ;

Bhavavippahānāya kho panidaṃ brahmacariyaṃ vussati”.

“The world, attached to becoming, is of a nature to become otherwise. Overcome by becoming, it delights only in becoming. What it delights in is a danger; what it fears is suffering. This holy life is lived for the abandoning of becoming.”

“‘Ye hi keci samaṇā vā brāhmaṇā vā bhavena bhavassa vippamokkhamāhaṃsu, sabbe te avippamuttā [V 0.0106] bhavasmā’ti vadāmi. ‘Ye vā pana keci samaṇā vā brāhmaṇā vā vibhavena bhavassa nissaraṇamāhaṃsu, sabbe te anissaṭā bhavasmā’ti vadāmi.

“Whatever ascetics or brahmins have declared liberation from becoming by means of becoming, I say that all of them are not liberated from becoming. And whatever ascetics or brahmins have declared an escape from becoming by means of non-becoming, I say that all of them have not escaped from becoming.”

“Upadhiñhi paṭicca dukkhamidaṃ sambhoti, sabbupādānakkhayā natthi dukkhassa sambhavo. Lokamimaṃ passa; puthū avijjāya paretā bhūtā bhūtaratā aparimuttā; ye hi keci bhavā sabbadhi sabbatthatāya sabbe te bhavā aniccā dukkhā vipariṇāmadhammā”ti.

“For this suffering arises dependent on acquisitions; with the destruction of all clinging, there is no arising of suffering. Behold this world: many beings, overcome by ignorance, delighting in what has come to be, are unliberated. Whatever states of becoming there are, in all places and in every way, all those states of becoming are impermanent, suffering, and of a nature to change.”

“Evametaṃ [M 0.0116] yathābhūtaṃ, sammappaññāya passato;

Bhavataṇhā pahīyati, vibhavaṃ nābhinandati.

“For one who sees this as it really is with right wisdom, craving for becoming is abandoned; one does not delight in non-becoming.”

“Sabbaso taṇhānaṃ khayā,

Asesavirāganirodho nibbānaṃ;

Tassa nibbutassa bhikkhuno,

Anupādā anupādānā (sī.) punabbhavo na hoti;

Abhibhūto māro vijitasaṅgāmo,

Upaccagā sabbabhavāni tādī”ti. dasamaṃ;

“From the complete destruction of all cravings, the remainderless fading away and cessation is Nibbāna. For that cooled monk, through non-clinging, there is no future becoming. Māra is overcome, the battle is won; such a one has transcended all states of becoming.” The tenth.

Nandavaggo tatiyo niṭṭhito.

The Nanda Chapter, the third, is concluded.

Tassuddānaṃ –

The summary verse for this is:

Kammaṃ [T 0.0123] nando yasojo ca, sāriputto ca kolito;

Pilindo pilindi (sī.) kassapo piṇḍo, sippaṃ lokena te dasāti.

Kamma, Nanda, and Yasoja, Sāriputta and Kolita; Pilindakassapa, Piṇḍa, Sippa, and with Loka—these are the ten.

4. Meghiyavaggo

4. The Meghiya Chapter

1. Meghiyasuttaṃ

1. The Meghiya Discourse

31. Evaṃ [P 0.0034] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā cālikāyaṃ viharati cālike pabbate. Tena [V 0.0107] kho pana samayena āyasmā meghiyo bhagavato upaṭṭhāko hoti. Atha kho āyasmā meghiyo yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhāsi. Ekamantaṃ ṭhito kho āyasmā meghiyo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “icchāmahaṃ, bhante, jantugāmaṃ piṇḍāya pavisitu”nti. “Yassadāni tvaṃ, meghiya, kālaṃ maññasī”ti.

31. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Cālikā, on Cālika Mountain. At that time, the Venerable Meghiya was the Blessed One's attendant. Then the Venerable Meghiya approached the Blessed One; having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and stood to one side. Standing to one side, the Venerable Meghiya said this to the Blessed One: 'Venerable sir, I wish to enter Jantugāma for alms.' 'Now, Meghiya, you may go at the time you think fit.'

Atha kho āyasmā meghiyo pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya jantugāmaṃ piṇḍāya pāvisi. Jantugāme piṇḍāya caritvā pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkanto yena kimikāḷāya nadiyā tīraṃ tenupasaṅkami. upasaṅkamitvā (sabbattha) a. ni. 9.3 passitabbaṃ Addasā [M 0.0117] kho āyasmā meghiyo upasaṅkamitvā (sabbattha) a. ni. 9.3 passitabbaṃ kimikāḷāya nadiyā tīre jaṅghāvihāraṃ jaṅghavihāraṃ (ka.) anucaṅkamamāno anuvicaramāno anuvicaramāno addasā kho (sī. syā. pī.), anuvicaramāno addasa (ka.) ambavanaṃ pāsādikaṃ manuññaṃ ramaṇīyaṃ. Disvānassa etadahosi – “pāsādikaṃ vatidaṃ ambavanaṃ manuññaṃ idaṃ padaṃ videsapotthakesu natthi, aṅguttarepi ramaṇīyaṃ. Alaṃ vatidaṃ kulaputtassa padhānatthikassa padhānāya. Sace maṃ bhagavā anujāneyya, āgaccheyyāhaṃ imaṃ ambavanaṃ padhānāyā”ti.

Then, in the forenoon, the Venerable Meghiya dressed, and taking his bowl and robe, entered Jantugāma for alms. Having gone for alms in Jantugāma, after the meal, having returned from his alms-round, he approached the bank of the river Kimikāḷā. While walking back and forth for exercise along the bank of the river Kimikāḷā, he saw a mango grove that was inspiring, pleasing, and delightful. Having seen it, this thought occurred to him: 'Indeed, this mango grove is inspiring, pleasing, and delightful. It is truly suitable for the striving of a clansman who is intent on striving. If the Blessed One were to permit me, I would come to this mango grove for striving.'

Atha kho āyasmā meghiyo yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā [T 0.0124] bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho āyasmā meghiyo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca –

Then the Venerable Meghiya approached the Blessed One; having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, the Venerable Meghiya said this to the Blessed One:

“Idhāhaṃ, bhante, pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya jantugāmaṃ piṇḍāya pāvisiṃ. Jantugāme piṇḍāya caritvā pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkanto yena kimikāḷāya nadiyā tīraṃ tenupasaṅkamiṃ upasaṅkamitvā (sabbattha). Addasaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante upasaṅkamitvā (sabbattha), kimikāḷāya nadiyā tīre jaṅghāvihāraṃ anucaṅkamamāno anuvicaramāno anuvicaramāno addasaṃ (sabbattha) ambavanaṃ pāsādikaṃ manuññaṃ ramaṇīyaṃ. Disvāna me etadahosi – ‘pāsādikaṃ vatidaṃ ambavanaṃ manuññaṃ ramaṇīyaṃ. Alaṃ vatidaṃ kulaputtassa padhānatthikassa padhānāya. Sace maṃ bhagavā anujāneyya, āgaccheyyāhaṃ imaṃ ambavanaṃ padhānāyā’ti. Sace maṃ, bhante, bhagavā anujānāti anujāneyya (a. ni. 9.3), gaccheyyāhaṃ taṃ [P 0.0035] ambavanaṃ padhānāyā”ti.

“Venerable sir, this forenoon I dressed, and taking my bowl and robe, entered Jantugāma for alms. Having gone for alms in Jantugāma, after the meal, having returned from my alms-round, I approached the bank of the river Kimikāḷā. While walking back and forth for exercise along the bank of the river Kimikāḷā, I saw a mango grove that was inspiring, pleasing, and delightful. Having seen it, this thought occurred to me: 'Indeed, this mango grove is inspiring, pleasing, and delightful. It is truly suitable for the striving of a clansman who is intent on striving. If the Blessed One were to permit me, I would come to this mango grove for striving.' Venerable sir, if the Blessed One permits me, I would go to that mango grove for striving.”

Evaṃ vutte, bhagavā āyasmantaṃ meghiyaṃ etadavoca – “āgamehi tāva, meghiya, ekakamhi ekakamhā (sī. pī.), ekakomhi (syā.) tāva, yāva aññopi koci bhikkhu āgacchatī”ti.

When this was said, the Blessed One said this to the Venerable Meghiya: 'Wait for now, Meghiya, as I am alone, until some other monk comes.'

Dutiyampi kho āyasmā meghiyo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “bhagavato, bhante, natthi kiñci [V 0.0108] uttari uttariṃ (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.) karaṇīyaṃ, natthi katassa vā paticayo. Mayhaṃ kho pana, bhante, atthi uttari karaṇīyaṃ, atthi katassa paticayo. Sace maṃ bhagavā anujānāti, gaccheyyāhaṃ taṃ ambavanaṃ padhānāyā”ti. Dutiyampi kho [M 0.0118] bhagavā āyasmantaṃ meghiyaṃ etadavoca – “āgamehi tāva, meghiya, ekakamhi tāva, yāva aññopi koci bhikkhu āgacchatī”ti.

For a second time, the Venerable Meghiya said this to the Blessed One: 'Venerable sir, for the Blessed One there is nothing further to be done, nor is there any adding to what has been done. But for me, venerable sir, there is something further to be done, and there is adding to what has been done. If the Blessed One permits me, I would go to that mango grove for striving.' For a second time, the Blessed One said this to the Venerable Meghiya: 'Wait for now, Meghiya, as I am alone, until some other monk comes.'

Tatiyampi kho āyasmā meghiyo bhagavantaṃ [T 0.0125] etadavoca – “bhagavato, bhante, natthi kiñci uttari karaṇīyaṃ, natthi katassa vā paticayo. Mayhaṃ kho pana, bhante, atthi uttari karaṇīyaṃ, atthi katassa paticayo. Sace maṃ bhagavā anujānāti, gaccheyyāhaṃ taṃ ambavanaṃ padhānāyā”ti. “Padhānanti kho, meghiya, vadamānaṃ kinti vadeyyāma? Yassadāni tvaṃ, meghiya, kālaṃ maññasī”ti.

For a third time, the Venerable Meghiya said this to the Blessed One: 'Venerable sir, for the Blessed One there is nothing further to be done, nor is there any adding to what has been done. But for me, venerable sir, there is something further to be done, and there is adding to what has been done. If the Blessed One permits me, I would go to that mango grove for striving.' 'Meghiya, to one who speaks of striving, what should we say? Now, Meghiya, you may go at the time you think fit.'

Atha kho āyasmā meghiyo uṭṭhāyāsanā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā yena taṃ ambavanaṃ tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā taṃ ambavanaṃ ajjhogāhetvā ajjhogahetvā (sī. syā. pī.) aññatarasmiṃ rukkhamūle divāvihāraṃ nisīdi. Atha kho āyasmato meghiyassa tasmiṃ ambavane viharantassa yebhuyyena tayo pāpakā akusalā vitakkā samudācaranti, seyyathidaṃ – kāmavitakko, byāpādavitakko, vihiṃsāvitakko vitakkoti (sī. pī. ka.).

Then the Venerable Meghiya rose from his seat, paid homage to the Blessed One, and having circumambulated him to the right, went to that mango grove. Having gone, he entered that mango grove and sat down at the foot of a certain tree for the day's abiding. Then, while the Venerable Meghiya was dwelling in that mango grove, for the most part three kinds of evil, unwholesome thoughts arose in him, namely: the thought of sensual desire, the thought of ill will, and the thought of cruelty.

Atha kho āyasmato meghiyassa etadahosi – “acchariyaṃ vata bho, abbhutaṃ vata bho! Saddhāya ca vatamhā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajitā. Atha ca panimehi tīhi pāpakehi akusalehi vitakkehi anvāsattā, seyyathidaṃ – kāmavitakkena, byāpādavitakkena, vihiṃsāvitakkena”.

Then this thought occurred to the Venerable Meghiya: 'It is wonderful indeed, sirs! It is astonishing indeed, sirs! We have gone forth from the household life into homelessness out of faith. And yet we are assailed by these three evil, unwholesome thoughts, namely: the thought of sensual desire, the thought of ill will, and the thought of cruelty.'

Atha kho āyasmā meghiyo sāyanhasamayaṃ paṭisallānā vuṭṭhito yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho āyasmā meghiyo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “idha mayhaṃ, bhante, tasmiṃ ambavane viharantassa yebhuyyena tayo [T 0.0126] pāpakā akusalā vitakkā samudācaranti, seyyathidaṃ – kāmavitakko, byāpādavitakko, vihiṃsāvitakko[P 0.0036]. Tassa mayhaṃ, bhante, etadahosi – ‘acchariyaṃ vata, bho, abbhutaṃ vata, bho! Saddhāya ca vatamhā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajitā. Atha ca panimehi tīhi pāpakehi akusalehi [M 0.0119] vitakkehi anvāsattā, seyyathidaṃ – kāmavitakkena, byāpādavitakkena, vihiṃsāvitakkena’”.

Then, in the evening, the Venerable Meghiya arose from his solitude and approached the Blessed One; having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down at a suitable place. Seated at a suitable place, the Venerable Meghiya said this to the Blessed One: 'Venerable sir, while I was dwelling in that mango grove, for the most part three kinds of evil, unwholesome thoughts arose in me, namely: the thought of sensual desire, the thought of ill will, and the thought of cruelty. Then, venerable sir, this thought occurred to me: "It is wonderful indeed, sirs! It is astonishing indeed, sirs! We have gone forth from the household life into homelessness out of faith. And yet we are assailed by these three evil, unwholesome thoughts, namely: the thought of sensual desire, the thought of ill will, and the thought of cruelty."'"

“Aparipakkāya[V 0.0109], meghiya, cetovimuttiyā pañca dhammā paripākāya saṃvattanti. Katame pañca?

Meghiya, for the liberation of mind that is immature, five things conduce to its ripening. What are the five?

“Idha, meghiya, bhikkhu kalyāṇamitto hoti kalyāṇasahāyo kalyāṇasampavaṅko. Aparipakkāya, meghiya, cetovimuttiyā ayaṃ paṭhamo dhammo paripākāya saṃvattati.

Here, Meghiya, a bhikkhu has good friends, good companions, and good intimates. Meghiya, for the liberation of mind that is immature, this is the first thing that conduces to its ripening.

“Puna caparaṃ, meghiya, bhikkhu sīlavā hoti, pātimokkhasaṃvarasaṃvuto viharati ācāragocarasampanno, aṇumattesu vajjesu bhayadassāvī, samādāya sikkhati sikkhāpadesu. Aparipakkāya, meghiya, cetovimuttiyā ayaṃ dutiyo dhammo paripākāya saṃvattati.

Furthermore, Meghiya, a bhikkhu is virtuous; he dwells restrained by the restraint of the Pātimokkha, is accomplished in conduct and resort, and sees danger in the slightest faults. Having undertaken the training rules, he trains in them. Meghiya, for the liberation of mind that is immature, this is the second thing that conduces to its ripening.

“Puna caparaṃ, meghiya, bhikkhu yāyaṃ kathā abhisallekhikā cetovivaraṇasappāyā ekantanibbidāya virāgāya nirodhāya upasamāya abhiññāya sambodhāya nibbānāya saṃvattati, seyyathidaṃ – appicchakathā, santuṭṭhikathā, pavivekakathā, asaṃsaggakathā, vīriyārambhakathā, sīlakathā, samādhikathā, paññākathā, vimuttikathā, vimuttiñāṇadassanakathā; evarūpāya kathāya nikāmalābhī hoti akicchalābhī akasiralābhī. Aparipākāya, meghiya, cetovimuttiyā ayaṃ tatiyo dhammo paripākāya saṃvattati.

Furthermore, Meghiya, a bhikkhu is one who obtains at will, without difficulty, and without trouble, such talk as is conducive to effacement, suitable for the opening of the mind, and which leads exclusively to disenchantment, dispassion, cessation, peace, direct knowledge, full enlightenment, and Nibbāna—that is to say: talk on fewness of wishes, talk on contentment, talk on seclusion, talk on non-association, talk on arousing energy, talk on virtue, talk on concentration, talk on wisdom, talk on deliverance, and talk on the knowledge and vision of deliverance. Meghiya, for the liberation of mind that is immature, this is the third thing that conduces to its ripening.

“Puna caparaṃ[T 0.0127], meghiya, bhikkhu āraddhavīriyo viharati, akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ pahānāya, kusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ upasampadāya uppādāya (syā.), thāmavā daḷhaparakkamo anikkhittadhuro kusalesu dhammesu. Aparipakkāya, meghiya, cetovimuttiyā ayaṃ catuttho dhammo paripākāya saṃvattati.

Furthermore, Meghiya, a bhikkhu dwells with energy aroused for the abandoning of unwholesome states and for the undertaking of wholesome states; he is steadfast, firm in exertion, and does not lay down the burden in regard to wholesome states. Meghiya, for the liberation of mind that is immature, this is the fourth thing that conduces to its ripening.

“Puna caparaṃ, meghiya, bhikkhu paññavā hoti udayatthagāminiyā paññāya samannāgato ariyāya nibbedhikāya sammā dukkhakkhayagāminiyā. Aparipakkāya, meghiya, cetovimuttiyā ayaṃ pañcamo dhammo paripākāya saṃvattati. Aparipakkāya, meghiya, cetovimuttiyā ime pañca dhammā paripākāya saṃvattanti.

Furthermore, Meghiya, a bhikkhu is wise; he is endowed with wisdom that discerns arising and passing away, which is noble, penetrative, and leads rightly to the complete destruction of suffering. Meghiya, for the liberation of mind that is immature, this is the fifth thing that conduces to its ripening. Meghiya, for the liberation of mind that is immature, these five things conduce to its ripening.

“Kalyāṇamittassetaṃ[M 0.0120], meghiya, bhikkhuno pāṭikaṅkhaṃ kalyāṇasahāyassa kalyāṇasampavaṅkassa yaṃ sīlavā bhavissati, pātimokkhasaṃvarasaṃvuto viharissati, ācāragocarasampanno, aṇumattesu vajjesu bhayadassāvī, samādāya sikkhissati sikkhāpadesu.

Meghiya, this is to be expected of a bhikkhu who has good friends, good companions, and good intimates: that he will be virtuous, will dwell restrained by the restraint of the Pātimokkha, will be accomplished in conduct and resort, will see danger in the slightest faults, and, having undertaken the training rules, will train in them.

“Kalyāṇamittassetaṃ[V 0.0110], meghiya, bhikkhuno pāṭikaṅkhaṃ kalyāṇasahāyassa kalyāṇasampavaṅkassa yaṃ [P 0.0037] yāyaṃ kathā abhisallekhikā cetovivaraṇasappāyā ekantanibbidāya virāgāya nirodhāya upasamāya abhiññāya sambodhāya nibbānāya saṃvattati, seyyathidaṃ – appicchakathā, santuṭṭhikathā, pavivekakathā, asaṃsaggakathā, vīriyārambhakathā, sīlakathā, samādhikathā, paññākathā, vimuttikathā, vimuttiñāṇadassanakathā; evarūpāya kathāya nikāmalābhī bhavissati akicchalābhī akasiralābhī.

Meghiya, this is to be expected of a bhikkhu who has good friends, good companions, and good intimates: that he will be one who obtains at will, without difficulty, and without trouble, such talk as is conducive to effacement, suitable for the opening of the mind, and which leads exclusively to disenchantment, dispassion, cessation, peace, direct knowledge, full enlightenment, and Nibbāna—that is to say: talk on fewness of wishes, talk on contentment, talk on seclusion, talk on non-association, talk on arousing energy, talk on virtue, talk on concentration, talk on wisdom, talk on deliverance, and talk on the knowledge and vision of deliverance.

“Kalyāṇamittassetaṃ, meghiya, bhikkhuno pāṭikaṅkhaṃ kalyāṇasahāyassa kalyāṇasampavaṅkassa yaṃ āraddhavīriyo viharissati [T 0.0128] akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ pahānāya, kusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ upasampadāya, thāmavā daḷhaparakkamo anikkhittadhuro kusalesu dhammesu.

Meghiya, this is to be expected of a bhikkhu who has good friends, good companions, and good intimates: that he will dwell with energy aroused for the abandoning of unwholesome states and for the undertaking of wholesome states; that he will be steadfast, firm in exertion, and will not lay down the burden in regard to wholesome states.

“Kalyāṇamittassetaṃ, meghiya, bhikkhuno pāṭikaṅkhaṃ kalyāṇasahāyassa kalyāṇasampavaṅkassa yaṃ paññavā bhavissati, udayatthagāminiyā paññāya samannāgato ariyāya nibbedhikāya sammā dukkhakkhayagāminiyā.

Meghiya, this is to be expected of a bhikkhu who has good friends, good companions, and good intimates: that he will be wise, endowed with wisdom that discerns arising and passing away, which is noble, penetrative, and leads rightly to the complete destruction of suffering.

“Tena ca pana, meghiya, bhikkhunā imesu pañcasu dhammesu patiṭṭhāya cattāro dhammā uttari bhāvetabbā – asubhā bhāvetabbā rāgassa pahānāya, mettā bhāvetabbā byāpādassa pahānāya, ānāpānassati bhāvetabbā vitakkupacchedāya, aniccasaññā bhāvetabbā asmimānasamugghātāya. Aniccasaññino hi, meghiya, anattasaññā saṇṭhāti, anattasaññī asmimānasamugghātaṃ pāpuṇāti diṭṭheva dhamme nibbāna”nti.

Moreover, Meghiya, that bhikkhu, being established in these five things, should further develop four things. The perception of foulness should be developed for the abandoning of lust. Loving-kindness should be developed for the abandoning of ill will. Mindfulness of breathing should be developed for the cutting off of discursive thought. The perception of impermanence should be developed for the uprooting of the conceit 'I am.' Indeed, Meghiya, for one who perceives impermanence, the perception of not-self is established. One who perceives not-self attains the uprooting of the conceit 'I am' and Nibbāna in this very life.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood the significance of this, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Khuddā [M 0.0121] vitakkā sukhumā vitakkā,

Anugatā anuggatā (sī. ka. aṭṭhakathāyaṃ pāṭhantaraṃ) manaso uppilāvā ubbilāpā (sī. syā. pī.);

Ete avidvā manaso vitakke,

Hurā huraṃ dhāvati bhantacitto.

Petty thoughts and subtle thoughts, followers of the mind, its agitations—not knowing these thoughts of the mind, one with a bewildered mind runs here and there.

“Ete ca vidvā manaso vitakke,

Ātāpiyo saṃvaratī satīmā;

Anugate [V 0.0111][T 0.0129] manaso uppilāve,

Asesamete pajahāsi buddho”ti. paṭhamaṃ;

But one who knows these thoughts of the mind, being ardent and mindful, restrains them. These followers of the mind, its agitations—the enlightened one has abandoned them completely.

2. Uddhatasuttaṃ

2. The Uddhata Sutta

32. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā kusinārāyaṃ viharati upavattane mallānaṃ sālavane. Tena kho pana samayena sambahulā bhikkhū bhagavato avidūre araññakuṭikāyaṃ viharanti uddhatā unnaḷā capalā mukharā vikiṇṇavācā muṭṭhassatino asampajānā asamāhitā vibbhantacittā pākatindriyā.

32. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Kusinārā, in the Mallas’ Upavattana sāl grove. Now at that time, many bhikkhus were dwelling not far from the Blessed One in a forest hut—restless, conceited, fickle, harsh-spoken, of scattered speech, unmindful, lacking clear comprehension, uncomposed, with wandering minds, and with faculties unrestrained.

Addasā kho bhagavā te sambahule [P 0.0038] bhikkhū avidūre araññakuṭikāyaṃ viharante uddhate unnaḷe capale mukhare vikiṇṇavāce muṭṭhassatino asampajāne asamāhite vibbhantacitte pākatindriye.

The Blessed One saw those many bhikkhus dwelling not far away in a forest hut—restless, conceited, fickle, harsh-spoken, of scattered speech, unmindful, lacking clear comprehension, uncomposed, with wandering minds, and with faculties unrestrained.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, having understood the significance of this, on that occasion the Blessed One uttered this inspired utterance:

“Arakkhitena kāyena cittena (nettiyaṃ), micchādiṭṭhihatena micchādiṭṭhigatena (bahūsu) ca;

Thinamiddhā thīnamiddhā (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.) bhibhūtena, vasaṃ mārassa gacchati.

With body unguarded, and assailed by wrong view, overcome by sloth and torpor, one goes under Māra’s control.

“Tasmā rakkhitacittassa, sammāsaṅkappagocaro;

Sammādiṭṭhipurekkhāro, ñatvāna udayabbayaṃ;

Thīnamiddhābhibhū bhikkhu, sabbā duggatiyo jahe”ti. dutiyaṃ;

Therefore, with mind well-guarded, with right thought as one's domain, with right view as the forerunner, knowing arising and passing away, a bhikkhu, having overcome sloth and torpor, would abandon all bad destinations.

3. Gopālakasuttaṃ

3. The Cowherd Sutta

33. Evaṃ [M 0.0122] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā kosalesu cārikaṃ carati mahatā bhikkhusaṅghena saddhiṃ. Atha kho bhagavā maggā okkamma yena aññataraṃ rukkhamūlaṃ tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā paññatte āsane nisīdi.
