33. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was traveling on a walking tour among the Kosalans with a great Saṅgha of bhikkhus. Then the Blessed One, turning aside from the road, approached the root of a certain tree; having approached, he sat down on the prepared seat.

Atha kho aññataro gopālako yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami[T 0.0130]; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā [V 0.0112] ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho taṃ gopālakaṃ bhagavā dhammiyā kathāya sandassesi samādapesi samādāpesi (?) samuttejesi sampahaṃsesi.

Then a certain cowherd approached the Blessed One; having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. As he was sitting to one side, the Blessed One instructed, inspired, roused, and gladdened him with a Dhamma talk.

Atha kho so gopālako bhagavatā dhammiyā kathāya sandassito samādapito samādipito (?) samuttejito sampahaṃsito bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “adhivāsetu me, bhante, bhagavā svātanāya bhattaṃ saddhiṃ bhikkhusaṅghenā”ti. Adhivāsesi bhagavā tuṇhībhāvena. Atha kho so gopālako bhagavato adhivāsanaṃ viditvā uṭṭhāyāsanā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā pakkāmi.

Then that cowherd, having been instructed, inspired, roused, and gladdened by the Blessed One with a Dhamma talk, said to him: “Venerable sir, may the Blessed One, together with the Saṅgha of bhikkhus, accept tomorrow’s meal from me.” The Blessed One consented by silence. Then that cowherd, knowing the Blessed One’s consent, rose from his seat, paid homage to the Blessed One, circumambulated him to the right, and departed.

Atha kho so gopālako tassā rattiyā accayena sake nivesane pahūtaṃ appodakapāyasaṃ appodakapāyāsaṃ (sabbattha) paṭiyādāpetvā navañca sappiṃ bhagavato kālaṃ ārocesi – “kālo, bhante, niṭṭhitaṃ bhatta”nti. Atha kho bhagavā pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya saddhiṃ bhikkhusaṅghena yena tassa gopālakassa nivesanaṃ tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā paññatte āsane nisīdi. Atha kho so gopālako buddhappamukhaṃ bhikkhusaṅghaṃ appodakapāyasena appodakapāyāsena ca (syā. pī.) navena ca sappinā sahatthā santappesi sampavāresi. Atha kho so gopālako bhagavantaṃ bhuttāviṃ onītapattapāṇiṃ aññataraṃ nīcaṃ [P 0.0039] āsanaṃ gahetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho taṃ gopālakaṃ bhagavā dhammiyā kathāya sandassetvā samādapetvā samuttejetvā sampahaṃsetvā uṭṭhāyāsanā pakkāmi. Atha kho acirapakkantassa bhagavato taṃ gopālakaṃ aññataro [T 0.0131] puriso sīmantarikāya jīvitā voropesi.

Then, when the night had passed, that cowherd had a large quantity of thick milk-rice with little water prepared in his own dwelling, along with fresh ghee, and he had the time announced to the Blessed One: “It is time, venerable sir, the meal is ready.” Then the Blessed One, having dressed in the morning, took his bowl and robe, and went together with the Saṅgha of bhikkhus to the cowherd’s dwelling. Having arrived, he sat down on the prepared seat. Then that cowherd personally served and satisfied the Saṅgha of bhikkhus headed by the Buddha with the thick milk-rice with little water and fresh ghee. Then, when the Blessed One had finished eating and had withdrawn his hand from the bowl, the cowherd took a low seat and sat to one side. The Blessed One instructed, inspired, roused, and gladdened the cowherd, who was sitting to one side, with a Dhamma talk, then rose from his seat and departed. Soon after the Blessed One had left, a certain man deprived that cowherd of life between the village boundaries.

Atha [M 0.0123] kho sambahulā bhikkhū yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu. Ekamantaṃ nisinnā kho te bhikkhū bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ – “yena, bhante, gopālakena ajja buddhappamukho bhikkhusaṅgho appodakapāyasena navena ca sappinā sahatthā santappito sampavārito so kira, bhante, gopālako aññatarena purisena sīmantarikāya jīvitā voropito”ti.

Then many bhikkhus approached the Blessed One; having approached, they paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Sitting to one side, those bhikkhus said to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, the cowherd by whom the Saṅgha of bhikkhus, headed by the Buddha, was today personally served and satisfied with thick milk-rice with little water and fresh ghee—that cowherd, venerable sir, has reportedly been deprived of life by a certain man between the village boundaries.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, having understood the significance of this, on that occasion the Blessed One uttered this inspired utterance:

“Diso disaṃ yaṃ taṃ kayirā, verī vā pana verinaṃ;

Micchāpaṇihitaṃ cittaṃ, pāpiyo naṃ tato kare”ti. tatiyaṃ;

Whatever harm a foe may do to a foe, or an enemy to an enemy, a wrongly directed mind can do one far worse.

4. Yakkhapahārasuttaṃ

4. The Discourse on the Yakkha's Strike

34. Evaṃ [V 0.0113] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā rājagahe viharati veḷuvane kalandakanivāpe. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā ca sāriputto āyasmā ca mahāmoggallāno kapotakandarāyaṃ viharanti. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā sāriputto juṇhāya rattiyā navoropitehi kesehi abbhokāse nisinno hoti aññataraṃ samādhiṃ samāpajjitvā.

34. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Rājagaha, in the Bamboo Grove, the Squirrels’ Feeding Place. Now at that time, Venerable Sāriputta and Venerable Mahāmoggallāna were dwelling at Kapotakandara. And at that time, Venerable Sāriputta, with freshly shaven hair, was seated in the open during a moonlit night, having entered upon a certain concentration.

Tena kho pana samayena dve yakkhā sahāyakā uttarāya disāya dakkhiṇaṃ disaṃ gacchanti kenacideva karaṇīyena. Addasaṃsu kho te yakkhā āyasmantaṃ sāriputtaṃ juṇhāya rattiyā navoropitehi kesehi abbhokāse nisinnaṃ. Disvāna eko yakkho dutiyaṃ yakkhaṃ etadavoca [T 0.0132] – “paṭibhāti maṃ, samma, imassa samaṇassa sīse pahāraṃ dātu”nti. Evaṃ vutte, so yakkho taṃ yakkhaṃ etadavoca – “alaṃ, samma, mā samaṇaṃ āsādesi. Uḷāro so, samma, samaṇo mahiddhiko mahānubhāvo”ti.

Now at that time, two yakkhas, companions, were traveling from the northern direction to the southern direction for some business or other. They saw the Venerable Sāriputta seated in the open during a moonlit night, with his hair freshly shaven. Having seen him, one yakkha said to the other yakkha: “It occurs to me, friend, to strike this ascetic on the head.” When this was said, that yakkha said to the other yakkha: “Enough, friend, do not attack the ascetic. He is an eminent ascetic, friend, of great psychic power and great influence.”

Dutiyampi kho so yakkho taṃ yakkhaṃ etadavoca – “paṭibhāti maṃ, samma, imassa samaṇassa [P 0.0040] sīse pahāraṃ dātu”nti. Dutiyampi kho so yakkho taṃ yakkhaṃ etadavoca – “alaṃ, samma, mā samaṇaṃ āsādesi. Uḷāro so, samma, samaṇo mahiddhiko mahānubhāvo”ti. Tatiyampi kho so yakkho taṃ yakkhaṃ etadavoca – “paṭibhāti maṃ, samma, imassa samaṇassa sīse pahāraṃ [M 0.0124] dātu”nti. Tatiyampi kho so yakkho taṃ yakkhaṃ etadavoca – “alaṃ, samma, mā samaṇaṃ āsādesi. Uḷāro so, samma, samaṇo mahiddhiko mahānubhāvo”ti.

A second time, that yakkha said to the other yakkha: “Friend, it occurs to me to strike this ascetic on the head.” A second time, the other yakkha said to that yakkha: “Enough, friend, do not attack the ascetic. Friend, that ascetic is eminent, of great psychic power and great influence.” A third time, that yakkha said to the other yakkha: “Friend, it occurs to me to strike this ascetic on the head.” A third time, the other yakkha said to that yakkha: “Enough, friend, do not attack the ascetic. Friend, that ascetic is eminent, of great psychic power and great influence.”

Atha kho so yakkho taṃ yakkhaṃ anādiyitvā āyasmato sāriputtattherassa sīse pahāraṃ adāsi. Tāva mahā pahāro ahosi, api tena pahārena sattaratanaṃ vā aḍḍhaṭṭhamaratanaṃ vā nāgaṃ osādeyya, mahantaṃ vā pabbatakūṭaṃ padāleyya. Atha ca pana so yakkho ‘ḍayhāmi ḍayhāmī’ti vatvā tattheva mahānirayaṃ apatāsi avatthāsi (ka. sī.).

Then that yakkha, disregarding the other yakkha, struck the Venerable Sāriputta on the head. So mighty was the blow that it could have made an elephant of seven or seven and a half cubits sink into the ground, or split a great mountain peak. But then that yakkha, crying, “I am burning, I am burning!” fell right there into the great hell.

Addasā kho āyasmā mahāmoggallāno dibbena cakkhunā visuddhena atikkantamānusakena tena yakkhena āyasmato sāriputtattherassa sīse pahāraṃ dīyamānaṃ. Disvā yena āyasmā sāriputto tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā āyasmantaṃ sāriputtaṃ etadavoca – “kacci [V 0.0114] te, āvuso, khamanīyaṃ, kacci yāpanīyaṃ, kacci na kiñci dukkha”nti[T 0.0133]? “Khamanīyaṃ me, āvuso moggallāna, yāpanīyaṃ me, āvuso moggallāna; api ca me sīsaṃ thokaṃ dukkha”nti.

The Venerable Mahāmoggallāna saw with the divine eye, purified and surpassing the human, that yakkha striking the Venerable Sāriputta on the head. Having seen this, he approached the Venerable Sāriputta and said to him: “Friend, are you enduring? Are you managing? Do you have no discomfort?” “I am enduring, friend Moggallāna, I am managing, friend Moggallāna; but there is a slight pain in my head.”

“Acchariyaṃ, āvuso sāriputta, abbhutaṃ, āvuso sāriputta! Yāva yaṃ tvaṃ (sī. ka.), yaṃ (syā.) mahiddhiko āyasmā sāriputto mahānubhāvo! Idha te, āvuso sāriputta, aññataro yakkho sīse pahāraṃ adāsi. Tāva mahā pahāro ahosi, api tena pahārena sattaratanaṃ vā aḍḍhaṭṭhamaratanaṃ vā nāgaṃ osādeyya, mahantaṃ vā pabbatakūṭaṃ padāleyya, atha ca panāyasmā sāriputto evamāha – ‘khamanīyaṃ me, āvuso moggallāna, yāpanīyaṃ me, āvuso moggallāna; api ca me sīsaṃ thokaṃ dukkha’”nti.

“Wonderful, friend Sāriputta, marvelous, friend Sāriputta! So great in psychic power is the Venerable Sāriputta, so great his influence! Here, friend Sāriputta, a certain yakkha struck you on the head. So great was the blow that with it he might have made an elephant of seven or seven and a half cubits sink into the ground, or split a great mountain peak, yet the Venerable Sāriputta says: ‘I am enduring, friend Moggallāna, I am managing, friend Moggallāna; but there is a slight pain in my head.’”

“Acchariyaṃ, āvuso moggallāna, abbhutaṃ, āvuso moggallāna! Yāva yaṃ (syā.) mahiddhiko āyasmā mahāmoggallāno mahānubhāvo yatra hi nāma yakkhampi passissati! Mayaṃ panetarahi paṃsupisācakampi na passāmā”ti.

“Wonderful, friend Moggallāna, marvelous, friend Moggallāna! So great in psychic power is the Venerable Mahāmoggallāna, so great his influence, that he can even see a yakkha! We, however, do not see even a dust-goblin nowadays.”

Assosi kho bhagavā dibbāya sotadhātuyā visuddhāya atikkantamānusikāya tesaṃ ubhinnaṃ mahānāgānaṃ imaṃ evarūpaṃ kathāsallāpaṃ.

The Blessed One, with his divine ear element—purified, surpassing the human—heard this conversation between those two great nāgas.

Atha [M 0.0125] kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, having understood the significance of this, on that occasion the Blessed One uttered this solemn utterance:

“Yassa [P 0.0041] selūpamaṃ cittaṃ, ṭhitaṃ nānupakampati;

Virattaṃ rajanīyesu, kopaneyye na kuppati;

Yassevaṃ bhāvitaṃ cittaṃ, kuto taṃ dukkhamessatī”ti. catutthaṃ;

“Whose mind is like a rock, firm and unshaken; dispassionate towards the alluring, unangered by the offensive; for one whose mind is thus developed, from where can suffering come?”

5. Nāgasuttaṃ

5. The Discourse on the Great Being

35. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā kosambiyaṃ viharati ghositārāme[T 0.0134]. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā ākiṇṇo viharati bhikkhūhi bhikkhūnīhi upāsakehi upāsikāhi rājūhi rājamahāmattehi titthiyehi titthiyasāvakehi. Ākiṇṇo dukkhaṃ na phāsu viharati. Atha kho bhagavato etadahosi – “ahaṃ kho etarahi ākiṇṇo viharāmi bhikkhūhi [V 0.0115] bhikkhūnīhi upāsakehi upāsikāhi rājūhi rājamahāmattehi titthiyehi titthiyasāvakehi. Ākiṇṇo dukkhaṃ na phāsu viharāmi. Yaṃnūnāhaṃ eko gaṇasmā vūpakaṭṭho vihareyya”nti.

35. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Kosambī in Ghosita’s Park. At that time the Blessed One was dwelling amidst a crowd of monks, nuns, male lay followers, female lay followers, kings, royal ministers, sectarians, and their disciples. Dwelling amidst a crowd, he lived with difficulty and not at ease. Then this thought occurred to the Blessed One: “I am now dwelling amidst a crowd of monks, nuns, male lay followers, female lay followers, kings, royal ministers, sectarians, and their disciples. Dwelling amidst a crowd, I live with difficulty and not at ease. What if I were to dwell alone, withdrawn from the group?”

Atha kho bhagavā pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya kosambiṃ piṇḍāya pāvisi. Kosambiyaṃ piṇḍāya caritvā pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkanto sāmaṃ senāsanaṃ saṃsāmetvā pattacīvaramādāya anāmantetvā upaṭṭhākaṃ anapaloketvā bhikkhusaṅghaṃ eko adutiyo yena pālileyyakaṃ tena cārikaṃ pakkāmi. Anupubbena cārikaṃ caramāno yena pālileyyakaṃ tadavasari. Tatra sudaṃ bhagavā pālileyyake viharati rakkhitavanasaṇḍe bhaddasālamūle.

Then, in the morning, the Blessed One dressed, took his bowl and robe, and entered Kosambī for alms. Having gone for alms in Kosambī, after the meal, upon returning from the alms-round, he set his own lodging in order, took his bowl and robe, and without informing his attendant or taking leave of the Saṅgha of monks, he departed alone, without a companion, on a journey toward Pāḷileyyaka. Traveling stage by stage, he arrived at Pāḷileyyaka. There the Blessed One dwelled in Pāḷileyyaka, in the Rakkhita forest grove, at the foot of a beautiful sal tree.

Aññataropi kho hatthināgo ākiṇṇo viharati hatthīhi hatthinīhi hatthikalabhehi hatthicchāpehi. Chinnaggāni ceva tiṇāni khādati, obhaggobhaggañcassa sākhābhaṅgaṃ khādanti, āvilāni ca pānīyāni pivati, ogāhā cassa uttiṇṇassa hatthiniyo kāyaṃ upanighaṃsantiyo gacchanti. Ākiṇṇo dukkhaṃ na phāsu viharati. Atha kho tassa hatthināgassa etadahosi – “ahaṃ kho etarahi ākiṇṇo viharāmi [T 0.0135] hatthīhi hatthinīhi hatthikalabhehi hatthicchāpehi, chinnaggāni ceva tiṇāni khādāmi, obhaggobhaggañca me sākhābhaṅgaṃ khādanti, āvilāni ca pānīyāni pivāmi, ogāhā ca me uttiṇṇassa [M 0.0126] hatthiniyo kāyaṃ upanighaṃsantiyo gacchanti, ākiṇṇo dukkhaṃ na phāsu viharāmi. Yaṃnūnāhaṃ eko gaṇasmā vūpakaṭṭho vihareyya”nti.

A certain bull elephant was also dwelling amidst a crowd of young elephants, she-elephants, adolescent elephants, and elephant calves. He had to eat grass with its tips already cut, and they would eat the branches he had broken and dropped. He had to drink turbid water, and when he emerged from the watering hole, the she-elephants would move along, pressing against his body. Being crowded, he dwelled with difficulty and not at ease. Then this thought occurred to that bull elephant: “Now I am dwelling amidst a crowd of young elephants, she-elephants, adolescent elephants, and elephant calves. I have to eat grass with its tips already cut, and they eat the branches that I break and drop. I have to drink turbid water, and when I emerge from the watering hole, the she-elephants move along, pressing against my body. Being crowded, I dwell with difficulty and not at ease. What if I were to dwell alone, withdrawn from the herd?”

Atha kho so hatthināgo yūthā apakkamma yena pālileyyakaṃ rakkhitavanasaṇḍo [P 0.0042] bhaddasālamūlaṃ yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami. Tatra sudaṃ upasaṅkamitvā tatra sudaṃ (syā. pī. ka.) so hatthināgo yasmiṃ padese bhagavā viharati taṃ padesaṃ appaharitañca karoti, soṇḍāya (bahūsu) appaharitaṃ karoti, soṇḍāya ca appaharitañca karoti, soṇḍāya (bahūsu) bhagavato pānīyaṃ paribhojanīyaṃ upaṭṭhāpeti upaṭṭhapeti (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.).

Then that bull elephant, having departed from the herd, approached the place where the Blessed One was, at Pāḷileyyaka, in the Rakkhita forest grove, at the root of the beautiful sal tree. There that bull elephant kept the place where the Blessed One was dwelling free from grass and young plants, and with his trunk he would set out drinking water and water for use for the Blessed One.

Atha kho bhagavato rahogatassa paṭisallīnassa evaṃ cetaso parivitakko udapādi – “ahaṃ kho pubbe ākiṇṇo vihāsiṃ bhikkhūhi bhikkhūnīhi upāsakehi upāsikāhi rājūhi rājamahāmattehi titthiyehi titthiyasāvakehi, ākiṇṇo dukkhaṃ na phāsu vihāsiṃ. Somhi etarahi anākiṇṇo viharāmi bhikkhūhi bhikkhunīhi upāsakehi upāsikāhi rājūhi rājamahāmattehi titthiyehi titthiyasāvakehi, anākiṇṇo sukhaṃ phāsu viharāmī”ti.

Then, while the Blessed One was in solitude and seclusion, this reflection arose in his mind: “Formerly, I dwelled crowded by monks, nuns, male lay followers, female lay followers, kings, royal ministers, sectarians, and their disciples. Being crowded, I dwelled with difficulty and not at ease. But now I am dwelling uncrowded by monks, nuns, male lay followers, female lay followers, kings, royal ministers, sectarians, and their disciples. Uncrowded, I dwell happily and at ease.”

Tassapi [V 0.0116] kho hatthināgassa evaṃ cetaso parivitakko udapādi – “ahaṃ kho pubbe ākiṇṇo vihāsiṃ hatthīhi hatthinīhi hatthikalabhehi hatthicchāpehi, chinnaggāni ceva tiṇāni khādiṃ, obhaggobhaggañca me sākhābhaṅgaṃ khādiṃsu, āvilāni ca pānīyāni apāyiṃ, ogāhā [T 0.0136] ca me uttiṇṇassa hatthiniyo kāyaṃ upanighaṃsantiyo agamaṃsu, ākiṇṇo dukkhaṃ na phāsu vihāsiṃ. Somhi etarahi anākiṇṇo viharāmi hatthīhi hatthinīhi hatthikalabhehi hatthicchāpehi, acchinnaggāni ceva tiṇāni khādāmi, obhaggobhaggañca me sākhābhaṅgaṃ na khādanti, anāvilāni ca pānīyāni pivāmi, ogāhā ca me uttiṇṇassa hatthiniyo na kāyaṃ upanighaṃsantiyo gacchanti, anākiṇṇo sukhaṃ phāsu viharāmī”ti.

To that bull elephant, too, this reflection arose: “Formerly, I dwelled crowded by young elephants, she-elephants, adolescent elephants, and elephant calves. I had to eat grass with its tips already cut, and they would eat the branches that I broke and dropped. I had to drink turbid water, and when I emerged from the watering hole, the she-elephants would move along, pressing against my body. Being crowded, I dwelled with difficulty and not at ease. But now I am dwelling uncrowded by young elephants, she-elephants, adolescent elephants, and elephant calves. I eat grass with its tips intact, and they do not eat the branches that I break and drop. I drink clear water, and when I emerge from the watering hole, the she-elephants do not move along, pressing against my body. Uncrowded, I dwell happily and at ease.”

Atha kho bhagavā attano ca pavivekaṃ viditvā tassa ca hatthināgassa cetasā cetoparivitakkamaññāya tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood his own seclusion and having known with his mind the mental reflection of that bull elephant, at that time uttered this inspired utterance:

“Etaṃ [M 0.0127] evaṃ (ka.) nāgassa nāgena, īsādantassa hatthino;

Sameti cittaṃ cittena, yadeko ramatī mano”ti. pañcamaṃ;

“This mind of the Nāga aligns with the mind of the Nāga, the bull elephant with pole-like tusks, for the mind delights in being alone.”

6. Piṇḍolasuttaṃ

6. The Discourse on Piṇḍola

36. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā piṇḍolabhāradvājo bhagavato avidūre nisinno hoti pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya āraññiko piṇḍapātiko paṃsukūliko tecīvariko appiccho santuṭṭho pavivitto asaṃsaṭṭho āraddhavīriyo āraddhaviriyo (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.) dhutavādo adhicittamanuyutto.

36. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. Now at that time, the Venerable Piṇḍola Bhāradvāja was seated not far from the Blessed One, having folded his legs crosswise and set his body erect; being a forest-dweller, an alms-food-eater, a refuse-rag-wearer, a triple-robe-wearer, of few wishes, easily contented, secluded, unassociated, with aroused energy, a speaker on the ascetic practices, and constantly engaged in the higher mind.

Addasā kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ piṇḍolabhāradvājaṃ [P 0.0043] avidūre nisinnaṃ pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya āraññikaṃ piṇḍapātikaṃ paṃsukūlikaṃ tecīvarikaṃ appicchaṃ santuṭṭhaṃ pavivittaṃ asaṃsaṭṭhaṃ āraddhavīriyaṃ dhutavādaṃ adhicittamanuyuttaṃ[T 0.0137].

The Blessed One indeed saw the Venerable Piṇḍola Bhāradvāja seated not far away, having folded his legs crosswise and set his body erect; being a forest-dweller, an alms-food-eater, a refuse-rag-wearer, a triple-robe-wearer, of few wishes, easily contented, secluded, unassociated, with aroused energy, a speaker on the ascetic practices, and constantly engaged in the higher mind.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood the meaning of this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Anūpavādo [V 0.0117] anūpaghāto anupavādo anupaghāto (syā. pī. ka.), pātimokkhe ca saṃvaro;

Mattaññutā ca bhattasmiṃ, pantañca sayanāsanaṃ;

Adhicitte ca āyogo, etaṃ buddhāna sāsana”nti. chaṭṭhaṃ;

“Not blaming, not harming, restraint in the Pātimokkha, moderation in food, a secluded dwelling, and exertion in the higher mind: this is the teaching of the Buddhas.”

7. Sāriputtasuttaṃ

7. The Discourse on Sāriputta

37. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā sāriputto bhagavato avidūre nisinno hoti pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya appiccho santuṭṭho pavivitto asaṃsaṭṭho āraddhavīriyo adhicittamanuyutto.

37. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. Now at that time, the Venerable Sāriputta was seated not far from the Blessed One, having folded his legs crosswise and set his body erect; being of few wishes, easily contented, secluded, unassociated, with aroused energy, and constantly engaged in the higher mind.

Addasā [M 0.0128] kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ sāriputtaṃ avidūre nisinnaṃ pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya appicchaṃ santuṭṭhaṃ pavivittaṃ asaṃsaṭṭhaṃ āraddhavīriyaṃ adhicittamanuyuttaṃ.

The Blessed One indeed saw the Venerable Sāriputta seated not far away, having folded his legs crosswise and set his body erect; being of few wishes, easily contented, secluded, unassociated, with aroused energy, and constantly engaged in the higher mind.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood the meaning of this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Adhicetaso appamajjato,

Munino monapathesu sikkhato;

Sokā na bhavanti tādino,

Upasantassa sadā satīmato”ti. sattamaṃ;

“For the sage who is endowed with a higher mind, who is heedful, and who trains in the paths of sagehood—for such a one, who is tranquil and ever mindful, sorrows do not arise.”

8. Sundarīsuttaṃ

8. The Discourse on Sundarī

38. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā sakkato hoti garukato mānito pūjito apacito lābhī cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ[T 0.0138]. Bhikkhusaṅghopi sakkato hoti garukato mānito pūjito apacito lābhī cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ. Aññatitthiyā pana paribbājakā asakkatā honti agarukatā amānitā apūjitā anapacitā na lābhino cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ.

38. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. At that time the Blessed One was honored, respected, esteemed, venerated, revered, and was a recipient of robes, alms-food, lodgings, and medicines and requisites for the sick. The community of monks was also honored, respected, esteemed, venerated, revered, and was a recipient of robes, alms-food, lodgings, and medicines and requisites for the sick. But the wandering ascetics of other sects were not honored, not respected, not esteemed, not venerated, not revered, and were not recipients of robes, alms-food, lodgings, or medicines and requisites for the sick.

Atha kho te aññatitthiyā paribbājakā bhagavato sakkāraṃ asahamānā bhikkhusaṅghassa ca [V 0.0118] yena sundarī paribbājikā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā [P 0.0044] sundariṃ paribbājikaṃ etadavocuṃ – “ussahasi tvaṃ, bhagini, ñātīnaṃ atthaṃ kātu”nti? “Kyāhaṃ, ayyā, karomi? Kiṃ mayā na sakkā kiṃ mayā sakkā (syā. pī.) kātuṃ? Jīvitampi me pariccattaṃ ñātīnaṃ atthāyā”ti.

Then those wandering ascetics of other sects, unable to endure the honor shown to the Blessed One and the community of monks, approached the female wanderer Sundarī. Having approached, they said to the female wanderer Sundarī: “Sister, are you able to act for the benefit of your relatives?” “What shall I do, venerable sirs? What is it that cannot be done by me? Even my life is given up for the benefit of my relatives.”

“Tena hi, bhagini, abhikkhaṇaṃ jetavanaṃ gacchāhī”ti. “Evaṃ, ayyā”ti kho sundarī paribbājikā tesaṃ aññatitthiyānaṃ paribbājakānaṃ paṭissutvā abhikkhaṇaṃ jetavanaṃ agamāsi.

“Then, sister, go frequently to Jetavana.” “Very well, venerable sirs,” replied Sundarī the female wanderer, and having assented to those wandering ascetics of other sects, she went frequently to Jetavana.

Yadā [M 0.0129] te aññiṃsu aññatitthiyā paribbājakā – “vodiṭṭhā kho sundarī paribbājikā bahujanena abhikkhaṇaṃ jetavanaṃ gacchatī”ti gacchatīti (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.). Atha naṃ jīvitā voropetvā tattheva jetavanassa parikhākūpe nikkhipitvā nikhanitvā (sī. syā. pī.) yena rājā pasenadi kosalo tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā rājānaṃ pasenadiṃ kosalaṃ etadavocuṃ – “yā sā, mahārāja, sundarī paribbājikā; sā no na dissatī”ti. “Kattha pana tumhe āsaṅkathā”ti[T 0.0139]? “Jetavane, mahārājā”ti. “Tena hi jetavanaṃ vicinathā”ti.

When those wanderers of other sects knew—“The female wanderer Sundarī is seen by many people frequently going to Jetavana”—they deprived her of life, and having placed her in a moat-pit of Jetavana right there, they approached King Pasenadi of Kosala. Having approached, they said to King Pasenadi of Kosala: “Great king, that female wanderer Sundarī of ours is not to be seen.” “But where do you suspect her to be?” “In Jetavana, great king.” “Then search Jetavana.”

Atha kho te aññatitthiyā paribbājakā jetavanaṃ vicinitvā yathānikkhittaṃ parikhākūpā uddharitvā mañcakaṃ āropetvā sāvatthiṃ pavesetvā rathiyāya rathiyaṃ siṅghāṭakena siṅghāṭakaṃ upasaṅkamitvā manusse ujjhāpesuṃ –

Then those wandering ascetics of other sects, having searched Jetavana, lifted her from the moat-pit just as she had been placed, put her on a litter, brought her into Sāvatthī, and going from street to street, from crossroad to crossroad, they caused the people to be indignant, saying:

“Passathāyyā samaṇānaṃ sakyaputtiyānaṃ kammaṃ! Alajjino ime samaṇā sakyaputtiyā dussīlā pāpadhammā musāvādino abrahmacārino. Ime hi nāma dhammacārino samacārino brahmacārino saccavādino sīlavanto kalyāṇadhammā paṭijānissanti! Natthi imesaṃ sāmaññaṃ, natthi imesaṃ brahmaññaṃ. Naṭṭhaṃ imesaṃ sāmaññaṃ, naṭṭhaṃ imesaṃ brahmaññaṃ. Kuto imesaṃ sāmaññaṃ, kuto imesaṃ brahmaññaṃ? Apagatā ime sāmaññā, apagatā ime brahmaññā. Kathañhi nāma puriso purisakiccaṃ karitvā itthiṃ jīvitā voropessatī”ti!

“Look, sirs, at the deed of the ascetics who are sons of the Sakyans! Shameless are these ascetics, these sons of the Sakyans—immoral, of evil nature, speakers of falsehood, unchaste. These very ones indeed claim to be practicing the Dhamma, behaving righteously, living the holy life, speaking the truth, virtuous, and of good character! There is no asceticism in them, no state of holiness in them. Lost is their asceticism, lost is their state of holiness. Whence is their asceticism? Whence is their state of holiness? They have departed from asceticism; they have departed from the state of holiness. For how could a man, having done a man's deed, deprive a woman of life?”

Tena kho pana samayena sāvatthiyaṃ manussā bhikkhū disvā asabbhāhi pharusāhi vācāhi akkosanti paribhāsanti rosanti vihesanti –

Now at that time in Sāvatthī, the people, upon seeing the monks, would abuse, scold, vex, and harass them with uncivilized and harsh words:

“Alajjino ime samaṇā sakyaputtiyā dussīlā pāpadhammā musāvādino abrahmacārino[V 0.0119]. Ime hi nāma dhammacārino samacārino brahmacārino saccavādino sīlavanto kalyāṇadhammā paṭijānissanti! Natthi imesaṃ sāmaññaṃ, natthi imesaṃ brahmaññaṃ. Naṭṭhaṃ imesaṃ sāmaññaṃ, naṭṭhaṃ imesaṃ brahmaññaṃ. Kuto imesaṃ sāmaññaṃ, kuto imesaṃ brahmaññaṃ? Apagatā ime sāmaññā, apagatā ime [T 0.0140] brahmaññā. Kathañhi nāma puriso purisakiccaṃ karitvā itthiṃ jīvitā voropessatī”ti!

“These ascetics, sons of the Sakyans, are shameless, immoral, of evil nature, speakers of falsehood, and unchaste. These very ones indeed claim to be practicing the Dhamma, behaving righteously, living the holy life, speaking the truth, virtuous, and of good character! There is no asceticism in them, no state of holiness in them. Lost is their asceticism, lost is their state of holiness. Whence is their asceticism? Whence is their state of holiness? They have departed from asceticism; they have departed from the state of holiness. For how could a man, having done a man's deed, deprive a woman of life?”

Atha kho sambahulā bhikkhū pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya sāvatthiṃ piṇḍāya pāvisiṃsu. Sāvatthiyaṃ [P 0.0045] piṇḍāya caritvā pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkantā [M 0.0130] yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu. Ekamantaṃ nisinnā kho te bhikkhū bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ –

Then, many monks, having dressed in the morning time and taking their bowls and robes, entered Sāvatthī for alms-food. Having wandered for alms-food in Sāvatthī and returning from their alms-round after the meal, they approached the Blessed One. Having approached, they paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, those monks said this to the Blessed One:

“Etarahi, bhante, sāvatthiyaṃ manussā bhikkhū disvā asabbhāhi pharusāhi vācāhi akkosanti paribhāsanti rosanti vihesanti – ‘alajjino ime samaṇā sakyaputtiyā dussīlā pāpadhammā musāvādino abrahmacārino. Ime hi nāma dhammacārino samacārino brahmacārino saccavādino sīlavanto kalyāṇadhammā paṭijānissanti. Natthi imesaṃ sāmaññaṃ, natthi imesaṃ brahmaññaṃ. Naṭṭhaṃ imesaṃ sāmaññaṃ, naṭṭhaṃ imesaṃ brahmaññaṃ. Kuto imesaṃ sāmaññaṃ, kuto imesaṃ brahmaññaṃ? Apagatā ime sāmaññā, apagatā ime brahmaññā. Kathañhi nāma puriso purisakiccaṃ karitvā itthiṃ jīvitā voropessatī’”ti!

“Venerable Sir, now in Sāvatthī, people, upon seeing the monks, abuse, scold, vex, and harass them with uncivil and harsh words, saying: ‘These ascetics, sons of the Sakyans, are shameless, immoral, of evil nature, speakers of falsehood, and unchaste. These very ones indeed claim to be practicing the Dhamma, behaving righteously, living the holy life, speaking the truth, virtuous, and of good character! There is no asceticism in them, no state of holiness in them. Lost is their asceticism, lost is their state of holiness. Whence is their asceticism? Whence is their state of holiness? They have departed from asceticism; they have departed from the state of holiness. For how could a man, having done a man's deed, deprive a woman of life?’”

“Neso, bhikkhave, saddo ciraṃ bhavissati sattāhameva bhavissati. Sattāhassa accayena antaradhāyissati. Tena hi, bhikkhave, ye manussā bhikkhū disvā asabbhāhi pharusāhi vācāhi akkosanti paribhāsanti rosanti vihesanti, te tumhe imāya gāthāya paṭicodetha –

“Monks, this sound will not last long; it will last for only seven days. After the passing of seven days, it will disappear. Therefore, monks, those people who, upon seeing the monks, abuse, scold, vex, and harass them with uncivil and harsh words—you should admonish them in return with this verse:

“‘Abhūtavādī [T 0.0141] nirayaṃ upeti,

Yo vāpi yo cāpi (sī. pī. ka.) katvā na karomi cāha;

Ubhopi te pecca samā bhavanti,

Nihīnakammā manujā paratthā’”ti.

“‘One who speaks falsehood goes to hell; and also he who, having done a deed, says, ‘I did not do it.’ Both those persons with base deeds, having passed from here to the next world, become equal.’”

Atha kho te bhikkhū bhagavato santike imaṃ gāthaṃ pariyāpuṇitvā ye manussā bhikkhū disvā [V 0.0120] asabbhāhi pharusāhi vācāhi akkosanti paribhāsanti rosanti vihesanti te imāya gāthāya paṭicodenti –

Then those monks, having thoroughly learned this verse in the presence of the Blessed One, admonished in return with this verse those people who, upon seeing the monks, abused, reviled, vexed, and harassed them with uncivil and harsh words:

“Abhūtavādī nirayaṃ upeti,

Yo vāpi katvā na karomicāha;

Ubhopi te pecca samā bhavanti,

Nihīnakammā manujā paratthā”ti.

“One who speaks falsehood goes to hell; and also he who, having done an evil deed, says, ‘I did not do it.’ Both those persons with base deeds, having passed from here to the next world, become equal.”

Manussānaṃ [M 0.0131] etadahosi – “akārakā ime samaṇā sakyaputtiyā. Nayimehi kataṃ. Sapantime samaṇā sakyaputtiyā”ti. Neva so saddo ciraṃ ahosi. Sattāhameva ahosi. Sattāhassa accayena antaradhāyi.

This thought occurred to the people: ‘These recluses, sons of the Sakyan, are not the perpetrators. It was not done by them. These recluses, sons of the Sakyan, are taking an oath.’ That sound did not last long. It lasted for only seven days. After the passing of seven days, it disappeared.

Atha kho sambahulā bhikkhū yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu. Ekamantaṃ nisinnā kho te bhikkhū bhagavato etadavocuṃ –

Then, many monks approached the Blessed One. Having approached, they paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, those monks said this to the Blessed One:

“Acchariyaṃ, bhante, abbhutaṃ, bhante! Yāva subhāsitaṃ cidaṃ bhante bhagavatā – ‘neso, bhikkhave, saddo ciraṃ bhavissati. Sattāhameva bhavissati. Sattāhassa accayena [T 0.0142] antaradhāyissatī’ti. Antarahito so, bhante, saddo”ti.

“Wonderful, Venerable Sir! Marvelous, Venerable Sir! How very well-spoken was this by the Blessed One, Venerable Sir: ‘Monks, this sound will not last long; it will last for only seven days. After the passing of seven days, it will disappear.’ That sound, Venerable Sir, has disappeared!”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood this matter, at that time uttered this inspired utterance:

“Tudanti vācāya janā asaññatā,

Sarehi saṅgāmagataṃva kuñjaraṃ;

Sutvāna vākyaṃ pharusaṃ udīritaṃ,

Adhivāsaye bhikkhu aduṭṭhacitto”ti. aṭṭhamaṃ;

“Unrestrained people pierce with speech, as warriors pierce an elephant gone to battle with arrows. Having heard a harsh word that has been spoken, a monk should endure with a mind free from malice.”

9. Upasenasuttaṃ

9. The Discourse about Upasena

39. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā rājagahe viharati veḷuvane kalandakanivāpe. Atha kho āyasmato upasenassa [P 0.0046] vaṅgantaputtassa rahogatassa paṭisallīnassa evaṃ cetaso parivitakko [V 0.0121] udapādi – “lābhā vata me, suladdhaṃ vata me, satthā ca me bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho; svākkhāte camhi dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajito; sabrahmacārino ca me sīlavanto kalyāṇadhammā; sīlesu camhi paripūrakārī; susamāhito camhi ekaggacitto; arahā camhi khīṇāsavo; mahiddhiko camhi mahānubhāvo. Bhaddakaṃ me jīvitaṃ, bhaddakaṃ maraṇa”nti.

39. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Rājagaha in the Bamboo Grove, the Squirrels’ Sanctuary. Then, while the Venerable Upasena, son of Vaṅganta, was in seclusion and solitude, this reflection arose in his mind: 'Indeed, it is a gain for me! Indeed, it is well-gained by me that my Teacher is the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One; that I have gone forth from the household life into homelessness in the well-proclaimed Dhamma and Discipline; that my companions in the holy life are virtuous and of excellent qualities; that I am a fulfiller of the precepts; that I am well-concentrated and of one-pointed mind; that I am an Arahant whose taints are destroyed; that I am of great psychic power and great might. Good is my life, good is my death.'

Atha kho bhagavā āyasmato upasenassa vaṅgantaputtassa cetasā cetoparivitakkamaññāya tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having known with his own mind the reflection in the mind of the Venerable Upasena, son of Vaṅganta, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Yaṃ [M 0.0132] jīvitaṃ na tapati, maraṇante na socati;

Sa ve diṭṭhapado dhīro, sokamajjhe na socati.

“He whom life does not torment, who does not grieve when death is near; that steadfast one who has seen the state, grieves not amidst the grieving.

“Ucchinnabhavataṇhassa[T 0.0143], santacittassa bhikkhuno;

Vikkhīṇo jātisaṃsāro, natthi tassa punabbhavo”ti. navamaṃ;

For the bhikkhu whose craving for existence is cut off, whose mind is tranquil, the saṃsāra of birth is exhausted; there is no renewed existence for him.”

10. Sāriputtaupasamasuttaṃ

10. The Discourse on Sāriputta's Tranquility

40. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā sāriputto bhagavato avidūre nisinno hoti pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya attano upasamaṃ paccavekkhamāno.

40. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. On that occasion, the Venerable Sāriputta was sitting not far from the Blessed One, having folded his legs crosswise and set his body erect, reviewing his own tranquility.

Addasā kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ sāriputtaṃ avidūre nisinnaṃ pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya attano upasamaṃ paccavekkhamānaṃ.

The Blessed One saw the Venerable Sāriputta sitting not far away, having folded his legs crosswise, his body set erect, reviewing his own tranquility.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood the meaning of this, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Upasantasantacittassa, netticchinnassa bhikkhuno;

Vikkhīṇo jātisaṃsāro, mutto so mārabandhanā”ti. dasamaṃ;

“For the bhikkhu with a tranquil, pacified mind, whose leader—craving—has been cut, the saṃsāra of birth is exhausted; he is freed from the bonds of Māra.”

Meghiyavaggo catuttho niṭṭhito.

The Fourth Chapter, the Meghiya Chapter, is concluded.

Tassuddānaṃ [V 0.0122] –

The summary verse for this chapter:

Meghiyo uddhatā gopālo, yakkho juṇhā (sī. syā. pī.), juṇhaṃ (ka.) nāgena pañcamaṃ;

Piṇḍolo sāriputto ca, sundarī bhavati aṭṭhamaṃ;

Upaseno vaṅgantaputto, sāriputto ca te dasāti.

Meghiya, Uddhata, Gopāla, Yakkha, with Nāga as the fifth; Piṇḍola and Sāriputta, with Sundarī as the eighth; Upasena son of Vaṅganta, and Sāriputta—these are the ten.

5. Soṇavaggo mahāvagga (aṭṭhakathāya sameti)

5. The Chapter on Soṇa

1. Piyatarasuttaṃ

1. The Discourse on What is Dearer

41. Evaṃ [M 0.0133][P 0.0047][T 0.0144] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena rājā pasenadi kosalo mallikāya deviyā saddhiṃ uparipāsādavaragato hoti. Atha kho rājā pasenadi kosalo mallikaṃ deviṃ etadavoca – “atthi nu kho te, mallike, kocañño attanā piyataro”ti?

41. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. At that time, King Pasenadi of Kosala, along with Queen Mallikā, had gone up to the uppermost excellent palace. Then King Pasenadi of Kosala said to Queen Mallikā: “Mallikā, is there anyone dearer to you than yourself?”

“Natthi kho me, mahārāja, kocañño attanā piyataro. Tuyhaṃ pana, mahārāja, atthañño koci attanā piyataro”ti? “Mayhampi kho, mallike, natthañño koci attanā piyataro”ti.

“Great king, there is no one dearer to me than myself. But for you, great king, is there anyone dearer than yourself?” “For me too, Mallikā, there is no one dearer than myself.”

Atha kho rājā pasenadi kosalo pāsādā orohitvā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho rājā pasenadi kosalo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca –

Then King Pasenadi of Kosala, descending from the palace, approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, King Pasenadi of Kosala said this to the Blessed One:

“Idhāhaṃ, bhante, mallikāya deviyā saddhiṃ uparipāsādavaragato mallikaṃ deviṃ etadavocaṃ – ‘atthi nu kho te, mallike, kocañño attanā piyataro’ti? Evaṃ vutte, mallikā devī maṃ etadavoca – ‘natthi kho me, mahārāja, kocañño attanā piyataro. Tuyhaṃ pana, mahārāja, atthañño koci attanā piyataro’ti? Evaṃ vutte, ahaṃ, bhante, mallikaṃ deviṃ etadavocaṃ – ‘mayhampi kho, mallike, natthañño koci attanā piyataro’”ti.

“Here, venerable sir, while I was with Queen Mallikā, having gone up to the uppermost excellent palace, I said this to her: ‘Mallikā, is there anyone dearer to you than yourself?’ When this was said, Queen Mallikā said to me: ‘There is no one, great king, dearer to me than myself. But for you, great king, is there anyone dearer than yourself?’ When this was said, venerable sir, I said this to Queen Mallikā: ‘For me too, Mallikā, there is no one dearer than myself.’”

Atha [V 0.0123] kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Sabbā [T 0.0145] disā anuparigamma cetasā,

Nevajjhagā piyataramattanā kvaci;

Evaṃ piyo puthu attā paresaṃ,

Tasmā na hiṃse paramattakāmo”ti. paṭhamaṃ;

“Having traversed all directions with one’s mind, one nowhere finds anyone dearer than oneself. In the same way, for others, their own self is distinctly dear; therefore, one who loves oneself should not harm another.”

2. Appāyukasuttaṃ

2. The Discourse on a Short Lifespan

42. Evaṃ [M 0.0134] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Atha kho āyasmā ānando [P 0.0048] sāyanhasamayaṃ paṭisallānā paṭisallāṇā (sī.) vuṭṭhito yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho āyasmā ānando bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “acchariyaṃ, bhante, abbhutaṃ, bhante! Yāva appāyukā hi, bhante, bhagavato mātā ahosi, sattāhajāte bhagavati bhagavato mātā kālamakāsi, tusitaṃ kāyaṃ upapajjī”ti.

42. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī, in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. Then, in the evening, the Venerable Ānanda, having arisen from seclusion, approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, the Venerable Ānanda said to the Blessed One: “It is wonderful, venerable sir! It is amazing, venerable sir! How very short-lived, venerable sir, was the Blessed One’s mother. Seven days after the Blessed One was born, the Blessed One’s mother passed away and was reborn in the Tusita realm.”

“Evametaṃ, ānanda evametaṃ ānanda evametaṃ ānanda (syā.), appāyukā hi, ānanda, bodhisattamātaro honti. Sattāhajātesu bodhisattesu bodhisattamātaro kālaṃ karonti, tusitaṃ kāyaṃ upapajjantī”ti.

“So it is, Ānanda. The mothers of Bodhisattas are indeed short-lived, Ānanda. Seven days after Bodhisattas are born, their mothers pass away and are reborn in the Tusita realm.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, having understood this matter, the Blessed One on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Ye keci bhūtā bhavissanti ye vāpi,

Sabbe gamissanti pahāya dehaṃ;

Taṃ sabbajāniṃ kusalo viditvā,

Ātāpiyo brahmacariyaṃ careyyā”ti. dutiyaṃ;

“Whatever beings have been or will yet be, all will depart, abandoning the body. Knowing this universal loss, the wise one, being ardent, should practice the holy life.”

3. Suppabuddhakuṭṭhisuttaṃ

3. The Discourse on Suppabuddha the Leper

43. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā rājagahe viharati veḷuvane [T 0.0146] kalandakanivāpe. Tena kho pana samayena rājagahe suppabuddho nāma kuṭṭhī ahosi – manussadaliddo, manussakapaṇo[V 0.0124], manussavarāko. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā mahatiyā parisāya parivuto dhammaṃ desento nisinno hoti.

43. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Rājagaha, in the Bamboo Grove, the Squirrel Sanctuary. At that time in Rājagaha there was a leper named Suppabuddha, a man who was poor, destitute, and wretched. At that time the Blessed One was seated, surrounded by a great assembly, teaching the Dhamma.

Addasā kho suppabuddho kuṭṭhī taṃ mahājanakāyaṃ dūratova sannipatitaṃ. Disvānassa etadahosi – “nissaṃsayaṃ kho ettha kiñci khādanīyaṃ vā bhojanīyaṃ vā bhājīyati bhājīyissati (sī.). Yaṃnūnāhaṃ yena so mahājanakāyo tenupasaṅkameyyaṃ. Appeva nāmettha kiñci khādanīyaṃ vā bhojanīyaṃ vā labheyya”nti.

Suppabuddha the leper saw that great body of people gathered from afar. On seeing this, he thought: “Surely, some hard food or soft food is being distributed here. Suppose I were to approach that great body of people; perhaps I might get some hard food or soft food there.”

Atha [M 0.0135] kho suppabuddho kuṭṭhī yena so mahājanakāyo tenupasaṅkami. Addasā kho suppabuddho kuṭṭhī bhagavantaṃ mahatiyā parisāya parivutaṃ dhammaṃ desentaṃ nisinnaṃ. Disvānassa etadahosi – “na kho ettha kiñci khādanīyaṃ vā bhojanīyaṃ vā bhājīyati. Samaṇo ayaṃ gotamo parisati dhammaṃ deseti. Yaṃnūnāhampi dhammaṃ suṇeyya”nti. Tattheva ekamantaṃ nisīdi – “ahampi dhammaṃ sossāmī”ti.

Then Suppabuddha the leper approached that great body of people. Suppabuddha the leper saw the Blessed One seated, surrounded by a great assembly, teaching the Dhamma. On seeing this, he thought: “No hard food or soft food is being distributed here. This recluse Gotama is teaching the Dhamma in the assembly. Suppose I too were to listen to the Dhamma.” Right there he sat down to one side, thinking, “I too will listen to the Dhamma.”

Atha kho bhagavā sabbāvantaṃ [P 0.0049] parisaṃ cetasā ceto paricca manasākāsi “ko nu kho idha bhabbo dhammaṃ viññātu”nti? Addasā kho bhagavā suppabuddhaṃ kuṭṭhiṃ tassaṃ parisāyaṃ nisinnaṃ. Disvānassa etadahosi – “ayaṃ kho idha bhabbo dhammaṃ viññātu”nti. Suppabuddhaṃ kuṭṭhiṃ ārabbha ānupubbiṃ kathaṃ ānupubbikathaṃ (sī.), anupubbikathaṃ (syā. pī. ka.) kathesi, seyyathidaṃ – dānakathaṃ sīlakathaṃ saggakathaṃ; kāmānaṃ ādīnavaṃ okāraṃ saṅkilesaṃ; nekkhamme nekkhamme ca (sī. syā. pī.) ānisaṃsaṃ pakāsesi. Yadā bhagavā aññāsi suppabuddhaṃ kuṭṭhiṃ [T 0.0147] kallacittaṃ muducittaṃ vinīvaraṇacittaṃ udaggacittaṃ pasannacittaṃ, atha yā buddhānaṃ sāmukkaṃsikā dhammadesanā taṃ pakāsesi – dukkhaṃ, samudayaṃ, nirodhaṃ, maggaṃ. Seyyathāpi nāma suddhaṃ vatthaṃ apagatakāḷakaṃ sammadeva rajanaṃ paṭiggaṇheyya, evameva suppabuddhassa kuṭṭhissa tasmiṃyeva āsane virajaṃ vītamalaṃ dhammacakkhuṃ udapādi – “yaṃ kiñci samudayadhammaṃ sabbaṃ taṃ nirodhadhamma”nti.

Then the Blessed One, having encompassed the entire assembly with his mind, considered: “Who here is capable of understanding the Dhamma?” The Blessed One saw Suppabuddha the leper seated in that assembly. Seeing him, he thought: “This one here is capable of understanding the Dhamma.” Directing his teaching toward Suppabuddha the leper, he gave a progressive discourse—namely, a discourse on giving, a discourse on virtue, and a discourse on heaven; he explained the danger, degradation, and defilement of sensual pleasures, and the benefit of renunciation. When the Blessed One knew that Suppabuddha the leper’s mind was receptive, tender, free from hindrances, uplifted, and serene, he then proclaimed the Dhamma teaching that is the special province of the Buddhas: suffering, its origin, its cessation, and the path. Just as a clean cloth, free from any stain, would properly receive dye, so too for Suppabuddha the leper, right there on that very seat, the dust-free, stainless Dhamma-eye arose with the knowledge: ‘Whatever is subject to arising is all subject to cessation.’”

Atha kho suppabuddho kuṭṭhī diṭṭhadhammo pattadhammo viditadhammo pariyogāḷhadhammo tiṇṇavicikiccho vigatakathaṃkatho vesārajjappatto aparappaccayo satthu sāsane uṭṭhāyāsanā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho suppabuddho kuṭṭhī bhagavantaṃ etadavoca –

Then Suppabuddha the leper, having seen the Dhamma, attained the Dhamma, understood the Dhamma, and penetrated the Dhamma, having crossed over doubt, put away perplexity, gained confidence, and become independent of others in the Teacher’s Dispensation, rose from his seat and approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, Suppabuddha the leper said this to the Blessed One:

“Abhikkantaṃ[V 0.0125], bhante, abhikkataṃ, bhante! Seyyathāpi, bhante, nikkujjitaṃ vā ukkujjeyya, paṭicchannaṃ vā vivareyya, mūḷhassa vā maggaṃ ācikkheyya, andhakāre vā telapajjotaṃ dhāreyya – cakkhumanto rūpāni dakkhantīti; evamevaṃ bhagavatā anekapariyāyena dhammo pakāsito. Esāhaṃ, bhante, bhagavantaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāmi dhammañca bhikkhusaṅghañca. Upāsakaṃ maṃ bhagavā dhāretu ajjatagge pāṇupetaṃ saraṇaṃ gata”nti.

“Excellent, venerable sir! Excellent, venerable sir! Just as, venerable sir, one might set upright what was overturned, or reveal what was hidden, or show the way to one who is lost, or hold up an oil lamp in the darkness so that those with eyes might see forms; just so has the Blessed One in many ways made the Dhamma clear. Venerable sir, I go for refuge to the Blessed One, to the Dhamma, and to the Saṅgha of bhikkhus. May the Blessed One remember me as a lay follower who has gone for refuge from this day forth for as long as life lasts.”

Atha [M 0.0136] kho suppabuddho kuṭṭhī bhagavatā dhammiyā kathāya sandassito samādapito samuttejito sampahaṃsito bhagavato bhāsitaṃ abhinanditvā anumoditvā uṭṭhāyāsanā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ [T 0.0148] katvā pakkāmi. Atha kho acirapakkantaṃ suppabuddhaṃ kuṭṭhiṃ gāvī taruṇavacchā adhipatitvā jīvitā voropesi.

Then Suppabuddha the leper, having been instructed, established, roused, and gladdened by the Blessed One with a discourse on the Dhamma, rejoiced and delighted in the Blessed One’s words. Rising from his seat, he paid homage to the Blessed One, circumambulated him with his right side toward him, and departed. Then, not long after Suppabuddha the leper had departed, a cow with a young calf charged and deprived him of life.

Atha kho sambahulā [P 0.0050] bhikkhū yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu. Ekamantaṃ nisinnā kho te bhikkhū bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ – “yo so, bhante, suppabuddho nāma kuṭṭhī bhagavatā dhammiyā kathāya sandassito samādapito samuttejito sampahaṃsito, so kālaṅkato. Tassa kā gati, ko abhisamparāyo”ti?

Then a number of bhikkhus approached the Blessed One; having approached, they paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, those bhikkhus said to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, that leper named Suppabuddha, who was instructed, established, roused, and gladdened by the Blessed One with a discourse on the Dhamma, has passed away. What is his destination? What is his future course?”

“Paṇḍito, bhikkhave, suppabuddho kuṭṭhī; paccapādi dhammassānudhammaṃ; na ca maṃ dhammādhikaraṇaṃ vihesesi. Suppabuddho, bhikkhave, kuṭṭhī tiṇṇaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā sotāpanno avinipātadhammo niyato sambodhiparāyaṇo”ti.

“Wise, bhikkhus, was Suppabuddha the leper. He practiced the Dhamma in accordance with the Dhamma, and he did not trouble me concerning the Dhamma. Bhikkhus, with the destruction of the three fetters, Suppabuddha the leper is a stream-enterer, not subject to falling into states of woe, certain, and bound for enlightenment.”

Evaṃ vutte, aññataro bhikkhu bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “ko nu kho, bhante, hetu, ko paccayo yena suppabuddho kuṭṭhī ahosi – manussadaliddo, manussakapaṇo, manussavarāko”ti?

When this was said, a certain bhikkhu said to the Blessed One: “What, venerable sir, is the cause, what is the reason that Suppabuddha the leper was a man who was poor, a man who was destitute, a man who was wretched?”

“Bhūtapubbaṃ, bhikkhave, suppabuddho kuṭṭhī imasmiṃyeva rājagahe seṭṭhiputto ahosi. So uyyānabhūmiṃ niyyanto addasa tagarasikhiṃ taggarasikhiṃ (ka.) paccekabuddhaṃ nagaraṃ piṇḍāya pavisantaṃ. Disvānassa etadahosi – ‘kvāyaṃ kuṭṭhī kuṭṭhicīvarena vicaratī’ti? Niṭṭhubhitvā apasabyato apabyāmato (syā. saṃ. ni. 1.255) karitvā pakkāmi. So tassa kammassa vipākena bahūni vassasatāni bahūni vassasahassāni bahūni vassasatasahassāni niraye paccittha. Tasseva kammassa vipākāvasesena [T 0.0149] imasmiṃyeva rājagahe kuṭṭhī ahosi manussadaliddo, manussakapaṇo, manussavarāko. So tathāgatappaveditaṃ dhammavinayaṃ āgamma saddhaṃ samādiyi sīlaṃ samādiyi sutaṃ samādiyi cāgaṃ samādiyi paññaṃ samādiyi. So [V 0.0126] tathāgatappaveditaṃ dhammavinayaṃ āgamma saddhaṃ samādiyitvā sīlaṃ samādiyitvā sutaṃ samādiyitvā cāgaṃ samādiyitvā paññaṃ samādiyitvā kāyassa [M 0.0137] bhedā paraṃ maraṇā sugatiṃ saggaṃ lokaṃ upapanno devānaṃ tāvatiṃsānaṃ sahabyataṃ. So tattha aññe deve atirocati vaṇṇena ceva yasasā cā”ti.

“Formerly, bhikkhus, in this very Rājagaha, Suppabuddha the leper was the son of a wealthy merchant. As he was going out to the park land, he saw the Paccekabuddha Tagarasikhi entering the city for alms. Seeing him, this thought occurred to him: ‘Who is this leper wandering about in his leper’s garment?’ He spat, turned aside to his left, and departed. As a result of that kamma, he was cooked in hell for many hundreds of years, many thousands of years, many hundreds of thousands of years. As a remaining result of that very kamma, in this very Rājagaha he became a leper—a man who was poor, a man who was destitute, a man who was wretched. Relying on the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata, he undertook faith, undertook virtue, undertook learning, undertook generosity, and undertook wisdom. Relying on the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata, having undertaken faith, having undertaken virtue, having undertaken learning, having undertaken generosity, and having undertaken wisdom, with the breaking up of the body, after death, he was reborn in a good destination, the heavenly world, in the company of the Tāvatiṃsa devas. There he outshines the other devas in beauty and in glory.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Cakkhumā visamānīva, vijjamāne parakkame;

Paṇḍito jīvalokasmiṃ, pāpāni parivajjaye”ti. tatiyaṃ;

“Just as one with sight, being diligent, avoids treacherous places, so too the wise person in the world of the living should avoid evil deeds.”

4. Kumārakasuttaṃ

4. The Discourse on the Boys

44. Evaṃ [P 0.0051] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena sambahulā kumārakā antarā ca sāvatthiṃ antarā ca jetavanaṃ macchake bādhenti.

44. Thus have I heard. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī, in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. Now on that occasion, many boys were killing fish between Sāvatthī and Jeta’s Grove.

Atha kho bhagavā pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya sāvatthiṃ piṇḍāya pāvisi. Addasā kho bhagavā te sambahule kumārake antarā ca sāvatthiṃ antarā ca jetavanaṃ macchake bādhente. Disvāna yena te kumārakā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā te kumārake etadavoca – “bhāyatha vo, tumhe kumārakā, dukkhassa, appiyaṃ vo dukkha”nti? “Evaṃ, bhante, bhāyāma mayaṃ, bhante, dukkhassa[T 0.0150], appiyaṃ no dukkha”nti.

Then, in the morning, the Blessed One dressed, took his bowl and robe, and entered Sāvatthī for alms. The Blessed One saw those many boys killing fish between Sāvatthī and Jeta’s Grove. Having seen them, he went to where those boys were; having approached, he said this to those boys: “Boys, do you fear suffering? Is suffering disagreeable to you?” “Yes, Venerable Sir, we fear suffering, Venerable Sir. Suffering is disagreeable to us.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Sace bhāyatha dukkhassa, sace vo dukkhamappiyaṃ;

Mākattha pāpakaṃ kammaṃ, āvi vā yadi vā raho.

“If you fear suffering, if suffering is disagreeable to you, do no evil deed, whether openly or in secret.

“Sace ca pāpakaṃ kammaṃ, karissatha karotha vā;

Na vo dukkhā pamutyatthi, upeccapi upaccapi (ka.), uppaccapi (?), uppatitvāpi iti attho palāyata”nti. catutthaṃ;

“And if you will do an evil deed, or are doing one, there is no escape from suffering for you, even if you fly up and flee away.”

5. Uposathasuttaṃ

5. The Uposatha Discourse

45. Evaṃ [M 0.0138][V 0.0127] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati pubbārāme migāramātupāsāde. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā tadahuposathe bhikkhusaṅghaparivuto nisinno hoti.

45. Thus have I heard. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī, in the Pubbārāma, the mansion of Migāra’s mother. Now on that occasion, on the Uposatha day, the Blessed One was seated surrounded by the Saṅgha of bhikkhus.

Atha kho āyasmā ānando abhikkantāya rattiyā, nikkhante paṭhame yāme, uṭṭhāyāsanā ekaṃsaṃ uttarāsaṅgaṃ cīvaraṃ (sabbattha) karitvā yena bhagavā tenañjaliṃ paṇāmetvā bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “abhikkantā, bhante, ratti; nikkhanto paṭhamo yāmo; ciranisinno bhikkhusaṅgho; uddisatu, bhante, bhagavā bhikkhūnaṃ pātimokkha”nti. Evaṃ vutte, bhagavā tuṇhī ahosi.

Then, when the night had advanced and the first watch had passed, the Venerable Ānanda rose from his seat, arranged his upper robe over one shoulder, extended his joined hands towards the Blessed One, and said to him: “Venerable Sir, the night has advanced; the first watch has passed; the Saṅgha of bhikkhus has been sitting for a long time. Let the Blessed One recite the Pātimokkha for the bhikkhus.” When this was said, the Blessed One remained silent.

Dutiyampi kho āyasmā ānando abhikkantāya rattiyā, nikkhante majjhime yāme, uṭṭhāyāsanā ekaṃsaṃ uttarāsaṅgaṃ karitvā yena bhagavā tenañjaliṃ paṇāmetvā bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “abhikkantā, bhante, ratti; nikkhanto majjhimo yāmo; ciranisinno bhikkhusaṅgho; uddisatu, bhante, bhagavā bhikkhūnaṃ pātimokkha”nti. Dutiyampi kho bhagavā tuṇhī [P 0.0052] ahosi.

A second time, when the night had advanced and the middle watch had passed, the Venerable Ānanda rose from his seat, arranged his upper robe over one shoulder, extended his joined hands towards the Blessed One, and said to him: “Venerable Sir, the night has advanced; the middle watch has passed; the Saṅgha of bhikkhus has been sitting for a long time. Let the Blessed One recite the Pātimokkha for the bhikkhus.” A second time, the Blessed One remained silent.

Tatiyampi kho āyasmā ānando abhikkantāya rattiyā, nikkhante [T 0.0151] pacchime yāme, uddhaste aruṇe, nandimukhiyā rattiyā uṭṭhāyāsanā ekaṃsaṃ uttarāsaṅgaṃ karitvā yena bhagavā tenañjaliṃ paṇāmetvā bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “abhikkantā, bhante, ratti; nikkhanto pacchimo yāmo; uddhasto aruṇo; nandimukhī ratti; ciranisinno bhikkhusaṅgho; uddisatu, bhante, bhagavā bhikkhūnaṃ pātimokkha”nti. “Aparisuddhā, ānanda, parisā”ti.

A third time, when the night had advanced and the last watch had passed, when the dawn had risen and the night was bright-faced, the Venerable Ānanda rose from his seat, arranged his upper robe over one shoulder, extended his joined hands towards the Blessed One, and said to him: “Venerable Sir, the night has advanced; the last watch has passed; the dawn has risen; the night is bright-faced; the Saṅgha of bhikkhus has been sitting for a long time. Let the Blessed One recite the Pātimokkha for the bhikkhus.” “Ānanda, the assembly is not pure.”

Atha kho āyasmato mahāmoggallānassa etadahosi – “kaṃ nu kho bhagavā puggalaṃ sandhāya evamāha – ‘aparisuddhā, ānanda, parisā’ti? Atha kho āyasmā mahāmoggallāno sabbāvantaṃ bhikkhusaṅghaṃ cetasā ceto paricca manasākāsi. Addasā kho āyasmā mahāmoggallāno taṃ puggalaṃ dussīlaṃ pāpadhammaṃ asuciṃ saṅkassarasamācāraṃ paṭicchannakammantaṃ asamaṇaṃ samaṇapaṭiññaṃ abrahmacāriṃ brahmacāripaṭiññaṃ antopūtiṃ avassutaṃ kasambujātaṃ majjhe bhikkhusaṅghassa nisinnaṃ. Disvāna uṭṭhāyāsanā yena so puggalo tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā taṃ [V 0.0128] puggalaṃ etadavoca [M 0.0139] – “uṭṭhehi, āvuso, diṭṭhosi bhagavatā; natthi te bhikkhūhi saddhiṃ saṃvāso”ti. Evaṃ vutte atha kho (sabbattha), cūḷava. 383; a. ni. 8.20 passitabbaṃ, so puggalo tuṇhī ahosi.

Then this thought occurred to the Venerable Mahāmoggallāna: “To what person was the Blessed One referring when he said: ‘Ānanda, the assembly is not pure’?” Then the Venerable Mahāmoggallāna attended to the entire Saṅgha of bhikkhus, encompassing their minds with his own mind. The Venerable Mahāmoggallāna saw that person—immoral, of evil nature, impure, of suspicious conduct, of secretive deeds, not a recluse but claiming to be a recluse, not celibate but claiming to be celibate, rotten within, oozing with defilements, having become like refuse—sitting in the midst of the Saṅgha of bhikkhus. Having seen him, he rose from his seat, approached that person, and said to him: “Get up, friend. You have been seen by the Blessed One. There is no communion for you with the bhikkhus.” When this was said, that person remained silent.

Dutiyampi kho āyasmā mahāmoggallāno taṃ puggalaṃ etadavoca – “uṭṭhehi, āvuso, diṭṭhosi bhagavatā; natthi te bhikkhūhi saddhiṃ saṃvāso”ti. Dutiyampi kho…pe… [T 0.0152] tatiyampi kho so puggalo tuṇhī ahosi.

A second time, the Venerable Mahāmoggallāna said to that person: “Get up, friend. You have been seen by the Blessed One. There is no communion for you with the bhikkhus.” A second time that person remained silent. A third time, the Venerable Mahāmoggallāna said to that person: “Get up, friend. You have been seen by the Blessed One. There is no communion for you with the bhikkhus.” And a third time that person remained silent.

Atha kho āyasmā mahāmoggallāno taṃ puggalaṃ bāhāyaṃ gahetvā bahidvārakoṭṭhakā nikkhāmetvā sūcighaṭikaṃ datvā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “nikkhāmito, bhante, so puggalo mayā. Parisuddhā parisā. Uddisatu, bhante, bhagavā bhikkhūnaṃ pātimokkha”nti. “Acchariyaṃ, moggallāna, abbhutaṃ, moggallāna! Yāva bāhāgahaṇāpi nāma so moghapuriso āgamessatī”ti!

Then the Venerable Mahāmoggallāna took that person by the arm, expelled him from outside the gatehouse, secured the bolt, and approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he said to the Blessed One: “Venerable Sir, that person has been expelled by me. The assembly is pure. Let the Blessed One recite the Pātimokkha for the bhikkhus.” “It is wonderful, Moggallāna, it is amazing, Moggallāna, that this worthless man would wait until being taken by the arm!”

Atha kho [P 0.0053] bhagavā bhikkhū āmantesi – “na dānāhaṃ, bhikkhave, ito paraṃ na dānāhaṃ bhikkhave ajjatagge (a. ni. 8.20) uposathaṃ karissāmi, pātimokkhaṃ uddisissāmi. Tumheva dāni, bhikkhave, ito paraṃ uposathaṃ kareyyātha, pātimokkhaṃ uddiseyyātha. Aṭṭhānametaṃ, bhikkhave, anavakāso yaṃ tathāgato aparisuddhāya parisāya uposathaṃ kareyya, pātimokkhaṃ uddiseyya.

Then the Blessed One addressed the bhikkhus: “Bhikkhus, from now on I will not perform the Uposatha or recite the Pātimokkha. From now on, you yourselves should perform the Uposatha and recite the Pātimokkha. It is impossible, bhikkhus, it cannot be that the Tathāgata would perform the Uposatha or recite the Pātimokkha in an impure assembly.”

“Aṭṭhime, bhikkhave, mahāsamudde acchariyā abbhutā dhammā, ye disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti. Katame aṭṭha?

“Bhikkhus, there are these eight wonderful and amazing qualities of the great ocean, seeing which again and again, the asuras delight in the great ocean. What are the eight?

“Mahāsamuddo, bhikkhave, anupubbaninno anupubbapoṇo anupubbapabbhāro, na āyatakeneva papāto. Yampi yaṃ (sī. syā. ka.), bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo anupubbaninno anupubbapoṇo anupubbapabbhāro na āyatakeneva papāto; ayaṃ, bhikkhave, mahāsamudde paṭhamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti.

“Bhikkhus, the great ocean gradually slopes, gradually inclines, and gradually deepens; it is not an abrupt precipice. That the great ocean, bhikkhus, gradually slopes, gradually inclines, and gradually deepens, and is not an abrupt precipice—this, bhikkhus, is the first wonderful and amazing quality of the great ocean, seeing which again and again, the asuras delight in the great ocean.

“Puna [M 0.0140][T 0.0153] caparaṃ, bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo ṭhitadhammo velaṃ nātivattati. Yampi, bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo ṭhitadhammo velaṃ nātivattati; ayaṃ, bhikkhave ayampi (sabbattha), mahāsamudde dutiyo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, the great ocean is of a stable nature and does not transgress the shore. That the great ocean, bhikkhus, is of a stable nature and does not transgress the shore—this, bhikkhus, is the second wonderful and amazing quality of the great ocean, seeing which again and again, the asuras delight in the great ocean.

“Puna [V 0.0129] caparaṃ, bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo na matena kuṇapena saṃvasati. Yaṃ hoti mahāsamudde mataṃ kuṇapaṃ taṃ khippameva khippaññeva (sī.), khippaṃyeva (ka.) tīraṃ vāheti, thalaṃ ussāreti. Yampi, bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo na matena kuṇapena saṃvasati, yaṃ hoti mahāsamudde mataṃ kuṇapaṃ taṃ khippameva tīraṃ vāheti thalaṃ ussāreti; ayaṃ, bhikkhave, mahāsamudde tatiyo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, the great ocean does not coexist with a dead corpse. Whatever dead corpse is in the great ocean, it quickly carries it to the shore and casts it up on dry land. That the great ocean, bhikkhus, does not coexist with a dead corpse, and that whatever dead corpse is in the great ocean, it quickly carries it to the shore and casts it up on dry land—this, bhikkhus, is the third wonderful and amazing quality of the great ocean, seeing which again and again, the asuras delight in the great ocean.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, yā kāci mahānadiyo, seyyathidaṃ – gaṅgā yamunā aciravatī sarabhū mahī, tā mahāsamuddaṃ patvā pattā (syā. pī. ka.) jahanti purimāni nāmagottāni; ‘mahāsamuddo’tveva saṅkhaṃ gacchanti. Yampi, bhikkhave, yā kāci mahānadiyo, seyyathidaṃ – gaṅgā yamunā aciravatī sarabhū mahī tā mahāsamuddaṃ patvā jahanti purimāni nāmagottāni, ‘mahāsamuddo’tveva saṅkhaṃ gacchanti; ayaṃ, bhikkhave, mahāsamudde catuttho acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, whatever great rivers there are—such as the Gaṅgā, the Yamunā, the Aciravatī, the Sarabhū, and the Mahī—upon reaching the great ocean, they relinquish their former names and clans and are simply reckoned as ‘the great ocean.’ That whatever great rivers there are, bhikkhus—such as the Gaṅgā, the Yamunā, the Aciravatī, the Sarabhū, and the Mahī—upon reaching the great ocean, relinquish their former names and clans and are simply reckoned as ‘the great ocean’; this, bhikkhus, is the fourth wonderful and amazing quality in the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in the great ocean.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, yā ca loke savantiyo mahāsamuddaṃ appenti, yā ca antalikkhā dhārā papatanti, na tena mahāsamuddassa [T 0.0154] ūnattaṃ vā pūrattaṃ vā paññāyati. Yampi, bhikkhave[P 0.0054], yā ca loke savantiyo mahāsamuddaṃ appenti, yā ca antalikkhā dhārā papatanti, na tena mahāsammuddassa ūnattaṃ vā pūrattaṃ vā paññāyati; ayaṃ, bhikkhave, mahāsamudde pañcamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, whatever streams in the world flow into the great ocean, and whatever showers of rain fall from the sky, no lessening or filling of the great ocean is thereby apparent. That whatever streams in the world, bhikkhus, flow into the great ocean, and whatever showers of rain fall from the sky, no lessening or filling of the great ocean is thereby apparent; this, bhikkhus, is the fifth wonderful and amazing quality in the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in the great ocean.”

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo ekaraso loṇaraso. Yampi, bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo ekaraso loṇaraso; ayaṃ, bhikkhave, mahāsamudde chaṭṭho acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, the great ocean has a single taste, the taste of salt. That the great ocean has a single taste, bhikkhus, the taste of salt; this, bhikkhus, is the sixth wonderful and amazing quality in the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in the great ocean.”

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo bahuratano anekaratano. Tatrimāni ratanāni, seyyathidaṃ – muttā maṇi veḷuriyo saṅkho silā pavāḷaṃ [M 0.0141] rajataṃ jātarūpaṃ lohitaṅgo masāragallaṃ. Yampi, bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo bahuratano anekaratano, tatrimāni ratanāni, seyyathidaṃ – muttā maṇi veḷuriyo saṅkho silā pavāḷaṃ rajataṃ jātarūpaṃ lohitaṅgo lohitaṅko (sī. pī.), lohitako (?) masāragallaṃ; ayaṃ, bhikkhave, mahāsamudde sattamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, the great ocean has many jewels, various kinds of jewels. Therein are these jewels, namely: pearls, gems, beryl, conch, crystal, coral, silver, gold, rubies, and cat's-eye gems. That the great ocean, bhikkhus, has many jewels, various kinds of jewels, and that therein are these jewels—namely: pearls, gems, beryl, conch, crystal, coral, silver, gold, rubies, and cat's-eye gems; this, bhikkhus, is the seventh wonderful and marvelous quality in the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in the great ocean.

“Puna [V 0.0130] caparaṃ, bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo mahataṃ bhūtānaṃ āvāso. Tatrime bhūtā – timi timiṅgalo timitimiṅgalo timi timiṅgalo timirapiṅgalo (sī. pī., a. ni. 8.19) asurā nāgā gandhabbā. Santi mahāsamudde yojanasatikāpi attabhāvā, dviyojanasatikāpi attabhāvā, tiyojanasatikāpi attabhāvā, catuyojanasatikāpi attabhāvā, pañcayojanasatikāpi [T 0.0155] attabhāvā. Yampi, bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo mahataṃ bhūtānaṃ āvāso, tatrime bhūtā – timi timiṅgalo timitimiṅgalo asurā nāgā gandhabbā, santi mahāsamudde yojanasatikāpi attabhāvā dviyojanasatikāpi attabhāvā…pe… pañcayojanasatikāpi attabhāvā; ayaṃ, bhikkhave, mahāsamudde aṭṭhamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti. Ime kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha mahāsamudde acchariyā abbhutā dhammā ye disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, the great ocean is the abode of great beings. Therein are these beings: the timi, the timiṅgala, the timitimiṅgala, asuras, nāgas, and gandhabbas. In the great ocean there are beings with individual existences of one hundred yojanas, beings with individual existences of two hundred yojanas, beings with individual existences of three hundred yojanas, beings with individual existences of four hundred yojanas, and beings with individual existences of five hundred yojanas. That the great ocean, bhikkhus, is the abode of great beings, and that therein are these beings—the timi, the timiṅgala, the timitimiṅgala, asuras, nāgas, and gandhabbas; and that in the great ocean there are beings with individual existences of one hundred yojanas... up to... five hundred yojanas; this, bhikkhus, is the eighth wonderful and marvelous quality in the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in the great ocean. These, bhikkhus, are the eight wonderful and marvelous qualities in the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in the great ocean.

“Evameva kho, bhikkhave, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye aṭṭha acchariyā abbhutā dhammā, ye disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti. Katame aṭṭha?

“In the same way, bhikkhus, in this Dhamma and Discipline there are eight wonderful and marvelous qualities, seeing which again and again the bhikkhus delight in this Dhamma and Discipline. What are the eight?

“Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo anupubbaninno anupubbapoṇo anupubbapabbhāro, na āyatakeneva papāto; evameva kho, bhikkhave, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye anupubbasikkhā anupubbakiriyā anupubbapaṭipadā, na āyatakeneva aññāpaṭivedho. Yampi, bhikkhave, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye anupubbasikkhā anupubbakiriyā anupubbapaṭipadā, na āyatakeneva aññāpaṭivedho; ayaṃ, bhikkhave, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye paṭhamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti.

“Just as, bhikkhus, the great ocean gradually slopes, gradually inclines, gradually deepens, and is not an abrupt precipice; in the same way, bhikkhus, in this Dhamma and Discipline there is gradual training, gradual action, and gradual practice; there is no penetration to final knowledge right at the outset. That in this Dhamma and Discipline, bhikkhus, there is gradual training, gradual action, and gradual practice, and there is no penetration to final knowledge right at the outset; this, bhikkhus, is the first wonderful and marvelous quality in this Dhamma and Discipline, seeing which again and again the bhikkhus delight in this Dhamma and Discipline.

“Seyyathāpi[M 0.0142][P 0.0055], bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo ṭhitadhammo velaṃ nātivattati; evameva [T 0.0156] kho, bhikkhave, yaṃ mayā sāvakānaṃ sikkhāpadaṃ paññattaṃ taṃ mama sāvakā jīvitahetupi nātikkamanti. Yampi, bhikkhave, mayā sāvakānaṃ sikkhāpadaṃ paññattaṃ taṃ mama sāvakā jīvitahetupi nātikkamanti; ayaṃ, bhikkhave, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye dutiyo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti.

“Just as, bhikkhus, the great ocean is of a stable nature and does not transgress its boundary; in the same way, bhikkhus, whatever training rule has been laid down by me for my disciples, my disciples do not transgress it even for the sake of their lives. That whatever training rule, bhikkhus, has been laid down by me for my disciples, my disciples do not transgress it even for the sake of their lives; this, bhikkhus, is the second wonderful and marvelous quality in this Dhamma and Discipline, seeing which again and again the bhikkhus delight in this Dhamma and Discipline.

“Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo na matena kuṇapena saṃvasati; yaṃ hoti mahāsamudde mataṃ kuṇapaṃ taṃ khippameva tīraṃ vāheti, thalaṃ ussāreti; evameva kho, bhikkhave, yo so puggalo dussīlo pāpadhammo asuci saṅkassarasamācāro paṭicchannakammanto assamaṇo samaṇapaṭiñño [V 0.0131] abrahmacārī brahmacāripaṭiñño antopūti avassuto kasambujāto, na tena saṅgho saṃvasati; atha kho naṃ khippameva sannipatitvā ukkhipati. Kiñcāpi so hoti majjhe bhikkhusaṅghassa nisinno, atha kho so ārakāva saṅghamhā, saṅgho ca tena. Yampi, bhikkhave, yo so puggalo dussīlo pāpadhammo asuci saṅkassarasamācāro paṭicchannakammanto assamaṇo samaṇapaṭiñño abrahmacārī brahmacāripaṭiñño antopūti avassuto kasambujāto, na tena saṅgho saṃvasati; khippameva naṃ sannipatitvā ukkhipati. Kiñcāpi so hoti majjhe bhikkhusaṅghassa nisinno, atha kho so ārakāva saṅghamhā, saṅgho ca tena; ayaṃ, bhikkhave, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye [T 0.0157] tatiyo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti.

“Just as, bhikkhus, the great ocean does not associate with a dead corpse; whatever dead corpse is in the great ocean, it quickly washes it to the shore and casts it up on dry land—even so, bhikkhus, the Saṅgha does not associate with a person who is immoral, of evil nature, impure, of suspect conduct, of concealed actions, not an ascetic but claiming to be an ascetic, not living the holy life but claiming to live the holy life, inwardly rotten, leaking with defilements, and like rubbish. Instead, having assembled, they quickly expel that person. Even though such a person is seated in the midst of the Saṅgha of bhikkhus, they are far from the Saṅgha, and the Saṅgha is far from them. That, bhikkhus, the Saṅgha does not associate with a person who is immoral, of evil nature, impure, of suspect conduct, of concealed actions, not an ascetic but claiming to be an ascetic, not living the holy life but claiming to live the holy life, inwardly rotten, leaking with defilements, and like rubbish; that having assembled, they quickly expel that person; and that even though such a person is seated in the midst of the Saṅgha of bhikkhus, they are far from the Saṅgha, and the Saṅgha is far from them—this, bhikkhus, is the third wonderful and marvelous quality in this Dhamma and Discipline, seeing which again and again the bhikkhus delight in this Dhamma and Discipline.”

“Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, yā kāci mahānadiyo, seyyathidaṃ – gaṅgā yamunā aciravatī sarabhū mahī tā mahāsamuddaṃ patvā jahanti purimāni nāmagottāni, ‘mahāsamuddo’tveva saṅkhaṃ gacchanti; evameva kho, bhikkhave, cattāro vaṇṇā – khattiyā, brāhmaṇā, vessā, suddā te tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajitvā pabbajitā (ka. sī.) jahanti purimāni nāmagottāni, ‘samaṇā sakyaputtiyā’tveva saṅkhaṃ gacchanti. Yampi, bhikkhave, cattāro vaṇṇā – khattiyā, brāhmaṇā, vessā, suddā te tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajitvā jahanti purimāni nāmagottāni[M 0.0143], ‘samaṇā sakyaputtiyā’tveva saṅkhaṃ gacchanti; ayaṃ, bhikkhave, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye catuttho acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti.

“Just as, bhikkhus, whatever great rivers there are—namely, the Gaṅgā, Yamunā, Aciravatī, Sarabhū, and Mahī—upon reaching the great ocean, abandon their former names and clans and are reckoned simply as ‘the great ocean’; even so, bhikkhus, these four castes—Khattiyas, Brāhmaṇas, Vessas, and Suddas—when they go forth from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Discipline proclaimed by the Tathāgata, abandon their former names and clans and are reckoned simply as ‘ascetics, sons of the Sakyan.’ That, bhikkhus, these four castes—Khattiyas, Brāhmaṇas, Vessas, and Suddas—when they go forth from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Discipline proclaimed by the Tathāgata, abandon their former names and clans and are reckoned simply as ‘ascetics, sons of the Sakyan’—this, bhikkhus, is the fourth wonderful and marvelous quality in this Dhamma and Discipline, seeing which again and again the bhikkhus delight in this Dhamma and Discipline.”

“Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, yā ca loke savantiyo mahāsamuddaṃ appenti, yā ca antalikkhā dhārā papatanti, na tena mahāsamuddassa ūnattaṃ vā pūrattaṃ vā paññāyati; evameva kho, bhikkhave, bahū cepi bhikkhū anupādisesāya nibbānadhātuyā parinibbāyanti, na tena nibbānadhātuyā [T 0.0158] ūnattaṃ vā pūrattaṃ vā paññāyati. Yampi, bhikkhave, bahū cepi bhikkhū anupādisesāya nibbānadhātuyā parinibbāyanti, na tena nibbānadhātuyā ūnattaṃ vā pūrattaṃ vā paññāyati; ayaṃ, bhikkhave, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye pañcamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti.

O monks, just as all the rivers that flow into the great ocean, and all the streams that fall from the sky, yet no decrease or increase in the great ocean is perceived; even so, O monks, even if many monks attain final Nibbāna in the Nibbāna-element without residue, no decrease or increase in the Nibbāna-element is perceived. That even if many monks attain final Nibbāna in the Nibbāna-element without residue, no decrease or increase in the Nibbāna-element is perceived—this, O monks, is the fifth marvelous and wondrous quality in this Dhamma and Vinaya, seeing which again and again, the monks delight in this Dhamma and Vinaya.

“Seyyathāpi[P 0.0056], bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo ekaraso loṇaraso; evameva kho, bhikkhave, ayaṃ dhammavinayo ekaraso vimuttiraso.
