Yampi, bhikkhave, ayaṃ dhammavinayo ekaraso vimuttiraso; ayaṃ[V 0.0132], bhikkhave, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye chaṭṭho acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti.

O monks, just as the great ocean has one taste, the taste of salt; even so, O monks, this Dhamma and Vinaya has one taste, the taste of liberation. That this Dhamma and Vinaya has one taste, the taste of liberation—this, O monks, is the sixth marvelous and wondrous quality in this Dhamma and Vinaya, seeing which again and again, the monks delight in this Dhamma and Vinaya.

“Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo bahuratano anekaratano, tatrimāni ratanāni, seyyathidaṃ – muttā maṇi veḷuriyo saṅkho silā pavāḷaṃ rajataṃ jātarūpaṃ lohitaṅgo masāragallaṃ; evameva kho, bhikkhave, ayaṃ dhammavinayo bahuratano anekaratano; tatrimāni ratanāni, seyyathidaṃ – cattāro satipaṭṭhānā, cattāro sammappadhānā, cattāro iddhipādā, pañcindriyāni, pañca balāni, satta bojjhaṅgā, ariyo aṭṭhaṅgiko maggo. Yampi, bhikkhave, ayaṃ dhammavinayo bahuratano anekaratano, tatrimāni ratanāni, seyyathidaṃ – cattāro satipaṭṭhānā, cattāro sammappadhānā, cattāro iddhipādā, pañcindriyāni, pañca balāni, satta bojjhaṅgā, ariyo aṭṭhaṅgiko maggo; ayaṃ, bhikkhave, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye sattamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti.

O monks, just as the great ocean is rich in many kinds of jewels, and these are the jewels therein, namely: pearls, gems, beryl, conch, crystal, coral, silver, gold, rubies, and cat's-eye gems; even so, O monks, this Dhamma and Vinaya is rich in many kinds of jewels, and these are the jewels therein, namely: the four foundations of mindfulness, the four right strivings, the four bases of spiritual power, the five faculties, the five powers, the seven factors of enlightenment, and the Noble Eightfold Path. That this Dhamma and Vinaya is rich in many kinds of jewels, and these are the jewels therein, namely: the four foundations of mindfulness, the four right strivings, the four bases of spiritual power, the five faculties, the five powers, the seven factors of enlightenment, and the Noble Eightfold Path—this, O monks, is the seventh marvelous and wondrous quality in this Dhamma and Vinaya, seeing which again and again, the monks delight in this Dhamma and Vinaya.

“Seyyathāpi[T 0.0159], bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo mahataṃ bhūtānaṃ āvāso, tatrime bhūtā – timi timiṅgalo timitimiṅgalo asurā nāgā gandhabbā, santi [M 0.0144] mahāsamudde yojanasatikāpi attabhāvā dviyojanasatikāpi attabhāvā tiyojanasatikāpi attabhāvā catuyojanasatikāpi attabhāvā pañcayojanasatikāpi attabhāvā; evameva kho, bhikkhave, ayaṃ dhammavinayo mahataṃ bhūtānaṃ āvāso; tatrime bhūtā – sotāpanno, sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno, sakadāgāmi, sakadāgāmiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno, anāgāmī, anāgāmīphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno, arahā, arahattāya paṭipanno arahattaphalasacchikiriyāya (sī.). Yampi, bhikkhave, ayaṃ dhammavinayo mahataṃ bhūtānaṃ āvāso, tatrime bhūtā – sotāpanno, sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno, sakadāgāmī, sakadāgāmiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno, anāgāmī, anāgāmiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno, arahā, arahattāya paṭipanno; ayaṃ, bhikkhave, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye aṭṭhamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti. Ime kho, bhikkhave, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye aṭṭha acchariyā abbhutā dhammā, ye disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramantī”ti.

Just as, O monks, the great ocean is the abode of great beings—that is, the timi, the timiṅgala, and the timitimiṅgala fish, asuras, nāgas, and gandhabbas—and there are in the great ocean beings with bodies one hundred yojanas long, two hundred yojanas long, three hundred yojanas long, four hundred yojanas long, and five hundred yojanas long; even so, O monks, this Dhamma and Vinaya is the abode of great beings—that is, the stream-enterer, the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry; the once-returner, the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of once-returning; the non-returner, the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of non-returning; the Arahant, and the one practicing for Arahantship. That this Dhamma and Vinaya is the abode of great beings—that is, the stream-enterer, the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry; the once-returner, the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of once-returning; the non-returner, the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of non-returning; the Arahant, and the one practicing for Arahantship—this, O monks, is the eighth marvelous and wondrous quality in this Dhamma and Vinaya, seeing which again and again, monks delight in this Dhamma and Vinaya. These, O monks, are the eight marvelous and wondrous qualities in this Dhamma and Vinaya, seeing which again and again, monks delight in this Dhamma and Vinaya.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Channamativassati, vivaṭaṃ nātivassati;

Tasmā channaṃ vivaretha, evaṃ taṃ nātivassatī”ti. pañcamaṃ;

“Rain wets what is covered; it does not wet what is open. Therefore, uncover what is covered; thus it will not be wetted by the rain.”

6. Soṇasuttaṃ

6. The Sutta on Soṇa

46. Evaṃ [V 0.0133][P 0.0057] me [T 0.0160] sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā mahākaccāno avantīsu viharati kuraraghare kururaghare (syā. mahāva. 257), kulaghare (ka.) pavatte pabbate. Tena kho pana samayena soṇo upāsako kuṭikaṇṇo āyasmato mahākaccānassa upaṭṭhāko hoti.

46. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s monastery. At that time, the Venerable Mahākaccāna was dwelling in the Avanti country, at Kuraraghara on Pavatta Mountain. At that time, the lay follower Soṇa Kuṭikaṇṇa was an attendant of the Venerable Mahākaccāna.

Atha kho soṇassa upāsakassa kuṭikaṇṇassa rahogatassa paṭisallīnassa evaṃ cetaso parivitakko udapādi – “yathā yathā kho ayyo mahākaccāno dhammaṃ deseti nayidaṃ sukaraṃ agāraṃ ajjhāvasatā ekantaparipuṇṇaṃ ekantaparisuddhaṃ saṅkhalikhitaṃ brahmacariyaṃ carituṃ. Yaṃnūnāhaṃ [M 0.0145] kesamassuṃ ohāretvā kāsāyāni vatthāni acchādetvā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajeyya”nti.

Then, while the lay follower Soṇa Kuṭikaṇṇa was in seclusion, gone to a solitary place, this reflection arose in his mind: “In the way that the noble Mahākaccāna teaches the Dhamma, it is not easy for one dwelling in a household to practice this holy life perfectly complete, perfectly pure, like a polished conch shell. What if I were to shave off my hair and beard, put on the ochre robes, and go forth from the household life into homelessness?”

Atha kho soṇo upāsako kuṭikaṇṇo yenāyasmā mahākaccāno tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā āyasmantaṃ mahākaccānaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho soṇo upāsako kuṭikaṇṇo āyasmantaṃ mahākaccānaṃ etadavoca –

Then the lay follower Soṇa Kuṭikaṇṇa approached the Venerable Mahākaccāna; having approached and paid homage to the Venerable Mahākaccāna, he sat down to one side. Seated to one side, the lay follower Soṇa Kuṭikaṇṇa said this to the Venerable Mahākaccāna:

“Idha mayhaṃ, bhante, rahogatassa paṭisallīnassa evaṃ cetaso parivitakko udapādi – ‘yathā yathā kho ayyo mahākaccāno dhammaṃ deseti nayidaṃ sukaraṃ agāraṃ ajjhāvasatā ekantaparipuṇṇaṃ ekantaparisuddhaṃ saṅkhalikhitaṃ brahmacariyaṃ carituṃ. Yaṃnūnāhaṃ kesamassuṃ ohāretvā kāsāyāni vatthāni acchādetvā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajeyya’nti. Pabbājetu maṃ, bhante[T 0.0161], ayyo mahākaccāno”ti.

“Venerable sir, while I was in seclusion, gone to a solitary place, this reflection arose in my mind: ‘In the way that the Venerable Mahākaccāna teaches the Dhamma, it is not easy for one dwelling in a household to practice this holy life perfectly complete, perfectly pure, like a polished conch shell. What if I were to shave off my hair and beard, put on the ochre robes, and go forth from the household life into homelessness?’ May the Venerable Mahākaccāna, venerable sir, give me the going forth.”

Evaṃ vutte, āyasmā mahākaccāno soṇaṃ upāsakaṃ kuṭikaṇṇaṃ etadavoca – “dukkaraṃ kho, soṇa, yāvajīvaṃ ekabhattaṃ ekaseyyaṃ brahmacariyaṃ. Iṅgha tvaṃ, soṇa, tattheva āgārikabhūto samāno buddhānaṃ sāsanaṃ anuyuñja kālayuttaṃ ekabhattaṃ ekaseyyaṃ brahmacariya”nti. Atha kho soṇassa upāsakassa kuṭikaṇṇassa yo ahosi pabbajjābhisaṅkhāro so paṭipassambhi.

When this was said, the Venerable Mahākaccāna said this to the lay follower Soṇa Kuṭikaṇṇa: “Soṇa, the holy life, practiced for one's whole life with one meal a day and a solitary bed, is indeed difficult. Come now, Soṇa, while remaining right there as a householder, you should apply yourself to the teaching of the Buddhas: the holy life with one meal a day and a solitary bed, practiced at the proper time.” Then, whatever intention for going forth the lay follower Soṇa Kuṭikaṇṇa had, it subsided.

Dutiyampi kho…pe… [T 0.0162] dutiyampi kho āyasmā mahākaccāno soṇaṃ upāsakaṃ kuṭikaṇṇaṃ etadavoca – “dukkaraṃ kho, soṇa, yāvajīvaṃ ekabhattaṃ ekaseyyaṃ brahmacariyaṃ. Iṅgha tvaṃ, soṇa, tattheva āgārikabhūto samāno buddhānaṃ sāsanaṃ anuyuñja kālayuttaṃ ekabhattaṃ ekaseyyaṃ [V 0.0134] brahmacariya”nti. Dutiyampi kho soṇassa upāsakassa kuṭikaṇṇassa yo ahosi pabbajjābhisaṅkhāro so paṭipassambhi.

For a second time... For a second time, the Venerable Mahākaccāna said this to the lay follower Soṇa Kuṭikaṇṇa: “Soṇa, the holy life, practiced for one's whole life with one meal a day and a solitary bed, is indeed difficult. Come now, Soṇa, while remaining right there as a householder, you should apply yourself to the teaching of the Buddhas: the holy life with one meal a day and a solitary bed, practiced at the proper time.” For a second time, the intention for going forth that the lay follower Soṇa Kuṭikaṇṇa had, subsided.

Tatiyampi kho soṇassa upāsakassa kuṭikaṇṇassa rahogatassa paṭisallīnassa evaṃ cetaso parivitakko udapādi – “yathā yathā kho ayyo mahākaccāno dhammaṃ deseti nayidaṃ sukaraṃ agāraṃ ajjhāvasatā ekantaparipuṇṇaṃ ekantaparisuddhaṃ saṅkhalikhitaṃ brahmacariyaṃ carituṃ. Yaṃnūnāhaṃ kesamassuṃ ohāretvā kāsāyāni vatthāni acchādetvā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajeyya”nti. Tatiyampi kho soṇo upāsako kuṭikaṇṇo [M 0.0146] yenāyasmā mahākaccāno tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā āyasmantaṃ mahākaccānaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho soṇo upāsako kuṭikaṇṇo āyasmantaṃ mahākaccānaṃ etadavoca [P 0.0058] –

For a third time, while the lay follower Soṇa Kuṭikaṇṇa was in seclusion, gone to a solitary place, this reflection arose in his mind: “In the way that the Venerable Mahākaccāna teaches the Dhamma, it is not easy for one dwelling in a household to practice this holy life perfectly complete, perfectly pure, like a polished conch shell. What if I were to shave off my hair and beard, put on the ochre robes, and go forth from the household life into homelessness?” For a third time, the lay follower Soṇa Kuṭikaṇṇa approached the Venerable Mahākaccāna; having approached and paid homage to the Venerable Mahākaccāna, he sat down to one side. Seated to one side, the lay follower Soṇa Kuṭikaṇṇa said this to the Venerable Mahākaccāna:

“Idha mayhaṃ, bhante, rahogatassa paṭisallīnassa evaṃ cetaso parivitakko udapādi – ‘yathā yathā kho ayyo mahākaccāno dhammaṃ deseti nayidaṃ sukaraṃ agāraṃ ajjhāvasatā ekantaparipuṇṇaṃ ekantaparisuddhaṃ saṅkhalikhitaṃ [T 0.0163] brahmacariyaṃ carituṃ. Yaṃnūnāhaṃ kesamassuṃ ohāretvā kāsāyāni vatthāni acchādetvā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajeyya’nti. Pabbājetu maṃ, bhante, ayyo mahākaccāno”ti.

“Here, venerable sir, while I was in seclusion, gone to a solitary place, this reflection arose in my mind: ‘In the way that the Venerable Mahākaccāna teaches the Dhamma, it is not easy for one dwelling in a household to practice this holy life perfectly complete, perfectly pure, like a polished conch shell. What if I were to shave off my hair and beard, put on the ochre robes, and go forth from the household life into homelessness?’ May the Venerable Mahākaccāna, venerable sir, give me the going forth.”

Atha kho āyasmā mahākaccāno soṇaṃ upāsakaṃ kuṭikaṇṇaṃ pabbājesi. Tena kho pana samayena avantidakkhiṇāpatho avanti dakkhiṇapatho (sī.) appabhikkhuko hoti. Atha kho āyasmā mahākaccāno tiṇṇaṃ vassānaṃ accayena kicchena kasirena tato tato dasavaggaṃ bhikkhusaṅghaṃ sannipātetvā āyasmantaṃ soṇaṃ upasampādesi.

Then the Venerable Mahākaccāna gave the going forth to the lay follower Soṇa Kuṭikaṇṇa. Now at that time, the southern region of Avanti had few bhikkhus. Then, after three years had passed, the Venerable Mahākaccāna, with difficulty and hardship, having gathered a community of ten bhikkhus from here and there, gave the Venerable Soṇa the higher ordination.

Atha kho āyasmato soṇassa vassaṃvuṭṭhassa vassaṃvutthassa (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.) rahogatassa paṭisallīnassa evaṃ cetaso parivitakko udapādi – “na kho me so bhagavā sammukhā diṭṭho, api ca sutoyeva me so bhagavā – ‘īdiso ca īdiso cā’ti. Sace maṃ upajjhāyo anujāneyya, gaccheyyāhaṃ taṃ bhagavantaṃ dassanāya arahantaṃ sammāsambuddha”nti.

Then, after the Venerable Soṇa had completed the rains-residence, while he was in seclusion, gone to a solitary place, this thought arose in his mind: “I have not seen the Blessed One face-to-face, but I have only heard of that Blessed One thus: ‘He is of such a nature, and of such a nature.’ If my preceptor would permit me, I would go to see that Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One.”

Atha kho āyasmā soṇo sāyanhasamayaṃ paṭisallānā vuṭṭhito yenāyasmā mahākaccāno tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā āyasmantaṃ mahākaccānaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho āyasmā soṇo āyasmantaṃ mahākaccānaṃ etadavoca –

Then, in the evening, the Venerable Soṇa rose from seclusion and approached the Venerable Mahākaccāna; having approached, he paid homage to the Venerable Mahākaccāna and sat down at one side. Seated at one side, the Venerable Soṇa said this to the Venerable Mahākaccāna:

“Idha [V 0.0135] mayhaṃ, bhante, rahogatassa paṭisallīnassa evaṃ cetaso parivitakko udapādi – ‘na kho me so bhagavā sammukhā diṭṭho, api ca sutoyeva me so bhagavā – īdiso ca īdiso cā’ti. Sace maṃ upajjhāyo [M 0.0147] anujāneyya, gaccheyyāhaṃ [T 0.0164] taṃ bhagavantaṃ dassanāya arahantaṃ sammāsambuddha”nti ( ) (gaccheyyāhaṃ bhante taṃ bhagavantaṃ dassanāya arahantaṃ sammāsambuddhaṃ, sace maṃ upajjhāyo anujānātīti (mahāva. 257).

“Here, venerable sir, while I was in seclusion, gone to a solitary place, this thought arose in my mind: ‘I have not seen the Blessed One face-to-face, but I have only heard of that Blessed One thus: ‘He is of such a nature, and of such a nature.’ If my preceptor would permit me, I would go to see that Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One.’”

“Sādhu sādhu, soṇa; gaccha tvaṃ, soṇa, taṃ bhagavantaṃ dassanāya arahantaṃ sammāsambuddhaṃ samāsambuddhanti (sabbattha). Dakkhissasi tvaṃ, soṇa, taṃ bhagavantaṃ pāsādikaṃ pasādanīyaṃ santindriyaṃ santamānasaṃ uttamadamathasamathamanuppattaṃ dantaṃ guttaṃ yatindriyaṃ nāgaṃ. Disvāna mama vacanena bhagavato pāde sirasā vandāhi, appābādhaṃ appātaṅkaṃ lahuṭṭhānaṃ balaṃ phāsuvihāraṃ phāsuvihārañca (sī.) puccha – ‘upajjhāyo me, bhante, āyasmā mahākaccāno bhagavato pāde sirasā vandati, appābādhaṃ appātaṅkaṃ lahuṭṭhānaṃ balaṃ phāsuvihāraṃ phāsuvihārañca (sī.) pucchatī’”ti.

“Good, good, Soṇa. Go, Soṇa, to see that Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One. You will see, Soṇa, that Blessed One—inspiring, pleasing, with peaceful faculties, with a peaceful mind, who has attained the highest taming and tranquility, disciplined, guarded, with restrained faculties, the unshakable one. Having seen him, with my words, bow with your head at the feet of the Blessed One, and ask about his freedom from illness, freedom from affliction, lightness in rising, strength, and comfortable dwelling: ‘My preceptor, venerable sir, the Venerable Mahākaccāna, bows with his head at the feet of the Blessed One and asks about his freedom from illness, freedom from affliction, lightness in rising, strength, and comfortable dwelling.’”

“Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho āyasmā soṇo āyasmato mahākaccānassa bhāsitaṃ abhinanditvā anumoditvā uṭṭhāyāsanā āyasmantaṃ mahākaccānaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā senāsanaṃ saṃsāmetvā pattacīvaramādāya yena sāvatthi tena cārikaṃ pakkāmi. Anupubbena cārikaṃ caramāno yena sāvatthi jetavanaṃ anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāmo, yena [P 0.0059] bhagavā tenupasaṅkami, upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho āyasmā soṇo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “upajjhāyo me, bhante, āyasmā mahākaccāno bhagavato pāde sirasā vandati, appābādhaṃ appātaṅkaṃ lahuṭṭhānaṃ balaṃ phāsuvihāraṃ phāsuvihārañca (sī.) pucchatī”ti.

“So be it, venerable sir.” Thus the Venerable Soṇa, delighting and rejoicing in the words of the Venerable Mahākaccāna, rose from his seat, paid homage to the Venerable Mahākaccāna, circumambulated him, set his lodging in order, took his bowl and robe, and set out on a journey toward Sāvatthī. Traveling in stages, he eventually arrived at Sāvatthī, at Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park, where the Blessed One was. He approached the Blessed One, paid homage to him, and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, the Venerable Soṇa said to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, my preceptor, the Venerable Mahākaccāna, bows with his head at the feet of the Blessed One and asks about his freedom from illness, freedom from affliction, lightness in rising, strength, and comfortable dwelling.”

“Kacci, bhikkhu, khamanīyaṃ, kacci yāpanīyaṃ, kaccisi appakilamathena addhānaṃ āgato, na ca piṇḍakena kilantosī”ti? “Khamanīyaṃ bhagavā, yāpanīyaṃ bhagavā, appakilamathena cāhaṃ, bhante, addhānaṃ āgato, na piṇḍakena kilantomhī”ti.

“Is it tolerable, bhikkhu, is it manageable? Have you come this long way without fatigue, and were you not weary from the alms-food?” “It is tolerable, Blessed One, it is manageable, Blessed One. I have come this long way without fatigue, venerable sir, and I was not weary from the alms-food.”

Atha [T 0.0165] kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi – “imassānanda, āgantukassa bhikkhuno senāsanaṃ paññāpehī”ti. Atha kho āyasmato ānandassa etadahosi – “yassa kho maṃ bhagavā āṇāpeti [M 0.0148] – ‘imassānanda, āgantukassa bhikkhuno senāsanaṃ paññāpehī’ti, icchati bhagavā tena bhikkhunā saddhiṃ ekavihāre vatthuṃ, icchati bhagavā āyasmatā soṇena saddhiṃ ekavihāre vatthu”nti. Yasmiṃ vihāre bhagavā viharati, tasmiṃ vihāre āyasmato soṇassa senāsanaṃ paññāpesi.

Then the Blessed One addressed the Venerable Ānanda: “Ānanda, prepare a lodging for this visiting bhikkhu.” Then this thought occurred to the Venerable Ānanda: “For whichever bhikkhu the Blessed One commands me to prepare a lodging, the Blessed One wishes to live in the same dwelling with that bhikkhu. The Blessed One wishes to live in the same dwelling with the Venerable Soṇa.” In the same dwelling where the Blessed One was staying, he prepared a lodging for the Venerable Soṇa.

Atha [V 0.0136] kho bhagavā bahudeva rattiṃ abbhokāse nisajjāya vītināmetvā pāde pakkhāletvā vihāraṃ pāvisi. Āyasmāpi kho soṇo bahudeva rattiṃ abbhokāse nisajjāya vītināmetvā pāde pakkhāletvā vihāraṃ pāvisi. Atha kho bhagavā rattiyā paccūsasamayaṃ paccuṭṭhāya āyasmantaṃ soṇaṃ ajjhesi – “paṭibhātu taṃ bhikkhu dhammo bhāsitu”nti.

Then the Blessed One, having spent much of the night sitting in the open, washed his feet and entered the dwelling. The Venerable Soṇa also, having spent much of the night sitting in the open, washed his feet and entered the dwelling. Then the Blessed One, having risen at dawn, invited the Venerable Soṇa: “May the Dhamma, bhikkhu, occur to you to be spoken.”

“Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho āyasmā soṇo bhagavato paṭissutvā soḷasa aṭṭhakavaggikāni sabbāneva sarena abhaṇi. Atha kho bhagavā āyasmato soṇassa sarabhaññapariyosāne abbhanumodi – “sādhu sādhu, bhikkhu, suggahitāni te, bhikkhu, soḷasa aṭṭhakavaggikāni sumanasikatāni sūpadhāritāni, kalyāṇiyāsi kalyāṇiyā ca (ka.), kalyāṇiyā cāsi (?) vācāya samannāgato vissaṭṭhāya anelagaḷāya atthassa viññāpaniyā. Kati vassosi tvaṃ, bhikkhū”ti? “Ekavasso ahaṃ bhagavā”ti. “Kissa pana tvaṃ[T 0.0166], bhikkhu, evaṃ ciraṃ akāsī”ti? “Ciraṃ diṭṭho ciradiṭṭho (sī.) me, bhante, kāmesu ādīnavo; api ca sambādho gharāvāso bahukicco bahukaraṇīyo”ti.

“So be it, venerable sir.” The Venerable Soṇa assented to the Blessed One and recited all sixteen sections of the Aṭṭhakavagga in full voice. Then, at the conclusion of the Venerable Soṇa’s recitation, the Blessed One expressed his approval: “Good, good, bhikkhu! These sixteen sections of the Aṭṭhakavagga are well-grasped by you, well-considered, and well-retained. You are endowed with a beautiful voice that is distinct, free from hoarseness, and makes the meaning clear. How many years have you been ordained, bhikkhu?” “I have been ordained for one year, Blessed One.” “But why, bhikkhu, did you delay so long?” “Venerable sir, I have long seen the danger in sensual pleasures; moreover, household life is confining, full of duties and many obligations.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood the meaning of this, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Disvā ādīnavaṃ loke, ñatvā dhammaṃ nirūpadhiṃ;

Ariyo na ramatī pāpe, pāpe na ramatī sucī”ti. chaṭṭhaṃ;

“Having seen the danger in the world, and knowing the Dhamma that is without substrate, the noble one does not delight in evil; the pure one does not delight in evil.” Sixth.

7. Kaṅkhārevatasuttaṃ

7. The Sutta on Kaṅkhārevata

47. Evaṃ [P 0.0060] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā kaṅkhārevato bhagavato avidūre nisinno hoti pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya attano kaṅkhāvitaraṇavisuddhiṃ paccavekkhamāno.

47. Thus have I heard. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling in Sāvatthī, in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. At that time, the Venerable Kaṅkhārevata was sitting not far from the Blessed One, having folded his legs crosswise, holding his body erect, and reflecting on his own purification through the overcoming of doubt.

Addasā [M 0.0149] kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ kaṅkhārevataṃ avidūre nisinnaṃ pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya attano kaṅkhāvitaraṇavisuddhiṃ paccavekkhamānaṃ.

The Blessed One saw the Venerable Kaṅkhārevata sitting not far away, having folded his legs crosswise, holding his body erect, and reflecting on his own purification through the overcoming of doubt.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood the meaning of this, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Yā [V 0.0137] kāci kaṅkhā idha vā huraṃ vā,

Sakavediyā vā paravediyā vā;

Ye jhāyino tā pajahanti sabbā,

Ātāpino brahmacariyaṃ carantā”ti. sattamaṃ;

“Whatever doubts there are, whether here or hereafter, whether concerning oneself or concerning others—those who are meditative and ardent, living the holy life, abandon them all.” Seventh.

8. Saṅghabhedasuttaṃ

8. The Sutta on Schism in the Saṅgha

48. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā rājagahe viharati [T 0.0167] veḷuvane kalandakanivāpe. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā ānando tadahuposathe pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya rājagahaṃ piṇḍāya pāvisi.

48. Thus have I heard. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling in Rājagaha, in the Bamboo Grove, the Squirrels’ Feeding Place. At that time, on that Uposatha day, in the morning, the Venerable Ānanda, having put on his lower robe and taking his bowl and outer robe, entered Rājagaha for alms.

Addasā kho devadatto āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ rājagahe piṇḍāya carantaṃ. Disvāna yenāyasmā ānando tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ etadavoca – “ajjatagge dānāhaṃ, āvuso ānanda, aññatreva bhagavatā aññatra bhikkhusaṅghā uposathaṃ karissāmi saṅghakammāni cā”ti.

Devadatta saw the Venerable Ānanda wandering for alms in Rājagaha. Having seen him, he went up to where the Venerable Ānanda was, and on arrival said this to him: “From this day forward, friend Ānanda, I will hold the Uposatha and perform the formal acts of the Saṅgha apart from the Blessed One and apart from the community of bhikkhus.”

Atha kho āyasmā ānando rājagahe piṇḍāya caritvā pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkanto yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho āyasmā ānando bhagavantaṃ etadavoca –

Then the Venerable Ānanda, having wandered for alms in Rājagaha, after his meal, on returning from his alms-round, went up to the Blessed One. Having approached and paid homage to the Blessed One, he sat down to one side. Seated to one side, the Venerable Ānanda said this to the Blessed One:

“Idhāhaṃ, bhante, pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya rājagahaṃ piṇḍāya pāvisi. Addasā kho maṃ, bhante, devadatto rājagahe piṇḍāya carantaṃ. Disvāna yenāhaṃ tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā maṃ etadavoca – ‘ajjatagge dānāhaṃ, āvuso ānanda, aññatreva bhagavatā aññatra bhikkhusaṅghā uposathaṃ karissāmi saṅghakammāni cā’ti. Ajja, bhante, devadatto saṅghaṃ bhindissati, uposathañca karissati saṅghakammāni cā”ti.

“Venerable sir, this morning, having dressed and taken my bowl and robes, I entered Rājagaha for alms. Devadatta saw me, venerable sir, wandering for alms in Rājagaha. Having seen me, he approached me and said: ‘From this day forward, friend Ānanda, I will now hold the Uposatha and perform the formal acts of the Saṅgha apart from the Blessed One and apart from the Saṅgha of monks.’ Today, venerable sir, Devadatta will divide the Saṅgha; he will hold the Uposatha and perform the formal acts of the Saṅgha.”

Atha [M 0.0150] kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood the meaning of this, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Sukaraṃ [P 0.0061] sādhunā sādhu, sādhu pāpena dukkaraṃ sukaraṃ sādhunā sādhuṃ, sādhuṃ pāpena dukkaraṃ (ka.);

Pāpaṃ [T 0.0168] pāpena sukaraṃ, pāpamariyehi dukkara”nti. aṭṭhamaṃ;

“Good is easy to do for the good; good is hard to do for the evil. Evil is easy to do for the evil; evil is hard to do for the noble.” Eighth.

9. Sadhāyamānasuttaṃ

9. The Discourse on Disputing

49. Evaṃ [V 0.0138] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā kosalesu cārikaṃ carati mahatā bhikkhusaṅghena saddhiṃ. Tena kho pana samayena sambahulā māṇavakā bhagavato avidūre sadhāyamānarūpā saddāyamānarūpā (syā. pī. aṭṭhakathāyaṃ pāṭhantaraṃ), pathāyamānarūpā (ka.), vadhāyamānarūpā (ka. sī., ka. aṭṭha.), saddhāyamānarūpā (?), saddhudhātuyā sadhudhātuyā vā siddhamidanti veditabbaṃ atikkamanti. Addasā kho bhagavā sambahule māṇavake avidūre sadhāyamānarūpe atikkante.

49. Thus have I heard: At one time the Blessed One was traveling through the Kosala country with a great Saṅgha of bhikkhus. At that time, a number of young men were passing not far from the Blessed One, appearing to be disputing. The Blessed One saw those young men passing nearby, appearing to be disputing.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion, uttered this inspired utterance:

“Parimuṭṭhā paṇḍitābhāsā, vācāgocarabhāṇino;

Yāvicchanti mukhāyāmaṃ, yena nītā na taṃ vidū”ti. navamaṃ;

“Unmindful, though appearing wise, speakers whose pasture is speech; they open their mouths as they wish, but know not that by which they are led.” Ninth.

10. Cūḷapanthakasuttaṃ

10. The Discourse on Cūḷapanthaka

50. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā cūḷapanthako cullapanthako (sī.), cūlapanthako (pī.) bhagavato avidūre nisinno hoti pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya parimukhaṃ satiṃ upaṭṭhapetvā.

50. Thus have I heard: At one time the Blessed One was dwelling in Sāvatthī at Jeta's Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika's Park. At that time, the Venerable Cūḷapanthaka was sitting not far from the Blessed One, having folded his legs crosswise, set his body erect, and established mindfulness before him.

Addasā kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ cūḷapanthakaṃ avidūre nisinnaṃ pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya parimukhaṃ satiṃ upaṭṭhapetvā.

The Blessed One saw the Venerable Cūḷapanthaka sitting not far away, having folded his legs crosswise, set his body erect, and established mindfulness before him.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion, uttered this inspired utterance:

“Ṭhitena [M 0.0151] kāyena ṭhitena cetasā,

Tiṭṭhaṃ nisinno uda vā sayāno;

Etaṃ evaṃ (ka.) satiṃ bhikkhu adhiṭṭhahāno,

Labhetha [T 0.0169] pubbāpariyaṃ visesaṃ;

Laddhāna pubbāpariyaṃ visesaṃ,

Adassanaṃ maccurājassa gacche”ti. dasamaṃ;

“With body steadfast and mind steadfast, whether standing, sitting, or lying down, a bhikkhu establishing such mindfulness would attain a special distinction in the former and latter stages. Having attained this special distinction, he would go beyond the sight of the King of Death.” Tenth.

Soṇavaggo soṇatheravaggo (syā. kaṃ. ka.) mahāvaggo (aṭṭhakathāya sameti) pañcamo niṭṭhito.

The Soṇa Chapter, the fifth, is concluded.

Tassuddānaṃ [V 0.0139] –

The summary thereof:

Piyo appāyukā kuṭṭhī, kumārakā uposatho;

Soṇo ca revato bhedo, sadhāya panthakena cāti.

The King, Short-livedness, the Leper, the Boys, the Uposatha; Soṇa and Revata, the Schism, Disputing, and with Panthaka.

6. Jaccandhavaggo

6. The Chapter on One Blind from Birth

1. Āyusaṅkhārossajjanasuttaṃ

1. The Discourse on Relinquishing the Life-Formation

51. Evaṃ [P 0.0062] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā vesāliyaṃ viharati mahāvane kūṭāgārasālāyaṃ. Atha kho bhagavā pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya vesāliṃ piṇḍāya pāvisi. Vesāliyaṃ piṇḍāya caritvā pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkanto āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi – “gaṇhāhi, ānanda, nisīdanaṃ. Yena cāpālaṃ pāvālaṃ (syā.) cetiyaṃ tenupasaṅkamissāma divāvihārāyā”ti.

51. Thus have I heard: At one time the Blessed One was dwelling in Vesālī, in the Great Wood, at the Hall with the Peaked Roof. Then the Blessed One, having dressed in the morning and taking his bowl and robe, entered Vesālī for alms. Having walked for alms in Vesālī and returning from the alms round after his meal, he addressed the Venerable Ānanda: “Take a sitting mat, Ānanda. We shall go to the Cāpāla Shrine for the day's sojourn.”

“Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho āyasmā ānando bhagavato paṭissutvā nisīdanaṃ ādāya bhagavantaṃ piṭṭhito piṭṭhito anubandhi. Atha kho bhagavā yena cāpālaṃ cetiyaṃ tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā paññatte āsane nisīdi. Nisajja [T 0.0170] kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi –

“Even so, venerable sir,” the Venerable Ānanda replied to the Blessed One. Taking a sitting mat, he followed closely behind the Blessed One. Then the Blessed One went to the Cāpāla Shrine; having approached, he sat down on the prepared seat. Having sat down, the Blessed One addressed the Venerable Ānanda:

“Ramaṇīyā, ānanda, vesālī; ramaṇīyaṃ udenaṃ cetiyaṃ; ramaṇīyaṃ gotamakaṃ cetiyaṃ; ramaṇīyaṃ sattambaṃ cetiyaṃ; ramaṇīyaṃ bahuputtaṃ cetiyaṃ; ramaṇīyaṃ sārandadaṃ cetiyaṃ; ramaṇīyaṃ cāpālaṃ cetiyaṃ. Yassa kassaci, ānanda[M 0.0152], cattāro iddhipādā bhāvitā bahulīkatā yānīkatā vatthukatā anuṭṭhitā paricitā susamāraddhā, so ākaṅkhamāno ( ) (ānanda) (ka.) kappaṃ vā tiṭṭheyya kappāvasesaṃ vā. Tathāgatassa kho, ānanda, cattāro iddhipādā bhāvitā bahulīkatā yānīkatā vatthukatā anuṭṭhitā paricitā susamāraddhā. Ākaṅkhamāno, ānanda, tathāgato kappaṃ vā tiṭṭheyya kappāvasesaṃ vā”ti.

“Delightful, Ānanda, is Vesālī; delightful is the Udena Shrine; delightful is the Gotamaka Shrine; delightful is the Sattamba Shrine; delightful is the Bahuputta Shrine; delightful is the Sārandada Shrine; delightful is the Cāpāla Shrine. Ānanda, anyone who has developed, cultivated, made a vehicle of, made a basis of, established, consolidated, and well undertaken the four bases of psychic power, could—if they so wished—remain for an aeon or the remainder of an aeon. The Tathāgata, Ānanda, has developed, cultivated, made a vehicle of, made a basis of, established, consolidated, and well undertaken the four bases of psychic power. If he so wished, Ānanda, the Tathāgata could remain for an aeon or the remainder of an aeon.”

Evampi [V 0.0140] kho āyasmā ānando bhagavatā oḷārike nimitte kayiramāne, oḷārike obhāse kayiramāne, nāsakkhi paṭivijjhituṃ; na bhagavantaṃ yāci – “tiṭṭhatu, bhante, bhagavā kappaṃ; tiṭṭhatu sugato kappaṃ bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussāna”nti, yathā taṃ mārena pariyuṭṭhitacitto[P 0.0063]. Dutiyampi kho…pe… [T 0.0171] tatiyampi kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi –

Even so, although the Blessed One was giving such a palpable sign, such a palpable indication, the Venerable Ānanda was unable to comprehend it; he did not request the Blessed One: ‘May the Blessed One remain for an aeon, venerable sir; may the Fortunate One remain for an aeon, for the welfare and happiness of the many, out of compassion for the world, for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of gods and humans’—as his mind was possessed by Māra. A second time… and a third time, the Blessed One addressed the Venerable Ānanda:

“Ramaṇīyā, ānanda, vesālī; ramaṇīyaṃ udenaṃ cetiyaṃ; ramaṇīyaṃ gotamakaṃ cetiyaṃ; ramaṇīyaṃ sattambaṃ cetiyaṃ; ramaṇīyaṃ bahuputtaṃ cetiyaṃ; ramaṇīyaṃ sārandadaṃ cetiyaṃ; ramaṇīyaṃ cāpālaṃ cetiyaṃ. Yassa kassaci, ānanda, cattāro iddhipādā bhāvitā bahulīkatā yānīkatā vatthukatā anuṭṭhitā paricitā susamāraddhā, so ākaṅkhamāno kappaṃ vā tiṭṭheyya kappāvasesaṃ vā. Tathāgatassa kho, ānanda, cattāro iddhipādā bhāvitā bahulīkatā yānīkatā vatthukatā anuṭṭhitā paricitā susamāraddhā. Ākaṅkhamāno, ānanda, tathāgato kappaṃ vā tiṭṭheyya kappāvasesaṃ [T 0.0172] vā”ti.

“Delightful, Ānanda, is Vesālī; delightful is the Udena Shrine; delightful is the Gotamaka Shrine; delightful is the Sattamba Shrine; delightful is the Bahuputta Shrine; delightful is the Sārandada Shrine; delightful is the Cāpāla Shrine. Ānanda, anyone who has developed, cultivated, made a vehicle of, made a basis of, established, consolidated, and well undertaken the four bases of psychic power, could—if they so wished—remain for an aeon or the remainder of an aeon. The Tathāgata, Ānanda, has developed, cultivated, made a vehicle of, made a basis of, established, consolidated, and well undertaken the four bases of psychic power. If he so wished, Ānanda, the Tathāgata could remain for an aeon or the remainder of an aeon.”

Evampi kho āyasmā ānando bhagavatā oḷārike nimitte kayiramāne, oḷārike obhāse kayiramāne, nāsakkhi paṭivijjhituṃ; na bhagavantaṃ yāci – “tiṭṭhatu, bhante, bhagavā kappaṃ; tiṭṭhatu sugato kappaṃ bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussāna”nti, yathā taṃ mārena pariyuṭṭhitacitto.

Even so, although the Blessed One gave such a palpable sign, such a palpable indication, the Venerable Ānanda was unable to comprehend; he did not request the Blessed One, "May the Blessed One remain for an aeon, venerable sir; may the Fortunate One remain for an aeon, for the welfare and happiness of the many, out of compassion for the world, for the good, benefit, and happiness of gods and humans"—as his mind was assailed by Māra.

Atha kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi – “gaccha tvaṃ, ānanda, yassadāni kālaṃ maññasī”ti. “Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho āyasmā ānando bhagavato [M 0.0153] paṭissutvā uṭṭhāyāsanā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā avidūre aññatarasmiṃ rukkhamūle nisīdi.

Then the Blessed One addressed the Venerable Ānanda: "You may go now, Ānanda, at the time you deem fit." "Even so, venerable sir," the Venerable Ānanda replied to the Blessed One. Rising from his seat, he paid homage to the Blessed One, circumambulated him with his right side toward him, and sat down at the foot of a certain tree not far away.

Atha kho māro pāpimā, acirapakkante āyasmante ānande, yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhāsi. Ekamantaṃ ṭhito kho māro pāpimā bhagavantaṃ etadavoca –

Then Māra the Evil One, soon after the Venerable Ānanda had departed, approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he stood to one side. Standing to one side, Māra the Evil One said this to the Blessed One:

“Parinibbātu dāni, bhante, bhagavā; parinibbātu sugato; parinibbānakālo dāni, bhante, bhagavato. Bhāsitā kho panesā, bhante, bhagavatā vācā – ‘na tāvāhaṃ, pāpima, parinibbāyissāmi [V 0.0141] yāva me bhikkhū na sāvakā bhavissanti viyattā vinītā visāradā visāradā pattayogakhemā (a. ni. 8.70), visāradappattā yogakhemā (sī. pī. ka.), visāradappattā yogakhemakāmā (syā.) bahussutā dhammadharā dhammānudhammappaṭipannā sāmīcippaṭipannā anudhammacārino, sakaṃ ācariyakaṃ uggahetvā ācikkhissanti desessanti paññapessanti paṭṭhapessanti vivarissanti vibhajissanti uttānīkarissanti [T 0.0173] uppannaṃ parappavādaṃ sahadhammena suniggahitaṃ niggahetvā sappāṭihāriyaṃ dhammaṃ desessantī’ti. Etarahi kho pana, bhante santi kho pana bhante etarahi (sī. pī. saṃ. ni. 5.822) bhikkhū bhagavato sāvakā viyattā vinītā visāradā bahussutā dhammadharā dhammānudhammappaṭipannā sāmīcippaṭipannā anudhammacārino sakaṃ ācariyakaṃ uggahetvā ācikkhanti desenti paññapenti paṭṭhapenti vivaranti vibhajanti uttānīkaronti uppannaṃ parappavādaṃ sahadhammena suniggahitaṃ niggahetvā sappāṭihāriyaṃ dhammaṃ desenti. Parinibbātu dāni, bhante, bhagavā; parinibbātu sugato; parinibbānakālo dāni, bhante, bhagavato.

“Now, venerable sir, let the Blessed One attain final Parinibbāna; let the Well-gone One attain final Parinibbāna. Now, venerable sir, is the time for the Blessed One's final Parinibbāna. For, venerable sir, this word was spoken by the Blessed One: ‘I will not attain final Parinibbāna, Evil One, so long as my disciples, the monks, have not become accomplished, disciplined, confident, learned, upholders of the Dhamma, practicing in accordance with the Dhamma, practicing rightly, and living in conformity with the Dhamma; so long as they, having learned their own teacher's doctrine, do not teach, preach, proclaim, establish, reveal, analyze, and make it clear; and so long as they, having thoroughly refuted opposing doctrines that have arisen in accordance with the Dhamma, do not teach the Dhamma with its wonders.’ But now, venerable sir, the Blessed One's disciples, the monks, are accomplished, disciplined, confident, learned, upholders of the Dhamma, practicing in accordance with the Dhamma, practicing rightly, and living in conformity with the Dhamma. Having learned their own teacher's doctrine, they teach, preach, proclaim, establish, reveal, analyze, and make it clear; and having thoroughly refuted opposing doctrines that have arisen in accordance with the Dhamma, they teach the Dhamma with its wonders. Now, venerable sir, let the Blessed One attain final Parinibbāna; let the Well-gone One attain final Parinibbāna. Now, venerable sir, is the time for the Blessed One's final Parinibbāna.”

“Bhāsitā kho panesā, bhante, bhagavatā vācā – ‘na tāvāhaṃ, pāpima, parinibbāyissāmi yāva me bhikkhuniyo na sāvikā bhavissanti viyattā vinītā visāradā bahussutā dhammadharā dhammānudhammappaṭipannā sāmīcippaṭipannā anudhammacāriniyo sakaṃ ācariyakaṃ uggahetvā ācikkhissanti desessanti paññapessanti paṭṭhapessanti vivarissanti vibhajissanti uttānīkarissanti uppannaṃ parappavādaṃ sahadhammena suniggahitaṃ niggahetvā sappāṭihāriyaṃ dhammaṃ desessantī’ti. Etarahi kho pana, bhante[P 0.0064], bhikkhuniyo bhagavato sāvikā viyattā vinītā visāradā bahussutā dhammadharā dhammānudhammappaṭipannā sāmīcippaṭipannā [M 0.0154] anudhammacāriniyo sakaṃ ācariyakaṃ uggahetvā ācikkhanti desenti paññapenti paṭṭhapenti vivaranti vibhajanti uttānīkaronti [T 0.0174] uppannaṃ parappavādaṃ sahadhammena suniggahitaṃ niggahetvā sappāṭihāriyaṃ dhammaṃ desenti. Parinibbātu dāni, bhante, bhagavā; parinibbātu sugato; parinibbānakālo dāni, bhante, bhagavato.

“For, venerable sir, this word was spoken by the Blessed One: ‘I will not attain final Parinibbāna, Evil One, so long as my disciples, the nuns, have not become accomplished, disciplined, confident, learned, upholders of the Dhamma, practicing in accordance with the Dhamma, practicing rightly, and living in conformity with the Dhamma; so long as they, having learned their own teacher's doctrine, do not teach, preach, proclaim, establish, reveal, analyze, and make it clear; and so long as they, having thoroughly refuted opposing doctrines that have arisen in accordance with the Dhamma, do not teach the Dhamma with its wonders.’ But now, venerable sir, the Blessed One's disciples, the nuns, are accomplished, disciplined, confident, learned, upholders of the Dhamma, practicing in accordance with the Dhamma, practicing rightly, and living in conformity with the Dhamma. Having learned their own teacher's doctrine, they teach, preach, proclaim, establish, reveal, analyze, and make it clear; and having thoroughly refuted opposing doctrines that have arisen in accordance with the Dhamma, they teach the Dhamma with its wonders. Now, venerable sir, let the Blessed One attain final Parinibbāna; let the Well-gone One attain final Parinibbāna. Now, venerable sir, is the time for the Blessed One's final Parinibbāna.”

“Bhāsitā kho panesā, bhante, bhagavatā vācā – ‘na tāvāhaṃ, pāpima, parinibbāyissāmi yāva me upāsakā na sāvakā bhavissanti viyattā vinītā visāradā bahussutā dhammadharā dhammānudhammappaṭipannā sāmīcippaṭipannā anudhammacārino sakaṃ ācariyakaṃ uggahetvā ācikkhissanti desessanti paññapessanti paṭṭhapessanti vivarissanti vibhajissanti uttānīkarissanti uppannaṃ parappavādaṃ sahadhammena suniggahitaṃ niggahetvā sappāṭihāriyaṃ dhammaṃ desessantī’ti. Etarahi kho pana, bhante, upāsakā bhagavato sāvakā viyattā vinītā visāradā bahussutā dhammadharā dhammānudhammappaṭipannā sāmīcippaṭipannā anudhammacārino sakaṃ ācariyakaṃ [V 0.0142] uggahetvā ācikkhanti desenti paññapenti paṭṭhapenti vivaranti vibhajanti uttānīkaronti uppannaṃ parappavādaṃ sahadhammena suniggahitaṃ niggahetvā sappāṭihāriyaṃ dhammaṃ desenti. Parinibbātu dāni, bhante, bhagavā; parinibbātu sugato; parinibbānakālo dāni, bhante, bhagavato.

“For, venerable sir, this word was spoken by the Blessed One: ‘I will not attain final Parinibbāna, Evil One, so long as my disciples, the male lay followers, have not become accomplished, disciplined, confident, learned, upholders of the Dhamma, practicing in accordance with the Dhamma, practicing rightly, and living in conformity with the Dhamma; so long as they, having learned their own teacher's doctrine, do not teach, preach, proclaim, establish, reveal, analyze, and make it clear; and so long as they, having thoroughly refuted opposing doctrines that have arisen in accordance with the Dhamma, do not teach the Dhamma with its wonders.’ But now, venerable sir, the Blessed One's disciples, the male lay followers, are accomplished, disciplined, confident, learned, upholders of the Dhamma, practicing in accordance with the Dhamma, practicing rightly, and living in conformity with the Dhamma. Having learned their own teacher's doctrine, they teach, preach, proclaim, establish, reveal, analyze, and make it clear; and having thoroughly refuted opposing doctrines that have arisen in accordance with the Dhamma, they teach the Dhamma with its wonders. Now, venerable sir, let the Blessed One attain final Parinibbāna; let the Well-gone One attain final Parinibbāna. Now, venerable sir, is the time for the Blessed One's final Parinibbāna.”

“Bhāsitā kho panesā, bhante, bhagavatā vācā – ‘na tāvāhaṃ, pāpima, parinibbāyissāmi yāva me upāsikā na sāvikā bhavissanti viyattā vinītā visāradā bahussutā dhammadharā [T 0.0175] dhammānudhammappaṭipannā sāmīcippaṭipannā anudhammacāriniyo sakaṃ ācariyakaṃ uggahetvā ācikkhissanti desessanti paññapessanti paṭṭhapessanti vivarissanti vibhajissanti uttānīkarissanti uppannaṃ parappavādaṃ sahadhammena suniggahitaṃ niggahetvā sappāṭihāriyaṃ dhammaṃ desessantī’ti. Etarahi kho pana, bhante, upāsikā bhagavato sāvikā viyattā vinītā visāradā bahussutā dhammadharā dhammānudhammappaṭipannā sāmīcippaṭipannā anudhammacāriniyo sakaṃ ācariyakaṃ uggahetvā ācikkhanti desenti paññapenti paṭṭhapenti vivaranti vibhajanti uttānīkaronti uppannaṃ parappavādaṃ sahadhammena suniggahitaṃ niggahetvā sappāṭihāriyaṃ dhammaṃ desenti. Parinibbātu dāni, bhante, bhagavā; parinibbātu sugato; parinibbānakālo dāni, bhante, bhagavato.

“For, venerable sir, this word was spoken by the Blessed One: ‘I will not attain final Parinibbāna, Evil One, so long as my female lay disciples have not become accomplished, disciplined, confident, learned, upholders of the Dhamma, practicing in accordance with the Dhamma, practicing rightly, and living in conformity with the Dhamma; so long as they, having learned their own teacher's doctrine, do not teach, preach, proclaim, establish, reveal, analyze, and make it clear; and so long as they, having thoroughly refuted opposing doctrines that have arisen in accordance with the Dhamma, do not teach the Dhamma with its wonders.’ But now, venerable sir, the Blessed One's female lay disciples are accomplished, disciplined, confident, learned, upholders of the Dhamma, practicing in accordance with the Dhamma, practicing rightly, and living in conformity with the Dhamma. Having learned their own teacher's doctrine, they teach, preach, proclaim, establish, reveal, analyze, and make it clear; and having thoroughly refuted opposing doctrines that have arisen in accordance with the Dhamma, they teach the Dhamma with its wonders. Now, venerable sir, let the Blessed One attain final Parinibbāna; let the Well-gone One attain final Parinibbāna. Now, venerable sir, is the time for the Blessed One's final Parinibbāna.”

“Bhāsitā [M 0.0155] kho panesā, bhante, bhagavatā vācā – ‘na tāvāhaṃ, pāpima, parinibbāyissāmi yāva me idaṃ brahmacariyaṃ na iddhañceva bhavissati phītañca vitthārikaṃ bāhujaññaṃ puthubhūtaṃ yāva devamanussehi suppakāsita’nti. Etarahi kho pana, bhante tayidaṃ bhante (saṃ. ni. 5.822), bhagavato brahmacariyaṃ iddhañceva phītañca vitthārikaṃ bāhujaññaṃ puthubhūtaṃ yāva devamanussehi suppakāsitaṃ. Parinibbātu dāni, bhante, bhagavā; parinibbātu sugato; parinibbānakālo dāni, bhante, bhagavato”ti.

“Venerable sir, this word was indeed spoken by the Blessed One: ‘Evil One, I will not attain final Nibbāna so long as this holy life of mine has not become prosperous, flourishing, widespread, known to many, and extensive, so long as it has not been well-proclaimed among gods and humans.’ But now, venerable sir, the Blessed One’s holy life is prosperous, flourishing, widespread, known to many, and extensive, and is well-proclaimed among gods and humans. Let the Blessed One now attain final Nibbāna, venerable sir; let the Well-gone One attain final Nibbāna. Now, venerable sir, is the time for the Blessed One’s final Nibbāna.”

Evaṃ vutte, bhagavā māraṃ pāpimantaṃ etadavoca – “appossukko [T 0.0176] tvaṃ, pāpima, hohi. Na ciraṃ tathāgatassa parinibbānaṃ bhavissati. Ito tiṇṇaṃ māsānaṃ accayena tathāgato parinibbāyissatī”ti.

When this was said, the Blessed One said this to Māra the Evil One: “Be without concern, Evil One. The Tathāgata’s final Nibbāna will take place before long. Three months from now, the Tathāgata will attain final Nibbāna.”

Atha kho bhagavā cāpāle cetiye sato sampajāno āyusaṅkhāraṃ ossajji. Ossaṭṭhe ca bhagavatā āyusaṅkhāre mahābhūmicālo ahosi bhiṃsanako lomahaṃso, devadundubhiyo devadudrabhiyo (ka.) ca phaliṃsu.

Then the Blessed One, at the Cāpāla Shrine, mindful and clearly comprehending, relinquished the life-force volition. And when the life-force volition was relinquished by the Blessed One, there was a great earthquake, fearsome and hair-raising, and the celestial drums resounded.

Atha [V 0.0143] kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood the meaning of this, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Tulamatulañca sambhavaṃ,

Bhavasaṅkhāramavassaji muni;

Ajjhattarato samāhito,

Abhindi kavacamivattasambhava”nti. paṭhamaṃ;

“The Sage relinquished the comparable and incomparable cause of becoming, and the volitional formation of existence. Delighting in the internal, well-composed, he shattered the self-originated armor as if it were a coat of mail.”

2. Sattajaṭilasuttaṃ

2. The Discourse on the Seven Matted-Hair Ascetics

52. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati pubbārāme migāramātupāsāde. Tena kho pana samayena [P 0.0065] bhagavā sāyanhasamayaṃ paṭisallānā vuṭṭhito bahidvārakoṭṭhake nisinno hoti. Atha kho rājā pasenadi kosalo yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi.

52. Thus have I heard. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling in Sāvatthī at the Eastern Park, in the Mansion of Migāra's Mother. Now at that time, the Blessed One, having risen from seclusion in the evening, was sitting outside the gatehouse. Then King Pasenadi of Kosala went to the Blessed One, and having approached and paid homage to the Blessed One, he sat down at one side.

Tena [M 0.0156] kho pana samayena satta ca jaṭilā, satta ca nigaṇṭhā, satta ca acelakā, satta ca ekasāṭakā, satta ca paribbājakā, parūḷhakacchanakhalomā khārivividhamādāya khārīvidhamādāya (ka. saṃ. ni. 1.122; dī. ni. 1.280) bhagavato avidūre atikkamanti.

Now at that time, seven matted-hair ascetics, seven Nigaṇṭhas, seven naked ascetics, seven single-garment ascetics, and seven wanderers, with overgrown armpit hair, body hair, and nails, carrying various requisites on a carrying pole, were passing by not far from the Blessed One.

Addasā kho rājā pasenadi kosalo te satta ca jaṭile, satta ca nigaṇṭhe, satta ca acelake, satta ca ekasāṭake, satta ca paribbājake[T 0.0177], parūḷhakacchanakhalome khārivividhamādāya bhagavato avidūre atikkamante. Disvāna uṭṭhāyāsanā ekaṃsaṃ uttarāsaṅgaṃ karitvā dakkhiṇajāṇumaṇḍalaṃ pathaviyaṃ paṭhaviyaṃ (sī. syā. pī.) nihantvā yena te satta ca jaṭilā, satta ca nigaṇṭhā, satta ca acelakā, satta ca ekasāṭakā, satta ca paribbājakā, tenañjaliṃ paṇāmetvā tikkhattuṃ nāmaṃ sāvesi – “rājāhaṃ, bhante, pasenadi kosalo; rājāhaṃ, bhante, pasenadi kosalo; rājāhaṃ, bhante, pasenadi kosalo”ti.

King Pasenadi of Kosala saw those seven matted-hair ascetics, seven Nigaṇṭhas, seven naked ascetics, seven single-garment ascetics, and seven wanderers, with overgrown armpit hair, body hair, and nails, carrying various requisites on a carrying pole, passing by not far from the Blessed One. Having seen them, he rose from his seat, arranged his upper robe over one shoulder, placed his right knee on the ground, and extending his joined palms toward those seven matted-hair ascetics, seven Nigaṇṭhas, seven naked ascetics, seven single-garment ascetics, and seven wanderers, he announced his name three times: “Venerable sirs, I am King Pasenadi of Kosala; venerable sirs, I am King Pasenadi of Kosala; venerable sirs, I am King Pasenadi of Kosala.”

Atha kho rājā pasenadi kosalo acirapakkantesu tesu sattasu ca jaṭilesu, sattasu ca nigaṇṭhesu, sattasu ca acelakesu, sattasu ca ekasāṭakesu, sattasu ca paribbājakesu, yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno [V 0.0144] kho rājā pasenadi kosalo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “ye kho ye ca kho (sī.), ye ca te (syā.), ye nu keci kho (pī.), ye te (saṃ. ni. 1.122), ye nu kho keci (?) bhante, loke arahanto vā arahattamaggaṃ vā samāpannā ete tesaṃ aññatare”ti aññatarāti (sī. ka.), aññataroti (syā. pī.).

Then, not long after those seven matted-hair ascetics, seven Nigaṇṭhas, seven naked ascetics, seven single-garment ascetics, and seven wanderers had departed, King Pasenadi of Kosala went to the Blessed One. Having approached and paid homage to the Blessed One, he sat down at one side. Seated at one side, King Pasenadi of Kosala said this to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, those men are among those in the world who are either arahants or have entered the path to arahantship.”

“Dujjānaṃ kho etaṃ, mahārāja, tayā gihinā kāmabhoginā puttasambādhasayanaṃ ajjhāvasantena kāsikacandanaṃ paccanubhontena mālāgandhavilepanaṃ dhārayantena jātarūparajataṃ sādiyantena – ime vā arahanto, ime vā arahattamaggaṃ samāpannāti.

“Great king, it is difficult for you—a layman enjoying sensual pleasures, living in a home crowded with children, using sandalwood from Kāsī, wearing garlands, scents, and ointments, and delighting in gold and silver—to know whether these are arahants or have entered the path to arahantship.”

“Saṃvāsena kho, mahārāja, sīlaṃ veditabbaṃ. Tañca kho dīghena addhunā na ittaraṃ na ittarena (syā. sī. syā. aṭṭha.), manasikarotā no amanasikarotā, paññavatā no duppaññena. Saṃvohārena kho, mahārāja, soceyyaṃ veditabbaṃ. Tañca kho dīghena addhunā [M 0.0157][T 0.0178] na ittaraṃ, manasikarotā no amanasikarotā, paññavatā no duppaññena. Āpadāsu kho, mahārāja, thāmo veditabbo. So ca kho dīghena addhunā na ittaraṃ, manasikarotā no amanasikarotā, paññavatā no duppaññena. Sākacchāya kho, mahārāja, paññā veditabbā. Sā ca kho dīghena addhunā na ittaraṃ, manasikarotā no amanasikarotā, paññavatā no duppaññenā”ti[P 0.0066].

“Great king, it is through living together that virtue is to be known. And that, indeed, after a long time, not a short one; by one who pays close attention, not by one who is inattentive; by one who is wise, not by one who is unwise. Great king, it is through interaction that purity is to be known. And that, indeed, after a long time, not a short one; by one who pays close attention, not by one who is inattentive; by one who is wise, not by one who is unwise. Great king, it is in times of adversity that fortitude is to be known. And that, indeed, after a long time, not a short one; by one who pays close attention, not by one who is inattentive; by one who is wise, not by one who is unwise. Great king, it is through discussion that wisdom is to be known. And that, indeed, after a long time, not a short one; by one who pays close attention, not by one who is inattentive; by one who is wise, not by one who is unwise.”

“Acchariyaṃ, bhante, abbhutaṃ, bhante! Yāva subhāsitaṃ cidaṃ subhāsitamidaṃ (saṃ. ni. 1.122), bhante, bhagavatā – ‘dujjānaṃ kho etaṃ, mahārāja, tayā gihinā puttasambādhasayanaṃ ajjhāvasantena kāsikacandanaṃ paccanubhontena mālāgandhavilepanaṃ dhārayantena jātarūparajataṃ sādiyantena – ime vā arahanto, ime vā arahattamaggaṃ samāpannāti. Saṃvāsena kho, mahārāja, sīlaṃ veditabbaṃ…pe… sākacchāya kho, mahārāja, paññā veditabbā. Sā ca kho dīghena addhunā na ittaraṃ, manasikarotā no [T 0.0179] amanasikarotā, paññavatā no duppaññenā’”ti.

“Wonderful, venerable sir! Marvelous, venerable sir! How well this has been spoken by the Blessed One: ‘Great king, it is difficult for you—a layman enjoying sensual pleasures, living in a home crowded with children, using sandalwood from Kāsī, wearing garlands, scents, and ointments, and delighting in gold and silver—to know whether “These are arahants,” or “These have entered the path to arahantship.” Great king, it is through living together that virtue is to be known… and it is through discussion that wisdom is to be known. And that, indeed, after a long time, not a short one; by one who pays close attention, not by one who is inattentive; by one who is wise, not by one who is unwise.’”

“Ete, bhante, mama purisā corā carā (saṃ. ni. 1.122) ocarakā janapadaṃ ocaritvā gacchanti. Tehi paṭhamaṃ ociṇṇaṃ ahaṃ pacchā osārissāmi otarissāmi (sī. syā. pī.), oyāyissāmi (sī. syā. aṭṭha.), osāpayissāmi (saṃ. ni. 1.122). Idāni te, bhante, taṃ rajojallaṃ pavāhetvā sunhātā suvilittā kappitakesamassū odātavatthavasanā pañcahi kāmaguṇehi samappitā samaṅgibhūtā paricāressantī” cāriyanti (syā.) ti.

“Venerable sir, these men of mine are thieves and spies who go about investigating the countryside. I will later act upon what they have first investigated. Now, venerable sir, having washed away that dust and dirt, having bathed well and anointed themselves well, with their hair and beards trimmed, and wearing white clothes, they will enjoy themselves, furnished and endowed with the five strands of sensual pleasure.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, having understood this matter, on that occasion the Blessed One uttered this inspired utterance:

“Na [V 0.0145] vāyameyya sabbattha, nāññassa puriso siyā;

Nāññaṃ nissāya jīveyya, dhammena na vaṇiṃ vāṇiṃ (sī.), vaṇī (syā. pī.), vāṇijaṃ (ka.) care”ti. dutiyaṃ;

“One should not strive in all evil matters, nor be another’s man; one should not live dependent on another, nor trade with the Dhamma.” This is the second.

3. Paccavekkhaṇasuttaṃ

3. The Discourse on Reflection

53. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā attano aneke [M 0.0158] pāpake akusale dhamme pahīne paccavekkhamāno nisinno hoti, aneke ca kusale dhamme bhāvanāpāripūriṃ gate.

53. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī, in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s monastery. At that time the Blessed One was seated, reflecting on the many evil, unwholesome qualities he had abandoned and on the many wholesome qualities that had reached full maturity through development.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā (sī. ka.) attano aneke pāpake akusale dhamme pahīne viditvā aneke ca kusale dhamme bhāvanāpāripūriṃ gate etamatthaṃ viditvā (sī. ka.) tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, knowing that many evil, unwholesome qualities had been abandoned in himself and that many wholesome qualities had reached full maturity through development, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Ahu pubbe tadā nāhu, nāhu pubbe tadā ahu;

Na cāhu na ca bhavissati, na cetarahi vijjatī”ti. tatiyaṃ;

“What was before, then was no more; what was not before, then came to be. It neither was nor will be, nor is it found now.” This is the third.

4. Paṭhamanānātitthiyasuttaṃ

4. The First Discourse on Various Sectarians

54. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena sambahulā [T 0.0180] nānātitthiyasamaṇabrāhmaṇaparibbājakā [P 0.0067] sāvatthiyaṃ paṭivasanti nānādiṭṭhikā nānākhantikā nānārucikā nānādiṭṭhinissayanissitā.

54. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī, in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s monastery. At that time, many ascetics, brahmins, and wanderers of various sects were living in Sāvatthī, holding various views, various convictions, various inclinations, and relying on various doctrinal foundations.

Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “sassato loko, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “asassato loko, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “antavā loko, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “anantavā loko, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “taṃ jīvaṃ taṃ sarīraṃ, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “aññaṃ jīvaṃ aññaṃ sarīraṃ, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “hoti tathāgato paraṃ maraṇā[V 0.0146], idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “na hoti tathāgato paraṃ maraṇā, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “hoti ca na ca hoti tathāgato paraṃ maraṇā, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke [M 0.0159] samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “neva hoti na na hoti tathāgato paraṃ maraṇā, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti.

There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine and view: “The world is eternal. This alone is true; anything else is worthless.” There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine and view: “The world is not eternal. This alone is true; anything else is worthless.” There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine and view: “The world is finite. This alone is true; anything else is worthless.” There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine and view: “The world is infinite. This alone is true; anything else is worthless.” There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine and view: “The soul and the body are the same. This alone is true; anything else is worthless.” There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine and view: “The soul and the body are different. This alone is true; anything else is worthless.” There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine and view: “The Tathāgata exists after death. This alone is true; anything else is worthless.” There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine and view: “The Tathāgata does not exist after death. This alone is true; anything else is worthless.” There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine and view: “The Tathāgata both exists and does not exist after death. This alone is true; anything else is worthless.” There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine and view: “The Tathāgata neither exists nor does not exist after death. This alone is true; anything else is worthless.”

Te bhaṇḍanajātā [T 0.0181] kalahajātā vivādāpannā aññamaññaṃ mukhasattīhi vitudantā viharanti – “ediso dhammo, nediso dhammo; nediso dhammo, ediso dhammo”ti.

They live engaged in disputes, quarrels, and altercations, stabbing each other with verbal darts, saying: “This is the Dhamma, that is not the Dhamma; that is not the Dhamma, this is the Dhamma.”

Atha kho sambahulā bhikkhū pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya sāvatthiṃ piṇḍāya pāvisiṃsu. Sāvatthiyaṃ piṇḍāya caritvā pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkantā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu. Ekamantaṃ nisinnā kho te bhikkhū bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ –

Then, in the morning, a number of bhikkhus, having dressed, took their bowls and robes, and entered Sāvatthī for alms. Having wandered for alms in Sāvatthī, after the meal, having returned from their alms-round, they approached the Blessed One. Having approached, they paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, those bhikkhus said this to the Blessed One:

“Idha, bhante, sambahulā nānātitthiyasamaṇabrāhmaṇaparibbājakā sāvatthiyaṃ paṭivasanti nānādiṭṭhikā nānākhantikā nānārucikā nānādiṭṭhinissayanissitā.

“Venerable sir, here in Sāvatthī many ascetics, brahmins, and wanderers of various sects are living, having various views, various convictions, various inclinations, and relying on various supports for their views.”

“Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – ‘sassato loko, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña’nti…pe… [T 0.0182] te bhaṇḍanajātā kalahajātā vivādāpannā aññamaññaṃ mukhasattīhi vitudantā viharanti – ‘ediso dhammo, nediso dhammo; nediso dhammo, ediso dhammo’” ti.

“There are some ascetics and brahmins who hold such doctrines and views: ‘The world is eternal. This alone is truth, all else is empty.’ … They live engaged in quarrels, strife, and disputes, piercing one another with verbal spears: ‘This is Dhamma, this is not Dhamma; this is not Dhamma, this is Dhamma.’”

“Aññatitthiyā, bhikkhave, paribbājakā andhā acakkhukā; atthaṃ na jānanti, anatthaṃ na jānanti, dhammaṃ na jānanti, adhammaṃ [P 0.0068] na jānanti. Te atthaṃ ajānantā anatthaṃ ajānantā dhammaṃ ajānantā adhammaṃ ajānantā bhaṇḍanajātā kalahajātā vivādāpannā aññamaññaṃ mukhasattīhi vitudantā viharanti – ‘ediso dhammo, nediso dhammo; nediso dhammo, ediso dhammo’”ti.

Bhikkhus, the wanderers of other sects are blind, without eyes. They do not know what is beneficial, they do not know what is harmful; they do not know the Dhamma, they do not know what is not the Dhamma. Not knowing what is beneficial, not knowing what is harmful, not knowing the Dhamma, and not knowing what is not the Dhamma, they live engaged in disputes, quarrels, and altercations, piercing one another with verbal spears, saying: ‘This is the Dhamma, that is not the Dhamma; that is not the Dhamma, this is the Dhamma.’”

“Bhūtapubbaṃ, bhikkhave, imissāyeva sāvatthiyā aññataro rājā ahosi. Atha kho, bhikkhave, so rājā aññataraṃ purisaṃ āmantesi – ‘ehi tvaṃ, ambho purisa, yāvatakā sāvatthiyā jaccandhā te sabbe ekajjhaṃ sannipātehī’ti. ‘Evaṃ, devā’ti kho, bhikkhave, so puriso [V 0.0147] tassa rañño paṭissutvā yāvatakā sāvatthiyā jaccandhā te [T 0.0183] sabbe gahetvā yena so rājā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā taṃ rājānaṃ etadavoca – ‘sannipātitā kho te, deva, yāvatakā sāvatthiyā jaccandhā’ti[M 0.0160]. ‘Tena hi, bhaṇe, jaccandhānaṃ hatthiṃ dassehī’ti. ‘Evaṃ, devā’ti kho, bhikkhave, so puriso tassa rañño paṭissutvā jaccandhānaṃ hatthiṃ dassesi.

Formerly, Bhikkhus, in this very Sāvatthī, there was a certain king. Then, Bhikkhus, that king addressed a certain man: ‘Come, good man, gather together in one place all those in Sāvatthī who are blind from birth.’ ‘Yes, Your Majesty,’ that man replied to the king. Having assented to the king, he gathered all those in Sāvatthī who were blind from birth and approached that king. Having approached, he said this to that king: ‘Your Majesty, all those in Sāvatthī who are blind from birth have been gathered together.’ ‘In that case, my man, show the elephant to those who are blind from birth.’ ‘Yes, Your Majesty,’ that man replied to the king, and having assented to the king, he showed the elephant to those who are blind from birth.

“Ekaccānaṃ jaccandhānaṃ hatthissa sīsaṃ dassesi – ‘ediso, jaccandhā, hatthī’ti. Ekaccānaṃ jaccandhānaṃ hatthissa kaṇṇaṃ dassesi – ‘ediso, jaccandhā, hatthī’ti. Ekaccānaṃ jaccandhānaṃ hatthissa dantaṃ dassesi – ‘ediso, jaccandhā, hatthī’ti. Ekaccānaṃ jaccandhānaṃ hatthissa soṇḍaṃ dassesi – ‘ediso, jaccandhā, hatthī’ti. Ekaccānaṃ jaccandhānaṃ hatthissa kāyaṃ dassesi – ‘ediso, jaccandhā, hatthī’ti. Ekaccānaṃ jaccandhānaṃ hatthissa pādaṃ dassesi – ‘ediso, jaccandhā, hatthī’ti. Ekaccānaṃ jaccandhānaṃ hatthissa satthiṃ piṭṭhiṃ (syā.) dassesi – ‘ediso, jaccandhā, hatthī’ti. Ekaccānaṃ jaccandhānaṃ hatthissa naṅguṭṭhaṃ dassesi – ‘ediso, jaccandhā, hatthī’ti. Ekaccānaṃ jaccandhānaṃ hatthissa vāladhiṃ dassesi – ‘ediso, jaccandhā, hatthī’”ti.

To some of those blind from birth he showed the elephant’s head, saying: ‘The elephant is like this, O blind ones.’ To some of those blind from birth he showed the elephant’s ear, saying: ‘The elephant is like this, O blind ones.’ To some of those blind from birth he showed the elephant’s tusk, saying: ‘The elephant is like this, O blind ones.’ To some of those blind from birth he showed the elephant’s trunk, saying: ‘The elephant is like this, O blind ones.’ To some of those blind from birth he showed the elephant’s body, saying: ‘The elephant is like this, O blind ones.’ To some of those blind from birth he showed the elephant’s foot, saying: ‘The elephant is like this, O blind ones.’ To some of those blind from birth he showed the elephant’s thigh, saying: ‘The elephant is like this, O blind ones.’ To some of those blind from birth he showed the base of the elephant’s tail, saying: ‘The elephant is like this, O blind ones.’ To some of those blind from birth he showed the tip of the elephant’s tail, saying: ‘The elephant is like this, O blind ones.’”

“Atha kho, bhikkhave, so puriso jaccandhānaṃ hatthiṃ dassetvā yena so rājā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā taṃ rājānaṃ etadavoca – ‘diṭṭho kho tehi, deva, jaccandhehi hatthī; yassa dāni kālaṃ maññasī’ti.

Then, Bhikkhus, that man, having shown the elephant to those who were blind from birth, approached that king. Having approached, he said this to that king: ‘Your Majesty, the elephant has been seen by those who are blind from birth. Now is the time for whatever you deem fitting.’”

“Atha kho, bhikkhave, so rājā yena te jaccandhā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā te jaccandhe etadavoca – ‘diṭṭho vo, jaccandhā, hatthī’ti[T 0.0184]? ‘Evaṃ, deva, diṭṭho no hatthī’ti. ‘Vadetha, jaccandhā, kīdiso hatthī’ti?

Then, Bhikkhus, that king approached those who were blind from birth. Having approached, he said this to them: ‘Have you seen the elephant, O blind ones?’ ‘Yes, Your Majesty, we have seen the elephant.’ ‘Then tell me, O blind ones, what is the elephant like?’”

“Yehi, bhikkhave, jaccandhehi hatthissa sīsaṃ diṭṭhaṃ ahosi, te evamāhaṃsu – ‘ediso, deva, hatthī seyyathāpi kumbho’ti.

Bhikkhus, those blind from birth who had perceived the elephant’s head said this: ‘Your Majesty, the elephant is like a pot.’”

“Yehi, bhikkhave, jaccandhehi hatthissa kaṇṇo diṭṭho ahosi, te evamāhaṃsu – ‘ediso, deva, hatthī seyyathāpi suppo’ti.

Bhikkhus, those blind from birth who had perceived the elephant’s ear said this: ‘Your Majesty, the elephant is like a winnowing basket.’”

“Yehi, bhikkhave, jaccandhehi hatthissa danto diṭṭho ahosi, te evamāhaṃsu [P 0.0069] – ‘ediso, deva, hatthī seyyathāpi khīlo’ti.

Bhikkhus, those blind from birth who had perceived the elephant’s tusk said this: ‘Your Majesty, the elephant is like a stake.’”

“Yehi[V 0.0148], bhikkhave, jaccandhehi hatthissa soṇḍo diṭṭho ahosi, te evamāhaṃsu – ‘ediso, deva, hatthī seyyathāpi naṅgalīsā’ti.

Bhikkhus, those blind from birth who had perceived the elephant’s trunk said this: ‘Your Majesty, the elephant is like the pole of a plough.’”

“Yehi, bhikkhave, jaccandhehi hatthissa kāyo diṭṭho ahosi, te evamāhaṃsu – ‘ediso, deva, hatthī seyyathāpi koṭṭho’ti.

Bhikkhus, those blind from birth who had perceived the elephant’s body said this: ‘Your Majesty, the elephant is like a granary.’”

“Yehi[M 0.0161], bhikkhave, jaccandhehi hatthissa pādo diṭṭho ahosi, te evamāhaṃsu – ‘ediso, deva, hatthī seyyathāpi thūṇo’ti.

Bhikkhus, those blind from birth who had perceived the elephant’s foot said this: ‘Your Majesty, the elephant is like a pillar.’”

“Yehi, bhikkhave, jaccandhehi hatthissa satthi diṭṭho piṭṭhi diṭṭā (ka. sī. syā. pī.), satthi diṭṭhā (ka. sī.) hosi, te evamāhaṃsu – ‘ediso, deva, hatthī seyyathāpi udukkhalo’ti.

Bhikkhus, those blind from birth who had perceived the elephant’s thigh said this: ‘Your Majesty, the elephant is like a mortar.’”

“Yehi, bhikkhave, jaccandhehi hatthissa naṅguṭṭhaṃ diṭṭhaṃ ahosi, te evamāhaṃsu – ‘ediso, deva, hatthī seyyathāpi musalo’ti.

Bhikkhus, those blind from birth who had perceived the base of the elephant’s tail said this: ‘Your Majesty, the elephant is like a pestle.’”

“Yehi, bhikkhave, jaccandhehi hatthissa vāladhi diṭṭho ahosi, te evamāhaṃsu – ‘ediso, deva, hatthī seyyathāpi sammajjanī’ti.

Bhikkhus, those blind from birth who had perceived the tip of the elephant’s tail said this: ‘Your Majesty, the elephant is like a broom.’”

“Te ‘ediso hatthī, nediso hatthī; nediso hatthī, ediso hatthī’”ti aññamaññaṃ muṭṭhīhi saṃsumbhiṃsu saṃyujjhiṃsu (ka. sī., syā. pī.). Tena ca pana, bhikkhave, so rājā attamano ahosi.

Saying, ‘The elephant is like this! The elephant is not like this! The elephant is not like this! The elephant is like this!’, they came to blows with their fists. And at this, Bhikkhus, that king was delighted.

“Evameva kho, bhikkhave, aññatitthiyā [T 0.0185] paribbājakā andhā acakkhukā. Te atthaṃ na jānanti anatthaṃ na jānanti, dhammaṃ na jānanti adhammaṃ na jānanti. Te atthaṃ ajānantā anatthaṃ ajānantā, dhammaṃ ajānantā adhammaṃ ajānantā bhaṇḍanajātā kalahajātā vivādāpannā aññamaññaṃ mukhasattīhi vitudantā viharanti – ‘ediso dhammo, nediso dhammo; nediso dhammo, ediso dhammo’”ti.

“In the very same way, Bhikkhus, the wanderers of other sects are blind, without vision. They do not know what is beneficial and they do not know what is not beneficial; they do not know the Dhamma and they do not know what is not the Dhamma. Not knowing what is beneficial and what is not beneficial, not knowing the Dhamma and what is not the Dhamma, having become quarrelsome, contentious, and fallen into disputes, they dwell piercing one another with verbal spears, saying: ‘The Dhamma is like this! The Dhamma is not like this! The Dhamma is not like this! The Dhamma is like this!’”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Imesu kira sajjanti, eke samaṇabrāhmaṇā;

Viggayha naṃ vivadanti, janā ekaṅgadassino”ti. catutthaṃ;

“Indeed, some ascetics and brahmins cling to these views; having grasped their view, people who see only one aspect dispute.”

5. Dutiyanānātitthiyasuttaṃ

5. The Second Discourse on Various Sectarians

55. Evaṃ [V 0.0149] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena sambahulā nānātitthiyasamaṇabrāhmaṇaparibbājakā sāvatthiyaṃ paṭivasanti nānādiṭṭhikā nānākhantikā nānārucikā nānādiṭṭhinissayanissitā.

55. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. At that time, many ascetics, brahmins, and wanderers of various sects, holding diverse views, diverse inclinations, diverse preferences, and relying on diverse views as a basis, were dwelling in Sāvatthī.

Santeke [M 0.0162] samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “sassato attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “asassato attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “sassato ca asassato ca sassato asassato (sī.) attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke [T 0.0186] samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “neva sassato nāsassato attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “sayaṃkato attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “paraṃkato attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “sayaṃkato ca paraṃkato ca sayaṃkato paraṃkato (sī.) attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “asayaṃkāro aparaṃkāro asayaṃkāro ca aparaṃkāro ca (syā. pī.) adhiccasamuppanno attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “sassataṃ sukhadukkhaṃ attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “asassataṃ sukhadukkhaṃ attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santeke [P 0.0070] samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “sassatañca asassatañca sassataṃ asassataṃ (sī.) sukhadukkhaṃ attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “neva sassataṃ nāsassataṃ sukhadukkhaṃ attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “sayaṃkataṃ sukhadukkhaṃ attā ca [T 0.0187] loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evadiṭṭhino – “paraṃkataṃ sukhadukkhaṃ attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “sayaṃkatañca paraṃkatañca sayaṃkathaṃ paraṃkataṃ (sī.) sukhadukkhaṃ attā ca loko [M 0.0163] ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke samaṇabrāhmaṇā [V 0.0150] evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “asayaṃkāraṃ aparaṃkāraṃ adhiccasamuppannaṃ sukhadukkhaṃ attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti.

There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine, this view: ‘The self and the world are eternal; this alone is true, anything else is futile.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine, this view: ‘The self and the world are not eternal; this alone is true, anything else is futile.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine, this view: ‘The self and the world are both eternal and not eternal; this alone is true, anything else is futile.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine, this view: ‘The self and the world are neither eternal nor not eternal; this alone is true, anything else is futile.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine, this view: ‘The self and the world are self-made; this alone is true, anything else is futile.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine, this view: ‘The self and the world are made by another; this alone is true, anything else is futile.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine, this view: ‘The self and the world are both self-made and made by another; this alone is true, anything else is futile.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine, this view: ‘The self and the world are not self-made, not made by another, and have arisen without a cause; this alone is true, anything else is futile.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine, this view: ‘Pleasure and pain, the self and the world are eternal; this alone is true, anything else is futile.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine, this view: ‘Pleasure and pain, the self and the world are not eternal; this alone is true, anything else is futile.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine, this view: ‘Pleasure and pain, the self and the world are both eternal and not eternal; this alone is true, anything else is futile.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine, this view: ‘Pleasure and pain, the self and the world are neither eternal nor not eternal; this alone is true, anything else is futile.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine, this view: ‘Pleasure and pain, the self and the world are self-made; this alone is true, anything else is futile.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine, this view: ‘Pleasure and pain, the self and the world are made by another; this alone is true, anything else is futile.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine, this view: ‘Pleasure and pain, the self and the world are both self-made and made by another; this alone is true, anything else is futile.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine, this view: ‘Pleasure and pain, the self and the world are not self-made, not made by another, and have arisen without a cause; this alone is true, anything else is futile.’

Te bhaṇḍanajātā kalahajātā vivādāpannā aññamaññaṃ mukhasattīhi vitudantā viharanti – “ediso dhammo, nediso dhammo; nediso dhammo, ediso dhammo”ti.

Having become quarrelsome, contentious, and fallen into dispute, they dwell piercing each other with verbal spears, saying: ‘The Dhamma is of this nature, the Dhamma is not of this nature; the Dhamma is not of this nature, the Dhamma is of this nature.’”

Atha kho sambahulā bhikkhū pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya sāvatthiṃ piṇḍāya pāvisiṃsu. Sāvatthiyaṃ piṇḍāya caritvā pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkantā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu. Ekamantaṃ nisinnā kho te bhikkhū bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ –

Then, a number of monks, having dressed in the morning and taking their bowls and robes, entered Sāvatthī for alms. Having gone for alms in Sāvatthī and returned from the alms-round after the meal, they approached the Blessed One. Having approached and paid homage to the Blessed One, they sat down to one side. Seated to one side, those monks said this to the Blessed One:

“Idha, bhante, sambahulā nānātitthiyasamaṇabrāhmaṇaparibbājakā sāvatthiyaṃ paṭivasanti nānādiṭṭhikā nānākhantikā nānārucikā nānādiṭṭhinissayanissitā.
