“Venerable Sir, here in Sāvatthī dwell many ascetics, brahmins, and wanderers of various sects, holding diverse views, diverse inclinations, diverse preferences, and relying on diverse views as a basis.

“Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – ‘sassato attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña’nti…pe… te bhaṇḍanajātā kalahajātā [T 0.0188] vivādāpannā aññamaññaṃ mukhasattīhi vitudantā viharanti – ‘ediso dhammo, nediso dhammo; nediso dhammo, ediso dhammo’”ti.

“There are some ascetics and brahmins who have this doctrine, this view: ‘The self and the world are eternal; this alone is true, anything else is futile.’ … Having become quarrelsome, contentious, and fallen into dispute, they dwell piercing each other with verbal spears, saying: ‘The Dhamma is of this nature, the Dhamma is not of this nature; the Dhamma is not of this nature, the Dhamma is of this nature.’”

“Aññatitthiyā, bhikkhave, paribbājakā andhā acakkhukā; atthaṃ na jānanti anatthaṃ na jānanti, dhammaṃ na jānanti adhammaṃ na jānanti. Te atthaṃ ajānantā anatthaṃ ajānantā, dhammaṃ ajānantā adhammaṃ ajānantā bhaṇḍanajātā kalahajātā vivādāpannā aññamaññaṃ mukhasattīhi vitudantā viharanti – ‘ediso dhammo, nediso dhammo; nediso dhammo, ediso dhammo’”ti.

“Bhikkhus, the wanderers of other sects are blind, without vision. They do not know the meaning, they do not know what is not the meaning; they do not know the Dhamma, they do not know what is not the Dhamma. Not knowing the meaning and what is not the meaning, not knowing the Dhamma and what is not the Dhamma, having become quarrelsome, contentious, and fallen into dispute, they dwell piercing each other with verbal spears, saying: ‘The Dhamma is of this nature, the Dhamma is not of this nature; the Dhamma is not of this nature, the Dhamma is of this nature.’”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Imesu kira sajjanti, eke samaṇabrāhmaṇā;

Antarāva visīdanti, appatvāva tamogadha”nti. pañcamaṃ;

“Indeed, some ascetics and brahmins are attached to these views; they sink down midway, without having reached the goal beyond darkness.”

6. Tatiyanānātitthiyasuttaṃ

6. The Third Discourse on Various Sectarians

56. Evaṃ [M 0.0164][V 0.0151] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena sambahulā nānātitthiyasamaṇabrāhmaṇaparibbājakā sāvatthiyaṃ paṭivasanti nānādiṭṭhikā nānākhantikā nānārucikā nānādiṭṭhinissayanissitā.

56. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī, in Jeta's Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika's monastery. Now on that occasion, many ascetics, brahmins, and wanderers of various other sects were residing in Sāvatthī, holding diverse views, diverse inclinations, diverse preferences, and relying on the support of diverse views.

Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “sassato attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “asassato attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “sassato ca asassato ca [T 0.0189] attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “neva sassato nāsassato attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “sayaṃkato attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “paraṃkato attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “sayaṃkato ca paraṃkato ca attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “asayaṃkāro aparaṃkāro adhiccasamuppanno attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “sassataṃ sukhadukkhaṃ attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “asassataṃ sukhadukkhaṃ attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “sassatañca asassatañca sukhadukkhaṃ attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “neva sassataṃ nāsassataṃ sukhadukkhaṃ attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino [T 0.0190] evaṃdiṭṭhino – “sayaṃkataṃ sukhadukkhaṃ attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “paraṃkataṃ sukhadukkhaṃ attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti[M 0.0165]. Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “sayaṃkatañca paraṃkatañca sukhadukkhaṃ attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti. Santi paneke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – “asayaṃkāraṃ aparaṃkāraṃ adhiccasamuppannaṃ sukhadukkhaṃ attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti.

There are some ascetics and brahmins who hold such a view and declare: ‘The self and the world are eternal; this alone is true, all else is false.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who hold such a view and declare: ‘The self and the world are not eternal; this alone is true, all else is false.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who hold such a view and declare: ‘The self and the world are both eternal and not eternal; this alone is true, all else is false.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who hold such a view and declare: ‘The self and the world are neither eternal nor not eternal; this alone is true, all else is false.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who hold such a view and declare: ‘The self and the world are self-made; this alone is true, all else is false.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who hold such a view and declare: ‘The self and the world are made by another; this alone is true, all else is false.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who hold such a view and declare: ‘The self and the world are both self-made and made by another; this alone is true, all else is false.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who hold such a view and declare: ‘The self and the world are not self-made, not made by another, and have arisen without cause; this alone is true, all else is false.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who hold such a view and declare: ‘Pleasure and pain, the self, and the world are eternal; this alone is true, all else is false.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who hold such a view and declare: ‘Pleasure and pain, the self, and the world are not eternal; this alone is true, all else is false.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who hold such a view and declare: ‘Pleasure and pain, the self, and the world are both eternal and not eternal; this alone is true, all else is false.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who hold such a view and declare: ‘Pleasure and pain, the self, and the world are neither eternal nor not eternal; this alone is true, all else is false.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who hold such a view and declare: ‘Pleasure and pain, the self, and the world are self-made; this alone is true, all else is false.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who hold such a view and declare: ‘Pleasure and pain, the self, and the world are made by another; this alone is true, all else is false.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who hold such a view and declare: ‘Pleasure and pain, the self, and the world are both self-made and made by another; this alone is true, all else is false.’ There are some ascetics and brahmins who hold such a view and declare: ‘Pleasure and pain, the self, and the world are not self-made, not made by another, and have arisen without cause; this alone is true, all else is false.’”

Te [V 0.0152] bhaṇḍanajātā kalahajātā vivādāpannā aññamaññaṃ mukhasattīhi vitudantā viharanti – “ediso dhammo, nediso dhammo; nediso dhammo, ediso dhammo”ti.

Having become quarrelsome, contentious, and fallen into dispute, they dwell piercing each other with verbal spears, saying: ‘The Dhamma is of this nature, the Dhamma is not of this nature; the Dhamma is not of this nature, the Dhamma is of this nature.’

Atha kho sambahulā bhikkhū pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya sāvatthiṃ piṇḍāya pāvisiṃsu. Sāvatthiyaṃ piṇḍāya caritvā pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkantā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu. Ekamantaṃ nisinnā kho te bhikkhū bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ –

Then, in the morning, a number of bhikkhus, having dressed and taken their bowls and robes, entered Sāvatthī for alms. After going for alms in Sāvatthī and returning from their alms round after their meal, they approached the Blessed One. Having approached, they paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. As they were sitting to one side, those bhikkhus said this to the Blessed One:

“Idha, bhante, sambahulā nānātitthiyasamaṇabrāhmaṇaparibbājakā sāvatthiyaṃ paṭivasanti nānādiṭṭhikā nānākhantikā nānārucikā nānādiṭṭhinissayanissitā.

“Venerable sir, many ascetics, brahmins, and wanderers of various other sects are residing in Sāvatthī, holding diverse views, diverse inclinations, diverse preferences, and relying on the support of diverse views.

“Santeke samaṇabrāhmaṇā evaṃvādino evaṃdiṭṭhino – ‘sassato attā ca loko ca, idameva saccaṃ moghamañña’nti [T 0.0191] …pe… te bhaṇḍanajātā kalahajātā vivādāpannā aññamaññaṃ mukhasattīhi vitudantā viharanti – ‘ediso dhammo, nediso dhammo; nediso dhammo, ediso dhammo’”ti.

“There are some ascetics and brahmins who hold such a view and declare: ‘The self and the world are eternal; this alone is true, all else is false.’ … Having become quarrelsome, contentious, and fallen into dispute, they dwell piercing each other with verbal spears, saying: ‘The Dhamma is of this nature, the Dhamma is not of this nature; the Dhamma is not of this nature, the Dhamma is of this nature.’”

“Aññatitthiyā, bhikkhave, paribbājakā andhā acakkhukā. Te atthaṃ na jānanti anatthaṃ na jānanti, dhammaṃ na jānanti adhammaṃ na jānanti. Te atthaṃ ajānantā anatthaṃ ajānantā, dhammaṃ ajānantā adhammaṃ ajānantā bhaṇḍanajātā kalahajātā vivādāpannā aññamaññaṃ mukhasattīhi vitudantā viharanti – ‘ediso dhammo, nediso dhammo; nediso dhammo, ediso dhammo’”ti.

“Bhikkhus, the wanderers of other sects are blind and without sight. They do not know what is beneficial and what is unbeneficial; they do not know the Dhamma and what is not the Dhamma. Not knowing what is beneficial, not knowing what is unbeneficial, not knowing the Dhamma, not knowing what is not the Dhamma, they live engaged in disputes, quarrels, and contentions, wounding each other with verbal darts, saying: ‘The Dhamma is like this, the Dhamma is not like this; the Dhamma is not like this, the Dhamma is like this.’”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood the meaning of this, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Ahaṅkārapasutāyaṃ [M 0.0166] pajā, paraṃkārūpasaṃhitā;

Etadeke nābbhaññaṃsu, na naṃ sallanti addasuṃ.

“This generation is intent on ‘I-making,’ connected with ‘other-making.’ Some have not fully understood this; they have not seen it as a dart.

“Etañca sallaṃ paṭikacca paṭigacca (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.) passato;

Ahaṃ karomīti na tassa hoti;

Paro [T 0.0193] karotīti na tassa hoti.

For one who discerns this very dart from the outset, it does not occur to him, ‘I am the doer’; it does not occur to him, ‘Another is the doer’.

“Mānupetā [V 0.0153] ayaṃ pajā, mānaganthā mānavinibaddhā mānavinibandhā (sī.);

Diṭṭhīsu sārambhakathā, saṃsāraṃ nātivattatī”ti. chaṭṭhaṃ;

This generation is accompanied by conceit, bound by the knot of conceit, fettered by conceit. With contentious talk regarding views, it does not transcend the round of rebirth.” Sixth.

7. Subhūtisuttaṃ

7. The Discourse on Subhūti

57. Evaṃ [P 0.0071] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā subhūti bhagavato avidūre nisinno hoti pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya avitakkaṃ samādhiṃ samāpajjitvā.

57. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta's Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika's Park. At that time, the Venerable Subhūti was seated not far from the Blessed One, having folded his legs crosswise, set his body erect, and entered upon the concentration without applied thought.

Addasā kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ subhūtiṃ avidūre nisinnaṃ pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya avitakkaṃ samādhiṃ samāpannaṃ.

The Blessed One saw the Venerable Subhūti sitting not far away, having folded his legs crosswise, set his body erect, and entered upon the concentration without applied thought.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Yassa vitakkā vidhūpitā,

Ajjhattaṃ suvikappitā asesā;

Taṃ saṅgamaticca arūpasaññī,

Catuyogātigato na jātu metī”ti na jātimetīti (syā. pī. aṭṭha. pāṭhantaraṃ). sattamaṃ;

“For whom applied thoughts are dispelled, internally, well-severed without remainder; that one, having transcended attachment, a perceiver of the formless, gone beyond the four yokes, never comes to be again.” Seventh.

8. Gaṇikāsuttaṃ

8. The Discourse on the Courtesan

58. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā rājagahe viharati veḷuvane kalandakanivāpe. Tena kho pana samayena rājagahe dve pūgā aññatarissā gaṇikāya sārattā honti paṭibaddhacittā; bhaṇḍanajātā kalahajātā vivādāpannā aññamaññaṃ pāṇīhipi upakkamanti[T 0.0194], leḍḍūhipi upakkamanti[M 0.0167], daṇḍehipi upakkamanti, satthehipi upakkamanti. Te tattha maraṇampi nigacchanti maraṇamattampi dukkhaṃ.

58. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Rājagaha in the Bamboo Grove, the Squirrel Sanctuary. At that time in Rājagaha, two factions were deeply enamored of and mentally attached to a certain courtesan. They had become quarrelsome, contentious, and had fallen into dispute, attacking one another with their hands, with clods, with sticks, and with weapons. There they met with death, and with suffering equivalent to death.

Atha kho sambahulā bhikkhū pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya rājagahaṃ piṇḍāya pāvisiṃsu. Rājagahe piṇḍāya caritvā pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkantā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu[V 0.0154]; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu. Ekamantaṃ nisinnā kho te bhikkhū bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ –

Then, many monks, having dressed in the morning and taken their bowls and robes, entered Rājagaha for alms. Having wandered for alms in Rājagaha, after the meal, having returned from their alms round, they approached the Blessed One. Having approached, they paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, those monks said this to the Blessed One:

“Idha, bhante, rājagahe dve pūgā aññatarissā gaṇikāya sārattā paṭibaddhacittā; bhaṇḍanajātā kalahajātā vivādāpannā aññamaññaṃ pāṇīhipi upakkamanti, leḍḍūhipi upakkamanti, daṇḍehipi upakkamanti, satthehipi upakkamanti. Te tattha maraṇampi nigacchanti maraṇamattampi dukkha”nti.

“Here, venerable sir, in Rājagaha, two factions are deeply enamored of and mentally attached to a certain courtesan. They have become quarrelsome, contentious, and have fallen into dispute, attacking one another with their hands, with clods, with sticks, and with weapons. There they meet with death, and with suffering equivalent to death.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Yañca pattaṃ yañca pattabbaṃ, ubhayametaṃ rajānukiṇṇaṃ, āturassānusikkhato. Ye ca sikkhāsārā sīlabbataṃ jīvitaṃ brahmacariyaṃ upaṭṭhānasārā, ayameko anto. Ye ca evaṃvādino – ‘natthi kāmesu doso’ti, ayaṃ dutiyo anto. Iccete [P 0.0072] ubho antā kaṭasivaḍḍhanā, kaṭasiyo diṭṭhiṃ vaḍḍhenti. Etete ubho ante anabhiññāya olīyanti eke, atidhāvanti eke. Ye [T 0.0195] ca kho te abhiññāya tatra ca nāhesuṃ, tena ca nāmaññiṃsu, vaṭṭaṃ tesaṃ natthi paññāpanāyā”ti. Aṭṭhamaṃ.

“Both what has been attained and what is to be attained—both are strewn with the dust of passion for one who is afflicted and still training. Those for whom training is the essence, for whom virtue and observances, livelihood, the holy life, and service are the essence—this is one extreme. And those who speak thus, ‘There is no fault in sensual pleasures’—this is the second extreme. Thus these two extremes increase the charnel ground; the charnel grounds increase view. Not having directly known these two extremes, some shrink back, while others rush forward. But those who have directly known them are not established in them, and do not conceive by means of them; for them, there is no cycle of rebirth to be designated.” Eighth.

9. Upātidhāvantisuttaṃ

9. The Discourse on Rushing Past

59. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā rattandhakāratimisāyaṃ abbhokāse nisinno hoti telappadīpesu jhāyamānesu.

59. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling in Sāvatthī, in Jeta's Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika's Park. At that time, the Blessed One was sitting in the open air in the thick darkness of the night, while oil lamps were burning.

Tena kho pana samayena sambahulā adhipātakā tesu telappadīpesu āpātaparipātaṃ anayaṃ āpajjanti, byasanaṃ āpajjanti natthi sīhaḷapotthake, anayabyasanaṃ āpajjanti [M 0.0168] natthi sīhaḷapotthake. Addasā kho bhagavā te sambahule adhipātake tesu telappadīpesu āpātaparipātaṃ anayaṃ āpajjante, byasanaṃ āpajjante, anayabyasanaṃ āpajjante.

Now at that time, many moths, falling headlong and circling into those oil lamps, were meeting with misfortune, disaster, and ruin. The Blessed One saw those many moths falling headlong and circling into those oil lamps, meeting with misfortune, disaster, and ruin.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Upātidhāvanti [V 0.0155] na sāramenti,

Navaṃ navaṃ bandhanaṃ brūhayanti;

Patanti pajjotamivādhipātakā … dhipātā (sī. syā.),

Diṭṭhe sute itiheke niviṭṭhā”ti. navamaṃ;

“They rush past and do not reach the essence;

They increase newer and newer bonds;

Like moths that fall into a flame,

Some are entrenched in what is seen and heard.” Ninth.

10. Uppajjantisuttaṃ

10. The Discourse on Arising

60. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Atha kho āyasmā ānando yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho āyasmā ānando bhagavantaṃ etadavoca –

60. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta's Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika's Park. Then the Venerable Ānanda approached the Blessed One; having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Sitting to one side, the Venerable Ānanda said this to the Blessed One:

“Yāvakīvañca, bhante, tathāgatā loke nuppajjanti arahanto sammāsambuddhā [T 0.0196] tāva aññatitthiyā paribbājakā sakkatā honti garukatā mānitā pūjitā apacitā lābhino cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ. Yato ca kho, bhante, tathāgatā loke uppajjanti arahanto sammāsambuddhā atha aññatitthiyā paribbājakā asakkatā honti agarukatā amānitā apūjitā anapacitā na lābhino cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ. Bhagavā yeva [P 0.0073] bhagavā ceva (syā.) dāni, bhante, sakkato hoti garukato mānito pūjito apacito lābhī cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ, bhikkhusaṅgho cā”ti.

“Venerable sir, for as long as the Tathāgatas, the Arahants, the Perfectly Enlightened Ones, do not arise in the world, the wandering ascetics of other sects are honored, respected, esteemed, revered, venerated, and are recipients of robes, almsfood, lodgings, and medicinal requisites for the sick. But when, venerable sir, the Tathāgatas, the Arahants, the Perfectly Enlightened Ones, arise in the world, then the wandering ascetics of other sects are not honored, respected, esteemed, revered, venerated, nor are they recipients of robes, almsfood, lodgings, and medicinal requisites for the sick. Now, venerable sir, it is the Blessed One who is honored, respected, esteemed, revered, venerated, and is a recipient of robes, almsfood, lodgings, and medicinal requisites for the sick, and the community of monks as well.”

“Evametaṃ[M 0.0169], ānanda, yāvakīvañca, ānanda, tathāgatā loke nuppajjanti arahanto sammāsambuddhā tāva aññatitthiyā paribbājakā sakkatā honti garukatā mānitā pūjitā apacitā lābhino cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ. Yato ca kho, ānanda, tathāgatā loke uppajjanti arahanto sammāsambuddhā atha aññatitthiyā paribbājakā asakkatā honti agarukatā amānitā apūjitā anapacitā na lābhino cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ. Tathāgatova tathāgato ceva (syā.) dāni sakkato hoti garukato mānito pūjito apacito lābhī cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ, bhikkhusaṅgho cā”ti.

“So it is, Ānanda. For as long as, Ānanda, the Tathāgatas, the Arahants, the Perfectly Enlightened Ones do not arise in the world, the wandering ascetics of other sects are honored, respected, esteemed, revered, venerated, and are recipients of robes, almsfood, lodgings, and medicinal requisites for the sick. But when, Ānanda, the Tathāgatas, the Arahants, the Perfectly Enlightened Ones arise in the world, then the wandering ascetics of other sects are not honored, not respected, not esteemed, not revered, not venerated, nor are they recipients of robes, almsfood, lodgings, and medicinal requisites for the sick. Now it is the Tathāgata who is honored, respected, esteemed, revered, venerated, and is a recipient of robes, almsfood, lodgings, and medicinal requisites for the sick, and the community of monks as well.”

Atha [V 0.0156] kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Obhāsati [T 0.0197] tāva so kimi,

Yāva na unnamate uggamati (sī.), unnamati (syā.) pabhaṅkaro;

(Sa) ( ) natthi sī. syā. potthakesu verocanamhi uggate,

Hatappabho hoti na cāpi bhāsati.

“The glowworm shines only so long as the light-maker has not yet risen. But when the radiant sun has risen, its light is lost and it shines no more.

“Evaṃ obhāsitameva takkikānaṃ titthiyānaṃ (sī. syā. pī.),

Yāva sammāsambuddhā loke nuppajjanti;

Na takkikā sujjhanti na cāpi sāvakā,

Duddiṭṭhī na dukkhā pamuccare”ti. dasamaṃ;

“Just so is the shining of the speculators, so long as the Perfectly Enlightened Ones do not arise in the world. The speculators do not shine, nor do their disciples; those with wrong view are not freed from suffering.”

Tassuddānaṃ –

The summary:

Āyujaṭilavekkhaṇā, tayo titthiyā subhūti;

Gaṇikā upāti navamo, uppajjanti ca te dasāti.

Āyu, Jaṭila, Vekkhaṇa, the three Titthiya suttas, Subhūti, the Courtesan, Upāti as the ninth, and Uppajjanti—these are the ten.

Jaccandhavaggo chaṭṭho niṭṭhito.

The Chapter on the Blind from Birth, the Sixth, is finished.

7. Cūḷavaggo

7. The Minor Chapter

1. Paṭhamalakuṇḍakabhaddiyasuttaṃ

1. The First Discourse on Lakuṇḍaka Bhaddiya

61. Evaṃ [M 0.0170][P 0.0074] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā sāriputto āyasmantaṃ lakuṇḍakabhaddiyaṃ anekapariyāyena dhammiyā kathāya sandasseti samādapeti samādāpeti (?) samuttejeti sampahaṃseti.

61. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. At that time, the Venerable Sāriputta was instructing, urging, inspiring, and gladdening the Venerable Lakuṇḍaka Bhaddiya with a Dhamma talk in many ways.

Atha [T 0.0198] kho āyasmato lakuṇḍakabhaddiyassa āyasmatā sāriputtena anekapariyāyena dhammiyā [V 0.0157] kathāya sandassiyamānassa samādapiyamānassa samuttejiyamānassa sampahaṃsiyamānassa anupādāya āsavehi cittaṃ vimucci.

Then, as the Venerable Lakuṇḍaka Bhaddiya was being instructed, urged, inspired, and gladdened by the Venerable Sāriputta with a Dhamma talk in many ways, his mind was liberated from the taints through non-clinging.

Addasā kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ lakuṇḍakabhaddiyaṃ āyasmatā sāriputtena anekapariyāyena dhammiyā kathāya sandassiyamānaṃ samādapiyamānaṃ samuttejiyamānaṃ sampahaṃsiyamānaṃ anupādāya āsavehi cittaṃ vimuttaṃ vimuttacittaṃ (?).

The Blessed One saw the Venerable Lakuṇḍaka Bhaddiya, whose mind was liberated from the taints through non-clinging, while being instructed, urged, inspired, and gladdened in many ways with a Dhamma talk by the Venerable Sāriputta.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Uddhaṃ adho sabbadhi vippamutto, ayaṃhamasmīti ayamahamasmīti (sī. syā. pī.) anānupassī;

Evaṃ vimutto udatāri oghaṃ, atiṇṇapubbaṃ apunabbhavāyā”ti. paṭhamaṃ;

“Freed above, below, and in every way, not contemplating ‘This am I’; thus liberated, he has crossed the flood not crossed before, for the sake of no future existence.”

2. Dutiyalakuṇḍakabhaddiyasuttaṃ

2. The Second Discourse on Lakuṇḍaka Bhaddiya

62. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā sāriputto āyasmantaṃ lakuṇḍakabhaddiyaṃ sekhaṃ sekkhoti (syā.), sekhoti (pī.) maññamāno bhiyyosomattāya anekapariyāyena dhammiyā kathāya sandasseti samādapeti samuttejeti sampahaṃseti.

62. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. At that time, the Venerable Sāriputta, considering the Venerable Lakuṇḍaka Bhaddiya to be a trainee, instructed, urged, inspired, and gladdened him all the more with a Dhamma talk in many ways.

Addasā [M 0.0171] kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ sāriputtaṃ āyasmantaṃ lakuṇḍakabhaddiyaṃ sekhaṃ maññamānaṃ bhiyyosomattāya [P 0.0075] anekapariyāyena dhammiyā kathāya sandassentaṃ samādapentaṃ samuttejentaṃ sampahaṃsentaṃ.

The Blessed One saw the Venerable Sāriputta, who, considering the Venerable Lakuṇḍaka Bhaddiya to be a trainee, was all the more instructing, urging, inspiring, and gladdening him in many ways with a Dhamma talk.

Atha kho bhagavā [T 0.0199] etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Acchecchi acchejji (ka. sī.), acchijji (ka. sī. syā.), achijji (ka.) vaṭṭaṃ byagā nirāsaṃ, visukkhā saritā na sandati;

Chinnaṃ vaṭṭaṃ na vattati, esevanto dukkhassā”ti. dutiyaṃ;

“He has cut off the round, has reached the state without craving; the river is dried up, it flows no more. The round that is cut does not revolve; this itself is the end of suffering.”

3. Paṭhamasattasuttaṃ

3. The First Discourse on Beings

63. Evaṃ [V 0.0158] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena sāvatthiyā manussā yebhuyyena kāmesu ativelaṃ sattā ( ) (honti) (bahūsu) aṭṭhakathāya saṃsandetabbaṃ rattā giddhā gadhitā gathitā (sī.) mucchitā ajjhopannā sammattakajātā kāmesu viharanti.

63. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. Now on that occasion, the people of Sāvatthī were for the most part excessively attached, lustful, greedy, infatuated, obsessed, and utterly devoted to sensual pleasures; having become utterly ruined on account of sensual pleasures, they dwelt.

Atha kho sambahulā bhikkhū pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya sāvatthiyaṃ piṇḍāya pāvisiṃsu. Sāvatthiyaṃ piṇḍāya caritvā pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkantā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu. Ekamantaṃ nisinnā kho te bhikkhū bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ – “idha, bhante, sāvatthiyā manussā yebhuyyena kāmesu ativelaṃ sattā rattā giddhā gadhitā mucchitā ajjhopannā sammattakajātā kāmesu viharantī”ti.

Then many monks, having dressed in the morning, took their bowls and robes and entered Sāvatthī for alms. Having wandered for alms in Sāvatthī and returned from their alms round after the meal, they approached the Blessed One. Having approached and paid homage to the Blessed One, they sat down to one side. Sitting to one side, those monks said this to the Blessed One: ‘Venerable sir, here in Sāvatthī the people are for the most part excessively attached, lustful, greedy, infatuated, obsessed, and utterly devoted to sensual pleasures; having become utterly ruined on account of sensual pleasures, they dwell.’”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Kāmesu sattā kāmasaṅgasattā,

Saṃyojane vajjamapassamānā;

Na [T 0.0200] hi jātu saṃyojanasaṅgasattā,

Oghaṃ tareyyuṃ vipulaṃ mahanta”nti. tatiyaṃ;

“Attached to sensual pleasures, entangled by the attachment to sensual pleasures, not seeing the fault in the fetter; truly, those entangled by the attachment of the fetter can never cross the vast and great flood.” The Third.

4. Dutiyasattasuttaṃ

4. The Second Discourse on Beings

64. Evaṃ [M 0.0172] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena sāvatthiyā manussā yebhuyyena kāmesu sattā ( ) (honti) (bahūsu) aṭṭhakathāya saṃsandetabbaṃ rattā giddhā gadhitā mucchitā ajjhopannā andhīkatā sammattakajātā kāmesu viharanti.

64. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. Now on that occasion, the people of Sāvatthī were for the most part dwelling in sensual pleasures—attached, lustful, greedy, ensnared, infatuated, deeply immersed, blinded, and having become utterly intoxicated.

Atha kho bhagavā pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya sāvatthiṃ piṇḍāya pāvisi. Addasā kho bhagavā sāvatthiyā te manusse yebhuyyena [P 0.0076] kāmesu satte ratte giddhe gadhite mucchite ajjhopanne andhīkate sammattakajāte kāmesu viharante.

Then the Blessed One, having dressed in the morning and taking his bowl and robe, entered Sāvatthī for alms. The Blessed One indeed saw those people of Sāvatthī, for the most part, dwelling in sensual pleasures—attached, lustful, greedy, ensnared, infatuated, deeply immersed, blinded, and having become utterly intoxicated.

Atha [V 0.0159] kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Kāmandhā jālasañchannā, taṇhāchadanachāditā;

Pamattabandhunā baddhā, macchāva kumināmukhe;

Jarāmaraṇamanventi jarāmaraṇaṃ gacchanti (sī. syā.), vaccho khīrapakova mātara”nti. catutthaṃ;

“Blinded by sensual pleasure, covered by the net, shrouded by the covering of craving, bound by the kinsman of heedlessness—like fish in the mouth of a fish-trap—they follow to aging and death, just as a suckling calf follows its mother.” The Fourth.

5. Aparalakuṇḍakabhaddiyasuttaṃ

5. The Second Discourse on Lakuṇḍaka Bhaddiya

65. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā lakuṇḍakabhaddiyo sambahulānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ piṭṭhito piṭṭhito yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami.

65. Thus have I heard. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. At that time the Venerable Lakuṇḍaka Bhaddiya, following behind many monks, approached the Blessed One.

Addasā kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ lakuṇḍakabhaddiyaṃ dūratova sambahulānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ piṭṭhito piṭṭhito āgacchantaṃ dubbaṇṇaṃ duddasikaṃ okoṭimakaṃ yebhuyyena bhikkhūnaṃ paribhūtarūpaṃ[T 0.0201]. Disvāna bhikkhū āmantesi –

The Blessed One saw the Venerable Lakuṇḍaka Bhaddiya coming from afar behind many monks—ill-favored, unpleasant to look at, dwarfish, and for the most part despised by the monks. Having seen him, he addressed the monks:

“Passatha no tumhe, bhikkhave, etaṃ bhikkhuṃ dūratova sambahulānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ piṭṭhito piṭṭhito āgacchantaṃ dubbaṇṇaṃ duddasikaṃ okoṭimakaṃ yebhuyyena bhikkhūnaṃ paribhūtarūpa”nti? “Evaṃ, bhante”ti.

“Monks, do you see that monk coming from afar behind many monks—ill-favored, unpleasant to look at, dwarfish, and for the most part despised by the monks?” “Yes, Venerable Sir.”

“Eso[M 0.0173], bhikkhave, bhikkhu mahiddhiko mahānubhāvo. Na ca sā samāpatti sulabharūpā yā tena bhikkhunā asamāpannapubbā. Yassa catthāya yassatthāya (sī. ka.) kulaputtā sammadeva agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajanti tadanuttaraṃ brahmacariyapariyosānaṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharatī”ti.

“Monks, that monk is of great psychic power and great might. There is no easily obtainable attainment that has not been previously attained by that monk. For the sake of which noble sons rightly go forth from the household life into homelessness, he dwells having, in this very life, realized for himself through direct knowledge and attained that unsurpassed culmination of the holy life.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Nelaṅgo setapacchādo, ekāro vattatī ratho;

Anīghaṃ passa āyantaṃ, chinnasotaṃ abandhana”nti. pañcamaṃ;

“With a flawless wheel, a white canopy, and a single axle, the chariot rolls on; behold him coming, untroubled, with the stream cut off, free from bonds.” The Fifth.

6. Taṇhāsaṅkhayasuttaṃ

6. The Discourse on the Destruction of Craving

66. Evaṃ [V 0.0160] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena [P 0.0077] āyasmā aññāsikoṇḍañño aññātakoṇḍañño (sabbattha) bhagavato avidūre nisinno hoti pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya taṇhāsaṅkhayavimuttiṃ paccavekkhamāno.

66. Thus have I heard. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. At that time, the Venerable Aññāsikoṇḍañña was sitting not far from the Blessed One, his legs crossed, his body held erect, reviewing the liberation that is the destruction of craving.

Addasā kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ aññāsikoṇḍaññaṃ avidūre nisinnaṃ pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya taṇhāsaṅkhayavimuttiṃ paccavekkhamānaṃ.

The Blessed One saw the Venerable Aññāsikoṇḍañña sitting not far away, his legs crossed, his body held erect, reviewing the liberation that is the destruction of craving.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Yassa mūlaṃ chamā natthi, paṇṇā natthi kuto latā;

Taṃ [T 0.0202] dhīraṃ bandhanā muttaṃ, ko taṃ ninditumarahati;

Devāpi naṃ pasaṃsanti, brahmunāpi pasaṃsito”ti. chaṭṭhaṃ;

“For whom there are no roots, no earth, and no leaves—from where could there be vines? That steadfast one, freed from bonds—who is fit to blame them? Even the gods praise them; they are praised even by Brahmā.” The Sixth.

7. Papañcakhayasuttaṃ

7. The Discourse on the Destruction of Proliferation

67. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā attano papañcasaññāsaṅkhāpahānaṃ paccavekkhamāno nisinno hoti.

67. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī, in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. At that time the Blessed One was seated, reviewing his own abandoning of the categories of perception associated with proliferation.

Atha [M 0.0174] kho bhagavā attano papañcasaññāsaṅkhāpahānaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, having understood his own abandoning of the categories of perception associated with proliferation, the Blessed One on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Yassa papañcā ṭhiti ca natthi,

Sandānaṃ palighañca vītivatto;

Taṃ nittaṇhaṃ muniṃ carantaṃ,

Nāvajānāti sadevakopi loko”ti. sattamaṃ;

“For whom proliferations and a standpoint exist not, who has gone beyond the cord and the bar—that craving-free sage who fares on, the world with its devas does not despise.” The Seventh.

8. Kaccānasuttaṃ

8. The Discourse to Kaccāna

68. Evaṃ [V 0.0161] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā mahākaccāno bhagavato avidūre nisinno hoti pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya kāyagatāya satiyā ajjhattaṃ parimukhaṃ sūpaṭṭhitāya.

68. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī, in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. At that time, the Venerable Mahākaccāna was sitting not far from the Blessed One, having folded his legs crosswise, holding his body erect, with mindfulness directed to the body well established internally before him.

Addasā kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ mahākaccānaṃ avidūre nisinnaṃ pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya kāyagatāya satiyā ajjhattaṃ parimukhaṃ sūpaṭṭhitāya.

The Blessed One saw the Venerable Mahākaccāna sitting not far away, having folded his legs crosswise, holding his body erect, with mindfulness directed to the body well established internally before him.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Yassa [P 0.0078] siyā sabbadā sati,

Satataṃ kāyagatā upaṭṭhitā;

No [T 0.0203] cassa no ca me siyā,

Na bhavissati na ca me bhavissati;

Anupubbavihāri tattha so,

Kāleneva tare visattika”nti. aṭṭhamaṃ;

“For whom mindfulness directed to the body is ever established, constantly, reflecting: ‘It was not, it would not be mine; it will not be, it will not be mine.’ Dwelling progressively therein, such a one in due time crosses over attachment.” The Eighth.

9. Udapānasuttaṃ

9. The Discourse on the Well

69. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā mallesu cārikaṃ caramāno mahatā bhikkhusaṅghena saddhiṃ yena thūṇaṃ thūnaṃ (sī. syā. pī.) nāma mallānaṃ brāhmaṇagāmo tadavasari. Assosuṃ kho thūṇeyyakā brāhmaṇagahapatikā – “samaṇo khalu, bho, gotamo sakyaputto sakyakulā pabbajito mallesu cārikaṃ caramāno [M 0.0175] mahatā bhikkhusaṅghena saddhiṃ thūṇaṃ anuppatto”ti.( ) (atha kho te thūṇeyyakā brāhmaṇagahapatikā) (?) Udapānaṃ tiṇassa ca bhusassa ca yāva mukhato pūresuṃ – “mā te muṇḍakā samaṇakā pānīyaṃ apaṃsū”ti.

69. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One, traveling among the Mallas with a large company of monks, arrived at Thūṇa, a brahmin village of the Mallas. The brahmin householders of Thūṇa heard: “Indeed, friends, the ascetic Gotama, the son of the Sakyans, gone forth from the Sakyan clan, is traveling among the Mallas with a large company of monks and has arrived at Thūṇa.” They filled the well right up to the mouth with grass and chaff, thinking: “Let these shaveling ascetics not drink the water.”

Atha kho bhagavā maggā okkamma yena rukkhamūlaṃ tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā paññatte āsane nisīdi. Nisajja kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi – “iṅgha me tvaṃ, ānanda, etamhā udapānā pānīyaṃ āharā”ti.

Then the Blessed One stepped off the road and went to the foot of a tree. Having approached, he sat down on the prepared seat. Having sat down, the Blessed One addressed the Venerable Ānanda: “Come, Ānanda, bring me some drinking water from that well.”

Evaṃ [V 0.0162] vutte, āyasmā ānando bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “idāni so, bhante, udapāno thūṇeyyakehi brāhmaṇagahapatikehi tiṇassa ca bhusassa ca yāva mukhato pūrito – ‘mā te muṇḍakā samaṇakā pānīyaṃ apaṃsū’”ti.

When this was said, the Venerable Ānanda said to the Blessed One: “Just now, Venerable Sir, that well has been filled right up to the mouth with grass and chaff by the brahmin householders of Thūṇa, who thought, ‘Let these shaveling ascetics not drink the water!’”

Dutiyampi kho…pe… [T 0.0204] tatiyampi kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi – “iṅgha me tvaṃ, ānanda, etamhā udapānā pānīyaṃ āharā”ti. “Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho āyasmā ānando bhagavato paṭissutvā pattaṃ gahetvā yena so udapāno tenupasaṅkami. Atha kho so udapāno āyasmante ānande upasaṅkamante sabbaṃ taṃ tiṇañca bhusañca mukhato ovamitvā acchassa udakassa anāvilassa vippasannassa yāva mukhato pūrito vissandanto vissando (ka.) maññe aṭṭhāsi.

A second time… and a third time the Blessed One addressed the Venerable Ānanda: “Come, Ānanda, fetch me drinking water from that well.” “Yes, Venerable Sir,” replied the Venerable Ānanda to the Blessed One, and taking his bowl, he approached the well. Then, as the Venerable Ānanda approached, that well spewed out all the grass and chaff from its mouth, and it seemed to stand filled to the brim with clear, undisturbed, perfectly clear water, overflowing.

Atha kho āyasmato ānandassa etadahosi – “acchariyaṃ vata, bho, abbhutaṃ vata, bho, tathāgatassa mahiddhikatā mahānubhāvatā! Ayañhi so udapāno mayi upasaṅkamante sabbaṃ taṃ tiṇañca bhusañca mukhato ovamitvā acchassa udakassa anāvilassa vippasannassa yāva mukhato pūrito vissandanto maññe ṭhito”ti!! Pattena pānīyaṃ ādāya yena [P 0.0079] bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “acchariyaṃ, bhante, abbhutaṃ, bhante, tathāgatassa mahiddhikatā mahānubhāvatā! Ayañhi so, bhante, udapāno mayi upasaṅkamante sabbaṃ taṃ tiṇañca bhusañca mukhato ovamitvā acchassa udakassa anāvilassa vippasannassa yāva mukhato pūrito vissandanto maññe aṭṭhāsi!! Pivatu bhagavā pānīyaṃ, pivatu sugato pānīya”nti.

Then this thought occurred to the Venerable Ānanda: “How astonishing, friends, how marvelous, friends, is the Tathāgata's great power and great might! For as I approached, this well, having spewed out all that grass and chaff from its mouth, seemed to stand filled to the brim with clear, undisturbed, perfectly clear water, overflowing!” Taking drinking water in the bowl, he approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he said to the Blessed One: “How astonishing, Venerable Sir, how marvelous, Venerable Sir, is the Tathāgata's great power and great might! For as I approached, Venerable Sir, this well, having spewed out all that grass and chaff from its mouth, seemed to stand filled to the brim with clear, undisturbed, perfectly clear water, overflowing! May the Blessed One drink the water, may the Sugata drink the water!”

Atha [M 0.0176] kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, having understood this matter, the Blessed One on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Kiṃ [T 0.0205] kayirā udapānena,

Āpā ce sabbadā siyuṃ;

Taṇhāya mūlato chetvā,

Kissa pariyesanaṃ care”ti. navamaṃ;

“What use is a well, if water were always available? Having cut off craving at the root, for what should one go about searching?” The ninth.

10. Utenasuttaṃ

10. The Utena Sutta

70. Evaṃ [V 0.0163] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā kosambiyaṃ viharati ghositārāme. Tena kho pana samayena rañño utenassa udenassa (sī. syā. pī.) uyyānagatassa antepuraṃ daḍḍhaṃ hoti, pañca ca itthisatāni pañca itthisatāni (sī. syā. pī.) kālaṅkatāni honti sāmāvatīpamukhāni.

70. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Kosambī, in Ghosita's Monastery. Now at that time, while King Utena was away in the pleasure grove, his inner palace was burned, and five hundred women, headed by Sāmāvatī, had died.

Atha kho sambahulā bhikkhū pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya kosambiṃ piṇḍāya pāvisiṃsu. Kosambiyaṃ piṇḍāya caritvā pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkantā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu. Ekamantaṃ nisinnā kho te bhikkhū bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ – “idha, bhante, rañño utenassa uyyānagatassa antepuraṃ daḍḍhaṃ, pañca ca itthisatāni kālaṅkatāni sāmāvatīpamukhāni. Tāsaṃ, bhante, upāsikānaṃ kā gati ko abhisamparāyo”ti?

Then, in the morning, a number of bhikkhus dressed, took their bowls and robes, and went into Kosambī for alms. Having wandered for alms in Kosambī, after the meal, having returned from the alms-round, they approached the Blessed One. Having approached and paid homage to the Blessed One, they sat down to one side. Seated to one side, those bhikkhus said to the Blessed One: “Here, Venerable Sir, while King Utena was away in the pleasure grove, his inner palace was burned, and five hundred women, headed by Sāmāvatī, died. What, Venerable Sir, is the destination of those female lay followers? What is their future state?”

“Santettha, bhikkhave, upāsikāyo sotāpannā, santi sakadāgāminiyo, santi anāgāminiyo. Sabbā tā, bhikkhave, upāsikāyo anipphalā kālaṅkatā”ti.

“Monks, among them there are female lay followers who are stream-enterers, there are once-returners, and there are non-returners. All those female lay followers, monks, have not died without fruit.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Mohasambandhano loko, bhabbarūpova dissati;

Upadhibandhano upadhisambandhano (ka. sī.) bālo, tamasā parivārito;

Sassatoriva [T 0.0206] sassati viya (ka. sī.) khāyati, passato natthi kiñcana”nti. dasamaṃ;

“The world is enveloped by delusion; it appears as if ready for release. The fool is bound by attachments, surrounded by darkness. It appears as if eternal, but for one who sees, there is no impediment.” The tenth.

Tassuddānaṃ –

The summary for this chapter is:

Dve [M 0.0177] bhaddiyā dve ca sattā, lakuṇḍako taṇhākhayo;

Papañcakhayo ca kaccāno, udapānañca utenoti.

Two Bhaddiyas, two Sattas, Lakuṇḍaka, Taṇhākhaya, Papañcakhaya, Kaccāna, Udapāna, and Utena.

Cūḷavaggo cullavaggo (sī.), cūlavaggo (pī.) sattamo niṭṭhito.

The Minor Chapter, the Seventh, is concluded.

8. Pāṭaligāmiyavaggo

8. The Pāṭaligāma Chapter

1. Paṭhamanibbānapaṭisaṃyuttasuttaṃ

1. The First Sutta Connected with Nibbāna

71. Evaṃ [V 0.0164][P 0.0080] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā bhikkhū nibbānapaṭisaṃyuttāya dhammiyā kathāya sandasseti samādapeti samuttejeti sampahaṃseti. Tedha bhikkhū te ca bhikkhū (sī. syā. pī. tadaṭṭhakathāpi oloketabbā aṭṭhiṃ katvā aṭṭhīkatvā (sī. syā.), aṭṭhikatvā (pī.) manasi katvā sabbaṃ cetaso sabbaṃ cetasā (itipi aññasuttesu) samannāharitvā ohitasotā dhammaṃ suṇanti.

71. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī, in Jeta's Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s monastery. At that time, the Blessed One was instructing, urging, rousing, and gladdening the bhikkhus with a Dhamma talk connected with Nibbāna. And those bhikkhus, taking it to heart, applying their minds, encompassing it all with their thought, listened to the Dhamma with attentive ears.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Atthi, bhikkhave, tadāyatanaṃ, yattha neva pathavī, na āpo, na tejo, na vāyo, na ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ, na viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ, na ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ, na nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ, nāyaṃ loko, na paraloko, na ubho candimasūriyā. Tatrāpāhaṃ, bhikkhave, neva āgatiṃ vadāmi[T 0.0207], na gatiṃ, na ṭhitiṃ, na cutiṃ, na upapattiṃ; appatiṭṭhaṃ, appavattaṃ, anārammaṇamevetaṃ. Esevanto dukkhassā”ti. Paṭhamaṃ.

“There is, monks, that sphere where there is neither earth, nor water, nor fire, nor wind; neither the sphere of infinite space, nor the sphere of infinite consciousness, nor the sphere of nothingness, nor the sphere of neither perception nor non-perception; neither this world, nor the other world, nor both sun and moon. There, monks, I say there is no coming, no going, no standing, no passing away, and no arising. It is without foundation, without continuation, without an object. This itself is the end of suffering.”

2. Dutiyanibbānapaṭisaṃyuttasuttaṃ

2. The Second Discourse Connected with Nibbāna

72. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā bhikkhū nibbānapaṭisaṃyuttāya [M 0.0178] dhammiyā kathāya sandasseti samādapeti samuttejeti sampahaṃseti. Tedha bhikkhū aṭṭhiṃ katvā manasi katvā sabbaṃ cetaso samannāharitvā ohitasotā dhammaṃ suṇanti.

72. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī, in Jeta's Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s monastery. At that time, the Blessed One was instructing, establishing, sharpening, and gladdening the monks with a Dhamma talk connected with Nibbāna. And those monks, taking it to heart, applying their minds, focusing their entire thought, listened to the Dhamma with attentive ears.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, having understood this meaning, on that occasion the Blessed One uttered this inspired utterance:

“Duddasaṃ anataṃ nāma, na hi saccaṃ sudassanaṃ;

Paṭividdhā taṇhā jānato, passato natthi kiñcana”nti. dutiyaṃ;

“That which is called the Uninclined is difficult to see, for indeed, the truth is not easily seen. For one who knows, who sees, craving is penetrated; there is no impediment whatsoever.”

3. Tatiyanibbānapaṭisaṃyuttasuttaṃ

3. The Third Discourse Connected with Nibbāna

73. Evaṃ [V 0.0165] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā bhikkhū nibbānapaṭisaṃyuttāya dhammiyā kathāya sandasseti samādapeti samuttejeti sampahaṃseti. Tedha bhikkhū aṭṭhiṃ katvā, manasi katvā, sabbaṃ cetaso samannāharitvā, ohitasotā dhammaṃ suṇanti.

73. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī, in Jeta's Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s monastery. At that time, the Blessed One was instructing, establishing, sharpening, and gladdening the monks with a Dhamma talk connected with Nibbāna. And those monks, taking it to heart, applying their minds, focusing their entire thought, listened to the Dhamma with attentive ears.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, having understood this meaning, on that occasion the Blessed One uttered this inspired utterance:

“Atthi, bhikkhave, ajātaṃ abhūtaṃ akataṃ asaṅkhataṃ. No cetaṃ, bhikkhave, abhavissa ajātaṃ abhūtaṃ akataṃ asaṅkhataṃ, nayidha jātassa bhūtassa katassa saṅkhatassa nissaraṇaṃ [P 0.0081] paññāyetha. Yasmā ca kho, bhikkhave, atthi ajātaṃ abhūtaṃ akataṃ asaṅkhataṃ, tasmā jātassa bhūtassa [T 0.0208] katassa saṅkhatassa nissaraṇaṃ paññāyatī”ti. Tatiyaṃ.

“There is, monks, an unborn, unbecome, unmade, unconditioned. If, monks, there were not this unborn, unbecome, unmade, unconditioned, an escape from the born, become, made, and conditioned would not be apparent here. But because there is an unborn, unbecome, unmade, unconditioned, therefore an escape from the born, become, made, and conditioned is apparent.”

4. Catutthanibbānapaṭisaṃyuttasuttaṃ

4. The Fourth Discourse Connected with Nibbāna

74. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā bhikkhū nibbānapaṭisaṃyuttāya dhammiyā kathāya sandasseti samādapeti samuttejeti sampahaṃseti. Tedha bhikkhū aṭṭhiṃ katvā manasi katvā sabbaṃ cetaso samannāharitvā ohitasotā dhammaṃ suṇanti.

74. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī, in Jeta's Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s monastery. At that time, the Blessed One was instructing, establishing, sharpening, and gladdening the monks with a Dhamma talk connected with Nibbāna. And those monks, taking it to heart, applying their minds, focusing their entire thought, listened to the Dhamma with attentive ears.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, having understood this meaning, on that occasion the Blessed One uttered this inspired utterance:

“Nissitassa [M 0.0179] calitaṃ, anissitassa calitaṃ natthi. Calite asati passaddhi, passaddhiyā sati nati na hoti. Natiyā asati āgatigati na hoti. Āgatigatiyā asati cutūpapāto na hoti. Cutūpapāte asati nevidha na huraṃ na ubhayamantarena na ubhayamantare (sabbattha) ma. ni. 3.393; saṃ. ni. 4.87 passitabbaṃ. Esevanto dukkhassā”ti. Catutthaṃ.

“For one who is dependent, there is agitation; for one who is independent, there is no agitation. When there is no agitation, there is tranquility; when there is tranquility, there is no inclination. When there is no inclination, there is no coming and going. When there is no coming and going, there is no passing away and taking rebirth. When there is no passing away and taking rebirth, there is neither here, nor beyond, nor in between the two. This itself is the end of suffering.”

5. Cundasuttaṃ

5. The Discourse about Cunda

75. Evaṃ [V 0.0166] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā mallesu cārikaṃ caramāno mahatā bhikkhusaṅghena saddhiṃ yena pāvā tadavasari. Tatra sudaṃ bhagavā pāvāyaṃ viharati cundassa kammāraputtassa ambavane.

75. Thus have I heard. On one occasion the Blessed One, while wandering on tour among the Mallas with a large community of monks, arrived at Pāvā. There, indeed, the Blessed One was dwelling in Pāvā, in the mango grove of Cunda the son of the smith.

Assosi kho cundo kammāraputto – “bhagavā kira mallesu cārikaṃ caramāno mahatā bhikkhusaṅghena saddhiṃ pāvaṃ anuppatto pāvāyaṃ viharati mayhaṃ ambavane”ti. Atha kho cundo kammāraputto yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho cundaṃ kammāraputtaṃ bhagavā dhammiyā [T 0.0209] kathāya sandassesi samādapesi samuttejesi sampahaṃsesi. Atha kho cundo kammāraputto bhagavatā dhammiyā kathāya sandassito samādapito samuttejito sampahaṃsito bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “adhivāsetu me, bhante, bhagavā svātanāya bhattaṃ saddhiṃ bhikkhusaṅghenā”ti. Adhivāsesi bhagavā tuṇhībhāvena.

Cunda the son of the smith heard: “The Blessed One, it is said, while wandering on tour among the Mallas with a large community of monks, has arrived at Pāvā and is dwelling in my mango grove.” Then Cunda the son of the smith approached the Blessed One; having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down at a suitable place. The Blessed One instructed, established, sharpened, and gladdened Cunda the son of the smith, who was sitting at a suitable place, with a talk on the Dhamma. Then Cunda the son of the smith, having been instructed, established, sharpened, and gladdened by the Blessed One with a talk on the Dhamma, said this to the Blessed One: “Venerable Sir, may the Blessed One, together with the community of monks, consent to my meal for tomorrow.” The Blessed One consented by means of silence.

Atha kho cundo kammāraputto bhagavato adhivāsanaṃ viditvā uṭṭhāyāsanā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā pakkāmi. Atha kho cundo kammāraputto tassā rattiyā accayena sake nivesane paṇītaṃ khādanīyaṃ bhojanīyaṃ paṭiyādāpetvā pahūtañca sūkaramaddavaṃ bhagavato kālaṃ ārocāpesi – “kālo, bhante, niṭṭhitaṃ bhatta”nti.

Then Cunda the son of the smith, having understood the Blessed One’s consent, rose from his seat, paid homage to the Blessed One, circumambulated him keeping him to his right, and departed. Then, when that night had passed, Cunda the son of the smith had excellent hard and soft food prepared in his own residence, along with an abundance of sūkaramaddava, and had the time announced to the Blessed One, saying: “Venerable Sir, it is time; the meal is ready.”

Atha [P 0.0082] kho bhagavā pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya saddhiṃ bhikkhusaṅghena yena cundassa kammāraputtassa nivesanaṃ tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā paññatte āsane nisīdi. Nisajja kho bhagavā cundaṃ kammāraputtaṃ āmantesi – “yaṃ te, cunda, sūkaramaddavaṃ paṭiyattaṃ tena maṃ parivisa, yaṃ panaññaṃ khādanīyaṃ [M 0.0180] bhojanīyaṃ paṭiyattaṃ tena bhikkhusaṅghaṃ parivisā”ti. “Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho cundo kammāraputto bhagavato paṭissutvā yaṃ ahosi sūkaramaddavaṃ paṭiyattaṃ tena bhagavantaṃ parivisi; yaṃ panaññaṃ khādanīyaṃ bhojanīyaṃ paṭiyattaṃ tena bhikkhusaṅghaṃ parivisi.

Then the Blessed One, having dressed in the morning, put on his lower robe, took his bowl and outer robe, and went together with the Saṅgha of bhikkhus to the residence of Cunda, the son of the smith. Having approached, he sat down on the prepared seat. Having sat down, the Blessed One addressed Cunda, the son of the smith: “Cunda, serve me with the sūkaramaddava that you have prepared, and serve the Saṅgha of bhikkhus with the other hard and soft food that has been prepared.” “Even so, Venerable Sir,” Cunda, the son of the smith, replied to the Blessed One, and with the sūkaramaddava that had been prepared, he served the Blessed One; and with the other hard and soft food that had been prepared, he served the Saṅgha of bhikkhus.

Atha kho bhagavā cundaṃ kammāraputtaṃ āmantesi – “yaṃ te, cunda, sūkaramaddavaṃ avasiṭṭhaṃ taṃ sobbhe nikhaṇāhi. Nāhaṃ taṃ, cunda, passāmi sadevake loke samārake [T 0.0210] sabrahmake sassamaṇabrāhmaṇiyā pajāya sadevamanussāya yassa taṃ paribhuttaṃ sammā pariṇāmaṃ gaccheyya aññatra [V 0.0167] tathāgatassā”ti aññatra tathāgatenāti (ka. sī.). “Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho cundo kammāraputto bhagavato paṭissutvā yaṃ ahosi sūkaramaddavaṃ avasiṭṭhaṃ taṃ sobbhe nikhaṇitvā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho cundaṃ kammāraputtaṃ bhagavā dhammiyā kathāya sandassetvā samādapetvā samuttejetvā sampahaṃsetvā uṭṭhāyāsanā pakkāmi.

Then the Blessed One addressed Cunda, the son of the smith: “Cunda, whatever sūkaramaddava is left over, bury that in a pit. Cunda, I do not see anyone in this world with its devas, Māras, and Brahmās, in this generation with its ascetics and brahmins, with its devas and humans, for whom that, having been eaten, could be properly digested, other than the Tathāgata.” “Even so, Venerable Sir,” Cunda, the son of the smith, replied to the Blessed One, and that sūkaramaddava which was left over, he buried in a pit. Then he approached the Blessed One; having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down at one side. The Blessed One, having instructed, established, roused, and gladdened Cunda, the son of the smith, who was sitting at one side, with a talk on the Dhamma, rose from his seat and departed.

Atha kho bhagavato cundassa kammāraputtassa bhattaṃ bhuttāvissa kharo ābādho uppajji. Lohitapakkhandikā pabāḷhā bāḷhā (sī. syā. pī.) vedanā vattanti māraṇantikā. Tatra sudaṃ bhagavā sato sampajāno adhivāsesi avihaññamāno. Atha kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi – “āyāmānanda, yena kusinārā tenupasaṅkamissāmā”ti. “Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho āyasmā ānando bhagavato paccassosi.

Then, after the Blessed One had eaten the meal of Cunda, the son of the smith, a severe affliction arose. Dysentery with blood occurred, and strong, death-ending pains set in. There, the Blessed One, mindful and clearly comprehending, endured it without being distressed. Then the Blessed One addressed the Venerable Ānanda: “Come, Ānanda, let us go to Kusinārā.” “Even so, Venerable Sir,” the Venerable Ānanda replied to the Blessed One.

“Cundassa bhattaṃ bhuñjitvā, kammārassāti me sutaṃ;

Ābādhaṃ samphusī dhīro, pabāḷhaṃ māraṇantikaṃ.

“Having eaten the meal of Cunda the smith, so I have heard, the steadfast one encountered a strong, death-ending affliction.”

“Bhuttassa ca sūkaramaddavena, byādhippabāḷho udapādi satthuno;

Viriccamāno viriñcamāno (?) virecamāno (dī. ni. 2.190) bhagavā avoca, ‘gacchāmahaṃ kusināraṃ nagara’”nti.

“After eating the sūkaramaddava, a strong affliction arose for the Teacher; while suffering from dysentery, the Blessed One said: ‘I will go to the city of Kusinārā.’”

Atha [P 0.0083][T 0.0211] kho bhagavā maggā okkamma yena aññataraṃ rukkhamūlaṃ tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi – “iṅgha me tvaṃ, ānanda, catugguṇaṃ saṅghāṭiṃ paññāpehi; kilantosmi, ānanda, nisīdissāmī”ti. “Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho āyasmā ānando bhagavato paṭissutvā catugguṇaṃ saṅghāṭiṃ [M 0.0181] paññāpesi. Nisīdi bhagavā paññatte āsane. Nisajja kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi – “iṅgha me tvaṃ, ānanda, pānīyaṃ āhara; pipāsitosmi, ānanda, pivissāmī”ti.

Then the Blessed One, having turned aside from the road, approached the root of a certain tree. Having approached, he addressed the Venerable Ānanda: “Come now, Ānanda, prepare for me the outer robe folded in four; I am weary, Ānanda, I will sit down.” “Even so, Venerable Sir,” the Venerable Ānanda replied to the Blessed One and prepared the outer robe folded in four. The Blessed One sat down on the prepared seat. Having sat down, the Blessed One addressed the Venerable Ānanda: “Come now, Ānanda, bring me drinking water; I am thirsty, Ānanda, I will drink.”

Evaṃ vutte, āyasmā ānando bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “idāni, bhante, pañcamattāni sakaṭasatāni atikkantāni. Taṃ cakkacchinnaṃ udakaṃ parittaṃ luḷitaṃ āvilaṃ sandati. Ayaṃ, bhante, kukuṭṭhā kakutthā (sī.), kukuṭā (syā.), kakudhā (dī. ni. 2.191) nadī avidūre acchodakā sātodakā sītodakā setodakā supatitthā ramaṇīyā. Ettha bhagavā pānīyañca pivissati gattāni ca sītīkarissatī”ti sītiṃ karissatīti (sī.), sītaṃ karissatīti (syā. pī. ka.).

When this was said, the Venerable Ānanda said this to the Blessed One: “Venerable Sir, just now five hundred carts have passed by. That little water, churned up by the wheels, flows stirred up and turbid. Venerable Sir, this Kukuṭṭhā river is not far, with clear water, pleasant water, cool water, pure water, with good fords, and is delightful. There the Blessed One will both drink water and cool his limbs.”

Dutiyampi kho…pe… tatiyampi kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi [T 0.0212] – “iṅgha me [V 0.0168] tvaṃ, ānanda, pānīyaṃ āhara; pipāsitosmi, ānanda, pivissāmī”ti. “Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho āyasmā ānando bhagavato paṭissutvā pattaṃ gahetvā yena sā nadī tenupasaṅkami. Atha kho sā nadī cakkacchinnā parittā luḷitā āvilā sandamānā āyasmante ānande upasaṅkamante acchā vippasannā anāvilā sandati.

A second time… and a third time the Blessed One addressed the Venerable Ānanda: “Come now, Ānanda, bring me drinking water; I am thirsty, Ānanda, I shall drink.” “Even so, Venerable Sir,” replied the Venerable Ānanda, and taking his bowl, he approached that stream. Then, that stream, which had been flowing churned up by wheels, shallow, stirred up, and turbid, as the Venerable Ānanda approached, began to flow clear, serene, and unturbid.

Atha kho āyasmato ānandassa etadahosi – “acchariyaṃ vata, bho, abbhutaṃ vata, bho, tathāgatassa mahiddhikatā mahānubhāvatā! Ayañhi sā nadī cakkacchinnā parittā luḷitā āvilā sandamānā mayi upasaṅkamante acchā vippasannā anāvilā sandatī”ti!! Pattena pānīyaṃ ādāya yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “acchariyaṃ, bhante, abbhutaṃ, bhante, tathāgatassa mahiddhikatā mahānubhāvatā! Ayañhi sā, bhante, nadī cakkacchinnā parittā luḷitā āvilā sandamānā mayi upasaṅkamante acchā vippasannā anāvilā sandati!! Pivatu bhagavā pānīyaṃ[P 0.0084], pivatu sugato pānīya”nti.

Then this thought occurred to the Venerable Ānanda: “It is wonderful, indeed, it is amazing, indeed, the great power and might of the Tathāgata! For this very stream, which was flowing churned up by wheels, shallow, stirred up, and turbid, upon my approach, now flows clear, serene, and unturbid!” Taking water in his bowl, he approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he said to the Blessed One: “It is wonderful, Venerable Sir, it is amazing, Venerable Sir, the great power and might of the Tathāgata! For this very stream, Venerable Sir, which was flowing churned up by wheels, shallow, stirred up, and turbid, upon my approach, now flows clear, serene, and unturbid! May the Blessed One drink the water, may the Sugata drink the water!”

Atha kho bhagavā pānīyaṃ apāyi apāsi (sī.). Atha kho bhagavā mahatā bhikkhusaṅghena saddhiṃ yena kukuṭṭhā nadī tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā kukuṭṭhaṃ nadiṃ ajjhogāhetvā nhatvā ca pivitvā ca paccuttaritvā yena ambavanaṃ tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā āyasmantaṃ cundakaṃ āmantesi – “iṅgha me tvaṃ, cundaka, catugguṇaṃ saṅghāṭiṃ paññāpehi; kilantosmi, cundaka, nipajjissāmī”ti[M 0.0182][T 0.0213]. “Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho āyasmā cundako bhagavato paṭissutvā catugguṇaṃ saṅghāṭiṃ paññāpesi. Atha kho bhagavā dakkhiṇena passena sīhaseyyaṃ kappesi pāde pādaṃ accādhāya sato sampajāno uṭṭhānasaññaṃ manasi karitvā. Āyasmā pana cundako tattheva bhagavato purato nisīdi.

Then the Blessed One drank the water. Then the Blessed One, together with a great Saṅgha of bhikkhus, approached the Kukuṭṭhā River. Having approached, he entered the Kukuṭṭhā River, bathed, drank, and came out. Then he approached the mango grove. Having approached, he addressed the Venerable Cundaka: “Come, Cundaka, prepare for me the outer robe folded in four; I am weary, Cundaka, I shall lie down.” “Yes, Bhante,” replied the Venerable Cundaka to the Blessed One, and prepared the outer robe folded in four. Then the Blessed One assumed the lion’s posture on his right side, placing one foot on the other, mindful and clearly comprehending, having established in his mind the perception of rising. The Venerable Cundaka then sat down right there in front of the Blessed One.

“Gantvāna buddho nadikaṃ kukuṭṭhaṃ,

Acchodakaṃ sātudakaṃ sātodakaṃ (sabbattha) vippasannaṃ;

Ogāhi satthā sukilantarūpo,

Tathāgato appaṭimodha loke.

Having gone to the river Kukuṭṭhā, with its clear, pleasant, and serene water, the Teacher, being very weary, entered it. The Tathāgata is without equal in this world.

“Nhatvā ca pivitvā cudatāri nhatvā ca uttari (ka.) satthā,

Purakkhato bhikkhugaṇassa majjhe;

Satthā [V 0.0169] pavattā bhagavā idha dhamme,

Upāgami ambavanaṃ mahesi;

Āmantayi cundakaṃ nāma bhikkhuṃ,

Catugguṇaṃ santhara patthara (sī. pī.) me nipajjaṃ.

Having bathed and drunk, the Teacher emerged, at the head of the assembly of bhikkhus. The Teacher, the Blessed One, who sets the Dhamma in motion, the Great Sage, approached the mango grove. He addressed the bhikkhu named Cundaka: ‘Spread out for me the four-fold robe to lie down on.’

“So codito bhāvitattena cundo,

Catugguṇaṃ santhari patthari (sī. pī.) khippameva;

Nipajji satthā sukilantarūpo,

Cundopi tattha pamukhe nisīdī”ti.

Urged by him of developed mind, Cunda quickly spread out the four-fold robe. The Teacher, being weary, lay down; and Cunda also sat down there before him.

Atha kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi – “siyā kho[T 0.0214], panānanda, cundassa kammāraputtassa koci vippaṭisāraṃ upadaheyya – ‘tassa te, āvuso cunda, alābhā, tassa te dulladdhaṃ yassa te tathāgato pacchimaṃ piṇḍapātaṃ bhuñjitvā parinibbuto’ti. Cundassānanda, kammāraputtassa [P 0.0085] evaṃ vippaṭisāro paṭivinodetabbo –

Then the Blessed One addressed the Venerable Ānanda: ‘It may be, Ānanda, that someone might cause remorse to arise in Cunda the smith's son, saying: “It is a loss for you, friend Cunda, it is a misfortune for you, that the Tathāgata, after eating his final alms-food from you, attained Parinibbāna.” Ānanda, the remorse of Cunda the smith's son should be dispelled in this way:’

“‘Tassa te, āvuso cunda, lābhā, tassa te suladdhaṃ yassa te tathāgato pacchimaṃ piṇḍapātaṃ paribhuñjitvā parinibbuto. Sammukhā metaṃ, āvuso cunda, bhagavato sutaṃ, sammukhā paṭiggahitaṃ – dveme piṇḍapātā samasamaphalā [M 0.0183] samasamavipākā ativiya aññehi piṇḍapātehi mahapphalatarā ca mahānisaṃsatarā ca. Katame dve? Yañca piṇḍapātaṃ paribhuñjitvā tathāgato anuttaraṃ sammāsambodhiṃ abhisambujjhati, yañca piṇḍapātaṃ paribhuñjitvā anupādisesāya nibbānadhātuyā parinibbāyati. Ime dve piṇḍapātā samasamaphalā samasamavipākā ativiya aññehi piṇḍapātehi mahapphalatarā ca mahānisaṃsatarā ca.

‘“It is a gain for you, friend Cunda, it is a great good fortune for you, that the Tathāgata, after eating his final alms-food from you, attained Parinibbāna. I have heard this in the presence of the Blessed One, friend Cunda, I have received it face to face: ‘These two alms-foods are of exactly equal fruit, of exactly equal result, and are far more fruitful and of greater benefit than any other alms-foods. Which two? The alms-food after eating which the Tathāgata awakens to unsurpassed, perfect enlightenment, and the alms-food after eating which he attains Parinibbāna with the Nibbāna-element without residue remaining. These two alms-foods are of exactly equal fruit, of exactly equal result, and are far more fruitful and of greater benefit than any other alms-foods.’”

“‘Āyusaṃvattanikaṃ āyasmatā cundena kammāraputtena kammaṃ upacitaṃ, vaṇṇasaṃvattanikaṃ āyasmatā cundena kammāraputtena kammaṃ upacitaṃ, sukhasaṃvattanikaṃ āyasmatā cundena kammāraputtena kammaṃ upacitaṃ, saggasaṃvattanikaṃ āyasmatā cundena kammāraputtena kammaṃ upacitaṃ, yasasaṃvattanikaṃ āyasmatā cundena kammāraputtena kammaṃ upacitaṃ, ādhipateyyasaṃvattanikaṃ āyasmatā cundena kammāraputtena kammaṃ upacita’nti. Cundassānanda, kammāraputtassa evaṃ vippaṭisāro [T 0.0215] paṭivinodetabbo”ti.

‘“The Venerable Cunda, the smith’s son, has accumulated kamma conducive to long life, kamma conducive to beauty, kamma conducive to happiness, kamma conducive to heaven, kamma conducive to fame, and kamma conducive to sovereignty.” Ānanda, the remorse of Cunda the smith’s son should be dispelled in this way.’”

Atha [V 0.0170] kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Dadato puññaṃ pavaḍḍhati,

Saṃyamato veraṃ na cīyati;

Kusalo ca jahāti pāpakaṃ,

Rāgadosamohakkhayā sanibbuto”ti parinibbutoti (sī. syā. pī.). pañcamaṃ;

“For the giver, merit increases; for the restrained, enmity is not accumulated; the skillful one abandons evil; through the destruction of lust, hatred, and delusion, one is extinguished.”

6. Pāṭaligāmiyasuttaṃ

6. The Discourse at Pāṭaligāma

76. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā magadhesu cārikaṃ caramāno mahatā bhikkhusaṅghena saddhiṃ yena pāṭaligāmo tadavasari. Assosuṃ kho pāṭaligāmiyā pāṭaligāmikā (dī. ni. 2.148) upāsakā – “bhagavā kira magadhesu cārikaṃ caramāno mahatā bhikkhusaṅghena saddhiṃ pāṭaligāmaṃ anuppatto”ti. Atha kho pāṭaligāmiyā upāsakā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu. Ekamantaṃ nisinnā kho pāṭaligāmiyā upāsakā bhagavantaṃ [P 0.0086] etadavocuṃ – “adhivāsetu no, bhante, bhagavā āvasathāgāra”nti. Adhivāsesi bhagavā tuṇhībhāvena.

76. Thus have I heard. At one time, the Blessed One was wandering on tour in the Magadhan country with a large Saṅgha of monks and arrived at Pāṭaligāma. The lay followers of Pāṭaligāma heard: “The Blessed One, it is said, while wandering on tour in the Magadhan country with a large Saṅgha of monks, has arrived at Pāṭaligāma.” Then the lay followers of Pāṭaligāma approached the Blessed One; having approached and paid homage to the Blessed One, they sat down at a suitable place. Seated at a suitable place, the lay followers of Pāṭaligāma said this to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, may the Blessed One consent to our rest house.” The Blessed One consented by way of silence.

Atha [M 0.0184] kho pāṭaligāmiyā upāsakā bhagavato adhivāsanaṃ viditvā uṭṭhāyāsanā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā yenāvasathāgāraṃ tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā sabbasanthariṃ āvasathāgāraṃ santharitvā āsanāni paññāpetvā udakamaṇikaṃ patiṭṭhāpetvā telappadīpaṃ āropetvā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhaṃsu. Ekamantaṃ ṭhitā kho pāṭaligāmiyā upāsakā bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ – “sabbasantharisanthataṃ sabbasanthariṃ santhataṃ (sī. syā. pī.), bhante, āvasathāgāraṃ; āsanāni [T 0.0216] paññattāni; udakamaṇiko patiṭṭhāpito udakamaṇikaṃ patiṭṭhāpitaṃ (syā.) telappadīpo āropito. Yassadāni, bhante, bhagavā kālaṃ maññatī”ti.

Then the lay followers of Pāṭaligāma, knowing the Blessed One’s consent, rose from their seats, paid homage to the Blessed One, circumambulated him to the right, and approached the rest house. Having approached, they spread the rest house with a complete layer of coverings, prepared the seats, placed a water pot, and lit an oil lamp. Then they approached the Blessed One; having approached and paid homage to the Blessed One, they stood at a suitable place. Standing at a suitable place, the lay followers of Pāṭaligāma said this to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, the rest house is completely spread with coverings, the seats are prepared, the water pot is placed, and the oil lamp is lit. Now, venerable sir, it is for the Blessed One to know the time.”

Atha kho bhagavā nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya saddhiṃ bhikkhusaṅghena yena āvasathāgāraṃ tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā pāde pakkhāletvā āvasathāgāraṃ pavisitvā majjhimaṃ thambhaṃ nissāya puratthābhimukho nisīdi. Bhikkhusaṅghopi kho pāde pakkhāletvā āvasathāgāraṃ pavisitvā pacchimaṃ bhittiṃ nissāya puratthābhimukho nisīdi bhagavantaṃyeva purakkhatvā. Pāṭaligāmiyāpi kho upāsakā pāde pakkhāletvā āvasathāgāraṃ pavisitvā puratthimaṃ bhittiṃ nissāya pacchimābhimukhā [V 0.0171] nisīdiṃsu bhagavantaṃyeva purakkhatvā. Atha kho bhagavā pāṭaligāmiye upāsake āmantesi –

Then the Blessed One, having adjusted his lower robe, taking his bowl and outer robe, approached the rest house together with the Saṅgha of monks. Having approached, he washed his feet, entered the rest house, and sat down facing east, leaning against the central pillar. The Saṅgha of monks also washed their feet, entered the rest house, and sat down facing east, leaning against the western wall, with the Blessed One right in front of them. The lay followers of Pāṭaligāma also washed their feet, entered the rest house, and sat down facing west, leaning against the eastern wall, with the Blessed One right in front of them. Then the Blessed One addressed the lay followers of Pāṭaligāma:

“Pañcime, gahapatayo, ādīnavā dussīlassa sīlavipattiyā. Katame pañca? Idha, gahapatayo, dussīlo sīlavipanno pamādādhikaraṇaṃ mahatiṃ bhogajāniṃ nigacchati. Ayaṃ paṭhamo ādīnavo dussīlassa sīlavipattiyā.

“Householders, there are these five dangers for an immoral person in the failure of virtue. What five? Here, householders, an immoral person who has failed in virtue, on account of negligence, incurs a great loss of wealth. This is the first danger for an immoral person in the failure of virtue.

“Puna caparaṃ, gahapatayo, dussīlassa sīlavipannassa pāpako kittisaddo abbhuggacchati. Ayaṃ dutiyo ādīnavo dussīlassa sīlavipattiyā.

“Furthermore, householders, for an immoral person who has failed in virtue, an evil reputation spreads. This is the second danger for an immoral person in the failure of virtue.

“Puna caparaṃ, gahapatayo, dussīlo sīlavipanno yaññadeva parisaṃ upasaṅkamati – yadi khattiyaparisaṃ, yadi brāhmaṇaparisaṃ, yadi gahapatiparisaṃ, yadi samaṇaparisaṃ – avisārado upasaṅkamati maṅkubhūto. Ayaṃ tatiyo ādīnavo [P 0.0087] dussīlassa sīlavipattiyā.

“Furthermore, householders, whatever assembly an immoral person who has failed in virtue approaches—be it an assembly of nobles, an assembly of brahmins, an assembly of householders, or an assembly of ascetics—he approaches without confidence, abashed. This is the third danger for an immoral person in the failure of virtue.

“Puna [T 0.0217] caparaṃ, gahapatayo, dussīlo sīlavipanno sammūḷho kālaṃ karoti. Ayaṃ catuttho ādīnavo dussīlassa sīlavipattiyā.

“Furthermore, householders, an immoral person who has failed in virtue dies confused. This is the fourth danger for an immoral person in the failure of virtue.

“Puna [M 0.0185] caparaṃ, gahapatayo, dussīlo sīlavipanno kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā apāyaṃ duggatiṃ vinipātaṃ nirayaṃ upapajjati. Ayaṃ pañcamo ādīnavo dussīlassa sīlavipattiyā. Ime kho, gahapatayo, pañca ādīnavā dussīlassa sīlavipattiyā.

“Furthermore, householders, an immoral person who has failed in virtue, with the breaking up of the body, after death, is reborn in a state of deprivation, a bad destination, a place of downfall, in hell. This is the fifth danger for an immoral person in the failure of virtue. These, householders, are the five dangers for an immoral person in the failure of virtue.

“Pañcime, gahapatayo, ānisaṃsā sīlavato sīlasampadāya. Katame pañca? Idha, gahapatayo, sīlavā sīlasampanno appamādādhikaraṇaṃ mahantaṃ bhogakkhandhaṃ adhigacchati. Ayaṃ paṭhamo ānisaṃso sīlavato sīlasampadāya.

“Householders, there are these five benefits for a virtuous person in the accomplishment of virtue. What five? Here, householders, a virtuous person who is accomplished in virtue, on account of diligence, acquires a great mass of wealth. This is the first benefit for a virtuous person in the accomplishment of virtue.

“Puna caparaṃ, gahapatayo, sīlavato sīlasampannassa kalyāṇo kittisaddo abbhuggacchati. Ayaṃ dutiyo ānisaṃso sīlavato sīlasampadāya.

“Furthermore, householders, for a virtuous person who is accomplished in virtue, a good reputation spreads. This is the second benefit for a virtuous person in the accomplishment of virtue.

“Puna caparaṃ, gahapatayo, sīlavā sīlasampanno yaññadeva parisaṃ upasaṅkamati – yadi khattiyaparisaṃ[V 0.0172], yadi brāhmaṇaparisaṃ, yadi gahapatiparisaṃ, yadi samaṇaparisaṃ – visārado upasaṅkamati amaṅkubhūto. Ayaṃ tatiyo ānisaṃso sīlavato sīlasampadāya.

“Furthermore, householders, whatever assembly a virtuous person who is accomplished in virtue approaches—be it an assembly of nobles, an assembly of brahmins, an assembly of householders, or an assembly of ascetics—he approaches with confidence, unabashed. This is the third benefit for a virtuous person in the accomplishment of virtue.

“Puna caparaṃ, gahapatayo, sīlavā sīlasampanno asammūḷho kālaṅkaroti. Ayaṃ catuttho ānisaṃso sīlavato sīlasampadāya.

“Furthermore, householders, a virtuous person who is accomplished in virtue dies unconfused. This is the fourth benefit for a virtuous person in the accomplishment of virtue.

“Puna caparaṃ, gahapatayo, sīlavā sīlasampanno kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā sugatiṃ saggaṃ lokaṃ upapajjati. Ayaṃ pañcamo ānisaṃso sīlavato sīlasampadāya. Ime kho, gahapatayo, pañca ānisaṃsā sīlavato sīlasampadāyā”ti.

“Furthermore, householders, a virtuous person who is accomplished in virtue, with the breaking up of the body, after death, is reborn in a good destination, in the heavenly world. This is the fifth benefit for a virtuous person in the accomplishment of virtue. These, householders, are the five benefits for a virtuous person in the accomplishment of virtue.”

Atha [T 0.0218] kho bhagavā pāṭaligāmiye upāsake bahudeva rattiṃ dhammiyā kathāya sandassetvā samādapetvā samutejetvā sampahaṃsetvā uyyojesi – “abhikkantā kho, gahapatayo, ratti; yassadāni tumhe kālaṃ maññathā”ti. “evaṃ bhante“ti khopāṭaligāmiyā upāsakā bhagavato paṭissutvā (mahāva. 285; dī. ni. 2.151) Atha kho pāṭaligāmiyā upāsakā bhagavato bhāsitaṃ abhinanditvā anumoditvā “evaṃ bhante“ti khopāṭaligāmiyā upāsakā bhagavato paṭissutvā (mahāva. 285; dī. ni. 2.151) uṭṭhāyāsanā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā pakkamiṃsu. Atha kho bhagavā acirapakkantesu pāṭaligāmiyesu upāsakesu suññāgāraṃ pāvisi.

Then the Blessed One, having instructed, established, roused, and gladdened the lay followers of Pāṭaligāma with a Dhamma talk for a great part of the night, dismissed them, saying: “The night is far advanced, householders. Now it is for you to know the time.” Then the lay followers of Pāṭaligāma, having delighted in and rejoiced at the Blessed One’s words, rose from their seats, paid homage to the Blessed One, circumambulated him to the right, and departed. Then, not long after the lay followers of Pāṭaligāma had departed, the Blessed One entered a secluded dwelling.
