Tena [M 0.0186] kho pana samayena sunidhavassakārā sunīdhavassakārā (sī. syā. pī.) magadhamahāmattā pāṭaligāme nagaraṃ māpenti [P 0.0088] vajjīnaṃ paṭibāhāya. Tena kho pana samayena sambahulā devatāyo sahassasahasseva sahasseva (syā. ka.), sahassasseva (pī.) pāṭaligāme vatthūni pariggaṇhanti. Yasmiṃ padese mahesakkhā devatā vatthūni pariggaṇhanti mahesakkhānaṃ tattha raññaṃ rājamahāmattānaṃ cittāni namanti nivesanāni māpetuṃ. Yasmiṃ padese majjhimā devatā vatthūni pariggaṇhanti majjhimānaṃ tattha raññaṃ rājamahāmattānaṃ cittāni namanti nivesanāni māpetuṃ. Yasmiṃ padese nīcā devatā vatthūni pariggaṇhanti nīcānaṃ tattha raññaṃ rājamahāmattānaṃ cittāni namanti nivesanāni māpetuṃ.

At that time, Sunidha and Vassakāra, the chief ministers of Magadha, were building a city at the village of Pāṭali to ward off the Vajjīs. At that time, numerous deities, in their thousands, were taking possession of sites in the village of Pāṭali. In whatever region deities of great power took possession of sites, the minds of kings and royal chief ministers of great power inclined toward building dwellings there. In whatever region deities of middling power took possession of sites, the minds of kings and royal chief ministers of middling power inclined toward building dwellings there. In whatever region deities of low power took possession of sites, the minds of kings and royal chief ministers of low power inclined toward building dwellings there.

Addasā kho bhagavā dibbena cakkhunā visuddhena atikkantamānusakena tā devatāyo sahassasahasseva pāṭaligāme vatthūni pariggaṇhantiyo. Yasmiṃ padese mahesakkhā devatā vatthūni pariggaṇhanti, mahesakkhānaṃ tattha raññaṃ rājamahāmattānaṃ cittāni namanti [T 0.0219] nivesanāni māpetuṃ. Yasmiṃ padese majjhimā devatā vatthūni pariggaṇhanti, majjhimānaṃ tattha raññaṃ rājamahāmattānaṃ cittāni namanti nivesanāni māpetuṃ. Yasmiṃ padese nīcā devatā vatthūni pariggaṇhanti, nīcānaṃ [V 0.0173] tattha raññaṃ rājamahāmattānaṃ cittāni namanti nivesanāni māpetuṃ. Atha kho bhagavā tassā rattiyā paccūsasamaye paccuṭṭhāya āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi –

The Blessed One, with the divine eye, which is pure and surpasses the human, saw those deities in their thousands taking possession of sites in the village of Pāṭali. In whatever region deities of great power take possession of sites, the minds of kings and royal chief ministers of great power incline toward building dwellings there. In whatever region deities of middling power take possession of sites, the minds of kings and royal chief ministers of middling power incline toward building dwellings there. In whatever region deities of low power take possession of sites, the minds of kings and royal chief ministers of low power incline toward building dwellings there. Then, the Blessed One, arising toward the end of that night, addressed the Venerable Ānanda:

“Ke nu kho ko nu kho (sabbattha) ānanda pāṭaligāme nagaraṃ māpentī”ti māpetīti (sabbattha). “Sunidhavassakārā, bhante, magadhamahāmattā pāṭaligāme nagaraṃ māpenti vajjīnaṃ paṭibāhāyā”ti. “Seyyathāpi, ānanda, devehi tāvatiṃsehi saddhiṃ mantetvā; evameva kho, ānanda, sunidhavassakārā magadhamahāmattā pāṭaligāme nagaraṃ māpenti vajjīnaṃ paṭibāhāya. Idhāhaṃ, ānanda, addasaṃ dibbena cakkhunā visuddhena atikkantamānusakena sambahulā devatāyo sahassasahasseva pāṭaligāme vatthūni pariggaṇhantiyo. Yasmiṃ padese mahesakkhā devatā vatthūni pariggaṇhanti mahesakkhānaṃ tattha raññaṃ rājamahāmattānaṃ cittāni namanti nivesanāni māpetuṃ. Yasmiṃ padese majjhimā devatā vatthūni pariggaṇhanti majjhimānaṃ tattha raññaṃ rājamahāmattānaṃ cittāni namanti nivesanāni māpetuṃ. Yasmiṃ [M 0.0187] padese nīcā devatā vatthūni pariggaṇhanti nīcānaṃ tattha raññaṃ rājamahāmattānaṃ cittāni namanti nivesanāni māpetuṃ. Yāvatā, ānanda, ariyaṃ āyatanaṃ yāvatā vaṇippatho idaṃ agganagaraṃ [T 0.0220] bhavissati pāṭaliputtaṃ puṭabhedanaṃ. Pāṭaliputtassa kho, ānanda, tayo antarāyā bhavissanti – aggito vā udakato vā mithubhedato vā”ti.

“Ānanda, who is building a city at the village of Pāṭali?” “Venerable sir, the chief ministers of Magadha, Sunidha and Vassakāra, are building a city at the village of Pāṭali to ward off the Vajjīs.” “Ānanda, it is as if they had consulted with the Tāvatiṃsa deities; even so, Ānanda, the chief ministers of Magadha, Sunidha and Vassakāra, are building a city at the village of Pāṭali to ward off the Vajjīs. Here, Ānanda, I saw with the divine eye, which is pure and surpasses the human, numerous deities in their thousands taking possession of sites in the village of Pāṭali. In whatever region deities of great power take possession of sites, the minds of kings and royal chief ministers of great power incline toward building dwellings there. In whatever region deities of middling power take possession of sites, the minds of kings and royal chief ministers of middling power incline toward building dwellings there. In whatever region deities of low power take possession of sites, the minds of kings and royal chief ministers of low power incline toward building dwellings there. Ānanda, as far as the noble domain extends, as far as commerce reaches, this Pāṭaliputta will be a chief city, a center for the distribution of goods. But, Ānanda, three dangers will befall Pāṭaliputta: from fire, from water, or from internal discord.”

Atha kho sunidhavassakārā magadhamahāmattā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavatā saddhiṃ sammodiṃsu. Sammodanīyaṃ kathaṃ sārāṇiyaṃ sārāṇīyaṃ (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.) vītisāretvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhaṃsu. Ekamantaṃ ṭhitā kho sunidhavassakārā [P 0.0089] magadhamahāmattā bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ – “adhivāsetu no bhavaṃ gotamo ajjatanāya bhattaṃ saddhiṃ bhikkhusaṅghenā”ti. Adhivāsesi bhagavā tuṇhībhāvena.

Then the chief ministers of Magadha, Sunidha and Vassakāra, approached the Blessed One. Having approached, they exchanged greetings with the Blessed One. After concluding a courteous and memorable conversation, they stood to one side. Standing to one side, the chief ministers of Magadha, Sunidha and Vassakāra, said this to the Blessed One: “May the honorable Gotama accept our meal for today, together with the Saṅgha of bhikkhus.” The Blessed One consented by remaining silent.

Atha kho sunidhavassakārā magadhamahāmattā bhagavato adhivāsanaṃ viditvā yena sako āvasatho tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā sake āvasathe paṇītaṃ khādanīyaṃ bhojanīyaṃ paṭiyādāpetvā bhagavato kālaṃ ārocesuṃ – “kālo, bho gotama, niṭṭhitaṃ bhatta”nti.

Then the chief ministers of Magadha, Sunidha and Vassakāra, having understood the Blessed One’s consent, went to their own residence. Having gone there, they had excellent hard and soft food prepared in their own residence and had the time announced to the Blessed One: “It is time, honorable Gotama, the meal is ready.”

Atha kho bhagavā pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya saddhiṃ bhikkhusaṅghena yena sunidhavassakārānaṃ magadhamahāmattānaṃ āvasatho tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā paññatte āsane nisīdi. Atha kho sunidhavassakārā magadhamahāmattā buddhappamukhaṃ bhikkhusaṅghaṃ paṇītena khādanīyena bhojanīyena sahatthā santappesuṃ sampavāresuṃ.

Then the Blessed One, having dressed in the morning, taking his bowl and robe, went with the Saṅgha of bhikkhus to the residence of Sunidha and Vassakāra, the chief ministers of Magadha. Having arrived, he sat down on the prepared seat. Then Sunidha and Vassakāra, the chief ministers of Magadha, with their own hands served and satisfied the Saṅgha of bhikkhus, headed by the Buddha, with excellent hard and soft food.

Atha [V 0.0174] kho sunidhavassakārā magadhamahāmattā bhagavantaṃ bhuttāviṃ onītapattapāṇiṃ aññataraṃ nīcaṃ āsanaṃ gahetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu. Ekamantaṃ nisinne kho sunidhavassakāre magadhamahāmatte bhagavā imāhi gāthāhi anumodi –

Then, when the Blessed One had finished his meal and withdrawn his hand from the bowl, the chief ministers of Magadha, Sunidha and Vassakāra, took a low seat and sat to one side. To Sunidha and Vassakāra, the chief ministers of Magadha, who were seated to one side, the Blessed One gave his blessing with these verses:

“Yasmiṃ [T 0.0221] padese kappeti, vāsaṃ paṇḍitajātiyo;

Sīlavantettha bhojetvā, saññate brahmacārayo brahmacārino (syā.), brahmacariye (pī. ka.).

“In whatever region a wise person makes his dwelling, there, having fed the virtuous, the restrained, those who practice the holy life,

“Yā [M 0.0188] tattha devatā āsuṃ, tāsaṃ dakkhiṇamādise;

Tā pūjitā pūjayanti, mānitā mānayanti naṃ.

“Whatever deities were there, to them a share of the offering should be dedicated; they, being worshipped, worship him; being honored, they honor him.

“Tato naṃ anukampanti, mātā puttaṃva orasaṃ;

Devatānukampito poso, sadā bhadrāni passatī”ti.

“Therefore, they have compassion for him, as a mother for her own-born son; a person looked upon with compassion by the deities always sees good fortune.”

Atha kho bhagavā sunidhavassakārānaṃ magadhamahāmattānaṃ imāhi gāthāhi anumoditvā uṭṭhāyāsanā pakkāmi.

Then the Blessed One, having given his blessing to Sunidha and Vassakāra, the chief ministers of Magadha, with these verses, rose from his seat and departed.

Tena kho pana samayena sunidhavassakārā magadhamahāmattā bhagavantaṃ piṭṭhito piṭṭhito anubandhā honti – “yenajja samaṇo gotamo dvārena nikkhamissati taṃ ‘gotamadvāraṃ’ nāma bhavissati. Yena titthena gaṅgaṃ nadiṃ tarissati taṃ ‘gotamatitthaṃ’ nāma bhavissatī”ti.

At that time, the chief ministers of Magadha, Sunidha and Vassakāra, were following closely behind the Blessed One, thinking: “Whichever gate the ascetic Gotama exits through today, that will be called the ‘Gotama Gate,’ and whichever ford he uses to cross the Ganges River, that will be called the ‘Gotama Ford.’”

Atha kho bhagavā yena dvārena nikkhami taṃ ‘gotamadvāraṃ’ [P 0.0090] nāma ahosi. Atha kho bhagavā yena gaṅgā nadī tenupasaṅkami. Tena kho pana samayena gaṅgā nadī pūrā hoti samatittikā kākapeyyā. Appekacce manussā nāvaṃ pariyesanti, appekacce uḷumpaṃ pariyesanti, appekacce kullaṃ bandhanti apārā pāraṃ gantukāmā. Atha kho bhagavā – seyyathāpi nāma balavā puriso samiñjitaṃ vā bāhaṃ pasāreyya, pasāritaṃ vā bāhaṃ samiñjeyya, evameva – gaṅgāya nadiyā orimatīre orimatīrā (bahūsu) mahāva. 286; dī. ni. 2.154 passitabbaṃ) antarahito pārimatīre paccuṭṭhāsi saddhiṃ bhikkhusaṅghena[T 0.0222].

Then the Blessed One went out by the gate that came to be known as the Gotama Gate. Then the Blessed One approached the Ganges River. At that time, the Ganges River was full, level with the banks, and fit for a crow to drink from. Some people, desiring to go from the near shore to the far shore, were looking for a boat, some were looking for a log raft, and some were binding together a raft. Then the Blessed One—just as a strong man might stretch out his bent arm or bend his outstretched arm—even so, he vanished from the near shore of the Ganges River and reappeared on the far shore together with the Saṅgha of bhikkhus.

Addasā kho bhagavā te manusse appekacce nāvaṃ pariyesante, appekacce uḷumpaṃ pariyesante, appekacce kullaṃ bandhante apārā pāraṃ gantukāme.

The Blessed One saw those people desiring to go from the near shore to the far shore: some seeking a boat, some seeking a log raft, and some binding together a float.

Atha [V 0.0175] kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, understanding the meaning of this, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Ye taranti aṇṇavaṃ saraṃ,

Setuṃ katvāna visajja pallalāni;

Kullañhi jano pabandhati bandhati (syā. pī.),

Tiṇṇā nitiṇṇā (ka.) medhāvino janā”ti. chaṭṭhaṃ;

“They cross the ocean, the flood, having made a causeway and left the marshlands behind. While a common person binds a raft, the wise have crossed over.”

7. Dvidhāpathasuttaṃ

7. The Sutta on the Forked Path

77. Evaṃ [M 0.0189] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā kosalesu addhānamaggapaṭipanno hoti āyasmatā nāgasamālena pacchāsamaṇena. Addasā kho āyasmā nāgasamālo antarāmagge dvidhāpathaṃ dvedhāpathaṃ (sī.). Disvāna bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “ayaṃ, bhante, bhagavā pantho; iminā gacchāmā”ti. Evaṃ vutte, bhagavā āyasmantaṃ nāgasamālaṃ etadavoca – “ayaṃ, nāgasamāla, pantho; iminā gacchāmā”ti.

77. Thus have I heard. On one occasion the Blessed One was traveling along a highway in the Kosala country with the Venerable Nāgasamāla as his attendant monk. The Venerable Nāgasamāla saw a fork in the road. Seeing it, he said to the Blessed One, “Venerable sir, Blessed One, this is the path; let us go by this one.” When this was said, the Blessed One said to the Venerable Nāgasamāla, “This, Nāgasamāla, is the path; let us go by this one.”

Dutiyampi…pe… tatiyampi kho āyasmā nāgasamālo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “ayaṃ, bhante, bhagavā pantho; iminā gacchāmā”ti[T 0.0223]. Tatiyampi kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ nāgasamālaṃ etadavoca – “ayaṃ, nāgasamāla, pantho; iminā gacchāmā”ti. Atha kho āyasmā nāgasamālo bhagavato pattacīvaraṃ tattheva chamāyaṃ nikkhipitvā pakkāmi – “idaṃ, bhante, bhagavato pattacīvara”nti.

For a second time… and for a third time, the Venerable Nāgasamāla said to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, Blessed One, this is the path; let us go by this one.” For a third time, the Blessed One said to the Venerable Nāgasamāla: “This, Nāgasamāla, is the path; let us go by this one.” Then the Venerable Nāgasamāla, placing the Blessed One’s bowl and robe right there on the ground, departed, saying: “Here, venerable sir, is the Blessed One’s bowl and robe.”

Atha kho āyasmato nāgasamālassa tena panthena gacchantassa antarāmagge corā nikkhamitvā hatthehi ca pādehi ca ākoṭesuṃ pattañca bhindiṃsu saṅghāṭiñca vipphālesuṃ. Atha kho [P 0.0091] āyasmā nāgasamālo bhinnena pattena vipphālitāya saṅghāṭiyā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho āyasmā nāgasamālo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “idha mayhaṃ, bhante, tena panthena gacchantassa antarāmagge corā nikkhamitvā hatthehi ca pādehi ca ākoṭesuṃ, pattañca bhindiṃsu, saṅghāṭiñca vipphālesu”nti.

Then, as the Venerable Nāgasamāla was going along that path, robbers came out on the road and struck him with their hands and feet, broke his bowl, and tore his outer robe. Then the Venerable Nāgasamāla, with his broken bowl and torn outer robe, approached the Blessed One. Having approached and paid homage to the Blessed One, he sat down to one side. Seated to one side, the Venerable Nāgasamāla said to the Blessed One: “Here, venerable sir, as I was going along that path, robbers came out on the road and struck me with their hands and feet, broke my bowl, and tore my outer robe.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, understanding the meaning of this, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Saddhiṃ [V 0.0176] caramekato vasaṃ,

Misso aññajanena vedagū;

Vidvā pajahāti pāpakaṃ,

Koñco khīrapakova ninnaga”nti. sattamaṃ;

“Though traveling with, dwelling together, or mixing with an ignorant person, the knower, the wise one, abandons what is evil, just as the milk-drinking heron leaves the water.”

8. Visākhāsuttaṃ

8. The Sutta on Visākhā

78. Evaṃ [M 0.0190] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati pubbārāme migāramātupāsāde. Tena kho pana samayena visākhāya migāramātuyā nattā kālaṅkatā hoti piyā manāpā. Atha kho visākhā migāramātā allavatthā allakesā divā divassa yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho visākhaṃ migāramātaraṃ bhagavā etadavoca [T 0.0224] –

78. Thus have I heard. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in the Eastern Park, in the mansion of Migāra’s mother. Now at that time Visākhā, Migāra’s mother, had a granddaughter who was beloved and dear, and who had passed away. Then Visākhā, Migāra’s mother, with wet clothes and wet hair, in the middle of the day, approached the Blessed One; having approached, she paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. The Blessed One then said to Visākhā, Migāra’s mother, as she sat to one side:

“Handa kuto nu tvaṃ, visākhe, āgacchasi allavatthā allakesā idhūpasaṅkantā divā divassā”ti? “Nattā me, bhante, piyā manāpā kālaṅkatā. Tenāhaṃ allavatthā allakesā idhūpasaṅkantā divā divassā”ti. “Iccheyyāsi tvaṃ, visākhe, yāvatikā yāvatakā (?) sāvatthiyā manussā tāvatike tāvatake (?) putte ca nattāro cā”ti? “Iccheyyāhaṃ, bhagavā iccheyyāhaṃ bhante bhagavā (syā.) yāvatikā sāvatthiyā manussā tāvatike putte ca nattāro cā”ti.

“Now then, Visākhā, where do you come from, with wet clothes and wet hair, having approached here in the middle of the day?” “My beloved and agreeable granddaughter, venerable sir, has passed away. That is why I, with wet clothes and wet hair, have approached here in the middle of the day.” “Would you wish, Visākhā, to have as many sons and grandsons as there are people in Sāvatthī?” “I would wish, Blessed One, to have as many sons and grandsons as there are people in Sāvatthī.”

“Kīvabahukā pana, visākhe, sāvatthiyā manussā devasikaṃ kālaṃ karontī”ti? “Dasapi, bhante, sāvatthiyā manussā devasikaṃ kālaṃ karonti; navapi, bhante… aṭṭhapi, bhante… sattapi, bhante… chapi, bhante… pañcapi, bhante… cattāropi, bhante… tīṇipi, bhante… dvepi, bhante, sāvatthiyā manussā devasikaṃ kālaṃ karonti. Ekopi, bhante, sāvatthiyā manusso devasikaṃ kālaṃ karoti. Avivittā, bhante, sāvatthi manussehi kālaṃ karontehī”ti.

“Visākhā, how many people in Sāvatthī pass away each day?” “Venerable sir, ten people in Sāvatthī pass away each day; nine, venerable sir… eight, venerable sir… seven, venerable sir… six, venerable sir… five, venerable sir… four, venerable sir… three, venerable sir… two people in Sāvatthī pass away each day. Even one person, venerable sir, in Sāvatthī passes away each day. Sāvatthī is never devoid of people passing away, venerable sir.”

“Taṃ kiṃ maññasi, visākhe, api nu tvaṃ kadāci karahaci anallavatthā vā bhaveyyāsi anallakesā vā”ti? “No hetaṃ, bhante[P 0.0092]. Alaṃ me, bhante, tāva bahukehi puttehi ca nattārehi cā”ti.

“What do you think, Visākhā? Would you then ever at any time be with clothes unwet or hair unwet?” “Certainly not, venerable sir. Enough for me, venerable sir, with so many sons and grandsons.”

“Yesaṃ [V 0.0177][T 0.0225] kho, visākhe, sataṃ piyāni, sataṃ tesaṃ dukkhāni; yesaṃ navuti piyāni, navuti tesaṃ dukkhāni; yesaṃ asīti piyāni, asīti tesaṃ dukkhāni; yesaṃ sattati piyāni, sattati tesaṃ dukkhāni; yesaṃ saṭṭhi piyāni, saṭṭhi tesaṃ dukkhāni; yesaṃ paññāsaṃ piyāni, paññāsaṃ tesaṃ dukkhāni; yesaṃ cattārīsaṃ piyāni, cattārīsaṃ tesaṃ dukkhāni, yesaṃ tiṃsaṃ piyāni, tiṃsaṃ tesaṃ dukkhāni; yesaṃ vīsati piyāni, vīsati tesaṃ dukkhāni, yesaṃ dasa [M 0.0191] piyāni, dasa tesaṃ dukkhāni; yesaṃ nava piyāni, nava tesaṃ dukkhāni; yesaṃ aṭṭha piyāni, aṭṭha tesaṃ dukkhāni; yesaṃ satta piyāni, satta tesaṃ dukkhāni; yesaṃ cha piyāni, cha tesaṃ dukkhāni; yesaṃ pañca piyāni, pañca tesaṃ dukkhāni; yesaṃ cattāri piyāni, cattāri tesaṃ dukkhāni; yesaṃ tīṇi piyāni, tīṇi tesaṃ dukkhāni; yesaṃ dve piyāni, dve tesaṃ dukkhāni; yesaṃ ekaṃ piyaṃ, ekaṃ tesaṃ dukkhaṃ; yesaṃ natthi piyaṃ, natthi tesaṃ dukkhaṃ, asokā te virajā anupāyāsāti vadāmī”ti.

“Visākhā, for those who have a hundred dear things, there are a hundred sufferings; for those who have ninety dear things, there are ninety sufferings; for those who have eighty dear things, there are eighty sufferings; for those who have seventy dear things, there are seventy sufferings; for those who have sixty dear things, there are sixty sufferings; for those who have fifty dear things, there are fifty sufferings; for those who have forty dear things, there are forty sufferings; for those who have thirty dear things, there are thirty sufferings; for those who have twenty dear things, there are twenty sufferings; for those who have ten dear things, there are ten sufferings; for those who have nine dear things, there are nine sufferings; for those who have eight dear things, there are eight sufferings; for those who have seven dear things, there are seven sufferings; for those who have six dear things, there are six sufferings; for those who have five dear things, there are five sufferings; for those who have four dear things, there are four sufferings; for those who have three dear things, there are three sufferings; for those who have two dear things, there are two sufferings; for one who has one dear thing, there is one suffering; for those who have no dear thing, there is no suffering. They, I say, are sorrowless, stainless, and without despair.”

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Ye keci sokā paridevitā vā,

Dukkhā ca dukkhā va (aṭṭha.) lokasmimanekarūpā;

Piyaṃ paṭiccappabhavanti ete,

Piye asante na bhavanti ete.

“Whatever sorrows, lamentations, and sufferings of many kinds there are in the world, these arise in dependence on what is dear. When what is dear does not exist, these do not come to be.

“Tasmā hi te sukhino vītasokā,

Yesaṃ [T 0.0226] piyaṃ natthi kuhiñci loke;

Tasmā asokaṃ virajaṃ patthayāno,

Piyaṃ na kayirātha kuhiñci loke”ti. aṭṭhamaṃ;

“Therefore, they are happy and free from sorrow, those for whom there is nothing dear anywhere in the world. Therefore, one aspiring to the sorrowless, stainless state should not make anything dear anywhere in the world.”

9. Paṭhamadabbasuttaṃ

9. The First Discourse on Dabba

79. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā rājagahe viharati veḷuvane kalandakanivāpe. Atha kho āyasmā dabbo mallaputto yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho āyasmā dabbo mallaputto bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “parinibbānakālo me dāni, sugatā”ti. “Yassadāni tvaṃ, dabba, kālaṃ maññasī”ti.

79. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Rājagaha, in the Bamboo Grove, the Squirrels’ Feeding Place. Then the Venerable Dabba Mallaputta approached the Blessed One, paid homage to him, and sat down to one side. Sitting to one side, the Venerable Dabba Mallaputta said to the Blessed One: “Now is the time for my final extinguishment, O Sugata.” “Now, Dabba, is the time for that which you deem fit.”

Atha [V 0.0178] kho āyasmā dabbo mallaputto uṭṭhāyāsanā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā vehāsaṃ abbhuggantvā ākāse antalikkhe pallaṅkena nisīditvā tejodhātuṃ samāpajjitvā vuṭṭhahitvā parinibbāyi.

Then the Venerable Dabba Mallaputta, rising from his seat, paid homage to the Blessed One, circumambulated him keeping him to his right, and rose into the air. Sitting cross-legged in the sky, in mid-air, he entered the fire element, emerged from it, and attained final extinguishment.

Atha [M 0.0192] kho āyasmato dabbassa mallaputtassa vehāsaṃ [P 0.0093] abbhuggantvā ākāse antalikkhe pallaṅkena nisīditvā tejodhātuṃ samāpajjitvā vuṭṭhahitvā parinibbutassa sarīrassa jhāyamānassa ḍayhamānassa neva chārikā paññāyittha na masi. Seyyathāpi nāma sappissa vā telassa vā jhāyamānassa ḍayhamānassa neva chārikā paññāyati na masi; evameva āyasmato dabbassa mallaputtassa vehāsaṃ abbhuggantvā ākāse antalikkhe pallaṅkena nisīditvā tejodhātuṃ samāpajjitvā vuṭṭhahitvā parinibbutassa sarīrassa jhāyamānassa ḍayhamānassa neva chārikā [T 0.0227] paññāyittha na masīti.

Then, for the Venerable Dabba Mallaputta, who had risen into the air, sat cross-legged in the sky, in mid-air, entered the fire element, emerged from it, and attained final extinguishment—as his body was burning and being consumed, neither ash nor soot was apparent. Just as when ghee or oil is burning and being consumed, neither ash nor soot is apparent, so too for the Venerable Dabba Mallaputta, who had risen into the air, sat cross-legged in the sky, in mid-air, entered the fire element, emerged from it, and attained final extinguishment—as his body was burning and being consumed, neither ash nor soot was apparent.

Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Abhedi kāyo nirodhi saññā,

Vedanā sītibhaviṃsu pītidahaṃsu (sī. pī.), sītidahiṃsu (ka.) sabbā;

Vūpasamiṃsu saṅkhārā,

Viññāṇaṃ atthamāgamā”ti. navamaṃ;

“The body broke apart, perception ceased, all feelings became cool, all formations were pacified, consciousness came to an end.”

10. Dutiyadabbasuttaṃ

10. The Second Discourse on Dabba

80. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tatra kho bhagavā bhikkhū āmantesi – “bhikkhavo”ti. “Bhadante”ti te bhikkhū bhagavato paccassosuṃ. Bhagavā etadavoca –

80. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. There the Blessed One addressed the monks: “Monks!” “Venerable Sir,” those monks replied to the Blessed One. The Blessed One said this:

“Dabbassa, bhikkhave, mallaputtassa vehāsaṃ abbhuggantvā ākāse antalikkhe pallaṅkena nisīditvā tejodhātuṃ samāpajjitvā vuṭṭhahitvā parinibbutassa sarīrassa jhāyamānassa ḍayhamānassa neva chārikā paññāyittha na masi. Seyyathāpi nāma sappissa vā telassa vā jhāyamānassa ḍayhamānassa neva chārikā paññāyati na masi; evameva kho, bhikkhave, dabbassa mallaputtassa vehāsaṃ abbhuggantvā ākāse antalikkhe pallaṅkena nisīditvā tejodhātuṃ [V 0.0179] samāpajjitvā vuṭṭhahitvā parinibbutassa sarīrassa jhāyamānassa ḍayhamānassa neva chārikā paññāyittha na masī”ti.

“Monks, for Dabba Mallaputta, who had risen into the air, sat cross-legged in the sky, in mid-air, entered the fire element, emerged from it, and attained final extinguishment—as his body was burning and being consumed, neither ash nor soot was apparent. Just as when ghee or oil is burning and being consumed, neither ash nor soot is apparent, so too, monks, for Dabba Mallaputta, who had risen into the air, sat cross-legged in the sky, in mid-air, entered the fire element, emerged from it, and attained final extinguishment—as his body was burning and being consumed, neither ash nor soot was apparent.”

Atha [M 0.0193] kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then, the Blessed One, having understood this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Ayoghanahatasseva, jalato jātavedaso jātavedassa (syā.);

Anupubbūpasantassa, yathā na ñāyate gati.

“Just as of a blazing fire struck by an iron hammer, as it is gradually extinguished, its destination is not known;

Evaṃ [T 0.0228] sammāvimuttānaṃ, kāmabandhoghatārinaṃ;

Paññāpetuṃ gati natthi, pattānaṃ acalaṃ sukha”nti. dasamaṃ;

“So it is for those perfectly liberated, who have crossed the flood of the bond of sensuality; there is no destination to be designated for those who have attained the unmoving bliss.”

Pāṭaligāmiyavaggo pāṭaligāmavaggo (ka.) aṭṭhamo.

The Pāṭaligāmiya Chapter, the eighth.

Tassuddānaṃ [P 0.0094] –

The summary for that chapter:

Nibbānā caturo vuttā, cundo pāṭaligāmiyā;

Dvidhāpatho visākhā ca, dabbena saha te dasāti.

Four on Nibbāna are spoken, Cunda, Pāṭaligāmiya, the Twofold Path, and Visākhā; together with Dabba, they are ten.

Udāne vaggānamuddānaṃ –

The summary of chapters in the Udāna:

Vaggamidaṃ paṭhamaṃ varabodhi, vaggamidaṃ dutiyaṃ mucalindo;

Nandakavaggavaro tatiyo tu, meghiyavaggavaro ca catuttho.

This first chapter is the excellent Bodhi, this second chapter is Mucalinda; the excellent Nandaka chapter is the third, and the excellent Meghiya chapter is the fourth.

Pañcamavaggavarantidha soṇo, chaṭṭhamavaggavaranti jaccandho chaṭṭhamavaggavaraṃ tu tamandho (sī. ka.);

Sattamavaggavaranti ca cūḷo, pāṭaligāmiyamaṭṭhamavaggo pāṭaligāmiyavaraṭṭhamavaggo (syā. kaṃ. pī.), pāṭaligāmavaraṭṭhamavaggo (sī. ka.).

Here, the excellent fifth chapter is Soṇa, the excellent sixth chapter is Jaccandha; the excellent seventh chapter is Cūḷa, the Pāṭaligāmiya is the eighth chapter.

Asītimanūnakasuttavaraṃ, vaggamidaṭṭhakaṃ suvibhattaṃ;

Dassitaṃ cakkhumatā vimalena, addhā hi taṃ udānamitīdamāhu atthāyetaṃ udānamitimāhu (ka.), saddhā hi taṃ udānantidamāhu (syā. kaṃ pī.).

The eighty excellent and complete discourses, this well-divided collection of eight chapters, was taught by the Visionary, the Stainless One. Truly, this is what is called the Udāna.

Udānapāḷi niṭṭhitā.

The Udāna Pāḷi is concluded.
